# Internal Architecture

## System Overview

The Slack Channel Router is a two-way bridge between Slack and Claude Code sessions via Socket Mode + MCP HTTP (StreamableHTTP). The server runs the persona configuration last applied (`config.json.last-applied`, a byte copy of `config.json` recorded when it was applied; `config.json` itself at a start with no record) and holds one Slack connection per persona: a Socket Mode client plus a Web API client, built from that persona's own credentials file by the connection manager (`src/persona-connections.ts`). Each persona runs one Claude Code instance under agent-director, `cscb_<key>`, in its own working directory. Each Claude Code session connects to its own MCP Server instance and is matched to one persona by its working directory. One server runs every persona; a persona that can't come up stays down on its own while the others serve.

## Module Map

```
cli.ts                          CLI entry point — start/stop/clean_restart/credentials subcommands. `start`, `stop` and `clean_restart` take every setting they use and their persona set from the configuration the server runs, through `readAppliedPersonaConfig` (`reload.ts`): the last-applied record beside the configuration file (`resolveServerConfigPath`, state directory from `resolveServerStateDir`) when it exists, else the configuration file. The CLI never writes a reload file, and its process reads no Slack token and no credentials file (b.av2 SR-8.7, SR-10.2). `credentials <persona>` (b.av2 SR-12, the setup wizard's credentials command) finds the persona by name or key (`resolvePersonaTarget`) in the configuration file as it stands (`loadPersonaConfig`, never the record, so a persona declared but not yet confirmed is found), prints the persona and its `credentials_file`, and runs the packaged `scripts/write-credentials.sh` (`CREDENTIALS_SCRIPT_PATH`) with `bash` for that path on the operator's terminal (stdio inherited; SIGINT and SIGQUIT ignored while it runs, as a shell does for a foreground job), exiting with the script's status. It exits 2 with the usage line unless given exactly one persona, and 1 when the configuration file can't be loaded (the loader's error) or no persona has that name or key (the declared ones listed, the argument never repeated); it needs no server and no agent-director. `start` checks only that the config file or its last-applied record exists, spawns the daemon and waits for its startup outcome (see [start command](#start-command)). `stop --stop-bots` and clean_restart share a `teardownBots(personas, exit_timeout)` closure that runs one pause/status/kill teardown per persona of the config (SR-11 Event 12), addressing each persona's instance directly as `cscb_<key>` (`personaInstanceId`); log lines name the persona as its JSON-quoted name with its key (`renderPersonaRef`). All file, spawn, clock and agent-director access goes through the injectable `CliDeps`.
└── server.ts                   Main entry point — HTTP server, per-persona Slack connection wiring. Embeds the SR-5.1 startup gate, SR-3.2 template install, the b.av2 SR-6.3 start sweep (`reconcileOrphans`, handed the persona config), SR-2.1 poller. At import it only computes the state paths (`resolveServerStateDir` / `resolveServerConfigPath`), builds its injected-dependency objects and registers the SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers: it builds no Slack client, reads no token and creates no directory (b.av2 SR-3.1, SR-13.1). `main()` creates the state and inbox directories, installs E2's `unhandledRejection` handler (`createUnhandledRejectionHandler`, once), then, after the startup gate and the PID check, builds the reload controller (`createReloadController`, `reload.ts`) and resolves the start through it (`resolveStart`): the applied persona config is the last-applied record when there is one, else `config.json`, which is recorded first, and a refused start exits 1 (see Server-Managed Startup step 2a). The controller also gets the production detection-tick driver (`createReloadTickDriver`, `reload-timer.ts`), `isDryRun()`, the bring-up controller's `credentialsDigest` as `heldCredentialsDigest` and its `state` as `bringUpState`, the session manager's `checkLaunchConfigDir` as `checkConfigDir` (the preview's check of an added persona's `claude_config_dir`), and a `lifecycle` whose `startBringUp` is the start pass and whose `teardown`, `updateInPlace`, `reconnectCredentials`, `refreshTemplate` and `bringUp` forward to the persona lifecycle (`createPersonaLifecycle`, `persona-lifecycle.ts`), which `main()` builds right after the bring-up controller with the production dependencies (its template refresh gets the params the boot template install wrote, `installSlackChannelBotTemplate`'s `params`, and `getClient`), its `isShuttingDown` bound to the same `shuttingDown` flag restart and the health check read; `main()` calls `startDetection()` as its last statement, right after `startHealthCheck`, and `shutdown()` calls `stopDetection()` right after `stopHealthCheck()` (see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83)). The module-level `personaConfig` is assigned from that outcome and, after that, only by the reload controller's `onApplied` at a confirmed apply's step 1 (`configInEffect`: the confirmed persona set over the start-time server-wide values, kept as `appliedConfig`), so every consumer below, reading it at call time, sees the applied set, never an unconfirmed `config.json` edit. `isPersonaUp` and the bring-up controller also get the applied-set check (`isApplied`, `appliedPersonas`; see Server-Managed Startup step 5, applied-set guard). Right after the resolution it installs `trustPatchPersona` (`trust-bootstrap.ts`) as the session manager's pre-launch trust patcher and, right after that, `preLaunchReplyGuard` (`stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`) as its pre-launch reply guard, bound to `STATE_DIR` and the applied persona set read at each launch — both before the template install, the Slack connections, `Bun.serve` and restart init, so no launch path runs unpatched, and hands the persona config to the persona-keyed consumers: the template install, the start sweep, the trust, JSONL-safeguard and Stop-hook start passes (startup steps 4a-bis, 4a-quater and 4a-ter; the Stop-hook pass also gets `STATE_DIR`), the start bring-up (run as the controller's `runStartBringUp`), restart (guards, backoff, cap, relaunch), the health-check work list, outage state, the per-persona notifier and the cron dispatcher. It builds one connection manager (`createPersonaConnectionManager`) whose event handler is the event router (`persona-event-router.ts`) and whose status listener (`composePersonaStatusListeners`) both flushes a persona's held notices when it comes up (`createPersonaUpFlushListener`) and tells the bring-up controller about every status change; the manager is closed on shutdown with `stopAll()`. Over the manager it builds the bring-up controller (`createPersonaBringUpController`, `persona-bringup-controller.ts`), whose launch after a successful retry is `spawnForPersona(persona, personaConfig ?? appliedConfig, false)`; shutdown cancels its retries with `cancelAll()`. Over the manager and the controller it builds the relaunch gate (`createPersonaRelaunchGate`, `canRelaunch`) and hands it to restart (`RestartDeps.canRestart`), the restart launch (`launchSession`) and the health-check work list (`buildPersonaWorkList`), so none of them touches a persona that is not up. At module scope it builds one up predicate (`isPersonaUp`, `createPersonaUpPredicate` over the manager view and the controller's `isUp`; false for every persona until `main()` builds the controller) and hands that same function to MCP session admission (`decideSessionAdmission` in `handleInitialized`), the permission poller (`PollerDeps.isPersonaUp`) and `/interject`, so every serving path reads one "is this persona up" state (b.av2 SR-6.4); `server.ts` holds no up-check logic of its own. The controller's `onLeftUp` is `createNotUpSessionDropper` over `dropPersonaSessionAndKeepAlive` (`dropPersonaSession` plus stopping the session's SSE keep-alive), so a persona that stops being up loses its registered MCP session and nothing else (see Session Lifecycle, Disconnection). At module scope it builds the one per-persona lifecycle serializer (`personaLifecycle`, `createPersonaSerializer`, `persona-serializer.ts`; b.av2 SR-6.6) and passes its `run` to restart (`RestartDeps.serialize`), the connection manager (`serialize`: only the network close of the socket a Web API bot-token refusal detached goes through it, bug b.ujn), the bring-up controller and the persona lifecycle (see Session Lifecycle, [Per-persona serialization](#per-persona-serialization-bav2-sr-66)). Restart's delay (`getRestartDelay`) reads `session_restart_delay` only from the applied persona config. `clientFor(key)` (`createPersonaClientLookup`) returns the persona's long-lived Web client from the manager while it is serving, and the bot-identity lookup (`createPersonaIdentityLookup`) its bot user and bot ID; both answer nothing before `main()` builds the manager. The same `clientFor` and the applied-persona lookup (`getAppliedPersona`) go to the notifier, the MCP tools, the permission poller, the event router's click path and the archive's per-persona name resolver. One destination resolver (`personaDestinations`, `createPersonaDestinations` from `persona-destination.ts`) is built at module scope, side-effect-free (an empty DM cache, no Slack call), and goes to both the notifier and the permission poller, so a persona's DM destination is opened once for both. Right after it, one destination hold (`personaDestinationHold`, `createPersonaDestinationHold` from `persona-destination-hold.ts`) is built at module scope over that resolver, `getAppliedPersona`, `clientFor` and the real clock, also side-effect-free (no Slack call, no timer), and goes to both as `destinationHold`, so one persona's prompts and notices share one failure episode; shutdown drops its held notices and timers with `cancelAll()`. It builds the one persona-routing instance (`persona-routing.ts`) over `clientFor`, the persona config, the identity lookup, the per-persona archive writer, `getReplySettings()` and a `notify` that calls the per-persona notifier's `notify`. The routing is built before the notifier, so `notify` is a lambda that reads the notifier at call time rather than at import. `getReplySettings()` is the one source of the server-wide reply settings (`ack_reaction`, `reply_chunk_limit`, `reply_chunk_mode`; b.av2 SR-1.6) for both the routing's ack step and the MCP tools (`SessionToolDeps.getReplySettings`): it returns `replySettingsOf(personaConfig)` (`config.ts`) at call time, so they are the start-time values (a confirmed apply keeps them, see [Next-launch and server-wide settings](#next-launch-and-server-wide-settings-bav2-sr-86)), and before `main()` resolves the start it answers no reaction and the default chunking. It never reads the reload controller's applied config or `config.json`. The server reads and writes no access-control file; an `access.json` in the state directory is ignored and left in place. The kill, liveness and reconnect adapters address a session as `cscb_<key>`; the kill adapter (`_buildKillSessionAdapter`) skips the kill while `isLaunchInFlight(key)` (see Auto-Restart step 4), and the reconnect adapter (`_buildReconnectSessionAdapter`) defers a `working` row while `isLaunchInFlight(key)` or while its tmux session is alive (see Auto-Restart step 3). The `/interject` route only wires the handler: it calls `handleInterject` (`interject.ts`) with the caller's address, the applied persona config, `isPersonaUp` and `getSessionByPersona`; the handler logic lives in that module. `isHttpVerbose()` (b.3k6) gates the per-request `/mcp` access line behind `CSCB_HTTP_VERBOSE` (truthy: 1/true/yes/on) — off by default, checked per request.
    ├── config.ts               The persona configuration loader — load, validate, defaults, tilde expansion. SR-4.1 agent_director_poll_interval_ms field. SR-4.2 unknown-field rejection; `describeUnknownKeys` names an unknown key only when it matches `ECHOABLE_KEY_NAME_RE` (letters and underscores, up to 48 characters) and counts the rest, so a pasted token is never echoed (b.av2 SR-10.3). The persona loader (b.av2 SR-1) is the only loader; the pre-persona keys appear only in the conversion-error check (`PRE_PERSONA_KEYS`). `parsePersonaConfigBytes(bytes, source, configDir, { home?, record? })` is the one validating JSON-parse path: it parses and validates the exact bytes that were read, so the bytes the reload controller records or applies are the bytes it checked (b.av2 SR-8.7). Record mode (`record: true`, used for the last-applied record) skips only the real-path collision step (b.av2 SR-1.5). The cross-persona rules include the prefix-related key rule (b.1ix follow-up): no persona's key may start with another's, checked by `personaKeysPrefixRelated`, with a new name for the persona with the shorter key from `suggestNonPrefixingName` (both exported, both pure); see the persona config section. `readPersonaConfigBytes` reads a file once and throws `PersonaConfigReadError` (with the errno code when it is safe to echo) on failure. It is stat-first, with the credentials check's rule: it opens the path read-only and non-blocking, stats the descriptor and reads only a regular file through it, so a FIFO or device at the configuration file or the record never hangs the start or the CLI. A directory fails with code `EISDIR`, any other non-regular file with `not a regular file` (`CONFIG_NOT_REGULAR_FILE_CODE`). It enforces the 64 KiB size cap (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES`, worded `MAX_RELOAD_FILE_SIZE_TEXT`; code `EFBIG`, `FILE_TOO_LARGE_CODE`), shared with `readCredentialsFile`; its third argument, `ReadPersonaConfigBytesOptions` (`{ uncapped?: boolean }`), lifts the cap for the file guard's read only. See Configuration, Reload files, Size cap. `configReadFailurePredicate(code)` is the one wording of a read failure after a file's name (`does not exist`, `is larger than the 64 KiB limit`, or `cannot be read (<code>)`), used by the start's messages, the record error, the invalid candidate and the stale-confirmation line. Its file-system seam is `PersonaConfigFs` (default `DEFAULT_PERSONA_CONFIG_FS`), which lets tests fake a FIFO or a failing call; `loadPersonaConfig(path, home?)` is that read plus a default-mode parse. `configFileReadFailureMessage` builds the start's message for a configuration file that does not exist, is larger than the cap or cannot be read, and `isMissingConfigCode` says whether a read failure means the file is absent (ENOENT, ENOTDIR). A malformed-JSON error names the file and the line and column where the text stops being JSON (`json-position.ts`) and echoes none of the text. See "Persona config" under Configuration. `resolveServerStateDir(home?, env?)` and `resolveServerConfigPath(home?, env?)` are the one definition of the state directory (`SLACK_STATE_DIR` made absolute, else `<home>/.claude/channels/slack`) and of `config.json` inside it, read at call time; `server.ts` and `cli.ts` both use them, so the CLI and the server read one configuration file and one last-applied record beside it. Exports `resolveRealPath` (b.av2 SR-1.5), the shared real-path-with-lexical-fallback comparison used by the persona collision rules (loader and bring-up checks), the stop-hook personal-config-dir refusal, the MCP session matcher and the file guard; see the persona config section. `SERVER_PATH_SETTINGS` is the one list of path-valued server-wide settings (`mcp_config_path`, `append_system_prompt_file`, `message_archive_db`, `claude_config_dir`, `cron_table_path`, `cron_log_path`): `resolveServerPaths` expands exactly these (a `satisfies` check keeps the two in step), and the reload change plan compares exactly these by real path. `tryResolveRealPath` is the same real-path lookup without the lexical fallback (undefined when realpath fails, never throws); `resolveRealPath` delegates to it, and `compareRowToPersona` (`session-manager.ts`) uses it to tell when a persona's working directory has no real path (b.av2 SR-6.4). `resolveRealPathStrict(path, fs?)` (bug b.g57) is the one for a persona's effective `claude_config_dir`, which may not exist yet: the real path when it resolves, else the nearest existing ancestor's real path plus the missing components when each fails with `ENOENT` and the first missing component does not exist either (with no symlink on the path that is the lexical form); a first missing component that exists (a symlink that points to nothing), any other error code (`EIO`, `ENOTCONN`, `ENOTDIR`, `ELOOP`, …) or no resolvable ancestor is unresolvable, returned with the errno code and never as the lexical form (realpath and lstat injectable, `StrictRealPathFs`; never throws, opens nothing). Also exports `credentialsFilesToProtect(appliedPersonas, configPath, home?)` (b.av2 SR-5.2), which lists every applied persona's `credentials_file` plus every `personas[i].credentials_file` named by the config file currently on disk (read through `readPersonaConfigBytes` with `uncapped`, the one read the size cap does not cover, so an oversized unapplied file's paths are still protected; parsed tolerantly and not validated; an unreadable, non-regular or unparseable file adds nothing; never throws); the reload controller's `protectedCredentialsFiles()` calls it for `assertSendable` to refuse. The config-file half is `referencedCredentialsPaths(bytes, home?)`: every string `personas[i].credentials_file` in the given bytes or text, in declaration order with duplicates kept, tilde-expanded and made absolute; parsed but not validated, so a file that fails validation still names its paths, and unparseable JSON or a missing `personas` array yields none. Pure and never throws. It is the one extraction of referenced credentials paths, shared by the file guard and the reload detection tick's fingerprint, and the one documented exception to parsing only through `parsePersonaConfigBytes`: its parse is tolerant, discards any parse error rather than reporting it and returns only paths, so nothing it reads is recorded, applied or echoed. `replySettingsOf(config)`: the three server-wide reply settings (`ack_reaction`, `reply_chunk_limit`, `reply_chunk_mode`) of a config, defaults when absent or when the config is null; pure. `server.ts`'s `getReplySettings()` delegates to it.
    ├── reload.ts               b.av2 SR-8.1 / SR-8.7 / SR-10.3 (part) / SR-13.1 the reload controller. `reloadFilePaths(configPath)` names the configuration file and the three reload files beside it, `config.json.pending`, `config.json.apply` and `config.json.last-applied` (pure; see Configuration, Reload files). `createReloadController(deps)` takes every dependency injected, as `createCronScheduler` does: `paths`; `lifecycle`, whose `startBringUp(applied)` (the start pass), `teardown(persona)`, `updateInPlace(change)`, `reconnectCredentials(persona, applied)`, `refreshTemplate(applied)` and `bringUp(persona, applied, options?)` are all required (the default bodies of apply steps 2–6 call the last five; production binds them to `persona-lifecycle.ts`); `log` (the server log); `write` (default `durableWriteFileSync`); `remove` (default `durableUnlinkSync`, the durable delete of `config.json.pending` and `config.json.apply`); `tickDriver` (production passes `createReloadTickDriver`, `reload-timer.ts`); `dryRun`; `heldCredentialsDigest(key)` (production binds the bring-up controller's `credentialsDigest`; without it credentials never count as changed); `bringUpState(key)` (production binds the bring-up controller's `state`; the preview asks it only for a persona whose credentials changed, to say whether it is reconnected or brought up, and apply steps 4 and 6 ask it again when they run; without it no persona counts as broken by its credentials in the preview, and the steps follow the plan's split); `credentialsFs` (the seam for the tick's credentials reads); `workingDirectoryFs` (the seam for the working-directory check of an added persona; the real file system by default); `checkConfigDir(persona)` (the `claude_config_dir` check of an added persona; production passes the session manager's `checkLaunchConfigDir`, the check the bring-up controller and every launch use, so the preview resolves the directory as the bring-up will; default `checkPersonaConfigDir` against `home` and the real file system); `slackClientFactory` (production passes `PRODUCTION_SLACK_CLIENT_FACTORY`, unused so far); `onApplied(config)` (told the new applied configuration by a confirmed apply's step 1, in the same synchronous step as the controller's own swap; production reassigns the server's `personaConfig` to `configInEffect(startTime, applied)`; a throw is logged and the apply goes on); `applySteps` (a test override: bodies of apply steps 2–6, `ApplyStepSlots` from `reload-apply.ts`, used in place of the default ones, and an unbound step then does nothing; production never sets it, so the controller runs `lifecycleApplySlots` over `lifecycle`); and `home`. The handle has `resolveStart()` (synchronous, once: the start-time choice between the record and the configuration file, writing the record when there was none; see Server-Managed Startup step 2a), `runStartBringUp()` (once, after an applied start: `lifecycle.startBringUp` over the applied set), `startDetection()` (arms the detection tick once, and only after an applied start's bring-up pass has returned; returns whether it armed it), `stopDetection()` (stops it at shutdown; idempotent), `applied()` (the applied config, the exact bytes it was validated from and whether they came from the record or the file; after a confirmed apply's step 1, the confirmed candidate with source `config`) and `protectedCredentialsFiles()` (the SR-5.2 file guard's source; see Security Model). The detection tick (b.av2 SR-8.2, SR-8.3, SR-8.7), the `config.json.pending` lifecycle, confirmation processing (SR-8.5) and the step runner of the confirmed apply (`applyConfirmed`, SR-8.6) live here; see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83). `configInEffect(startTime, applied)` (pure) is the configuration the server runs after an apply: the applied persona set over every server-wide setting of the start-time configuration, so a changed server-wide setting takes effect only at the next start (the top-level `claude_config_dir` and `stop_hook_bootstrap` reach the personas only as inherited defaults, already resolved into each persona). `readAppliedPersonaConfig(configPath, home?, fs?)` is the CLI's read-only resolver over the same rules: the record when it exists, else the configuration file; a record that exists but cannot be read or validated throws with the deletion hint and never falls back to the file. Each detection pass builds the change plan (`buildChangePlan`, `reload-plan.ts`) from the facts it gathered and writes and logs the preview rendered from it; the plan alone decides whether a credentials change is pending. `RELOAD_DIAGNOSTIC_CLASSES` (`reload-record-write-failed`, `reload-nothing-pending`, `reload-preview`, `reload-invalid`, `reload-applied`, `reload-noop`, `reload-stale-confirmation`) is the reload class list, kept apart from `PERSONA_DIAGNOSTIC_CLASSES`; `RELOAD_PREVIEW` and `RELOAD_INVALID` are defined in `reload-plan.ts`, and `RELOAD_APPLIED`, `RELOAD_NOOP` and `RELOAD_STALE_CONFIRMATION` in `reload-apply.ts`, all re-exported here. Importable without side effects (b.av2 SR-13.1): importing it creates no timer or Slack client, reads no file or environment variable and logs nothing, and the controller reads, writes and logs nothing until `resolveStart`, except `protectedCredentialsFiles()`, which reads the configuration file on each call (at any phase), uncapped, and writes and logs nothing; the detection tick reads, writes and logs only after `startDetection`. The record, the configuration file, the pending file and the confirmation are read only through `readPersonaConfigBytes` (the `configFs` seam), so a non-regular file cannot hang the start or a tick. Every one of those reads is capped except `protectedCredentialsFiles()`'s (see [Size cap](#reload-files-configjsonlast-applied-configjsonpending-configjsonapply)). The record is written only at a start without one and by a confirmed apply's step 1. The start reads no credentials file; the detection tick reads each referenced one through `readCredentialsFile` (`persona-credentials.ts`), outside dry run. Beyond those reads, a pass runs the working-directory and `claude_config_dir` checks of each added persona and resolves the real path of a path written differently in the two configurations; it makes no Slack call itself (a confirmed apply's bring-ups do, through the lifecycle operations). Its lines go to the server log only, never to Slack.
    ├── reload-plan.ts          b.av2 SR-8.4 / SR-8.6 / SR-10.3 (part) the change plan and the pending-change preview. `buildChangePlan(applied, candidate, facts)` classifies a candidate against the applied configuration in the terms of the SR-8.6 table: personas added (with why each cannot come up), removed, destructively modified, modified in place, with changed credentials, with next-launch changes (own or inherited) and unchanged, plus the changed server-wide settings (an inherited default lists the personas that inherit it). The candidate is a valid configuration, an invalid one with the loader's error, or a missing or unreadable configuration file. Every fact that needs I/O is injected as `facts`: `realPath` (required, the SR-1.5 comparison form), `home` (for the default config dir), `dryRun`, the current and held credentials digests, `credentialsProblem(path)` (why the new credentials content is not locally valid, token-free), the bring-up state, the added personas' can't-come-up causes and `configDirProblem(persona)`, why a persona's changed effective `claude_config_dir` can't be resolved (asked only for a persona whose `claude_config_dir` changed, own or inherited, a destructive modify included). Such a persona carries `configDirUnresolvable` (the problem, or `FACT_UNKNOWN`), and its line warns, as an added persona's does, without refusing anything: a next-launch line and a changed top-level `claude_config_dir` line end `; but at that launch <it|the inheriting personas> cannot come up: <problem>`, a `DESTRUCTIVE:` line `; but it cannot come up: <problem>` (bug b.g57). A credentials change carries `credentialsBroken`, `retrying` and `problem`, and its line states the real effect for each state. A fact the caller could not gather is passed as `FACT_UNKNOWN` and carried as unknown (an added persona's `cannotComeUp`, or a credentials change's `credentialsBroken` and `retrying`, is undefined), and the preview says it could not be checked. `isCredentialsBroken(state)`, re-exported from `persona-bringup-controller.ts`, is the SR-6.4 credentials-broken rule the plan applies to the bring-up state; the apply's steps 4 and 6 use the same rule. A persona whose first Slack attempt is still in flight (no outcome yet) counts as `retrying`. `configDirsChanged` tells the apply to refresh the agent-director template (SR-8.6 step 5); the apply selects step 5 whenever it is true, even for a plan with no effective change (`applyStepsFor`, `reload-apply.ts`). It is not rendered. The plan holds persona keys, names, entry positions, paths and setting names only, never a digest. `DESTRUCTIVE_SETTINGS`, `IN_PLACE_SETTINGS` and `NEXT_LAUNCH_SETTINGS` name each field's effect, and `stop_timeout` and `exit_timeout`, the server-wide settings only the CLI uses, get their own server-wide line (the CLI takes them from the record once applied); `addedPersonas(applied, candidate)` lets a caller gather facts about the added personas before building the plan. Renderers, all from the plan and never from a second diff: `changePlanCounts` and `renderChangePlanCounts` (the header's counts), `renderPreviewLines` and `renderPreview` (the body of `config.json.pending`), `renderPreviewLogLines` (the `reload-preview` log form) and `renderInvalidLogLine` (the one `reload-invalid` line). Defines `RELOAD_PREVIEW` and `RELOAD_INVALID`. Pure and deterministic (b.av2 SR-13.1): no file, Slack, agent-director, timer, log or state, nothing at import; the same plan always renders the same text. The reload detection tick builds the plan every pass (see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83)); a confirmed apply acts on the plan that pass built from the confirmed bytes, never a second diff, and its `reload-applied` line reuses the counts, so what is applied is what was previewed.
    ├── reload-apply.ts         b.av2 SR-8.5 / SR-8.6 / SR-10.3 (part) / SR-13.1 the step-ordered apply of a confirmed change and its log lines. `APPLY_STEPS` names steps 2–6 in order (`teardowns`, `in-place-updates`, `credentials-reconnects`, `template-refresh`, `bring-ups`); step 1 is the reload controller's own. `applyStepInputs(plan, previous, applied)` returns `ApplyStepInputs`: the plan's personas split by class (`removed`, `added`, `destructiveOld`, `destructiveNew`, `inPlace`, `credentials`, `credentialsBroken`), each `Persona` taken by key from the configuration that declares it (removed personas and the old half of a destructive modify from the previous configuration, everything else from the confirmed one), plus `configDirsChanged`; a step body reads only the classes it acts on. `applyStepsFor(plan)` picks the steps that run: all of them for a plan with an effect (step 5 only when `configDirsChanged`), and for a `no effective change` plan only step 5, and only when `configDirsChanged`. `runApplySteps(slots, inputs, steps, onFailure)` runs the bound bodies (`ApplyStepSlots`, one optional `ApplyStepBody` per step) in order, each awaited before the next starts; a body that throws is reported to `onFailure` and the next step still runs; it never rejects. `applyStepNumber(step)` gives a step's SR-8.6 number for log lines. `fanOutPersonas(personas, operation, onFailure)` runs one operation per persona at once and settles once every one has settled, never failing fast; each rejection goes to `onFailure` with its persona, in persona order. `lifecycleApplySlots(members, onFailure, credentialsBrokenNow?)` builds the controller's default bodies from the `teardown`, `updateInPlace`, `reconnectCredentials`, `refreshTemplate` and `bringUp` lifecycle members (`ApplyLifecycleMembers`; a failure is an `ApplyPersonaFailure`): step 2 fans `teardown` out over `removed` and `destructiveOld`, step 3 fans `updateInPlace` out over `inPlace` (each an `InPlaceApplyInput`: the persona as confirmed, its previous declaration and the changed settings; one update per persona key), step 4 fans `reconnectCredentials` out over the credentials-changed personas (`credentials` and `credentialsBroken`) that are not credentials-broken when step 4 runs, step 5 calls `refreshTemplate` once with the confirmed configuration, and step 6 fans `bringUp` out over `added` and `destructiveNew` and, with `{ recovery: true }` (`ApplyBringUpOptions`), over the credentials-changed personas that are credentials-broken when step 6 runs and that step 4 did not reconnect, all with the confirmed configuration. "Credentials-broken now" is asked of `credentialsBrokenNow(key)` (`CredentialsBrokenNowQuery`) when each step runs; with no answer, the plan's split decides. A reconnect that resolves `{ kind: 'credentials-broken' }` (`CredentialsBrokenAtReconnect`) is left to step 6. A persona with only next-launch changes is in no body's input: step 1's swap is all it needs. Renderers: `renderAppliedLogLine` (`reload-applied`), `renderNoopLogLine` (`reload-noop`), `renderConfirmedInvalidLogLine` (the confirmed `reload-invalid` line, distinct from the pending-time one) and `renderStaleConfirmationLogLine(applyPath, reason)` (`reload-stale-confirmation`, reason `mismatch`, `unreadable` with the reader's code, or `malformed`). Defines `RELOAD_APPLIED`, `RELOAD_NOOP` and `RELOAD_STALE_CONFIRMATION`. Pure (b.av2 SR-13.1): it reads and writes no file, calls neither Slack nor agent-director, arms no timer and logs nothing, and nothing runs at import; `runApplySteps` and the fan-outs only call and await the injected bodies, members and queries. Its only state is the default bodies' per-apply set of the personas step 4 reconnected, which step 6 reads. No output holds a token, credentials content, a digest or a fingerprint.
    ├── reload-fingerprint.ts   b.av2 SR-8.1 / SR-8.3 the pending-change fingerprint and the pending file's layout. `reloadFingerprint(config, credentials)` returns 64 lower-case hex digits: SHA-256 over one length-prefixed, tagged stream holding the configuration file's content and, per credentials entry in the order given, its path and content. A content is the exact bytes or the `FINGERPRINT_MISSING` / `FINGERPRINT_UNREADABLE` marker, and a marker never equals any bytes, an empty file included. The detection tick passes each distinct `credentials_file` the configuration file names (`referencedCredentialsPaths`, `config.ts`), in first-declaration order, and none in dry run. `composePendingFile(fingerprint, body)` builds the pending file (a fixed header line, a `fingerprint: sha256:<hex>` line, a blank line, then the body) and `parsePendingFingerprint(text)` recovers the fingerprint from it, or nothing. Pure: reads no file, logs nothing, holds no state; only a hash of credentials content ever leaves it.
    ├── reload-timer.ts         b.av2 SR-8.2 the production driver of the reload detection tick. `createReloadTickDriver({ log, clock? })` returns the controller's `ReloadTickDriver` (`start(tick)` / `stop()`), a serialized 5 s (`RELOAD_TICK_INTERVAL_MS`) `setTimeout` chain in the `cron-scheduler.ts` pattern. Injected: the logger and the clock (`setTimeout` / `clearTimeout`, so the shared fake clock fits). See Reload Detection Tick, The timer.
    ├── json-position.ts        b.av2 SR-10.3 where a JSON text first breaks the RFC 8259 grammar, for the persona loader's malformed-JSON error. `jsonSyntaxErrorOffset(text)` is a small iterative validating scan (no recursion, so deep nesting cannot overflow the stack) returning the offset of the first invalid character, the text length when it ends early, or undefined when it is valid; `positionAt(text, offset)` turns an offset into a 1-based line and a code-point column. The result holds numbers only, so the error never echoes file content (Bun's `JSON.parse` message can quote a pasted token and carries no position). Pure: no I/O, nothing at import.
    ├── persona-identity.ts     b.av2 SR-2.1 / SR-2.2 persona key rule and every identifier derived from the key. Key rule: a name of 1–40 chars drawn only from `a-z0-9_` is its own key; any other name is lower-cased, each run of other characters becomes one `_`, leading/trailing `_` are trimmed, the stem is truncated to 40 chars, and `_` plus the first 8 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the original name is appended (an empty stem leaves the 8 hex digits alone; max key length 49). `PERSONA_KEY_RE` (`^[a-z0-9_]{1,49}$`) matches every key, hashed keys included, and is the check before a key names a file; `PERSONA_KEY_IN_FORM_RE` only says whether a name is its own key. Derived from the key: instance ID `cscb_<key>` (keeps the `cscb_` anchor `permission-action-id.ts`'s regex relies on), tmux session name `slack_bot_<key>`, labels `service=cscb` / `persona=<key>` / `config_dir=<12 hex of SHA-256 of the effective claude_config_dir>` (`configDirLabelValue` resolves the dir lexically — tilde-expanded, made absolute, no symlink or existence check — and no configured dir means `~/.claude`; spawns don't call it directly but go through `session-manager.ts`'s `personaConfigDirLabelValue`, which real-paths the dir first, with no lexical fallback, so a symlink and its target get the same label), and spawn-environment values `CSCB_PERSONA` and `CLAUDE_MANAGED_CHANNEL` (both the key), `CSCB_CRONTABLE_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION=false` (`PROMPT_SUGGESTION_OFF_ENV`, always, last), and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` only when a dir is configured. Persona references in logs and errors render as the JSON-quoted name with the key beside it, e.g. `"Ops Bot" (key=ops_bot_5e2526f3)`. Pure (b.av2 SR-13.1): the module does no I/O and runs nothing at import; the home directory can be passed in, and is looked up from the OS only at call time when not given; must not import `config.ts`. `effectiveClaudeConfigDirs(personas, home?)` is the one rule for a persona set's effective config directories: each persona's `claude_config_dir` resolved lexically by `resolveClaudeConfigDir` (none means `<home>/.claude`), de-duplicated and sorted, and the default alone for an empty set; the template's memory-read rules and the reload plan's `configDirsChanged` both use it. Also holds the one tilde rule (`expandTilde(path, home?)`: only exactly `~` or a leading `~/` expands) and the persona target lookup (`resolvePersonaTarget(config, target)`: the applied persona whose name or key equals the target exactly — case-sensitive, untrimmed — or undefined, including for a null config) used by `/interject` and the cron dispatcher's resolver. Its importers in `src/` are `config.ts` (the tilde rule, the key and the persona reference), `persona-diagnostics.ts` / `persona-credentials.ts` / `persona-connections.ts` / `persona-notifier.ts` / `persona-routing.ts` / `registry.ts` / `trust-bootstrap.ts` / `stop-hook-bootstrap.ts` (the persona reference), `reply-guard-record.ts` (`PERSONA_KEY_RE`), `agent-director-template.ts` (the effective config-dir set, `effectiveClaudeConfigDirs`), `persona-slack-clients.ts` (the persona reference in forwarded Socket Mode library lines), `reload-plan.ts` (the effective config-dir set and the persona reference), `session-manager.ts` (spawn parameters and persona references in spawn-path logs), `jsonl-persistence-check.ts` (the instance ID, the persona reference and the default config dir), `permission-poller.ts` (the `persona` label's map key `PERSONA_LABEL_KEY` and the persona reference), `permission-click-handler.ts` (the instance ID and the persona reference), `permission-action-id.ts` (the `cscb_` instance-ID prefix), `interject.ts` (the target lookup and the persona reference), `server.ts` (the instance ID, the persona reference and the target lookup behind the cron dispatcher's `resolveTarget`) and `cli.ts` (the instance ID and the persona reference). Spawns carry the derived identifiers, and restart, the health check, outage state, server notices, the MCP session registry, the permission relay and inbound delivery (`persona-routing.ts`) are keyed by persona key.
    ├── persona-diagnostics.ts  b.av2 SR-10.3 persona diagnostic class labels and the broken-persona log line. Holds the closed label set as a type plus constants: `persona-start`, `persona-credentials-missing`, `persona-credentials-unreadable`, `persona-credentials-invalid`, `persona-credentials-refused`, `persona-slack-unreachable`, `persona-connection-lost`, `persona-connection-restored`, `persona-directory-missing`, `persona-directory-unusable`, `unclaimed-channel`, `persona-dm-dropped`, `persona-destination-failed`, `persona-credentials-change-failed`, `persona-config-dir-unresolvable`; later Epics extend it. `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` (bug b.g57) is built by `checkPersonaConfigDir` (`persona-bringup.ts`) and logged by the bring-up controller when its check right before the Slack step, or a launch, finds the persona's `claude_config_dir` unresolvable, with its cleared line when it resolves (see Server-Managed Startup step 5). `persona-credentials-change-failed` is built by `formatCredentialsChangeFailed` and logged by the bring-up controller when a confirmed credentials change can't be used (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)). `unclaimed-channel` and `persona-dm-dropped` are logged by the inbound pipeline (`persona-routing.ts`); see Data Flow, Inbound. `persona-destination-failed` is logged by the destination hold (`persona-destination-hold.ts`) once when a persona's destination failure episode starts and once when it clears; see Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures). `persona-connection-lost` is logged once when a connection that was up drops without the server closing it, and `persona-connection-restored` once when it is open again; failed reopen attempts in between log nothing, and a credentials error on reopen logs one `persona-credentials-refused` line instead of the restored line (the latch is `persona-slack-episodes.ts`). A Web API call of a running persona refused for its bot token (bug b.ujn) also logs one `persona-credentials-refused` line, with cause `bot_token refused by a Web API call (<method>): Slack error <code>`. `formatPersonaDiagnostic` builds one `[slack]` line from the class, the entry position `personas[<i>]`, the persona as its JSON-quoted name with the key beside it (`renderPersonaRef` from `persona-identity.ts`), the JSON-quoted path when there is one, and the cause, with every control character and U+2028/U+2029 escaped so the line is always one line (`escapeCause`, exported so each reload preview line stays on one line too). `personaCheckFailure` wraps that line into the failure result both checks below return, and emits it exactly once only when a logger is injected. Pure (b.av2 SR-13.1): no file-system, environment or console access, nothing at import. The lines never go to `startup-errors.log`. In the running server they are emitted by the bring-up controller (`persona-bringup-controller.ts`: the `persona-start` line, the credentials and working-directory check lines and the directory cleared line), by the connection manager and by `persona-routing.ts`. The bring-up lines are listed under Logging, Persona bring-up lines.
    ├── persona-credentials.ts  b.av2 SR-1.4 persona credentials-file reader, step 1 of the SR-6.1 bring-up. The file has a fixed shape: a JSON object with exactly two string keys, `bot_token` (prefix `xoxb-`) and `app_token` (prefix `xapp-`); each value has at least one character after its prefix and contains no whitespace or control character. `checkPersonaCredentials` finds it locally valid when it exists, is readable, parses and has that shape, and its real path is no other applied persona's credentials file. Checks run in this order: a real-path collision (`resolveRealPath`, lexical fallback) with another applied persona's credentials file makes the persona credentials-broken as `persona-credentials-invalid`, naming the other persona, and is checked first so the other persona's tokens are never parsed or used (the file is still read, stat-first, but only to hash it for the digest below); a missing path (ENOENT/ENOTDIR) is `persona-credentials-missing`; the path is then opened once, read-only and non-blocking (following symlinks), so opening a FIFO never waits for a writer, and the type check and the read both go through that one descriptor, so the file checked is the file read; anything but a regular file (a directory, FIFO, socket or device) is `persona-credentials-unreadable` and is never read, so a FIFO cannot block the read and a device cannot feed it for ever; a regular file over the size cap is `persona-credentials-unreadable` too (`credentials file is larger than the 64 KiB limit`; see Configuration, Reload files, Size cap), so its digest is the unreadable marker at bring-up and in the reload tick alike; the descriptor is always closed, and a close failure is ignored; permission denied or any other open, stat or read error is also `persona-credentials-unreadable`; content that is not JSON, not an object, lacks a key, has a non-string or wrongly prefixed value, a value with nothing after its prefix, a value containing whitespace or a control character, or any extra key is `persona-credentials-invalid`, with every problem listed. Causes name a bad token by its key and rule only; a parse failure is described generically and extra keys are counted, not named. Success returns a `PersonaSlackTokens` value that redacts itself when JSON-serialised, string-converted or inspected. All file access goes through an injectable seam (`openFile`, `fstatFile`, `readFileFd`, `closeFile`, `realpath`), with the real file system as the default; the names differ from the working-directory check's `stat` and `access` so an override of one in the combined bring-up seam never reaches the other. Nothing runs at import, and it reads no environment variable, writes nothing and checks no file mode. It is the server's only token source: step 1 of each persona's start bring-up (`checkPersonaLocalSteps` in `persona-start.ts`, run by the bring-up controller), which hands the tokens to the connection manager. It is read once per persona at start; bring-up retries reuse that read. Reader and digest (b.av2 SR-8.3): the stat-first open, type check and read above is its own export, `readCredentialsFile(path, fs?)`, returning the exact bytes or a missing/unreadable failure with its token-free cause; the seam's `readFileFd` returns bytes, decoded as UTF-8 only for validation. `credentialsDigest(read)` turns a read into what is held for it: `sha256:<hex>` over the exact bytes, or `CREDENTIALS_MISSING_MARKER` / `CREDENTIALS_UNREADABLE_MARKER`, which never equal a digest. `checkPersonaCredentialsAndDigest` returns the check's result beside the digest of what it read, valid or not. A real-path collision returns the digest too: the persona's own path is read stat-first and hashed, never parsed, and no token is taken from it, so re-saving the file can make a change pending for that persona once the collision is gone. `checkPersonaCredentials` is that check without the digest. The reload detection tick reads each referenced file through the same `readCredentialsFile`, so a FIFO or device never blocks a tick either. `credentialsReadProblem(read)` runs the content check over a read the caller already holds and returns the cause, worded as `checkPersonaCredentials` words it (the same `credentials file is invalid: ` prefix for bad content), or undefined when the file is locally valid; it skips the real-path collision rule and returns no token. The tick uses it to preview whether an added persona can come up without reading its file twice. A digest is held in memory only and never logged.
    ├── persona-bringup.ts      b.av2 SR-6.1 steps 1 and 2 as pure checks. `checkPersonaWorkingDirectory` (step 2) requires the working directory to exist, be a directory (after following symlinks), be readable and searchable, and have a real path that is no other applied persona's working directory. A missing path is `persona-directory-missing`; anything else, including a real-path collision (named with the other persona), is `persona-directory-unusable`. It reads no credentials file, so a dry-run bring-up can call it alone. `checkPersonaLocalBringUp` always runs both the credentials check and the directory check before returning, so when both fail both causes are reported: it returns both outcomes plus the failure list (credentials first) and, with an injected logger, emits each failure line exactly once. It also returns `credentialsDigest`, the digest or marker of the credentials bytes step 1 read, whatever step 1's outcome; for a real-path collision the persona's own file is only hashed, never parsed. It reuses `persona-credentials.ts`'s real-path collision and errno helpers rather than duplicating them. No Slack or agent-director call, no environment access, no writes, no timers, nothing at import; file access goes through an injectable seam (stat, access, realpath), and the combined check's seam also covers the credentials check's descriptor-based open, stat, read and close. The server runs them as steps 1 and 2 of each persona's start bring-up (`checkPersonaLocalSteps` in `persona-start.ts`, called by the bring-up controller): the combined check normally, the directory check alone in dry run. The controller also calls `checkPersonaWorkingDirectory` alone for each directory re-check. `checkPersonaConfigDir(persona, { home?, fs?, log? })` (bug b.g57) is the pre-launch check of the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (none means `<home>/.claude`) through `resolveRealPathStrict`: `{ ok: true, realPath }` (a directory not created yet under a resolvable parent passes, with the real path it will have) or a `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` failure naming the configured directory and the errno code; it never throws. A failure also carries `problem`, its cause without the held persona's consequence (`claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (<errno>[: a symlink on its path points to nothing])`), which the reload preview words itself. The session manager runs it before every launch (`checkLaunchConfigDir`), the bring-up controller runs it right before each Slack step (bring-up and directory retry) and re-checks a held persona with it, and the reload detection tick runs it on each added persona.
    ├── persona-slack-validation.ts  b.av2 SR-3.2 classifier for a persona's Slack token validation: `auth.test` checks `bot_token`, opening Socket Mode checks `app_token`. Three outcome classes: up, Slack-unreachable (retried for ever with backoff) and credentials-broken. `botIdentityFromAuthTest` returns up only when `auth.test` gave both the bot user ID and the bot ID (SR-3.1); a result lacking either is Slack-unreachable. `classifySlackValidationError(error, check)` maps a thrown or rejected value to one of the two failure outcomes. Slack-unreachable (`persona-slack-unreachable`) covers a network, DNS or request-timeout error, a non-200 HTTP status (429 included), a rate-limited error, a `start()` whose WebSocket phase was abandoned after 10 s (the `SlackStartTimeoutError` marker, SR-3.3), no WebSocket URL, a socket closed before `hello`, any other Socket Mode error, any unrecognised value, and a `PlatformError` of `internal_error`, `fatal_error`, `service_unavailable`, `request_timeout` or `ratelimited`. A `PlatformError` whose Slack error code is missing, empty or not a short lower-case identifier is also Slack-unreachable (reason `unknown`): the library builds such errors itself from a 200 answer that isn't JSON, such as a proxy page. Only a `PlatformError` carrying a well-formed Slack error code outside those five is credentials-broken (`persona-credentials-refused`), carrying the key and the Slack error code. The "no WebSocket URL" reason applies only to the library's plain, code-less error from the Socket Mode open; any other code-less failure is reported as an unrecognised failure (reason `unknown`, still Slack-unreachable). An unreachable outcome carries a reason kind, plus the HTTP status, the transient Slack error code or `retryAfter` where they apply, and, when the thrown value had a message (an error's `message`, or a thrown string), that message rendered by `describeLogMessage` as `message`: `message="…"`, redacted by `redactSlackLogText`, on one line, capped at 300 characters (the rendering every other persona error line uses, E14 operator decision B1). CSCB's own timeout markers carry none. The cause text never holds error text. Library errors are recognised by their `code` field, never by `instanceof`, so stub errors classify the same way. The classifier never throws. `slackRetryAfterSeconds(error)` reads the `retryAfter` a rejected Web API call carries (the library's rate-limited error, or a platform error whose code is `ratelimited`), copying nothing else; the classifier and the destination hold both use it. Waits come from `persona-retry-schedule.ts` (5 s doubling to 300 s, no cap, never shorter than `retryAfter`) and log lines from `persona-slack-episodes.ts`. Pure and injectable (SR-13.1): no timers, clock, Slack client, I/O or environment access, nothing at module scope. The connection manager (`persona-connections.ts`) calls it for every attempt. `classifyWebApiAuthFailure(error, method)` (bug b.ujn) is the running-persona counterpart used by `persona-web-api-watch.ts`: a refused outcome of the `web-api` check (`bot_token`) only for a platform error whose Slack error is exactly `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `account_inactive` or `not_authed`, undefined for anything else. Its cause names the method when it matches `SLACK_METHOD_NAME_RE`, e.g. `bot_token refused by a Web API call (chat.postMessage): Slack error token_revoked`. It copies nothing from the error but the Slack error code.
    ├── persona-retry-schedule.ts  b.av2 SR-3.2 backoff for the Slack-unreachable class. `createPersonaRetrySchedule` returns one independent schedule per persona and retry purpose; it holds only its own failure count. `nextDelayMs(retryAfterSeconds?)` records a failure and returns the next wait in milliseconds: 5 s doubling to a 300 s ceiling (5, 10, 20 … 300 s), with no attempt cap. When a `retryAfter` is given the wait is never shorter than it, clamped only at the largest delay a JavaScript timer honours (about 24.8 days). `reset()` returns to 5 s once the cause clears. The arithmetic is `backoff.ts`'s stateless `doublingBackoffDelay`; the session-restart counters (keyed by persona key), 900 s ceiling and 5-failure cap are not used. Returns waits only: no timers or clock, so the caller schedules through its injected timers (SR-13.1). The connection manager (`persona-connections.ts`) keeps one per persona for bring-up retries and Socket Mode reopens, and one per pending credentials reconnect, the bring-up controller (`persona-bringup-controller.ts`) one per persona for working-directory re-checks, and the destination hold (`persona-destination-hold.ts`) one per persona for destination retries.
    ├── persona-slack-episodes.ts  b.av2 SR-10.3 cause-start / cause-clear latch for Slack validation log lines, so `persona-slack-unreachable` is logged when the cause starts and when it clears, not on every attempt. `createSlackEpisodeTracker` takes one persona (name, key, entry position, credentials-file path, optional logger) and `record(outcome)` returns the lines it produced; `end(reason)` closes an open episode without an outcome (the attempts it covered were abandoned, such as a cancelled credentials reconnect) with one line carrying its class label and the cause `cleared: <reason>`, and does nothing when none is open. An episode is a run of outcomes of one class. When an episode starts it emits one line (`persona-slack-unreachable`, or `persona-credentials-refused` naming the key, check and Slack error code). When it ends, because the persona comes up or the class changes, it emits one cleared line with the same class label and a cause starting `cleared: `. A change of reason within Slack-unreachable stays in the same episode. A third episode kind, connection lost, opens when a connection that was up drops: it emits one `persona-connection-lost` line, absorbs every Slack-unreachable reopen outcome without a line, and closes with one `persona-connection-restored` line when the persona is up again; a refusal during it ends it silently and emits the `persona-credentials-refused` line in place of the restored line. Lines are built by `formatPersonaDiagnostic` from the outcome's own fields; a `persona-slack-unreachable` start line ends with the outcome's redacted `message` after the cause when it has one, and no cleared, lost or restored line carries one. With an injected logger each is emitted exactly once. No module-scope state or timers, so two personas' trackers are independent. The connection manager (`persona-connections.ts`) drives it, one tracker per persona plus one for each pending credentials reconnect, and is the only emitter of these lines for its personas, except that the bring-up controller logs a later refusal of a reconnect left retrying (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)). A connection stopped and brought up again gets a fresh tracker.
    ├── persona-slack-clients.ts  b.av2 SR-3.3 / SR-13.1 Slack client factory and option sets for persona connections. Three client kinds, each built through the injectable `PersonaSlackClientFactory`: the long-lived Socket Mode client (app token); a short-lived validation Web API client used only for `auth.test` (bot token); and the long-lived Web API client every posting path uses (bot token), including `reply` uploads and `download_attachment`. Settings, in prose: library auto-reconnect is off on the Socket Mode client. The Socket Mode client's own Web API calls and the validation client make no retries, time out after 10 s and reject rate-limited calls instead of waiting. The long-lived Web API client times out each attempt after 30 s and keeps the library's default retry policy (ten retries with randomised backoff over about 30 minutes) and its rate-limit waiting. None of the three attaches the original request to its errors. Library loggers: the Socket Mode client gets `socketModeSlackLogger(persona, log)` in place of the library's console logger; it drops every line except an allowlist of the library's fixed-text connection-health lines (ping and pong timeouts, and `Failed to send ping to Slack` with its error detail dropped), which it hands to the connection manager's `log` as `[slack] persona Socket Mode: personas[<i>] "<name>" (key=<key>): <text>`, at or above the level the library sets (default INFO); a throwing sink is swallowed. The socket client's own `clientOptions` (for `apps.connections.open`) carry no logger, so that client inherits the socket's. The validation and long-lived Web API clients get `redactingSlackLogger()`, which writes to the console as the library's console logger does (`[LEVEL]  web-api:WebClient …`, default INFO) after replacing URL-like substrings with `<redacted-url>` and token-like ones with `<redacted-token>` in every argument (non-strings via `util.inspect`); `redactSlackLogText` is the redaction, and its token pattern is deliberately wide. Why: the library defaults reconnect and retry on their own (the socket client retries opening up to 100 times), set no request timeout, and attach the raw request error, Authorization header included, to request errors; under Bun the socket library prints a failed WebSocket handshake's error text, which holds the WebSocket URL with its connection ticket, at its default level into `server.log`; and a Web API client's failed-request line on a 429 holds the request URL, which for a `filesUploadV2` upload is Slack's short-lived upload URL. Each option builder returns a fresh object, so no option object is shared between clients or personas. `PRODUCTION_SLACK_CLIENT_FACTORY` is the only place a token is handed to a Slack library, and it passes the token and options through unchanged. Also holds the 10 s `start()` bound and the 10 s / 30 s request-timeout constants. Also holds the integration suite's Slack API base URL override (see `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` under Environment Variables): `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride(env, log)` reads it and `loopbackSlackApiUrl` decides whether it is honoured; each option builder takes an optional `slackApiUrl` and, given one, adds it and changes nothing else (the socket client carries it inside its `clientOptions`). Nothing is constructed, read or logged at import; the environment is read only through the `env` passed to `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride`, and the loggers built here write only when a client calls them.
    ├── slack-log-redaction.ts  b.av2 SR-10.3 the URL-like and token-like text redactor. `redactSlackLogText(text)` replaces every `http`/`https`/`ws`/`wss` URL-like substring (any case, trailing punctuation kept) with `<redacted-url>`, then every `xox<letter>-` or `xapp-` run with `<redacted-token>` (deliberately wide: no boundary, nothing required after the dash). Used by `redactingSlackLogger` in `persona-slack-clients.ts` (which re-exports it), by the permission click handler on the trail's `raw_error_message`, by `describeLogMessage` in `persona-connection-errors.ts` (so by every describer of a thrown value) and by the session manager's spawn-failure notice. Pure: no imports, no module-scope state, nothing at import.
    ├── persona-connection-errors.ts  b.av2 SR-3.3 / SR-10.3 token-safe description of thrown values and the process-level `unhandledRejection` handler. `describeThrownValue` renders any thrown or rejected value as one line holding only the constructor name (or the value's type for a non-`Error`), the `code` when it is a short identifier (letters, digits, `_`, `$`; a Slack token contains `-` and can never pass; `isSafeIdentifier`), for a base `AgentDirectorError` (matched by constructor name, so the module imports nothing from agent-director) its `errName` under the same check, the message as `message="…"` (`describeLogMessage`), and up to 10 stack frame lines after the message has been cut out of the stack: `<Type>[ errName=<errName>][ code=<code>][ message="…"][ <frame> <- <frame> …]`. A thrown string renders as `string message="…"`; any other non-`Error` value by its type only. `describeLogMessage(text)` is the one rendering of free text in a describer's output: the text through `redactSlackLogText`, line breaks collapsed to one space, cut to `MAX_LOGGED_MESSAGE_LENGTH` (300) characters ending in `…` when cut, and JSON-quoted as `message="…"` (empty for a non-string or blank text). The describer never reads `original`, headers, request config or `data`, never serialises the value and never throws. A caller whose thrown value can quote a secret-holding file (a credentials file's JSON parse error) does not pass it here: `persona-credentials.ts` words its own fixed causes. `describeSlackCallFailure` builds the tail of a failed-Slack-call log line: ` (reason=<reason>)` when the error carries a Slack platform error code that is a safe identifier (`slackPlatformReason`), then `: ` and the `describeThrownValue` description; the permission poller and click handler use it for every Slack-call failure line. `createUnhandledRejectionHandler(log)` returns a listener that logs one `[slack] unhandled rejection (process keeps running): …` line built with the describer and returns: it never throws (a throwing logger is swallowed), rethrows or exits, so one persona's stray rejection cannot take the process down. The module never installs it; `server.ts` installs it once with `process.on`, at the top of `main()`, before any persona connects. Pure: no module-scope state, no environment access, nothing at import.
    ├── persona-connections.ts  b.av2 SR-3.1 (module part) / SR-3.2 / SR-3.3 / SR-3.4 / SR-10.3 / SR-13.1 supervised per-persona Slack connection manager. Per persona, `createPersonaConnectionManager` validates, then connects: a short-lived validation client runs `auth.test` and is discarded, then one long-lived Web API client and one long-lived Socket Mode client are built. The three client kinds and their settings are in the `persona-slack-clients.ts` entry. A persona is up only once `auth.test` returned both the bot user ID and the bot ID and the socket's `start()` resolved. The manager bounds a bring-up's `auth.test` at 10 s on its injected clock (`SLACK_AUTH_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), because the validation client's own request timeout starts only once the connection is made and so does not cover a DNS or TCP-connect stall; on expiry the call is abandoned and the attempt is Slack-unreachable (`SlackAuthTestTimeoutError`, reason `timeout`). Each `start()` bounds its WebSocket phase at 10 s; one that never settles is abandoned and counts as Slack-unreachable, not broken. Slack-unreachable is retried with the `persona-retry-schedule.ts` backoff; a refusal leaves the persona credentials-broken. Reopen: when an up persona's socket drops and the manager didn't close it, the first reopen attempt is immediate and later ones use the same backoff; a credentials error on reopen leaves the persona disconnected and credentials-broken. **Revoked bot token while running** (bug b.ujn): the socket runs on the app token, so a revoked bot token would leave it up while every Web API call fails. Every long-lived Web API client the manager hands out is therefore wrapped by `watchWebClientAuth` (`persona-web-api-watch.ts`). The first call on it rejected with `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `account_inactive` or `not_authed` latches that client, and, when it is still the persona's current client (a client replaced by a reconnect swap, `replaceRetryTokens` or a fresh bring-up marks nothing) of a persona neither stopped nor already broken, marks the persona like a refused reopen, synchronously in that rejection's handler (`markRefusedWhileRunning`): its retry timer and any attempt in flight are cancelled, its live socket is detached (it forwards nothing more), one `persona-credentials-refused` line is logged with cause `bot_token refused by a Web API call (<method>): Slack error <code>`, and its status becomes `broken` (phase `running`). None of that waits for the per-persona serializer, so a lifecycle operation holding the persona's turn cannot delay it; only the detached socket's network close is submitted through the injected `serialize`, never awaited (`stop` closes it at once if its turn has not come). From then on the persona is not serving, so the server's client lookup (`createPersonaClientLookup`, `persona-start.ts`) hands out no client, and a call on a client already handed out is refused locally by the latch (`WebApiCallRefusedLocallyError`), never sent. The persona is not up, so the up predicate, the relaunch gate, MCP admission, the permission poller and `/interject` all refuse it. A pending credentials reconnect is left to finish; its swap brings the persona back up. Every event reaches the handler tagged with its persona's key, and a handler throw affects no other event or persona. All state lives in one entry per persona: no lock, queue or timer spans two personas, and no handling path reads another persona's connection. Dry run (SR-3.4) calls no factory method, needs no credentials and makes no Slack call: each persona is up at once with a distinct placeholder identity derived from its key (`dryRunPersonaIdentity`: `U000DRY_<key>` / `B000DRY_<key>`), and no Web API client exists. The Slack client factory, clock and timers are injected, as `createCronScheduler` takes its clock; nothing is created, read or scheduled at import. `server.ts` constructs one manager in `main()` with the event router as `onEvent`, the dry-run flag, the lifecycle serializer's `run` as `serialize` and a status listener composed of the up-flush listener and the bring-up controller's `onConnectionStatus` (reached through the module-scope `bringUps` holder, because the controller is built over the manager and so after it; the holder is set before anything connects), and each persona is brought up through the bring-up controller (`persona-bringup-controller.ts`) at start, or at a confirmed apply for an added persona. `stop(key, endReason?)` closes one persona's connection: its retry is cancelled, its clients closed, its status becomes `stopped` and its entry is forgotten. A persona teardown calls it with no reason, so nothing is logged for an open episode: no lost, restored or unreachable line (b.av2 SR-6.5). With `endReason` (the bring-up controller's `claude_config_dir` hold, which brings the persona up again later on a fresh connection and tracker), the persona's own open episode (`persona-connection-lost`, `persona-slack-unreachable` or `persona-credentials-refused`) first ends with one line of its class and the cause `cleared: <endReason>`, so its start line gets an end; with no episode open it logs nothing. Shutdown closes every connection with `stopAll()`. **Credentials reconnect** (b.av2 SR-8.6 credentials row, SR-3.1, SR-3.3): `reconnectCredentials(key, tokens, onLaterOutcome?, beforeSwap?)` opens a second connection for a persona that has been up, with a new token pair, through the same attempt code: a new validation client and `auth.test`, a new long-lived Web API client, and a new Socket Mode client whose `start()` is bounded at 10 s like every other start. Every client is built through the factory with the SR-3.3 option sets. Identity is re-read from the new `auth.test`, with no check that the app is the same. Only when the new socket reaches `hello` does the persona switch to the new tokens, identity, Web API client and socket together; then any reopen of the old connection is cancelled and the old socket is closed as the manager's own close, with no lost line. Until then the old connection keeps its normal supervision, and both sockets forward events (the routing's dedupe delivers a message once); SR-3.1 allows two sockets and two Web API clients per persona only during this overlap. The first attempt resolves as a `PersonaReconnectOutcome`: `swapped`, `refused` (the new clients are discarded and the old connection is untouched), `retrying` (Slack unreachable, a `start()` abandoned at 10 s included: the old connection stays in use and the new one retries on its own SR-3.2 schedule and its own episode tracker, so its unreachable start and cleared lines are logged afresh), or `cancelled` (dry run, an unknown or stopped persona, or superseded). A later swap or refusal of a reconnect left retrying goes to `onLaterOutcome`; a refusal ends its retries. `beforeSwap` runs right before every swap, at the first attempt or later, while the persona still reports its old status, so no status listener sees it up on the new connection before the hook has run; a throw (or a rejected promise) is logged as a `credentials reconnect before-swap hook` failure and the swap goes ahead. A new reconnect, `stop` and `stopAll` cancel a pending one; its open Slack-unreachable episode then ends with one cleared line (the tracker's `end`), cause `cleared: the new connection of its confirmed credentials change is no longer retried: a later confirmed credentials change replaced it` or `…: the persona's connection was stopped`, so its start line always gets an end. `replaceRetryTokens(key, tokens)` is the retrying case instead: a persona whose bring-up is Slack-unreachable and retrying (or whose first attempt is in flight) takes the new pair for every later attempt; an attempt in flight with the old pair is cancelled and a new one starts, and the SR-3.2 schedule and episode logging carry on. It returns false for any other state, since a persona that has been up is reconnected instead. The held token pair is replaced only by a swap or by `replaceRetryTokens`.
    ├── persona-web-api-watch.ts  bug b.ujn / SR-13.1 watch on a persona's long-lived Web API client for a revoked bot token. `watchWebClientAuth(client, onRefused)` returns a wrapper the connection manager hands out in place of the client: an object whose prototype is the client, with its own wrapped copy of every method (`apiCall`, `filesUploadV2`, …) and of every plain-object method namespace (`chat`, `reactions`, `conversations`, `files`, …, recursively), so every Web API call made through it is seen (the library binds namespace methods to `apiCall` when the client is built, so overriding `apiCall` alone would miss them). Plain wrapper objects, not a `Proxy`, so a test's `spyOn` installs as usual. The caller gets the original promise and its original rejection unchanged. The first rejection that is a Web API platform error with Slack error exactly `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `account_inactive` or `not_authed` (`classifyWebApiAuthFailure` in `persona-slack-validation.ts`, a refused outcome of the `web-api` check on `bot_token`) latches the watch and tells `onRefused` once, with the method name (the property path such as `chat.postMessage`, or the method an `apiCall` names). Any other outcome (another Slack error, a network, HTTP or rate-limited error, a success, a synchronous throw) is ignored. After the latch every Web API method called through the wrapper is refused locally: the original is not called, and the call rejects with `WebApiCallRefusedLocallyError` (code `persona_web_api_refused_locally`, `data.error` the latched Slack error, so a caller's handling reads it as Slack's answer). The client's other methods still run; `paginate` is neither observed nor refused (unused in `src/`). The latch belongs to one wrapper: a new client starts unlatched. Pure: nothing at import, and a watch holds only its wrappers and its latch.
    ├── persona-notifier.ts     b.av2 SR-7.1 / SR-7.2 per-persona server notices. `createPersonaNotifier({ getPersona, clientFor, destinations?, destinationHold?, isDryRun, log })` returns `notify(key, text, options?)`, `flush(key)` and `forget(key)`; none throws or rejects. A notice goes only to the persona's destination (its `permission_prompts` channel, or its DM with `dm.contact`), posted through the persona's own Web client from `clientFor(key)` as one top-level message with only channel and text, so it appears under the persona's identity. The post is handed to the destination hold (`destinationHold`, `persona-destination-hold.ts`; when none is injected the notifier builds its own over `destinations`, or over a resolver of its own), which posts through the destination resolver (`persona-destination.ts`); the resolver opens the DM for a `dm` destination. The notifier prefixes every body with `Persona "<name>" (key=<key>): `, so callers pass the key and the body only. Routing, in order: an unknown key logs one line and drops the notice; dry run logs the persona reference and the notice's first line, with no Slack call; a persona with no serving client (`clientFor` returns undefined: not up yet, not brought up, or broken), whether its destination is a channel or `dm`, is held in that persona's own queue in raised order (the pre-validation hold); otherwise it is handed to the destination hold with `deliver`. For a `dm` destination the DM is opened (or taken from the resolver's cache) only when the notice is attempted, so a notice in the pre-validation queue opens it when flushed after the client is validated, never before. A queue holds at most `MAX_HELD_NOTICES_PER_PERSONA` (20) notices (defined in `persona-destination-hold.ts` and re-exported here), because a broken or retrying persona can go without a client for a long time: past the bound each new notice drops the oldest held one, which is logged (`[slack] persona-notifier: more than 20 notices held for <ref> — oldest held notice dropped, not posted: <first line>`) and never posted. `flush(key)` posts that persona's held notices in raised order once its client is validated and never touches another persona's queue; a notice raised for a validated persona that still has held notices posts after them. A notice whose open or post fails at the destination is kept by the destination hold and retried with the persona's prompts, under one `persona-destination-failed` line per episode; the notifier logs nothing for it (see Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures)). A notice whose post fails for the message itself (`MESSAGE_PAYLOAD_ERRORS`, such as `invalid_blocks` or `msg_too_long`) is dropped with one token-safe line, `[slack] persona-notifier: failed to post notice for <ref> to <channel>[ (reason=<reason>)]: <describeThrownValue>`. Either way the notice's `onPostFailure` callback is called once, at its first failed attempt, with the resolver's `DestinationFailure` (the step, the error code and the thrown value when there is one); later retries of the same notice don't call it again. A `dm` destination the resolver refuses (DMs off or no contact) is logged by the resolver and not posted, and `onPostFailure` is not called. Also exports `formatPersonaNotice` (also used by the permission poller for its stuck-prompt warning, which needs the post's outcome and so does not go through `notify`), `firstNoticeLine` and `notifySafely` (calls an injected notify sink so that a throw or rejection goes to an error callback). Pure (b.av2 SR-13.1): no module-scope state, no I/O of its own, nothing at import; each instance holds its own queues. `server.ts` builds one instance over `clientFor`, the shared destination resolver and the shared destination hold and installs it in outage state, the session manager, the JSONL safeguard and the persona routing (the lost-message notice, `persona-routing.ts`). The connection manager's status listener (`createPersonaUpFlushListener`) calls `flush(key)` each time a persona reports up, after its bring-up or a reopen, so a persona's held notices post once its own client is validated. `forget(key)` (a persona teardown) drops that persona's pre-validation queue unposted, so a persona added later with the same key never posts them; it leaves the destination hold alone and logs one line when notices were dropped, `[slack] persona-notifier: persona=<key>: dropped <n> held notice(s), not posted — the persona was torn down`.
    ├── persona-destination.ts  b.av2 SR-7.1 / SR-5.1 where a persona's permission prompts and server notices go, and the post there. A persona's destination is read from `permission_prompts`: a channel with its ID, or `dm`. `createPersonaDestinations({ log })` returns `{ post(persona, client, message), forget(personaKey) }`; `post` never rejects and returns `posted` (with the conversation and `ts`), `refused` or `failed`. A channel destination is the configured channel. A `dm` destination is the persona's DM conversation with `dm.contact`, obtained with `conversations.open` (`users` = the contact) on the persona's own client: Slack returns the existing conversation, or opens one when none exists, so a DM-only persona's first post needs no earlier DM. Posts name the returned `D…` conversation ID, never the contact's user ID, and carry only channel, text and optional blocks (no username or icon override), so they appear under the persona's identity. The resolved DM is cached per persona key, tied to the contact it was opened for: the same persona and contact reuse it with no second open; concurrent posts share the one in-flight open; a different contact opens afresh; a failed open, or one that returns no conversation ID, is not cached; a post to the cached conversation failing with `channel_not_found` drops, as soon as the post fails, the entry that post used and never a newer one opened meanwhile; `forget(personaKey)` clears the persona's entry; a persona teardown calls it, and so do an in-place update that changed `permission_prompts`, `dm.enabled` or `dm.contact`, a confirmed credentials change once the new connection is in use, and a recovery bring-up of a credentials-broken persona (the new credentials may belong to another app, with another DM conversation). The cache holds one entry per persona, so one persona's failed open never affects another's destination (b.av2 SR-3.3). Defensive refusal: a `dm` destination with `dm.enabled` not true or no `dm.contact` (the loader already rejects both) makes no Slack call, logs `[slack] persona-destination: <ref> has permission_prompts set to "dm" but <dm.enabled is not true|dm.contact is not set> — no DM opened and nothing posted` and returns `refused`; it does not go through the MCP tool-scope check (`checkPersonaTarget`). A failed open or post is returned as `failed` (a `DestinationFailure`) with the step (`conversations.open` or `chat.postMessage`), the error class (`classifySlackError`; `no_conversation_id` for an open with no ID) the thrown value when there is one (for a token-safe description only) and, for a post, the conversation; `describeDestinationFailure` renders its token-safe log tail.
```
