Also exports `dmDestinationRefusal` and `describeDmDestinationRefusal` (the poller checks the refusal first, to log it per request), `describeDestinationFailureCause` (the token-safe cause the session manager's `spawn-failure-post` record carries), `classifySlackError` (the trail's `error` class, used by the poller and the click handler), `safeFailureCode` (a failure's code when it is a short identifier, else `unknown_error`: the form a log line or trail `error` field carries), `DM_OPEN_SCOPE` (`im:write`) and `MISSING_SCOPE_ERROR` (used by `registry.ts` and the destination hold). The resolver never retries: a failed open or post is returned once, and holding and retrying it is the destination hold's job (next entry). Pure (b.av2 SR-13.1): no module-scope state, no timers, nothing at import; each instance holds its own cache.
```
    ├── persona-destination-hold.ts  b.av2 SR-7.1 (failure part) / SR-3.2 / SR-3.3 / SR-10.3 hold and retry of a persona's permission prompts and server notices while its destination fails. `createPersonaDestinationHold({ destinations, getPersona, clientFor, clock?, log })` returns `begin(key)`, `deliver(persona, client, notice)`, `cancel(key)`, `cancelAll()` and `view(key)`. It owns each persona's failure episode, retry schedule, FIFO of held notices, timer and in-flight guard, and the `persona-destination-failed` lines. See Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures).
    ├── persona-event-router.ts  b.av2 SR-3.1 / SR-4.1 / SR-7.1 / SR-13.1 the handler every persona connection forwards its events to. `createPersonaEventRouter({ routing, clientFor, getPersona, log, handleClick?, emitTrail? })` returns the connection manager's `onEvent` handler, called with the receiving persona's key, the event name and the library's listener argument. `message` and `app_mention`: one RAW line, `[slack] RAW <event> event persona=<key>: <first 300 chars of the event JSON>`, then the event and its `ack` go to persona-routing's `receive` with the receiving persona as the only receiver. `interactive`: the payload's channel, message `ts` and clicking user are read once; for each action `cscb.block_action.received` is emitted and `handlePermissionClick` runs with `receivingPersonaKey` set to the receiving persona's key, stopping at the first handled action. A throw while handling one action is logged as `[slack] persona=<key>: interactive action <i> of <n> handling failed: <describeThrownValue>` and the remaining actions still run. The payload is acked exactly once, after handling, on every path: when an action is handled, when none is, and when an action throws. Any other event name is logged, acked and ignored. A throw while handling one event is logged as `[slack] persona=<key>: <event> event handling failed: <describeThrownValue>` and never reaches the manager. No other persona's key, connection or client is consulted. Nothing runs at import.
    ├── persona-start.ts        b.av2 SR-6.1 / SR-3.1 / SR-3.4 / SR-7.2 / SR-13.1 the per-persona start procedure and the connection seams the server reads. The procedure's steps, in order: (1) the local credentials check and (2) the working-directory check, both evaluated before either stops the bring-up so both causes are reported (`checkPersonaLocalBringUp`; in dry run only `checkPersonaWorkingDirectory` runs and no credentials file is read); (3) Slack validation and connection through the connection manager's `bringUp(persona, tokens)` (no tokens in dry run); (4) the launch, only when step 3 reports the persona `up`. This module exports steps 1–3 as separate functions and runs none of them itself: `checkPersonaLocalSteps` (steps 1–2, returning each failure, the tokens read and the credentials digest (`credentialsDigest`, whatever step 1's outcome, a real-path collision included; none in dry run), logging only when given a logger), `connectPersonaSlack` (step 3, handing the persona and the tokens it is given to the manager) and `personaSlackStatusFailure` (the step-3 failure for a status other than `up`). A failure's step (`PersonaBringUpStep`) is `credentials`, `working-directory`, `slack` or `claude-config-dir`, the last for a persona held because its `claude_config_dir` cannot be resolved (bug b.g57), before its Slack step or at a later launch. The bring-up controller (`persona-bringup-controller.ts`) runs the server's start through them and keeps each persona's outcome; the start pass launches a persona that is `up` at start, and the controller launches one that reaches `up` through a retry. Steps 1–2 log through E2's checks and step 3 through the manager; a throw from `bringUp` is the only line `connectPersonaSlack` adds (`[slack] persona <ref> not brought up: Slack bring-up threw: <describeThrownValue>`), and it resolves a `slack` failure of class `error`. Nothing here posts to Slack, records a startup error or counts a failed spawn. Seams, built over the manager and the applied-config getter: `isPersonaClientServing(status)` (true for `up`, `lost` and `retrying` in its `reopen` phase; false while connecting, retrying its bring-up, broken, stopped or unmanaged), `createPersonaClientLookup` (`clientFor`: the manager's long-lived Web client for a serving applied persona, else nothing, and always nothing in dry run), `createPersonaIdentityLookup` (the serving persona's bot identity; the placeholder identity in dry run) `createPersonaUpFlushListener(notifier)` (a status listener: flush exactly that persona's held notices on every transition to `up`), `composePersonaStatusListeners(...listeners)` (several status listeners as the manager's one; each is called even when an earlier one throws), `createPersonaUpPredicate(connections, outcomes)` (`isPersonaUp(key)`, b.av2 SR-6.4: true only while `isPersonaClientServing` holds for the persona's status and its bring-up outcome is `up`; false for a persona the manager or the controller does not know; logs nothing; the one "is this persona up" check behind MCP session admission (Session Lifecycle, Connection), the permission poller's skip (Permission Relay, Per-tick flow) and `/interject`'s 503) and `createPersonaRelaunchGate(connections, log, outcomes?)` (`canRelaunch(key)`: decides through the same private check as `createPersonaUpPredicate` (`isPersonaUpGiven`), over one status read it also uses to word its log line, so the gate and the predicate can't drift: true only while `isPersonaClientServing` holds for the persona's status and, when `outcomes` is given, its bring-up outcome is `up`; false for a persona the manager does not know, such as one that failed its credentials or working-directory check; every brought-up persona is `up` in dry run). The server passes the bring-up controller as `outcomes` and hands the gate to restart (`RestartDeps.canRestart`), the restart launch and the health check's work list; see Auto-Restart. When it answers false it logs one of two lines, once per persona per reason: a repeat for the same reason is silent, and being eligible again re-arms it. While the connection is not serving: `[slack] persona=<key>: not relaunched — its Slack connection is <state>; eligible again once it is up`, with `<state>` one of `not brought up` (the manager does not know the persona), `connecting`, `retrying its bring-up`, `retrying`, `broken` or `stopped`. While the connection is serving but the bring-up outcome is not `up`: `[slack] persona=<key>: not relaunched — its bring-up has not succeeded; eligible again once it is up`. A key outside the applied set (`PersonaUpQuery.isApplied`, production: the bring-up controller's `isApplied`; b.av2 SR-8.6) is refused first, whatever its status: `[slack] persona=<key>: not relaunched — it is no longer in the applied configuration`; the up predicate answers false for it too. Each line carries only the key and the reason. Tokens exist only in memory: for the call, in the manager, and in the bring-up controller's entry for the persona (kept from the first read for later directory retries; see the next entry). Nothing runs at import.
    ├── persona-bringup-controller.ts  b.av2 SR-6.1 (outcomes) / SR-6.4 / SR-7.2 (part) / SR-10.3 (part) / SR-13.1 per-persona bring-up outcomes and retries. `createPersonaBringUpController({ connections, launch, dryRun, log, clock?, ... })` (`connections` is the connection manager's `bringUp`, `status` and `stop`, all required; `stop` closes a persona's connection when a `claude_config_dir` hold starts for it) returns `bringUp(persona, applied)`, `onConnectionStatus(key, status)` (the manager's status listener), `isUp(key)`, `isApplied(key)` (whether the key is in the applied set now, from the optional injected `appliedPersonas()` getter, which production binds to `personaConfig`'s personas; true for every key without it; see the applied-set guard under Server-Managed Startup step 5), `state(key)` (outcome and causes, never a token), `credentialsDigest(key)`, `changeCredentials(persona, applied, connections, hooks?)`, `holdForConfigDir(persona, failure)`, `cancel(key)` and `cancelAll()`. `credentialsDigest(key)` (b.av2 SR-8.3) is the read-only query behind the reload detection tick's credentials comparison: the digest or marker of the bytes the persona's bring-up read, or of its last confirmed credentials change, which is what it connected with, is retrying with or broke with. No retry re-reads the file, so it changes only at a confirmed credentials change. `changeCredentials` is apply step 4's per-persona body (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)). Beside the held content, each entry keeps the content its own connection uses (`live`: tokens and digest), set at bring-up, at every swap of a credentials reconnect (at once or later) and when a retrying persona takes a change; a refused change holds `live` again, so the change is pending. `isCredentialsBroken(state)`, exported here, is the SR-6.4 rule it, the change plan and the apply steps share. A persona broken by a credentials file another applied persona shares holds the digest of its own path's bytes (read only to be hashed, never parsed). It is undefined in dry run (no file is read) and for an unknown or cancelled persona. It is never logged. `bringUp` logs the persona's `persona-start` line, runs steps 1–2 (`checkPersonaLocalSteps`), then step 3 (`connectPersonaSlack`) when both pass, and resolves once the persona has an outcome: `up`, `broken` or `retrying` (see Server-Managed Startup step 5 for the cause mapping). It launches nothing itself at start. Causes are kept apart (credentials, directory, Slack, config dir). A directory-broken persona is re-checked on its own timer with its own `createPersonaRetrySchedule` (5 s doubling to 300 s, no cap), never through `backoff.ts`; a re-check is scheduled only once the previous one has finished. The tokens read at the persona's bring-up (or at its last confirmed credentials change) are held in its entry and handed to the manager when a directory retry reaches Slack; no retry re-reads the credentials file. A persona that reaches `up` through a retry is launched once through the injected `launch` (the server's `spawnForPersona`): when a directory retry's Slack step leaves the persona's outcome `up` (the manager's status is `up`, `lost` or `retrying` in the `reopen` phase), or when `onConnectionStatus` sees `up` right after `retrying` in the `bring-up` phase. An `up` after `lost` or a reopen launches nothing. The `claude_config_dir` hold (bug b.g57): the injected `checkConfigDir` (production: `checkLaunchConfigDir`) runs once steps 1 and 2 pass, right before step 3, at bring-up and when a directory retry reaches Slack; an unresolvable directory opens the hold there, so the persona never connects. `holdForConfigDir` (the session manager's hook, installed with `setConfigDirUnresolvableHook`) opens it for a persona whose later launch found the directory unresolvable, and closes its Slack connection (the manager's `stop` with the end reason `the persona's Slack connection was closed while its claude_config_dir cannot be resolved`, so an open lost, unreachable or refused episode ends with one cleared line; a failure logs `closing its Slack connection for its claude_config_dir failed: <error>`), so the persona leaves up (below) and its MCP session is dropped. Either way its outcome is `retrying` with a `configDir` cause and no connection, its instance and row are kept, and its notices are held by the notifier. The failure line is logged once when the episode starts, and the directory is re-checked on the persona's own timer and `createPersonaRetrySchedule`, outside the restart counter and cap, each attempt through the serializer. Once it resolves: one cleared line (`…; continuing the bring-up`), Slack validation and connection with the held credentials, then the launch (`up after its claude_config_dir resolved — launching`), which resumes the row through the collision ladder; Slack-unreachable then retries and launches on `up`. A persona Slack had refused when the hold started keeps that refusal as its cause and stays `broken` after the cleared line. A confirmed credentials change while held takes the new content (held and live) and opens no connection; the persona connects with it on its return. It returns false for a persona the controller does not know or has cancelled, and the caller then logs the line itself. An `up` right after `broken` (a pending credentials reconnect swapped in for a connection Slack had refused meanwhile) launches the persona the same way, logging `up after its confirmed credentials change — launching`. Each retry attempt, through to the launch it triggers, runs through the optional injected `serialize` (the server's lifecycle serializer, b.av2 SR-6.6): a directory re-check with its Slack step and launch, and the launch after a Slack retry reached `up`. Their checks (cancelled, no longer applied, already launched) run when the attempt starts, and the launch is awaited inside the attempt. `launchAfterRetry` also waits for up: it launches nothing unless the persona's outcome is `up` when its turn comes, so a Slack launch queued behind an operation that held the persona for its `claude_config_dir` (closing its connection) does nothing, even if the directory resolved by then; the hold's recovery launches it after the reconnect. The controller never submits `bringUp` itself; the apply bring-up does. `cancel(key)` (a persona teardown, or shutdown's `cancelAll()`) forgets the persona's entry and clears its timer, so retry work still queued for it does nothing. Each persona's state, timer and in-flight flag live in its own entry; nothing spans two personas. Lines go through the injected logger only (never `startup-errors.log`), and nothing here posts to Slack. Dry run reads no credentials file and makes no Slack call, but the directory check and its retry run as in a real start. Leaving up (b.av2 SR-6.3, SR-6.4): each time a persona's outcome changes from `up` to `broken` or `retrying` (a refused reopen of a running persona, or a Web API call refused for its bot token, bug b.ujn, both of which make it `broken`; or a `claude_config_dir` hold, which makes it `retrying` and closes its connection), the optional injected `onLeftUp(persona, state)` is told once; a persona that was never up is not reported, and a lost connection being reopened (`lost`, `retrying` in the `reopen` phase) stays `up` and is not reported. A throw or rejection from the listener is logged as `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): handling its change from up to <outcome> failed: <error>`. The module also exports `describePersonaNotUp(state)`, the token-free reason a persona is not up used by the refusal and drop lines (`<outcome>: <first cause>`, credentials before directory before Slack before config dir; `its bring-up has not run` with no state; `its Slack connection is not serving` when the outcome is `up` but the connection is not), and `createNotUpSessionDropper({ drop, log })`, the `onLeftUp` listener the server installs (Session Lifecycle, Disconnection). Clock, logger, checks, file-system seam, manager, launch, serializer and leaving-up listener are injected; nothing is created, read or scheduled at import or at construction.
    ├── persona-serializer.ts   b.av2 SR-6.6 / SR-13.1 per-persona lifecycle serialization. `createPersonaSerializer()` returns a `PersonaSerializer`: `run(key, operation)` (`PersonaSerialize`) starts the operation only once every operation already submitted for that key has settled, and resolves or rejects with the operation's own result; `whenIdle(key)` resolves (never rejects) once everything submitted for the key before the call has settled. Per key it holds one promise chain and nothing spans two keys, so one persona's operation never waits on another's. A rejected operation doesn't stop the next one for its key; its rejection goes to its submitter. Re-entrancy rule: an operation running for a key must never await `run` or `whenIdle` for the same key. What goes through it: Session Lifecycle, [Per-persona serialization](#per-persona-serialization-bav2-sr-66). No dependencies. Importable without side effects: importing it or calling the factory creates no timer, and an instance holds nothing until the first `run`. `server.ts` builds the one production instance at module scope.
    ├── persona-lifecycle.ts    b.av2 SR-6.1 / SR-6.2 / SR-6.5 / SR-6.6 / SR-8.6 / SR-13.1 the per-persona operations a confirmed apply runs. `createPersonaLifecycle(deps)` returns a `PersonaLifecycle`: `teardown(persona)`, the persona teardown of apply step 2, which never rejects; `bringUp(persona, applied, options?)`, the bring-up at apply of step 6, which resolves with the bring-up outcome once an `up` persona's launch has settled, and with `{ recovery: true }` is the recovery bring-up of a credentials-broken persona whose credentials file changed; `reconnectCredentials(persona, applied)`, the credentials change of step 4, which resolves with the controller's `PersonaCredentialsChangeResult` once an up persona's first reconnect attempt has settled; `updateInPlace(change)`, the in-place update of step 3 (an `InPlaceApplyInput`), which never rejects; and `refreshTemplate(applied)`, the template refresh of step 5 (`refreshSlackChannelBotTemplate`, `agent-director-template.ts`), which never rejects and, belonging to no persona, is not serialized. The four per-persona operations are submitted through the injected serializer and never submit to it again from inside; none touches another persona. Every `teardown` cancels the persona's launch wait for a `working` row as soon as it is submitted (`cancelLaunchWait`, b.f2b). `teardown` also serves the old half of a destructive modify (its key still applied): it then also cancels the persona's bring-up retries and restart timer at submit, before its serializer turn, and drops held notices again after the agent-director calls; `bringUp` serves the new half. A persona with only next-launch changes gets no operation. Injected dependencies (`PersonaLifecycleDeps`, all required): `serialize`; the bring-up controller's `bringUp`, `cancel`, `state` and `changeCredentials`; the connection manager's `stop`, `reconnectCredentials` and `replaceRetryTokens`; the routing's `forget`; `forgetAcks` (`forgetPersonaAcks`, the teardown); the destination resolver's `forget` (teardown, an in-place update that moves the DM destination, a credentials change right before its new connection comes into use, and a recovery bring-up); the destination hold's `cancel`; the notifier's `forget`; `appliedPersonas()` (the teardown's Stop-hook pass, whether a teardown's key is still applied, and whether an in-place update's key is still applied); `dryRun`; `isShuttingDown` (the server's `shuttingDown` flag, read at each use); `log`; `whenLaunchSettled`, `cancelLaunchWait` (`cancelWorkingRowWait`) and `forgetNotConnectedEpisode` (both optional in the type, always passed by production; b.f2b), `cancelRestartTimer`, `forgetFailures`, `forgetDisconnectedStreak`, `resetOutageState` (`resetAllToHealthy`), `forgetPersonaPrompts`, `dropSession` (the server's `dropPersonaSessionAndKeepAlive`), `killInstance` and `deleteInstance` (`killPersonaInstance`, `deletePersonaInstance`); `replyGuard` (`PersonaTeardownReplyGuard`: `launchedWithDir`, `teardown` and `launchPass`, with the state directory bound); `storageCheck` (`runPersonaStorageCheck` with the notifier); `launch` (`spawnForPersona` with the start-time server-wide values); and `templateRefresh` (`installed`, the params the boot install wrote, and `getClient`). See Session Lifecycle, [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61), [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65), [In-place update](#in-place-update-bav2-sr-86) and [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4); its lines are under Logging, [Persona lifecycle lines](#persona-lifecycle-lines-bav2-sr-65-sr-61). Importable without side effects: nothing is created, read or scheduled at import or at `createPersonaLifecycle`, so tests build it without importing `server.ts`.
    ├── delivery-decision.ts    b.av2 SR-4.2 / SR-4.3 / SR-4.4 / SR-13.1 the one place inbound delivery is decided. `decideDelivery(event, persona, applied)` takes one raw `message` / `app_mention` event, the receiving persona P (key, bot user ID, bot ID, channel entries and `dmEnabled`, P's `dm.enabled`) and every applied persona's key and channel entries, and returns deliver with `via`, or drop with a reason (`non-message`, `no-author`, `own`, `dm-disabled`, `group-dm`, `channel-not-configured` with an `unclaimed` flag, `not-mentioned`). What it covers: the deliverable subtypes (none, `file_share`, `bot_message`, `thread_broadcast`, `me_message`); an author must be present (`user` or `bot_id`); self-exclusion by P's bot user ID or P's bot ID, where an author with no `user` (a webhook or integration) is identified by its bot ID; the conversation kind (a DM is `channel_type` `im`, or, as a defensive fallback for an event without `channel_type`, a conversation ID starting with `D`; a group DM is recognised only by `channel_type` `mpim`), where a DM delivers with `via` `dm` when `dmEnabled` is on and is `dm-disabled` when it is off, an `mpim` event is always `group-dm`, and neither reaches the channel rules (an `app_mention` in a group DM has no `channel_type` and a `G…`/`C…` ID, so it takes the channel rules); channel membership; `delivery: all`; and `delivery: mentions`, which delivers on P's own user mention (`<@P>` or `<@P|label>`) or on `<!here>` / `<!channel>`, with or without a label. `<!everyone>` and user-group mentions do not count. An empty or unknown bot user ID or bot ID never matches. Self-exclusion is the only filter on the author: nothing arbitrates, counts or throttles exchanges between personas. `via`, first match wins: `dm`, `mention`, `broadcast`, `receive_all_shared`, `receive_all`. `receive_all_shared` needs at least one other applied persona with `delivery: all` in that channel, whether or not that persona is up; only a DM produces `dm`. Also exports `stripPersonaMention`, which removes P's own mention token (both forms) from the text and leaves other mentions and broadcasts. Pure: imports types only, keeps no state and has no side effects on import. Its only caller is the persona pipeline in `persona-routing.ts`.
    ├── persona-routing.ts      b.av2 SR-4.1 / SR-4.2 / SR-4.3 / SR-4.4 / SR-4.6 / SR-7.3 / SR-10.3 / SR-13.1 inbound delivery per persona. `createPersonaRouting(deps)` returns `receive(event, ack, personaKeys)` (one key or a list; the event router passes the receiving persona's key alone) and `forget(key)`: intake acks the event first, then, for each receiving persona key, writes the archive through the injected archive seam and runs that persona's pipeline, each run isolated (a throw is logged naming the persona and never stops another persona's run). It owns the per-persona dedupe: one `inbound-dedupe.ts` store per persona key, created on first use and kept until `forget(key)` drops it (a persona teardown, which stops the persona's connection first, since another event for the key would create a new store); the key is the event's (channel, ts), or (channel, ts, edited.ts) when the event carries a top-level `edited.ts` (read by the private `editedTs`; a `message_changed` event nests its edit under `message` and keeps the two-part key), and a duplicate key stops after the archive write with no decision and no log line. The pipeline asks `decideDelivery` (`delivery-decision.ts`) once per event whether the receiving persona gets it, passing the persona's bot identity (bot user ID and bot ID) from the injected `getBotIdentity` and the persona's `dm.enabled`; see Data Flow, Inbound for the rule order. The pipeline logs the drop lines (`unclaimed-channel`, `persona-dm-dropped`, and a plain dropped line for the other reasons, none carrying message text), resolves the author label, looks up the persona's session with `getSessionByPersona(key)` and probes its stream, sends `notifications/claude/channel` to that session only, with meta that adds the author's `user_id` (or `bot_id`) and the decision's `via`, and only once that send has succeeded records the persona's ack-tracker entry and adds the `ack_reaction` through the persona's own client (b.av2 SR-4.5). It also owns the b.kvq no-session branch and the b.9cj streamless branch (the private `handleLostMessage`): it reads whether the persona is up (the optional injected `isPersonaUp`; production passes the server's one predicate) and its real restart state into `decideLostMessageState` (`lost-message.ts`), fires the human-triggered `scheduleRestart` only in the `starting-now` state, and raises one lost-message notice (`buildLostMessageNotice`) through the injected `notify`, which goes to the persona's destination. Nothing else is posted in the source conversation (when the destination is that conversation, the notice is the one post there), and a lost message gets no ack reaction or ack-tracker entry. The notice is awaited; a failing `notify` logs `[slack] persona-routing: lost-message notice for persona <ref> failed: <describeThrownValue>` and is never thrown. `hasSessionStream(key)` is the one `_GET_stream` probe shared by dispatch, the restart guard and the health check. Every Slack client, the persona config, the bot identity, the name resolver, the archive writer, the ack-reaction source, the notice sink (`notify`), the logger and (optionally) the dedupe clock and the up predicate are injected; the session lookup and restart guards use the real registry, restart and backoff state. Side-effect free: no client, token, file, environment read, timer or log at import, and no agent-director call. Slack-call failures are logged through `describeSlackCallFailure`.
    ├── lost-message.ts         b.av2 SR-4.6 / SR-7.3 / SR-13.1 the lost-message recovery state and notice text. `decideLostMessageState(queries)` returns the closed `LostMessageState` (`not-up`, `restarting`, `starting-now`, `auto-restart-disabled`, `restart-limit-reached`) from injected queries asked in the human-trigger order, first true wins: the persona not up (the optional `isNotUp`; absent, the persona counts as up), a restart already pending or running, auto-restart disabled (`session_restart_delay` 0), the persona at the restart-failure cap; otherwise `starting-now`. `buildLostMessageNotice(senderLabel, state)` builds the one-line notice body: a message from the sender was lost, not delivered, not saved and won't be delivered later, plus the recovery state. It escapes `&`, `<` and `>` in the label (so a bot `username` such as `<!channel>` notifies no one) and folds line breaks. The body carries no message text, no mention and no persona name; the notifier adds the persona reference. Pure: no state, I/O, timers or logging, nothing at import. Its only caller is `persona-routing.ts`, which reads the up state (its injected `isPersonaUp`, the server's one `isPersonaUp`) and the real restart state and raises the notice through the per-persona notice sink.
    ├── inbound-dedupe.ts       b.av2 SR-4.1 / SR-13.1 one persona's inbound dedupe store. `createInboundDedupeStore(now?)` returns a store whose `record(conversationId, ts, editedTs?)` answers `new` (and records the key), `duplicate`, or `unkeyable` (`conversationId` or `ts` missing, empty or not a string; nothing recorded, and the caller treats the event as new). The key is (`conversationId`, `ts`, `editedTs`) when `editedTs` is a non-empty string, and (`conversationId`, `ts`) otherwise; the parts are joined with a separator no Slack ID or ts contains, so a two-part and a three-part key never collide. A key is remembered for `INBOUND_DEDUPE_RETENTION_MS` (10 minutes) from its first sighting; a duplicate does not extend it. Each `record` first prunes expired keys, so memory stays bounded with no timer. It knows nothing about personas, Slack or config and limits nothing: distinct keys are always new. State is in memory only. Clock injected (default `Date.now`); nothing at import. Its only caller is `persona-routing.ts`, which keeps one store per persona.
    ├── registry.ts             Persona-keyed MCP session registry (b.av2 SR-6.3), per-session MCP Server factory with the persona-scoped Slack tools, transport routing. A session is identified by its `roots/list` working directory, compared by real path; `matchPersonaByRootsPath` maps it to exactly one applied persona (a directory matching more than one persona matches none, with one log line). `decideSessionAdmission(rootsPath, personas, { isPersonaUp, describeNotUp?, ... })` wraps the match and answers `admitted`, `unmatched` or `not-up` (b.av2 SR-6.4); it logs the refusal line for `not-up` and registers, removes or closes nothing itself (see Session Lifecycle, Connection). `closePendingSession(pendingId, transport, { removePending, stopKeepAlive })` disconnects a pending session that won't be registered: it removes the pending entry, stops the SSE keep-alive, then closes the transport and ignores a close error; `server.ts` uses it for every such branch (see Session Lifecycle, Connection). `dropPersonaSession(key)` drops the session registered for a persona: it removes the persona's MCP session-ID mappings and registry entry first, marks the entry not connected, then closes its transport, so the close restarts nothing; it resolves whether a session was registered and logs nothing (see Session Lifecycle, Disconnection; a persona teardown drops the session the same way). The registry holds one session per persona key (`getSessionByPersona`); a newer session for a persona replaces the older one, and a stale close of the replaced session leaves the newer one registered. A pending session's entry stub is promoted in place, so its tool handlers read the persona key it was matched under. The posting-scope check `checkPersonaTarget(persona, target, action)` sorts the target with the module-private `classifyPersonaTarget` (a configured channel `id`, a `D…` DM conversation ID, a `U…`/`W…` user ID, or other) and allows a configured channel always, a `D…` conversation only while the persona's `dm.enabled` is on, and a user ID only for a `post` action (`reply`) while `dm.enabled` is on; each tool's target argument and action are in the private `TOOL_TARGET` map (see Outbound). A pure check: no Slack call, log or state. The MCP instructions (`MCP_INSTRUCTIONS`, exported as the exact string `createSessionServer` hands every instance, which the shipped-description audit in `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` covers) describe the `<channel>` tag (their example tag carries `source="slack-channel-router"`, the MCP server name the harness renders) (including the author's `user_id`, or `bot_id` for an author without a user, and the `via` values), say that `<@ID>` in text mentions a Slack user or another persona and is how to address another persona, say that a message without `via` is an injected prompt, state where the tools may act (configured channels, and with DMs on a DM conversation or, for `reply`, a user ID), and describe the tools. No session-id discovery (AD owns it).
    ├── lib.ts                  Pure utilities — chunking (`chunkText`), sanitization, the `_GET_stream` probe `hasGetStreamKey`, and the file exfiltration guard `assertSendable(filePath, stateDir, inboxDir, protectedPaths)` (see Security Model).
    ├── logging.ts              Log file setup — overrides console.error/console.log with timestamped writeSync to a log file. Size-based rotation on every write (b.brv): rotates server.log/clean_restart.log at CSCB_LOG_MAX_BYTES (default 10 MiB), keeps CSCB_LOG_KEEP generations (default 5; 0 = truncate).
    ├── agent-director-logger.ts  b.brv verbosity filter for the agent-director Client's diagnostic logger. `makeFilteredAdLogger(base)` drops routine `SubprocessClient: <verb> ok` per-poll success dumps at info/log level; warn/error always pass through. CSCB_AD_VERBOSE (truthy: 1/true/yes/on) returns the base logger unwrapped. Injected into `Client.create()` by `agent-director-startup.ts`.
    ├── startup-errors.ts       SR-5.1a startup-errors log — append-only to ~/.claude/channels/slack/startup-errors.log + stderr.
    ├── agent-director-client.ts   SR-0.1 singleton Client wrapper. `getClient()` is a sync cached accessor returning the Client previously installed by the startup gate via the production `setClient()` setter; calling `getClient()` before the gate runs throws a CSCB-internal "called before startup gate" error. CSCB no longer owns a runtime version pin — AD's library-side `Client.create()` enforces the floor from `dist/version-floor.json`. Exposes `decideWithToken` (SR-7.2 — single source of truth for the snake-case `request_token` field on the decide wire) and `getPermission` + `isErrPermissionRequestNotFound` (SR-7.1 — wraps the paired AD release's `get-permission --request-token <uuid>` verb).
    ├── agent-director-errors.ts   SR-0.2 typed Err* re-exports for instanceof branching, plus the CSCB-synthetic `ErrSpawnCapReached` (never emitted by the AD library; minted by the restart cap path — remediation hint "restart the server to retry — automatic restarts are suspended for this persona").
    ├── agent-director-startup.ts  SR-5.1 startup gate — async `Client.create()` injection / SR-4.2 system-install typed-error branches / SR-6.1 API surface probes / same-user stat.
    ├── install-skill-pointer.ts   SR-4.5 / SR-9.3 manual-skill-install instructions block. Renders a multi-line string containing the GitHub URL of `skills/install-cscb/SKILL.md` (derived from `package.json` `repository.url`), the target path `~/.claude/skills/install-cscb/SKILL.md`, and the invocation command `/install-cscb`. The startup gate appends this block to the three `ad-system-install-*` typed-error failure messages only — never to `ErrBunVersionTooOld`, the three `ad-shim-*` branches, or the same-user check.
    ├── install-check.ts           SR-5 shared install-check module. Exposes `runInstallCheck(): Promise<Result>` — the sole source of truth for the `semver.gte` + `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION` equality logic. Reads AD's `dist/version-floor.json` (via the `agent-director/dist/version-floor.json` subpath export), calls AD's standalone `resolveSystemBinary()`, and maps the three system-install typed errors plus the floor-mismatch case to four canonical class labels (`ad-system-install-not-found`, `ad-system-install-too-old`, `ad-system-install-unreachable`, `ad-version-floor-unreadable`). Side-effect-free: no `process.exit`, no disk writes, no stdout/stderr. Consumed by `scripts/install-check.ts` (the `bun run install-check` diagnostic) and the Epic-3 install-cscb skill. The startup gate does NOT call `runInstallCheck()` — AD's `Client.create()` enforces the same floor against the same data source; it imports only the exported `AD_SYSTEM_INSTALL_NOT_FOUND` label, so both emit the same `ad-system-install-not-found` string.
    ├── agent-director-template.ts SR-3.1 / SR-3.2 / b.av2 SR-8.6 step 5 — builds the slack-channel-bot MakeTemplateParams and calls client.makeTemplate({ overwrite: true }). `installSlackChannelBotTemplate(personaConfig, deps?)` is the boot install: fatal on any rejection (records `ad-template-install` and exits 1), and it resolves `InstalledTemplate`, agent-director's result plus the `params` it installed. `refreshSlackChannelBotTemplate(applied, installed, { getClient, log, home? })` is the apply-time refresh: `installed` with its memory-read `allow` rules derived afresh from `applied` (`deriveMemoryReadAllowRules`, the boot derivation, so never a config-dir root) and every other field, `claude_args` included, as the boot install wrote it. It never rejects and never exits: it logs one `[slack] template refresh:` line and resolves `{ kind: 'refreshed', path }` or `{ kind: 'failed' }`; nothing retries it. Importable without side effects.
    ├── session-manager.ts      SR-1 in full — startupSessionManager (one bring-up and one spawnForPersona per persona), spawnForPersona (SR-1.4 collision-then-act, persona-keyed), launchSession (restart relaunch by persona key), reconcileOrphans (the persona-keyed start sweep, b.av2 SR-6.3), reconnectMcp/waitForWaitingAndReconnect. At start, `startupSessionManager(config, { bringUp })` runs each persona through the b.av2 SR-6.1 procedure: steps 1–3 (the bring-up controller's `bringUp`, `persona-bringup-controller.ts`) for every persona at once, then the launch (`spawnForPersona`) for each persona that is `up`, through a first-in, first-out launch pool of at most `concurrency` (default 3; a value below 1 is treated as 1), in the order the personas become ready, so no persona's Slack connection waits behind another persona's launch; `bringUp` is the controller, and the server always passes it (without it each persona is launched directly, in config order through the same pool, which only launch-ladder unit tests do). A `broken` or `retrying` persona takes no pool slot and is counted in `notBroughtUp` and as a `not-brought-up` entry with its `outcome` and failures in `perPersona`; it records no startup error, raises no notice and is not a failed spawn. `launchSession(key, config, { canLaunch? })` (restart) runs the launch only; it asks `canLaunch` (the server passes the relaunch gate) before looking the persona up, and when that answers false (not up, or a key no longer applied) it launches nothing, runs no trust patch or reply-guard step and returns `'skipped'`. `spawnForPersona` is the one launch entry point and allows one in-flight launch per persona key: a second call for a key whose launch is still running joins it and gets its result, and `isLaunchInFlight(key)` reports whether one is running; `whenLaunchSettled(key)` resolves once that launch has settled, whatever its outcome (never rejects, starts nothing), for a persona teardown. `killPersonaInstance(key)` and `deletePersonaInstance(key)` kill and delete `cscb_<key>` quietly through `withOutageDetection`, also for a teardown: true when the row was there, false when it was already gone (`ErrSpawnNotFound`), every other error rethrown; they log nothing, record no startup error and raise no spawn-failure notice; the ladder itself is the private `runPersonaLadder`. The ladder's row guards and the sweep's `cwd` condition go through `compareRowToPersona` (see Server-Managed Startup steps 4 and 5). It also reports `workingDirectoryResolved` (the persona's working directory has a real path, `tryResolveRealPath`) and `cwdCheckDeferred`: the working directory has no real path and the row's `cwd` has none either (absent, missing, a dangling symlink's old target) or equals the configured `working_directory` lexically. For such a row neither the sweep nor the ladder kills or deletes on `cwd` grounds (b.av2 SR-6.4): the sweep keeps the row and defers the check to the launch, and the ladder keeps the row and fails the launch. A row whose `cwd` resolves to an existing directory elsewhere isn't deferred: it is still swept or replaced as a `cwd` mismatch, so a persona can't adopt another persona's directory. Raises its persona notices (b.av2 SR-7.2) through one installed sink, `setSessionNotifier(notify)`, which production sets to the per-persona notifier's `notify` (`persona-notifier.ts`); with no sink installed (unit tests, the integration driver) a notice is logged, not posted. `notifySpawnFailure(key, error, isStartup)` raises the spawn-failure notice (error name, truncated description, remediation hint); when a startup notice's post fails (at its first failed attempt; a notice the destination hold keeps can still post later) it records the `spawn-failure-post` startup error with a token-safe cause from `describeDestinationFailureCause` (a failed DM open names the step and code, `conversations.open code=<code>`; a failed post gives the rejection's description), and outside startup it logs the same cause. `notifyRestartCapReached(key)` is the cap-notice function restart's `onCapReached` calls: a non-startup spawn-failure notice carrying `ErrSpawnCapReached`. `notifyPersonaNotConnected(key, notice)` (b.f2b) raises the not-connected notice through one latch per persona (`notConnectedNoticeRaised`), so at most one of its texts is posted per episode: `blocked-on-prompt` (*Waiting on a prompt*: the session waits on a prompt or dialog, which CSCB never types into), `auto-restart-disabled` (with `session_restart_delay` 0 nothing will reconnect the persona: *Not connected* when its MCP session is not connected, *Not receiving messages* when it is connected but its message stream is gone) or `unproven-idle` (*Not connected*, at any delay: its row reads `working` and CSCB, unable to prove the session idle, has held back from it for `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS`, 10 min, from the first deferral; `noteWorkingRowDeferral(key, autoRestartDisabled)` counts the run of deferrals, `endWorkingRowDeferral(key)` ends it). The health check raises the latter through `notifyDisconnectedWithAutoRestartDisabled(key, cause)` (`cause` `disconnected` or `streamless`). `forgetNotConnectedEpisode(key)` ends the episode, silently, clearing the latch, the restart path's idle evidence and the run of deferrals: the server calls it when the persona's MCP session registers, the health check on every tick that finds the persona alive, connected and with its stream, and the persona teardown. The b.f2b stale-`working`-row evidence lives here too: the pure `classifyWorkingPane` (pane: `prompt`, `busy`, `blank` or `idle`) and `foldWorkingPaneRun` (the positive-idle rule, over a `TranscriptReading` from `session-transcript.ts`), `STALE_WORKING_WINDOW_MS` (60 s), `WORKING_ROW_READ_INTERVAL_MS` (5 s, the launch wait's read cadence) and `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS` (10 min), each with `_set…` / `_reset…` test seams, the clock seam `_setNow` / `_resetNow`, and the restart path's per-persona run behind `checkWorkingRowPane(key, persona?)`, `hasPendingWorkingRowEvidence(key)` and `forgetWorkingRowEvidence(key)`. `checkWaitingRowPane(key)` is the restart path's one pane read of a `waiting` row, and `PROMPT_ROW_STATES` names `ask_user` and `check_permission`. `checkPromptRowDeferral(key, state)` (b.jdc) keeps the restart path's run of deferrals on such a row whose tmux session lives: from `PROMPT_ROW_SWEEP_AFTER_MS` (10 min) after its first deferral, each one runs the memoized findMissing sweep and reads the row again, and a row then `ended` or `missing` escalates. `cancelWorkingRowWait(key)` cancels a launch's wait for a `working` row (or a wait its launch in flight has yet to start) for a persona teardown. The start pass parks a launch that waits for a `working` row (`launchOrPark`, over `spawnForPersona`'s `LaunchHooks.onWorkingRowWait`), so it returns without waiting it out; see Server-Managed Startup step 5. The lost-history and inconclusive-history notices of `diagnoseJsonlMissing` go through the same sink. Nothing in the session manager holds a Slack client. Before every launch the ladder calls one installed pre-launch trust patcher (`setPreLaunchTrustPatcher(patcher)`, type `PreLaunchTrustPatcher = (persona) => void`; b.av2 SR-6.2), which production sets to `trustPatchPersona` from `trust-bootstrap.ts`; with none installed (unit tests, the integration driver) no patch runs and nothing is written. The setter cannot clear it; tests use `_resetPreLaunchTrustPatcher()`. Immediately before each actual `client.spawn` / `client.resume` it calls one installed pre-launch reply guard (`setPreLaunchReplyGuard(guard)`, type `PreLaunchReplyGuard = (persona) => ReplyGuardUndo | void`; b.av2 SR-9.4), which production sets to `preLaunchReplyGuard` from `stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`; a path that only reconnects to a live instance or does nothing never calls it, and the optimistic first spawn runs its undo when it meets `ErrInstanceIdCollision`. A throw from the guard or its undo is logged and the launch goes on. With none installed nothing is written or patched; tests use `_resetPreLaunchReplyGuard()`. Builds spawn parameters from `persona-identity.ts` (b.av2 SR-2.2) and owns `personaConfigDirLabelValue`, the real-path form of the `config_dir` label: through `resolveRealPathStrict`, with no lexical fallback (bug b.g57), so an unresolvable directory throws `ConfigDirUnresolvableError` and `compareRowToPersona` reports it as `configDirResolved: false` with no `config_dir` verdict. Every launch first runs `checkLaunchConfigDir(persona)` (`checkPersonaConfigDir` with the spawn home and the `_setConfigDirFs` test seam); an unresolvable directory makes no agent-director call, goes to the one installed hook (`setConfigDirUnresolvableHook`; production: the bring-up controller's `holdForConfigDir`; with none, or when it does not hold the persona, the line is logged here) and returns `deferred` with the cause as `deferredBy` (step `claude-config-dir`), which `launchSession` maps to `'skipped'` and the start pass counts as `not-brought-up` `retrying` with that cause. A launch that finds the directory unresolvable only mid-ladder, and is held, returns `deferred` with `deferredBy` too. `holdLaunchIfConfigDirUnresolvable(persona)` does the same check for the restart module's kill adapter (`_buildKillSessionAdapter(getAppliedPersona)` in `server.ts`), which then leaves the instance and row alone. Spawn-path log lines name the persona as `"<name>" (key=<key>)`; key-only helpers log `persona=<key>`.
    ├── session-transcript.ts   b.f2b a persona's Claude Code transcript as the positive idle signal for a stale `working` row. `locateTranscript(row, configDir)` (pure) returns the row's `jsonl_path` when its file name is `<claude_session_id>.jsonl`, else `<configDir>/projects/<cwd slug>/<claude_session_id>.jsonl` through `resolveJsonlPath` (`cozempic.ts`), or undefined when neither can be had; a path from an earlier session is never read. `readTranscriptTurnState(path)` opens the file read-only and non-blocking, `fstat`s it, reads only a regular file and only its last `TRANSCRIPT_TAIL_BYTES` (256 KiB), and returns a `TranscriptReading`: `ended` or `open` with a `TranscriptSnapshot` (path, device, inode, size, mtime; compared by `sameTranscriptSnapshot`), or `unreadable` with a fixed, token-free reason; it never throws. `transcriptTailTurnState(tail, fromStart)` is the pure rule: scanning back past entries that are not part of a turn (every type but `user`, `assistant` and `queue-operation`, and a completed local command's three `user` lines), the first other entry decides: an `assistant` entry whose stop reason is `end_turn`, `stop_sequence` or `refusal` and which holds no `tool_use` block is `ended`, and so is an interrupt marker (a `user` entry whose only content block is the text `[Request interrupted by user]` or `[Request interrupted by user for tool use]`: Claude Code fires no Stop for an interrupted turn); anything else is `open`. An `attachment` that records a Stop hook keeping the turn going (`hook_additional_context` or `hook_blocking_error` with `hookEvent` `Stop`) is `open` too, where it comes after the last `assistant` entry. Importable without side effects.
    ├── permission-poller.ts    SR-2.1 polling loop + SR-2.2 Block Kit emitter. Owns the live LivePermission map, keyed on the composite `(claude_instance_id, request_token)` pair, consumes AD's plural `permission_requests` projection, resolves each listed row's persona from its `persona` label (b.av2 SR-7.1; the `channel` label is never read), posts one Slack message per open row to the persona's destination (its channel, or its DM with `dm.contact`, opened if needed) through that persona's client (`PollerDeps.clientFor` / `getPersona`) and the shared destination hold, attempting each new prompt and stuck-prompt warning only when the hold allows it (`PollerDeps.destinationHold`, `persona-destination-hold.ts`; a module-level default when none is injected, built over `PollerDeps.destinations` or a default resolver, which is that field's only use; cancelled and cleared by `_resetPollerState`), records on each live entry the conversation the prompt was posted in, keys its outage state by persona key, sends the wedge warning to the persona's destination the same way, skips every row of a persona that is not up (`PollerDeps.isPersonaUp`, b.av2 SR-6.4: no `get`, no prompt, no wedge tick, its tracked prompts and wedge state held; see Per-tick flow), and runs the SR-2.4 set-diff + `get-permission` closure reconciliation, whose `chat.update` goes to the entry's recorded conversation (see Permission Relay, [In-memory state](#in-memory-state-sr-1)), with four verdict-distinct chat.update renderings (operator-allow / operator-deny / timeout / find_missing) plus a fail-closed generic-deny fallback for unknown decision_reason and `ErrPermissionRequestNotFound`. `forgetPersonaPrompts(key)` (a persona teardown) drops everything the poller keeps for the persona and `cscb_<key>`: its tracked prompts, their not-posted records, its wedge and stuck-prompt state and its not-up skip episode, with no Slack call, no closing update and no trail event, so the posted prompts stay as they are; it logs one line when prompts were dropped, `[slack] permission-poller: persona=<key>: dropped <n> tracked prompt(s); their Slack messages stay as posted`.
    ├── permission-click-handler.ts  SR-4 single-decide-call relay; parses the action_id, resolves the receiving persona (`ClickDeps.receivingPersonaKey` through `getPersona`; b.av2 SR-7.1), calls `decideWithToken` unconditionally inside `withOutageDetection` keyed by that persona's key (always carrying the decoded `request_token`), renders one `chat.update` carrying the same verdict text the poller's SR-2.4 reconciliation produces (`*Permission* — Allowed` / `*Permission* — Denied by operator`) through that persona's client (`ClickDeps.clientFor`) to the conversation recorded on the live entry (see Permission Relay, [In-memory state](#in-memory-state-sr-1)) when a live entry is present, calls `markHandled` on success, swallows `ErrAlreadyDecided` silently, logs `ErrInvalidFlags` / `ErrAmbiguousRequest` without retry, and on `ErrRelayFallenBack` (b.qi1) repaints the prompt to point the operator at the tmux pane without touching the session or calling `markHandled`. Never calls `get` or `getPermission`; never mutates the pending map directly.
    ├── permission-action-id.ts SR-3 encode/decode helpers; anchored regex `^perm_(allow|deny)_(cscb_.+)_(<UUIDv4>)$` where the trailing UUIDv4-shape group is an outer-structure anchor disambiguating the underscore-bearing claude_instance_id capture — NOT a token-content validator (SR-1.3).
    ├── permission-trail.ts    SR-V visibility trail emitter — append-only JSONL store at `~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl`; lazy-open fd, `emitTrail()` / `emitTrailEvent()` API used by the poller, click handler, decide call, and closure render. See [permission-trail.jsonl](#permission-trailjsonl-claudechannelsslackpermission-trailjsonl).
    ├── restart.ts              Auto-restart — exponential-backoff relaunch on disconnect (SR-25), single-site failure counting, 5-failure cap with once-per-episode `onCapReached`/`ErrSpawnCapReached`, timer cancellation. Keyed by persona key (b.av2 SR-6.3): every dependency, the pending-timer map and the in-flight launch set take the key, and log lines name it as `persona=<key>`. `scheduleRestart` takes `opts.humanTrigger`, which clamps the computed delay down to `HUMAN_TRIGGER_DELAY_CEILING = 5` s for explicit inbound-message triggers (never up; counter/cap unchanged) (b.kvq). `RestartDeps.canRestart(key)` (the server passes the relaunch gate) keeps a persona that is not up from being restarted; Auto-Restart step 1 lists when it is asked. `RestartDeps.launchSession` returns `true`, `false` or `'skipped'`; a `'skipped'` launch (the persona is not up, or no longer applied) counts as neither success nor failure (see Auto-Restart step 5). `RestartDeps.serialize` (optional; production passes the lifecycle serializer's `run`) runs a fired timer's work through the per-persona serializer (b.av2 SR-6.6). `cancelRestartTimer(key)` cancels one persona's pending timer for a persona teardown, logging `[slack] Cancelled restart timer for persona=<key>` only when one was pending; work that has already started is left to finish.
    ├── backoff.ts              SR-25 pure per-persona session-restart failure counter, keyed by persona key — `nextBackoffDelay` (min(base·2^n, 900 s)), `isAtCap`, `shouldNotifyCap` once-per-episode latch, `recordFailure`/`recordSuccess`, and `forgetFailures(key)` (the same reset as `recordSuccess`, for a persona teardown; not a success). No imports, no timers/I/O; in-process state, lost on server restart by design. Also exports the stateless `doublingBackoffDelay(base, priorAttempts, ceiling)` = min(base·2^priorAttempts, ceiling), unit-agnostic and finite for any attempt count; `nextBackoffDelay` delegates to it with the 900 s ceiling and returns the same values as before. The persona Slack-unreachable policy (`persona-retry-schedule.ts`) is also per persona but reuses only the arithmetic: it counts different failures and shares no state with the session-restart counters.
    ├── outage-state.ts         Persona-scoped outage-flag state machine — tracks three orthogonal classes (`ad-unreachable`, `cwd-unreachable`, `tmux-unavailable`) per persona, keyed by persona key (b.av2 SR-6.3), and emits onset / all-clear notices through an injected `notify(key, text)` hook. Production wires the hook to the per-persona notifier, so each notice goes to that persona's destination and names the persona; the `cwd-unreachable` detail is the persona's working directory. `server.ts` calls `initOutageState` at boot and, outside dry run, `resetAllToHealthy` with the applied persona keys before any persona is brought up, and a persona teardown calls it with that persona's key alone (silent: no all-clear notice); AD verb calls run inside `withOutageDetection` / `withSpawnDetection`, which raise/clear flags on typed error/success. State mutates before every emit (SR-V-2.x state-before-emit); no timestamps by design. `_resetOutageState` is test-only.
    ├── interject.ts            b.av2 SR-9.1 / SR-9.2 / SR-13.1 `/interject` request handler. `handleInterject(req, remoteAddress, deps)` validates the request (method, localhost, `INTERJECT_BODY_CAP_BYTES` 32 KB cap, JSON, `persona` and `message` fields), resolves the persona by name or key with `resolvePersonaTarget`, refuses a persona that is not up with 503 (b.av2 SR-6.4), looks up its session, and sends one `notifications/claude/channel` with meta `user` and `ts` only. Injected dependencies: `getPersonaConfig` (the applied persona config, or null), `isPersonaUp` (production: the server's `createPersonaUpPredicate`), `getSessionByPersona`, and optional `now` and `log`. No import-time side effects: it reads no file, environment variable or config, binds nothing and never imports `server.ts`. Also exports `DEFAULT_INTERJECT_SENDER` (`interject`); `cron-dispatch.ts` imports the body cap. See [POST /interject](#post-interject).
    ├── crontable.ts            cscb_cron parser — `parseCrontable(text)` turns crontable text (5-field cron expression + prompt-file path + optional comma-separated persona list, each entry a persona name or key, space-delimited) into in-memory `{schedules, errors}` records. Pure module, no I/O, no timers (backoff.ts precedent); expression validity is delegated to the bundled zero-dependency `croner` library. Tolerant parse (PD-6): `#` comments and blank lines are skipped silently, each bad line becomes a structured parse-error record `{lineNumber, rawLine, reason class}` for the caller to log while sibling lines survive, and it never throws for content reasons. Duplicate lines are both kept; targets are kept verbatim, duplicates included, and are neither resolved nor validated here — name-to-key resolution and deduplication happen at fire time in `cron-dispatch.ts`. Each schedule carries identity `cscb-cron:<prompt-file-basename-without-extension>` (PD-1), the verbatim prompt path, and a `targets` union (all-bots marker vs. explicit list of targets as written). Parse-error reason classes keep their channel-list names (`wildcard-channel-list` for a literal `*`, `malformed-channel-list`, `path-with-spaces`).
    ├── cron-bootstrap.ts       cscb_cron boot-time crontable creator — `ensureCrontableExists(path)` guarantees a crontable file exists, returning `created` / `already-exists` / `failed(cause)`. Exclusive-create only: writes the template header with `{ flag: 'wx' }` when the path is absent, never write-then-rename and never mkdir's a missing parent. EEXIST is swallowed as `already-exists` (a human or another bot winning the create race is normal, not an error); a pre-existing file is left untouched byte-for-byte. Non-EEXIST errors surface as a `failed` outcome — never thrown, never `process.exit` — so a bad path can't crash boot. The module itself never logs; callers own log level and wording — `cron-scheduler.ts` uses it on both paths: an INFO line for the first-install create at `start()`, and, after a runtime deletion, a distinct re-created INFO line following the latched vanish WARN. D-Q1 invariant: this create is the server's ONLY write to the crontable, ever — the server never rewrites, reorders, normalizes, or prunes its content. The `#`-comment template header is exported as the shared constant `CRONTABLE_TEMPLATE_HEADER` (consumed by E6 bot discoverability); its documented line format tracks `crontable.ts`'s parser.
    ├── cron-log.ts             cscb_cron log writer — append-only writer for the dedicated cron log at `cron_log_path`. `createCronLog(path)` returns a handle whose `outcome`/`summary`/`info`/`warn` methods funnel through one internal append: per-(fire,target) outcome entries whose target field holds the target as written in the crontable, per-fire summary lines, info lines, and warn lines (the latter two are line kinds, not outcome classes). Write failures are swallowed (one `[slack] cron-log` console.error, fd dropped, self-heals next append) — they never propagate into dispatch. See [cron.log](#cronlog-cron_log_path). Pure half (record types + line formatting) is I/O-free.
    ├── cron-dispatch.ts        cscb_cron fire/delivery path — `createCronDispatcher({port, cronLog, cronTablePath, resolveTarget})` returns a handle whose `fire(schedule)` delivers ONE matched schedule; it logs every outcome and never throws. The scheduler's tick (`cron-scheduler.ts`) is the only caller — it hands the minute's matches to the dispatcher. The module imports nothing from server.ts, holds no timer, and reads no crontable. Each schedule delivers independently: every match becomes its own `/interject` POST and its own outcome line, so N schedules matching the same minute produce N separate messages. Fixed internal shape (E4's seam, do not collapse): resolve-targets → all-bots deferral branch → target-origin-agnostic per-target delivery. Targets resolve from the schedule's explicit persona list at FIRE time (`resolveDeliveryTargets`, pure): each written target goes through the injected `resolveTarget` (name or key → persona key, so a changed persona set applies at the next fire), then targets are deduplicated on the value to be POSTed in first-seen order — a persona named twice, by name or key, is delivered once; a target that resolves to nothing is POSTed as written, once per distinct text, so the handler answers 404. Each delivery target keeps the first-written text as its log target field. The all-bots marker is deferred to E4 — no read, no POST, one `fanout-deferred` outcome (target `-`) plus a `delivered=0 failed=0` summary (the target set is undefined until E4, and a no-match minute logs nothing, so 0/0 cannot read as quiet success). The prompt file is resolved and fresh-read every fire (never cached): leading `~` via `config.ts` `expandTilde`, absolute as-is, relative anchored to the CRONTABLE's directory — a deliberate divergence from system cron's $HOME convention so a prompt can colocate as `prompts/foo.md` and stay stable (E6's format reference carries this rationale). Missing → `prompt-missing`, unreadable → `prompt-unreadable` (errno), each target `-`, then a `failed=<targets>` summary and no POST. D-Q5 skip-never-truncate: the oversize predicate is UTF-8 byte length of the EXACT serialized `{persona, message, sender}` body > 32768 (`INTERJECT_BODY_CAP_BYTES`, imported from `interject.ts`), byte-identical to the /interject handler's own check — the measured string IS the sent string, so a 413 is structurally impossible; over-cap targets log `prompt-oversize` (size, cap) and are skipped, never truncated, never POSTed. Under-cap targets POST the measured body to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/interject` AS-IS (decision 7 — no extraction, no internal call) with `sender` = schedule identity (D-Q4 — else senderLabel defaults to `interject` and fires are indistinguishable from peer-bot traffic). Status → outcome: 200 `delivered`, 503 `no-session` (decision 1 — NO retry, no queue, no scheduleRestart), 404 `unknown-persona`, else/network error `http-error`. Sequential loop attempts every target; exactly one outcome line per (fire,target) plus one per-fire summary (`failed` = targets whose intended delivery did not happen). Known limitation (recorded, unsolved): a single non-loopback `bind` leaves no loopback listener, so every fire fails connection-refused → `http-error`; no loopback detection is added.
    ├── cron-scheduler.ts       cscb_cron once-per-minute dispatch tick — `createCronScheduler({dispatcher, cronLog, cronTablePath, clock?})` returns a `start`/`stop`/`tick` handle whose single self-re-arming minute-aligned `setTimeout` chain (NOT `setInterval` — ticks are serialized so the hot-reload step mutates the table only between whole passes) is the ONLY code path that dispatches schedules (decision 8). `start()` runs the crontable bootstrap FIRST (this `start()` is the sole `ensureCrontableExists` caller), performs the initial read + parse through the same shared load path the tick's reload uses (parse errors → `parse-error` outcome lines, target `-`), compiles each schedule's croner pattern once per load (queried, never started — no callback, so it schedules nothing itself), writes exactly ONE `scheduler started, N schedules loaded` info line (PD-4 outage-window marker), then arms the tick; `start()` is failure-isolated and never throws out to the server. Each tick first re-loads the crontable if its `(mtimeMs, size)` differs from the last successful load — no watcher, no dirty flag — then stamps the current wall-clock minute and skips the whole pass unless it is strictly after the last-ticked minute (monotonic guard — a backwards clock jump must not re-fire fired minutes), matches every in-memory schedule against that minute in server-local time via croner (DST is the library's, D-Q3), and hands each match, in crontable line order, to the dispatcher. No catch-up: a tick fires only the minute observed at its start; an overrun minute is simply skipped, and a no-match minute does nothing and logs nothing. The at-most-once `Map<schedule instance, epoch minute>` is a LOGGED ASSERTION, not the mechanism (decision 8): keyed on the compiled-schedule instance (NOT raw line text), so two identical operator lines are distinct schedules that EACH fire (PD-6) — only the SAME loaded schedule reappearing twice in one pass is skipped and shouted as a `SCHEDULER BUG` info line (an info line kind, deliberately NOT an outcome class, so it never pollutes the `grep http-error` triage view). NO rate cap / backlog cap / busy-detection / queue-depth check of any kind (owner gate answers 1-2). `stop()` is idempotent and cancels the pending timer.
    ├── health-check.ts         Periodic liveness poller — checks every applied persona on a timer via client.status. The work list comes from `HealthCheckDeps.getPersonas()` (production: the pure `buildPersonaWorkList(config, include?)`, persona key → working directory, one entry per persona however many channels it lists, filtered by `include`; the server passes the relaunch gate, so a persona that is not up, whether its Slack connection is not serving or its bring-up is broken or retrying, is left out of the tick); streaks, guard calls and the `cwd-unreachable` flag are keyed by persona key (b.av2 SR-6.3), and log lines name it as `persona=<key>`. It schedules recovery for dead sessions AND for sessions that are alive (AD live state) but not deliverable — either MCP-disconnected (b.9a7) OR connected-but-streamless (b.9cj — `connected === true` in the registry while the SDK has silently dropped the `_GET_stream` map entry, probed via `HealthCheckDeps.hasSessionStream`). Both non-deliverable cases require two consecutive failing ticks before acting, a shared false-positive guard for the freshly-launched window; routes all through `scheduleRestart` (restart.ts then reconnects vs. relaunches per case); skips personas at the failure cap via `HealthCheckDeps.isAtCap` (SR-25.3/25.4), including the reconnect path. b.f2b adds five optional dependencies, all wired in production: `isLaunchInFlight` (skip a persona whose launch is in flight, such as a parked start launch), `hasPendingWorkingRowEvidence` (schedule on the first undeliverable tick while the reconnect adapter gathers idle evidence for a `working` row), `isAutoRestartDisabled` with `notifyNotConnected(key, cause)` (with `session_restart_delay` 0, report an alive persona it would reconnect through the not-connected notice, worded for a `disconnected` or a `streamless` session), and `endNotConnectedEpisode` (end the persona's episode on every tick that finds it deliverable). `forgetDisconnectedStreak(key)` drops one persona's disconnected streak at once, for a persona teardown.
    ├── pid.ts                  PID file management — write, read, conflict detection, isProcessRunning.
    ├── cozempic.ts             Optional cozempic CLI integration — path resolution helpers retained for downstream callers.
    ├── tokens.ts               Dry-run detection only — `isDryRun()` reads `SLACK_DRY_RUN` at call time. The server reads no Slack token from the environment; each persona's tokens come only from its credentials file (`persona-credentials.ts`).
    ├── ack-tracker.ts          b.av2 SR-4.5 / SR-6.5 in-memory ack-reaction state. Entries are keyed by (persona key, conversation ID, message ts), encoded as a JSON array so no character inside a component can make two keys collide. `trackAck(personaKey, channelId, messageTs)` upserts an entry and prunes entries older than 30 days (`ACK_EXPIRY_MS`; there is no other bound or timer). `consumeAck(personaKey, channelId, messageTs)` deletes and reports only that persona's own entry, leaving another persona's entry for the same message alone. `forgetPersonaAcks(personaKey)` drops every entry of one persona (the persona teardown) and no other. Side-effect free at import; logs nothing.
    ├── trust-bootstrap.ts      b.uhv / b.k54 / b.av2 SR-6.2 trust patcher — sets `projects[<working_directory>].hasTrustDialogAccepted` and `hasCompletedProjectOnboarding` to `true` in each persona's `<effective claude_config_dir>/.claude.json` so the trust-folder dialog never appears at launch. Two entry points share one synchronous patch: the start pass `trustBootstrap(personaConfig)` (once per applied persona; failures go to `recordStartupError`, see startup step 4a-bis) and the per-launch `trustPatchPersona(persona)`, which `server.ts` installs into the session manager with `setPreLaunchTrustPatcher` so it runs before every spawn, resume and restart (failures are logged only). Idempotent (no rewrite when both flags set); atomic `.tmp` + `renameSync`; never auto-creates a missing file; never throws.
    ├── stop-hook-bootstrap.ts  SR-3 / b.av2 SR-9.4 patcher for `<claude_config_dir>/settings.json` — installs (or removes) the CSCB-managed Slack Reply Guard Stop-hook group, and runs the per-persona reply-guard steps before each launch. The managed command is the guard's absolute path followed by the record directory `<state dir>/reply-guard`, each single-quoted (`managedHookCommand(stateDir)`). Install rule, per directory (grouped by real path): the directory's personas are the applied personas whose effective `claude_config_dir` it is plus those whose running instance launched with it; the entry is ensured if any of them has an effective `stop_hook_bootstrap` of `true` or a record reading `true`, and removed otherwise. Two passes apply it: the start pass `stopHookBootstrap(personaConfig, stateDir)` over every applied persona (startup step 4a-ter) and the launch pass, which re-evaluates only a launching persona's new effective dir and its previous launched-with dir. `preLaunchReplyGuard(persona, personas, stateDir)` is the pre-launch step `server.ts` installs into the session manager: it writes the launching persona's record, updates its launched-with dir (kept in memory per persona key, empty at server start) and runs the launch pass, and returns an undo for a launch that turns out not to be one. It does nothing for a persona whose key is not in the applied set it reads at that moment (b.av2 SR-8.6), so a late launch of a persona a confirmed apply removed neither re-creates its record nor runs a launch pass. `teardownPersonaReplyGuard(stateDir, key)` deletes a persona's record and forgets its launched-with dir; the persona teardown calls it, then runs the launch pass (`stopHookLaunchPass`) over the removed persona's configured and launched-with dirs against the personas still applied. Never writes under the operator's `~/.claude`; no entry point throws.
    ├── reply-guard-record.ts   b.av2 SR-9.4 per-persona reply-guard record — the record directory `<state dir>/reply-guard` and the record `reply-guard/<key>` (see Configuration, reply-guard/<key>). Write (the exact text `true` or `false`, atomic, creating the directory), read (`true` only for `true` with at most one trailing newline; missing or unreadable is `absent`) and delete (a missing record is success). A key outside `PERSONA_KEY_RE` is refused before any filesystem call. Side-effect-free; every path comes from the caller's state directory.
    ├── atomic-write.ts         Atomic file writes. `atomicWriteFileSync(path, contents)` — write `<path>.tmp`, then rename it over `path`; on failure the `.tmp` file is removed and the error rethrown. Used by `stop-hook-bootstrap.ts` and `reply-guard-record.ts`. `durableWriteFileSync(path, bytes, fs?)` (b.av2 SR-8.1) is the atomic and durable write of the reload files: it creates a temporary file beside `path` named `<name>.<pid>.<random hex>.tmp` (opened exclusively, so each write has its own), writes every byte as given, fsyncs and closes it, renames it over `path`, then fsyncs the directory so the rename survives a crash. A failure before the rename leaves `path` as it was, removes the temporary file (best effort) and rethrows the error as raised. A failure to open or fsync the directory, after the rename, throws `DurableWriteUnsyncedError` (`path`, `code`, `syncError`): the new bytes are at `path` but may not survive a crash. A failure to close the directory after a successful fsync is ignored. The `fs` seam (`DurableWriteFs`) lets tests observe the call order or fail one call. `durableUnlinkSync(path, fs?)` is the durable delete: unlink, then fsync the directory. It returns true when a file was removed and false when `path` was already absent (ENOENT or ENOTDIR, with no directory sync). Any other failure is rethrown: an unlink failure is thrown as raised and leaves `path` as it was; a failure to open or fsync the directory comes after the unlink and throws `DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError` (`path`, `code`, `syncError`): `path` is gone, but the removal may not survive a crash. A failure to close the directory after a successful fsync is ignored, as for the write. Both are used by `reload.ts`, the delete for `config.json.pending`. Nothing here logs. Side-effect-free.
    ├── jsonl-persistence-check.ts  startup safeguard — detects JSONL transcript loss / non-persistent storage before the resume path; see startup step 4a-quater. Runs over the applied personas: `runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard(personaConfig, notify, deps?)`, with `resolveJsonlRoots(config, home?)` and `checkJsonlPersistence(config, readMountinfo?, home?)` for Layer 1. `runPersonaStorageCheck(persona, notify, deps?)` runs Layer 1 alone for one persona's effective root, for a persona a confirmed apply brings up (see Session Lifecycle, [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61)); it never throws. Also exports the b.av2 SR-7.4 archive-evidence helpers: `personaArchiveEvidenceScope(persona)` (the persona's `delivery: all` channel IDs, and whether a zero count is attributable), `UNATTRIBUTABLE_ZERO_REASON`, `makeDefaultArchiveCount(config)`, which returns an `ArchiveCountSince` `(channelIds, sinceEpochSeconds, ref)` counting over several channels at once, and `rfc3339ToEpochSeconds`. `session-manager.ts`'s b.wrb/b.fwu `diagnoseJsonlMissing` reuses the scope helper and the count to classify lost / never-created / inconclusive inside the resume path. Layer 2 imports `compareRowToPersona` from `session-manager.ts` (a function-scope use, so the import cycle is safe). Layer 2 skips a persona whose `claude_config_dir` cannot be resolved (`resolveRealPathStrict`, seam `configDirFs`) with one quiet line, before any agent-director call: no lost-history notice, record or label-mismatch verdict (bug b.g57).
    ├── postinstall.ts          npm/bun postinstall scaffold — `runPostinstall()` resolves the state dir with the server's `resolveServerStateDir()` (`SLACK_STATE_DIR` made absolute, a relative value resolving as the server resolves it and an empty value counting as unset, else `~/.claude/channels/slack`), creates it and the MCP config parent, then writes skeleton `config.json` (the empty persona config `{"personas": []}`, which loads with zero personas; b.av2 SR-1.7) and `slack-mcp.json` only when absent — an existing `config.json` is never touched, whatever its shape, so a pre-persona one gets the conversion error when the server starts (safe to re-run; migrates legacy `routing.json` → `config.json`). It creates no `access.json`, and one already in the state dir is ignored and left in place (b.av2 SR-10.1). Skills: it symlinks the bundled `debug-slack-channel-bots` skill into `~/.claude/skills/` (idempotent: a link already pointing at the package's copy is kept and logged `skipped: … (already linked)`; a link elsewhere, dangling included, is replaced; a real directory or regular file at that name is the operator's and is left in place, logged `skipped: … (not a link; left in place — remove it to let postinstall link the skill)`), and removes the link earlier releases created for the retired `claude-slack-channels-config` skill. It decides the link is its own by `lstat` and `readlink`: only a symbolic link whose target, resolved against the link's directory, is this package's `skills/claude-slack-channels-config` path (whether that path exists or not) is removed; a real directory, a regular file or a link to any other path at that name is left and logged `skipped: … (not created by postinstall; left in place)`. Link and removal failures log one `warning:` line through `describeThrownValue` and never fail the install. `PostinstallOptions.homeDir` overrides the home directory behind every default (the state dir when `SLACK_STATE_DIR` is unset, `~/.claude/slack-mcp.json`, `~/.claude/skills/`); tests run it against a temp home. `runAgentDirectorPostinstallProbe()` best-effort constructs an AD Client and logs one warning on failure — never throws, never fails the install; the SR-5.1 startup gate is the real enforcement.
    └── message-archive.ts      Optional SQLite archive of every inbound Slack message. Written through `persona-routing.ts`'s archive seam once per receiving persona, before any delivery decision; `INSERT OR IGNORE` keeps one row per (channel, ts), so a message received by two personas, or delivered twice to one (the `message` / `app_mention` pair, a redelivery), is stored once. The archive write comes before the per-persona dedupe. Names are resolved through the receiving persona's own client: `createPersonaNameResolverSource(clientFor)` looks the persona's client up on every call and keeps one name resolver per persona over that client, rebuilt (with fresh caches) when `clientFor` returns a different client, so names always resolve through the persona's current client, and `createPersonaArchiveWriter(db, source, onError)` is the fire-and-forget writer behind the archive seam. A persona with no client (dry run, not serving) still gets its row, with IDs in place of names and no Slack call. A write failure is logged as `[slack] message-archive write failed: <describeThrownValue>`.

stop-hooks/
└── slack-reply-guard.sh       Claude Code Stop hook — one-time, declinable reminder (exit 2) when the user messages that started the turn include a delivered Slack message (its envelope carries `via`) and no later assistant `tool_use` targets `mcp__slack-channel-router__reply`; runs only for a persona whose `reply-guard/<key>` record reads `true`; fail-open on any error. See [Slack Reply Guard](#slack-reply-guard-stop-hook). Bootstrapped into settings.json by `src/stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`.

scripts/
└── write-credentials.sh        b.av2 SR-12 / SR-1.4 (part) the credentials script `claude-slack-channel-bots credentials` runs, shipped in the package (package.json `files`): `write-credentials.sh <credentials file path>` under bash or zsh. Turns off tracing and automatic export (`set +xa`), checks the path (absolute or `~/…`, a file; exit 2 otherwise) and that `curl` is installed before any prompt, asks for `yes` before replacing an existing file, reads the bot token then the app token without echo (an empty answer re-asks on a terminal), checks each locally (not empty, `xoxb-` / `xapp-`, letters, digits and dashes only), then validates `bot_token` with `auth.test` and `app_token` with `apps.connections.open` through `curl -q … --config -` (the token only in a header line on curl's stdin, never in argv or the environment; a check passes only when Slack's reply starts `"ok":true`), and only when both pass writes `{bot_token, app_token}` through an exclusive `mktemp` file beside the target (mode 0600 from creation, removed by a trap on any failure or interrupt) and one rename, refusing when the target changed meanwhile. Never prints a token.
```
