    - **Record unreadable or invalid.** A record is absent only when reading it fails with ENOENT or ENOTDIR; any other read failure (`EISDIR` for a directory, `not a regular file` for a FIFO, socket or device, which is never read, or a record over the size cap; see Reload files, Size cap), and any validation failure, refuses the start without falling back to `config.json`. Logs one unclassed line, `[slack] Fatal: last-applied record error — <cause> Deleting the last-applied record "<record>" makes the next start apply the configuration file "<config>" as it stands.`, and exits 1.
    - **Real-path collision in the record (b.av2 SR-1.5, record-start part).** Record mode skips only the loader's shared-path rule, so a record whose personas share a `working_directory` or a `credentials_file` still starts. The collision is caught by each involved persona's bring-up checks (step 5), using the E5 outcomes: a shared working directory is `persona-directory-unusable` (`retrying`, re-checked on its timer) and a shared credentials file is `persona-credentials-invalid` (`broken`), each naming the other persona. The other personas come up normally. A persona broken by the shared credentials file still holds the digest of its own path's bytes (hashed, never parsed), so a later re-save of that file is a pending credentials change for it (Reload Detection Tick). In dry run the credentials check does not run, so a shared credentials file is not caught there.
    - **Zero personas.** An empty `personas` array is a valid applied set: the start runs with no persona brought up.

    Every refusal is logged by the controller as one line and happens after the PID check, so a duplicate start never writes the record, and before any port is bound, the PID file is written, a Slack connection opens or anything is spawned. Nothing is applied on a refusal. On an applied start `main()` sets `personaConfig` to the applied config and keeps it as `appliedConfig`, the start-time server-wide values (such as `getRestartDelay`'s delay, `bind` and `port`), which never change until the next start. The only other assignment of `personaConfig` is a confirmed apply's step 1 (the reload controller's `onApplied`), which sets it to `appliedConfig` with the confirmed persona set. `appliedConfig` is declared (`let appliedConfig!: PersonaConfig`) before `createReloadController`, so `onApplied`'s closure never depends on declaration order, and is assigned right after the start resolves; an `onApplied` that ran before that throws (`a confirmed apply ran before the start resolved its configuration`) and the controller logs it, rather than building a config without the start-time values. Detection is armed only after the start bring-up, so this is not expected. The Stop-hook start pass, the reply-guard getter, the bring-up controller's applied set and the launch after a bring-up retry all read `personaConfig` at call time, so they see the applied set as it stands. It then installs `trustPatchPersona` as the session manager's pre-launch trust patcher (`setPreLaunchTrustPatcher`) and `preLaunchReplyGuard` as its pre-launch reply guard (`setPreLaunchReplyGuard`, bound to `STATE_DIR` and the applied persona set), ahead of the Slack connections, `Bun.serve` and `initRestart`, so no launch path runs unpatched, and logs `[slack] Loaded persona config: <N> persona(s)`. `bind` and `port` come from the applied config. After a start from the record, the detection tick's first pass (step 9) compares `config.json` with the record per SR-8.3 (b.av2 SR-8.7), so an unconfirmed edit, destructive or not, stays pending and `config.json.pending` is written after the start; it is never applied at the start. A confirmation (`config.json.apply`) left while the server was down is processed by that first pass (see [Confirmation](#confirmation-sr-85)); the start itself never reads it.
3. **SR-3.2 template refresh** — `installSlackChannelBotTemplate(personaConfig)` builds the SR-3.1 `MakeTemplateParams` and calls `client.makeTemplate({ ..., overwrite: true })`. Atomic replacement is the library's responsibility (sibling-tempfile + `rename(2)`). Fatal on rejection. It runs right after step 2a, over the loaded persona config, and keeps the params it installed for the apply-time refresh. That refresh (apply step 5, see [Template refresh](#template-refresh-bav2-sr-86-step-5)) rewrites only the memory-read rules and is not fatal.
4. **b.av2 SR-6.3 start sweep** — `reconcileOrphans(personaConfig)`, run before the trust patch and any spawn. `client.list({ label: ['service=cscb'] })` enumerates every CSCB spawn, and each row is judged by its `persona` label against the applied personas.

    **Pre-persona rows are kept, never deleted (b.1ix).** A row with no `persona` label was spawned by a build that predates persona labels (instance IDs `cscb_<name>_<channel>`). No persona's `cscb_<key>` is ever such an ID, so no launch resumes or reuses it and keeping it is harmless; each persona's first launch spawns fresh (the old transcripts stay on disk, unresumed). `keepPrePersonaRow` kills such a row only when its state is live (`AGENT_DIRECTOR_LIVE_STATES`), with one kill call per start, and logs the result: `[slack] reconcileOrphans: pre-persona row (no persona label) instanceId=<id> state=<state> tmux_session=<name> is live — killing it; the row is kept (a pre-persona row is never deleted)`, then either `[slack] reconcileOrphans: kill reported success for pre-persona row instanceId=<id> — row kept; a reported success does not prove tmux session <name> is gone` or the `orphan-cleanup` record `kill failed for pre-persona row instanceId=<id>: <errName> message="…"; row kept, its session may still be running`. An `ended` or `missing` pre-persona row gets no call and no line. agent-director 0.10.0's `kill` leaves the row's state as it was, so a killed row would still read live at every later start and be killed again each time. After the loop, when it called `kill` on at least one pre-persona row (a failed kill included, since its session may be gone all the same), the sweep runs one findMissing (`reconcileKilledPrePersonaRows`): a killed row whose session is gone then reads `missing`, and later starts leave it alone. It shares `reconcileMissingSweep`'s memo and single-flight core (`sharedFindMissingSweep`, b.m4r); the start sweep runs before any launch or health check, so the sweep is a fresh one, and the launches right after reuse it within its TTL. The call goes to the start sweep's client directly, like its list and kills, so no persona's outage flag is raised or cleared. After the sweep's own `[slack] reconcileOrphans: findMissing sweep for killed pre-persona rows — count=<n> ids=[…] …` line comes `[slack] reconcileOrphans: findMissing after the kills of <n> live pre-persona row(s): missing=<n> [<ids>] still-live=<n> [<ids>] — a row that still reads live is killed again at the next start` (`still-live` holds each killed row the sweep didn't mark missing, an unverified one included). A failed sweep logs its failure line and `[slack] reconcileOrphans: findMissing after the kills of <n> live pre-persona row(s) failed — they still read live and are killed again at the next start`; the start goes on. The delete is withheld because agent-director 0.10.0's `kill` can report success while the session lives on, and a deleted row would leave that session running with nothing to find it by; at the upgrade to personas that is every bot at once. The README's upgrade runbook (stop the old bots with `stop --stop-bots`, wait for `ended`/`missing`, confirm with `tmux ls`, then start) keeps the first start from finding any live. The b.fmk version replaces the kill with agent-director's verified one.

    Every other row is killed + deleted when any of these holds, checked in this order:
    - its `persona` label names a persona absent from the applied set (`absent persona`); the delete stays until b.fmk, because agent-director 0.10.0 has no reuse and a persona added again must start fresh;
    - its instance ID is not that persona's `cscb_<key>` (`wrong instance ID`);
    - its `cwd` differs from that persona's working directory by real path (`wrong cwd`, via `compareRowToPersona`).

    Every other row is kept for step 5. **Unresolvable working directory (b.av2 SR-6.4).** When the persona's working directory has no real path (missing, not searchable, a dangling symlink) and the row's `cwd` has no real path either or equals the configured `working_directory` lexically (`compareRowToPersona`'s `cwdCheckDeferred`), the `cwd` condition cannot be evaluated and is deferred: the row is kept (the other three conditions still apply), with one line per persona, `[slack] reconcileOrphans: persona "<name>" (key=<key>) working_directory="<path>" cannot be resolved to a real path — keeping its rows; the cwd check is deferred to its launch`. Such a persona is not up (`retrying`, or `broken` when its credentials also fail; step 5), so its instance keeps running, unserved (Connection step 3, Permission Relay, `/interject`), and its row waits for the launch that follows the directory's return, where the ladder's `cwd` guard runs against the real path. A row whose `cwd` resolves to an existing directory elsewhere is swept as `wrong cwd` even while the working directory is missing, so a persona can't adopt another persona's directory; its launch then spawns fresh. A row whose `cwd` differs by real path from a resolvable working directory is swept as before. A `channel` label left on a row by an older spawn plays no part. Each removed row logs `[slack] reconcileOrphans: sweeping row (<reason>) persona=<persona> instanceId=<id> state=<state> — killing and deleting`, with ` cwd=<row cwd>` before the dash for `wrong cwd`; `<persona>` is `"<name>" (key=<key>)` for an applied persona and the raw label value for an absent one. A failed kill of a swept row still attempts the delete (`killAndDeleteSweptRow`). Kill and delete failures record `orphan-cleanup` (`kill failed for orphan instanceId=<id> persona=<persona>: <ErrName>`, likewise `delete failed …`) and a list failure records `orphan-cleanup-list-failed`; none blocks startup. The sweep ends with `[slack] reconcileOrphans: found=<n> killed=<n> failed=<n>; pre-persona rows kept=<n> live=<n> kill-failed=<n>`: `found`, `killed` and `failed` count the swept rows (found, deleted, delete failed), the rest the pre-persona rows (listed, live and killed, kill failed); `OrphanReconcileResult` returns the same counts. Dry run skips the sweep and logs `[slack] dry-run: skipping orphan reconciliation`.
4a-bis. **b.uhv / b.k54 trust-bootstrap** — `trustBootstrap(personaConfig)` (from `src/trust-bootstrap.ts`) runs over the applied personas: for each persona's working directory it patches the `.claude.json` in the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (its own, else the top-level one) so the Claude Code trust-folder dialog never appears at launch. Each persona is patched once, however many channels it lists. It reads `<dir>/.claude.json` and ensures `projects[<working_directory>].hasTrustDialogAccepted` and `projects[<working_directory>].hasCompletedProjectOnboarding` are both `true`. Idempotent: when both flags are already `true` the file is not rewritten (verifiable via unchanged `mtime`). Writes are atomic (`.tmp` + `renameSync`), and the read-modify-write is fully synchronous, so two personas sharing one config dir cannot lose each other's update. Soft-skip behavior: a persona with no configured `claude_config_dir` logs `[slack] trust-bootstrap: "<name>" (key=<key>) has no claude_config_dir — skipping` and continues without recording an error (Claude uses its own default; there is nothing to patch). A missing or unreadable `.claude.json` logs an info line and calls `recordStartupError('trust-bootstrap-config-missing', '"<name>" (key=<key>): cannot read <path>')` — the file is never auto-created. Malformed JSON records `trust-bootstrap-config-parse`, and any unexpected per-persona failure records `trust-bootstrap` (`unexpected error for "<name>" (key=<key>) cwd=<dir>`). The function never throws to the caller, so one bad persona cannot block the rest. Runs in both real and dry-run modes — it's a config-file patch, not a session operation. The call site is in `main()` right after step 4 (`reconcileOrphans`), before any spawn fires.

    - **Per-launch patch (b.av2 SR-6.2).** The same patch also runs before every launch: `trustPatchPersona(persona)`, installed as the pre-launch trust patcher, runs at the top of every ladder run — fresh spawn, resume or restart (see step 5 and Auto-Restart step 5). At launch time a patch failure is logged only, as `[slack] trust-bootstrap: pre-launch patch failed [<class>] <detail> — <cause>` with the same class names, and never adds a `startup-errors.log` entry; a missing `.claude.json` therefore logs two lines (the info line and the failure line). The start pass records failures as described above.
4a-quater. **b.zak JSONL-persistence safeguard** — `runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard(personaConfig, notify)` (from `src/jsonl-persistence-check.ts`), run over the applied personas. Call site: `main()` in `server.ts`, immediately after step 4a-bis (`trustBootstrap`) and before step 4a-ter (`stopHookBootstrap`) — critically, **before** step 5's resume path, which silently `delete+fresh`-spawns a memory-less bot when `resume` throws `ErrJsonlMissing`. Detection/reporting only: it never changes resume behavior, never throws, and never blocks startup (wrapped in its own `try/catch` at the call site as well). Every warning is a persona notice: the server passes the per-persona notifier's `notify`, which posts to the affected persona's destination, names the persona, and only logs in dry run. Sibling scope: the lost / never-created / inconclusive classification inside the resume path itself is **b.wrb / b.fwu**, not this module. Two layers:

    - **Layer 1 — non-persistent storage.** `checkJsonlPersistence` resolves the deduped set of the personas' effective `<claude_config_dir>/projects` roots (a persona with no configured dir uses `<home>/.claude`; with no personas at all, the home default root is checked) and classifies each root's mount fstype by longest-prefix match over `/proc/self/mountinfo` (string-prefix, not a stat — a not-yet-created dir classifies by its parent mount). A root on `tmpfs`/`ramfs` → `recordStartupError('jsonl-non-persistent', …)` plus one notice through the notifier to each affected persona's destination — those whose effective JSONL root is the flagged root, not every persona (resume is structurally impossible for those personas on this host). An undeterminable fstype (unreadable/unparseable/unresolvable) → `recordStartupError('jsonl-persistence-check-warning', …)`, no notice.
    - **Layer 2 — per-persona transcript loss.** For each applied persona it fetches the persona's AD row (through `withOutageDetection`, the sanctioned CSCB→AD entry point). A row the collision ladder will spawn fresh instead of resuming — its `cwd` differs from the persona's working directory by real path, or its `config_dir` label is missing or mismatched (`compareRowToPersona`, the same check as step 5's guards) — is not checked: one log line naming the persona, the mismatch (the row's `cwd` against the working directory, or the missing or changed `config_dir` label) and that the transcript was not checked because the collision ladder will spawn fresh, with no record and no notice. Otherwise, when the row carries a `claude_session_id`, it stats the AD-recorded `jsonl_path` **and** a config-dir-aware fallback path composed the same way agent-director composes it (`resolveJsonlPath`, under the persona's effective config dir). Outcomes: persisted exists → healthy, silent. Persisted stale/missing but fallback exists → `recordStartupError('jsonl-transcript-stale-path', …)` + a notice through the notifier to that persona's destination (history is on disk but AD 0.8.0's resume only stats the persisted path, so it would throw `ErrJsonlMissing` and wipe the persona's memory). Both missing → the `message_archive_db` archive (when configured) is consulted for evidence under the b.av2 SR-7.4 rule (`personaArchiveEvidenceScope`): only messages in the persona's `delivery: all` channels since `started_at` count, in one query over all of them. A persona with no `delivery: all` channel counts 0 without the archive being opened (even when none is configured), and since every persona has a channel or DMs on, that persona always takes (once `started_at` parses) the unattributable-zero path below. A count ≥1 → `recordStartupError('jsonl-transcript-lost', …)` + a notice to that persona's destination saying its conversation memory has been lost (conversation provably happened, transcript is gone). A zero count for a persona with any `delivery: mentions` channel or DMs on is not evidence of idleness, because the archive cannot attribute messages from `mentions` channels or DMs to a persona: one quiet log line saying the archive evidence is inconclusive (`UNATTRIBUTABLE_ZERO_REASON`), no record and no notice. The archive check is read-only: a missing archive DB file yields no evidence (it is never created here). Both missing with no archive evidence → a single quiet log line only: a persona idle since spawn has no transcript yet because Claude creates the `.jsonl` lazily on first message — normal, not an error, and must not alarm every boot. Log lines name the persona as `"<name>" (key=<key>)`. Per-persona AD/get errors (including `ErrSpawnNotFound`) are logged and skipped; the sweep never aborts. A persona whose `claude_config_dir` cannot be resolved to a real path (bug b.g57) is skipped before its row is fetched, with one quiet line: its launch waits for the directory with the row kept, so there is no lost history to report.
4a-ter. **b.osj / b.av2 SR-9.4 stop-hook bootstrap** — `stopHookBootstrap(personaConfig, STATE_DIR)` (from `src/stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`) is the start pass: over every applied persona (b.av2 SR-6.2) it installs or removes the CSCB-managed Slack Reply Guard Stop hook in `<claude_config_dir>/settings.json`. Call site: `main()` in `server.ts`, immediately after step 4a-quater (`runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard`) and before step 5 (`startupSessionManager`) — before any spawn fires. Runs in both real and dry-run modes (config-file patch, not a session operation). Never throws to the caller; per-dir failures are caught and recorded via `recordStartupError`. The same install rule runs again before every launch, as the launch pass (step 5, "Reply-guard steps before every spawn and resume"). Behavior:

    - **Install rule, per directory (b.av2 SR-9.4).** Each applied persona counts toward its effective `claude_config_dir` (its own, else the top-level one) and toward the directory its running instance launched with (the launched-with dir, kept in memory per persona key: set at each launch, cleared by `teardownPersonaReplyGuard`, empty at server start). Directories are grouped by real path (`resolveRealPath`, lexical fallback), so two spellings of one directory are one group and its `settings.json` is patched once, at the first contributing persona's configured path. A persona is enabled when its effective `stop_hook_bootstrap` is `true` or its record (`reply-guard/<key>`, see Configuration) reads `true`. The entry is ensured when any persona counting toward the directory is enabled and removed otherwise (emptied groups pruned). Whether one persona is reminded is its own record's business, read by the guard at hook time, so personas sharing a directory each follow their own value. Diagnostics name the contributing personas as `personas="<name>" (key=<key>), …`.
    - **Managed-entry shape.** Own group `{"hooks":[{"type":"command","command":"<managed command>"}]}`, no `matcher` field (Stop is not tool-scoped). Recognition rule: any Stop-hook `command` string containing the substring `slack-reply-guard.sh` is CSCB-managed.
    - **Canonical command.** `managedHookCommand(stateDir)` is the guard's absolute path followed by the record directory `<state dir>/reply-guard`, each POSIX single-quoted (`shellSingleQuote`), because Claude Code runs a hook command through a shell; the guard reads the directory as its first argument. The script path resolves relative to the installed package (same idiom as `src/install-skill-pointer.ts`), correct under both published-package layout and repo checkout. Only the exact current command is canonical: a managed entry with a bare path, a stale install path or another record directory is rewritten to it (self-heal).
    - **Idempotent write decision.** `scanStopGroups` returns `hasSingleCanonicalGroup=true` only when there is exactly one Stop group whose sole hook entry has `type=='command'`, `command==canonicalCmd`, no `matcher`, and no extra keys. In that state the write is skipped entirely. Any drift — stale command, duplicates, extra keys, extra hooks in the managed group — triggers a strip-all-managed + append-canonical rewrite, written atomically (`atomicWriteFileSync`, `.tmp` + rename). Every read-modify-write is synchronous, so two personas launching at once cannot interleave on one `settings.json`.
    - **Create-missing.** ENOENT on the target `settings.json` for an enabled dir → the file is created with just the managed group.
    - **Malformed JSON soft-fail.** Non-ENOENT read errors, unparseable JSON, and non-object top levels all report a class label of `stop-hook-bootstrap-settings-read` / `stop-hook-bootstrap-settings-parse` / `stop-hook-bootstrap-settings-shape` and leave the file untouched — never a clobber.
    - **Personal-config-dir refusal (SR-3.11).** `isForbiddenHomeClaudeDir` resolves both the candidate dir and `homedir()/.claude` through `resolveRealPath` from `src/config.ts` (`realpathSync`, with a lexical `resolve()` fallback whenever realpath fails), so symlinked personal dirs cannot slip past a string compare. On a match the dir is skipped and never written, so a persona whose directory resolves there gets no reminder. The start pass records `stop-hook-bootstrap-refuse-home` once per server start (all spellings of `~/.claude` are one group); the launch pass logs the refusal to the server log only.
    - **Skip conditions.** A persona with no effective `claude_config_dir` gets a server-log line only (`[slack] stop-hook-bootstrap: "<name>" (key=<key>) has no claude_config_dir — skipping`), never a `startup-errors.log` record, and no reminder. Empty or whitespace-only dirs are treated as absent (guards against `resolve("")` landing in the process cwd). A dir that fails `statSync` (or is not a directory) is skipped; the ensure path reports `stop-hook-bootstrap-dir-missing` / `stop-hook-bootstrap-not-a-dir`, and a missing dir on the remove path is silent.
    - **jq presence check.** A one-time `command -v jq` probe per server start: if `jq` is absent the entry is **still installed** (the hook fails open at hook time when `jq` is missing) but a `stop-hook-bootstrap-jq-missing` startup error is recorded so operators see the warning. The launch pass does not probe.
    - **Duplicate collapse and removal (SR-3.7, SR-3.8).** When rewriting an enabled dir, every managed `{type, command}` object across every Stop group is stripped before the canonical group is appended, so pre-existing duplicates collapse to one. Removal for a dir with no enabled persona strips every managed object and prunes any group left with an empty `hooks` array.
    - **Never-throws contract.** A top-level `try/catch` around the whole entry point plus per-dir `try/catch` blocks record `stop-hook-bootstrap` / `stop-hook-bootstrap-dir` classes on unexpected failure. Other dirs continue processing. Timing note (SR-3.15): on-disk state is refreshed every boot and every launch, and Claude Code normally picks up hook edits in `settings.json` while running (its settings file watcher), so the managed hook's presence in a directory may change under a running instance, for example when a neighbour sharing the directory launches. A running instance still keeps the value it launched with, because the guard decides by that instance's own record (`reply-guard/<CSCB_PERSONA>`), which is written only at the instance's launch. A live session that survives a CSCB restart via reconnect is not relaunched, so its record, and its value, change only at its next spawn or resume.
5. **Per-persona bring-up and launch (b.av2 SR-6.1)** — `startupSessionManager(applied, { bringUp: personaBringUps })` over the applied set runs after the start-wide passes (step 4 sweep, 4a-bis trust patch, 4a-quater JSONL safeguard, 4a-ter Stop-hook bootstrap). It is the reload controller's `lifecycle.startBringUp`, and `main()` runs it through `reload.runStartBringUp()`, so it brings up exactly the persona set step 2a applied. `personaBringUps` is the bring-up controller (`createPersonaBringUpController` in `src/persona-bringup-controller.ts`), built in `main()` right after the connection manager. It brings each persona up once, so a persona listed in several channels is brought up and spawned exactly once. Steps 1–3 run for every persona at once (the controller's `bringUp`), so no persona's Slack connection waits behind another persona's launch. Only step 4 goes through the launch pool: at most `concurrency` launches (default 3) run at a time, taken first in, first out in the order the personas become ready. A persona that is not `up` takes no launch slot. Per persona, in order:
   - **`persona-start` line** — one line per applied persona as its bring-up starts, naming the persona, its entry and its key (see Logging, Persona bring-up lines).
   - **Steps 1 and 2: local credentials check** (`checkPersonaCredentials`) **and working-directory check** (`checkPersonaWorkingDirectory`), both evaluated before either stops the bring-up, so both causes are logged (`persona-credentials-*`, `persona-directory-*` lines). Dry run skips the credentials check and reads no credentials file.
   - **`claude_config_dir` check** — once steps 1 and 2 pass, the controller's `checkConfigDir` resolves the persona's effective `claude_config_dir`; an unresolvable one holds the persona (`persona-config-dir-unresolvable`, see Config-dir-unresolvable below) and step 3 does not run.
   - **Step 3: Slack validation and connection** — the connection manager's `bringUp(persona, tokens)`: `auth.test` on a short-lived validation client, then the long-lived Web API and Socket Mode clients (see the `persona-connections.ts` entry for the client options, the 10 s bounds on `auth.test` and `start()`, backoff and reopen). The manager logs every Slack outcome itself (`persona-slack-unreachable`, `persona-credentials-refused`). In dry run the persona is up at once with its placeholder identity.
   - **Step 4: launch** — only when the persona's outcome is `up`: `spawnForPersona` (SR-1.4 collision-then-act dispatcher), below. The connection is therefore up before the instance is launched.

   **Outcomes (b.av2 SR-6.1, SR-6.4).** Every bring-up ends with one outcome, which the controller keeps per persona:

   | Cause | Class | Outcome |
   |---|---|---|
   | None: steps 1–3 passed | — | `up` |
   | Credentials file missing, unreadable or invalid (step 1) | `persona-credentials-missing` / `-unreadable` / `-invalid` | `broken` |
   | Slack refused a token (step 3, at bring-up or on a reopen), or refused the bot token in a Web API call of the running persona (bug b.ujn) | `persona-credentials-refused` | `broken` |
   | Slack unreachable (step 3) | `persona-slack-unreachable` | `retrying` |
   | Working directory missing or unusable (step 2) | `persona-directory-missing` / `-unusable` | `retrying` |
   | `claude_config_dir` cannot be resolved to a real path, found right before step 3 or by a later launch (bug b.g57) | `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` | `retrying` |

   A persona with both a credentials cause and a directory cause is `broken`. Its directory is still re-checked, and its start and cleared lines are logged, but it goes no further while the credentials cause holds; it comes up only once both clear. A credentials-broken persona is not retried. While the server runs, it recovers only through a confirmed change to its credentials file (b.av2 SR-6.4): apply step 6 brings it up afresh (see [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86) and [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61)). A start, from the record or not, also reads every credentials file as it stands (b.av2 SR-8.7), so a fixed file brings it up at the next start too.

   A persona that isn't `up` is not launched by the start, records no startup error, raises no notice and is not counted as a failed spawn. The lines above say why. Its agent-director row and any instance still running from before are kept (b.av2 SR-6.4): nothing kills them for the persona being down, but the server does not serve the instance. Its MCP registration is refused (Connection step 3), the permission poller skips its rows (Permission Relay, Per-tick flow) and `/interject` answers 503 (Endpoint Inventory). When the persona comes up, its launch reaches the row through the collision ladder below, so the history is kept. Personas are brought up independently, so one persona's failure stops neither the others nor the server.

   **Retries run on per-persona timers, outside `startupSessionManager`.** The start pass returns once every persona has an outcome and every `up` persona's launch has settled or is parked waiting for a `working` row (see the `working` branch below, b.f2b); it never waits on a retry, and a retrying persona never delays another. Two kinds of retry exist, both on the SR-3.2 backoff (5 s doubling to a 300 s ceiling, no cap, `persona-retry-schedule.ts`), and neither touches the restart failure counter or cap (`backoff.ts`):

   - **Slack-unreachable** — the connection manager retries on its own per-persona timer with the tokens it was handed.
   - **Directory-broken** — the controller opens no Slack connection and re-checks the directory (`checkPersonaWorkingDirectory`) on the persona's own timer. Once the directory is usable the bring-up continues with no restart and no confirmation: Slack validation and connection, then the launch. If Slack is then unreachable, the persona becomes Slack-unreachable `retrying`.
   - **Config-dir-unresolvable (bug b.g57)** — the controller checks the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (`checkLaunchConfigDir`) right before step 3, at start, at an apply bring-up and when a directory retry reaches Slack; an unresolvable directory holds the persona there, so it never connects. Every launch (start, retry, restart, apply) also resolves it first. When it can't be resolved, the launch makes no agent-director call and hands the persona to the controller (`holdForConfigDir`), which closes its Slack connection. Either way the persona is `retrying` with no connection and receives nothing: its MCP session is dropped if it was up, its notices are held (per persona, at most 20, oldest dropped), and its row and instance are kept (no kill, delete or spawn). The controller re-checks the directory on the persona's own timer (5 s doubling to 300 s). Once it resolves, the controller logs the cleared line, connects the persona with its held credentials and launches it, which resumes its row through the ladder with no confirmation. The restart path's kill is skipped too (`holdLaunchIfConfigDirUnresolvable`), and the relaunch is `'skipped'`, counting toward no failure or cap. A confirmed credentials change while held is taken as a retrying persona's is (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)).

   A retry keeps using the credentials content it holds, read at the persona's bring-up, until a confirmed change replaces it; the controller holds those tokens and never re-reads the file. An edit to the file made meanwhile is ignored by the retry. The detection tick notices it: the file's digest no longer matches the one the controller holds for the persona (`credentialsDigest`), so a credentials change is pending (see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83)). Once confirmed, apply step 4 hands a retrying persona the new content, and its later attempts use it (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)).

   **Launch after a retry.** A persona that reaches `up` through a retry is launched from its own retry path, not by the health check: when a directory retry's Slack step leaves the persona's outcome `up` (the manager's status is `up`, `lost` or `retrying` in the `reopen` phase, since a blip right after the connection came up does not undo it), or when the manager reports `up` right after `retrying` its bring-up (the controller's `onConnectionStatus`). The launch runs once per persona through `spawnForPersona(persona, personaConfig, false)` with the persona's current applied declaration, so it shares the one-launch-in-flight guard that restarts use and a launch failure takes the runtime spawn-failure path. An `up` after `lost` or a reopen launches nothing, since the instance is already running.

   **Applied-set guard (b.av2 SR-8.6).** From a confirmed apply's step 1 on, no launch or restart path acts for a key outside the applied set, even before that persona's teardown. The checks read the set live (`personaConfig`) and live in three places: the bring-up controller (`appliedPersonas` getter and `isApplied(key)`, `persona-bringup-controller.ts`), where a directory re-check for such a key stops (`[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): no longer applied — its working-directory retry stops`) and a launch after a retry is skipped (`… up after its bring-up retry (<via>) but no longer applied — not launching`), and a re-check tests the directory against the current set; the shared up check (`isPersonaUpGiven` in `persona-start.ts`, over `PersonaUpQuery.isApplied`), behind the relaunch gate (restart, `launchSession`, the health-check work list) and `isPersonaUp` (MCP session admission, the permission poller's skip, `/interject`'s 503); and `preLaunchReplyGuard` (`stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`), which does nothing for such a key. This is the one full statement of the guard; Auto-Restart and the Health-Check Poller refer here. What the guard does not do is stop anything: the removed persona's Slack connection and instance stay until its teardown in apply step 2 of the same apply (see [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65)). For what else a removal changes at step 1, see [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86).

   **Never posted to Slack (b.av2 SR-7.2, part).** Bring-up failures are logged to the server log only, at start and on every later retry, and never to `startup-errors.log`. No notice is posted or held for a later flush under any identity.

   Spawn-path log lines name the persona JSON-quoted with the key beside it, e.g. `spawnForPersona: spawned "Ops Bot" (key=ops_bot_5e2526f3) …`; under dry run each persona logs `dry-run: skipping spawn for "<name>" (key=<key>) cwd=<dir>` and nothing is spawned.
   - **Trust patch before every launch (b.av2 SR-6.2).** Each ladder run first calls the installed pre-launch trust patcher (`trustPatchPersona`, see step 4a-bis) for the persona's working directory in its effective config dir — once, before the first spawn or resume the ladder can make, so it covers fresh spawns, resumes and the replacement spawns below. It never runs under dry run and never for a call that joins an in-flight launch. A throw is logged as `[slack] spawnForPersona: pre-launch trust patch failed for "<name>" (key=<key>) — launching anyway: <error>` and the ladder continues.
   - **Reply-guard steps before every spawn and resume (b.av2 SR-9.4, SR-6.2).** Immediately before each actual `client.spawn` or `client.resume` the ladder makes — the fresh spawn, a resume, a dead-session or `ErrSpawnNotFound` recovery, the `cwd` and `config_dir` replacement spawns, the tmux-collision retry and the restart relaunch through the same ladder — it calls the installed pre-launch reply guard (`preLaunchReplyGuard`, `src/stop-hook-bootstrap.ts`). In order it (1) writes the launching persona's effective `stop_hook_bootstrap` to its record `reply-guard/<key>` (see Configuration), (2) sets the persona's launched-with dir to its effective `claude_config_dir` (or forgets it, with the no-configured-dir log line, when none is set), and (3) runs the launch pass of the step 4a-ter install rule over the new effective dir and the previous launched-with dir only. It writes only the launching persona's record, so a neighbour's launch in the same directory never rewrites another persona's record. The launch pass does no `jq` probe and never writes `startup-errors.log`: its failures, the `~/.claude` refusal included, are server-log lines (`[slack] stop-hook-bootstrap: pre-launch pass [<class>] …`). A failed record write removes any stale record (the guard then fails open to no reminder), logs one line naming the persona and the record path, and the launch proceeds; nothing in these steps blocks a launch. A path that only reconnects to a live instance or does nothing (`waiting`/`working` reconnect, the `pending`/`check_permission`/`ask_user` no-op) runs no reply-guard step. The optimistic first spawn runs the steps before it and, on `ErrInstanceIdCollision`, undoes them, so a later spawn or resume in that ladder runs them afresh. The undo restores the record (the previous text, or deleted when there was none) only if the step wrote it successfully, changed it, and the record still holds what the step wrote; it restores the launched-with dir only if the entry is still the one the step set; then it re-reads the applied persona set and re-runs the launch pass over the new and previous dirs. A teardown or another launch in between is never overwritten. Side effect: when the collision leads only to a reconnect and the running persona's record disagrees with its current config, the step may remove or add the managed hook, and the undo re-runs the install rule with the restored record: a hook the step removed comes back, and one it added stays when the config now enables it (correct under the install rule). When the step removed the hook and the undo re-adds it, that directory's `settings.json` is written twice and the managed group moves to the end of the Stop hooks. `server.ts` installs the step once a persona config is loaded; with none it does nothing (no record, no patch). It passes a getter, not a snapshot, so the step and its undo each read the live applied persona set. The effect: a running instance keeps the value it launched with, and a changed value takes effect at the persona's next launch; a confirmed change reaches that launch through step 1's swap of the applied set, with no call at apply. Dry run returns before the ladder, so it writes no record.
   - Attempt `client.spawn(SR-1.1 params)`, built from `persona-identity.ts` (b.av2 SR-2.2):
     - `template: 'slack-channel-bot'`, `relay_mode: 'on'`, `cwd` = the persona's working directory.
     - `claude_instance_id: cscb_<key>`, `tmux_session_name: slack_bot_<key>`.
     - `label: ['service=cscb', 'persona=<key>', 'config_dir=<12 hex>']`, and no other label. The `config_dir` value hashes the real path of the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (no configured dir means `~/.claude`), taken from the launch's pre-launch check (`checkLaunchConfigDir`, `resolveRealPathStrict`). There is no lexical fallback (bug b.g57). A directory not created yet under a resolvable parent gets the label of the real path it will have: its nearest existing ancestor's real path plus the rest, which is the lexical path when no symlink is involved. Claude Code creates it at first launch. A directory that can't be resolved (a symlink on its path that points to nothing, `ENOTDIR`, or any error other than `ENOENT`) gets no label and is not launched (see the Config-dir-unresolvable retry above). Known limit: a dropped mount that leaves an empty mount point looks like a directory not created yet. The start sweep (step 4) and the ladder read the `persona`, `config_dir` and `cwd` fields; the permission poller reads the `persona` label. The CLI's teardown reads no label: it addresses `cscb_<key>` directly.
     - `extra_env`: `CSCB_PERSONA=<key>`, `CLAUDE_MANAGED_CHANNEL=<key>`, `CSCB_CRONTABLE_PATH` on every spawn (the resolved tilde-expanded `cron_table_path`: the default `<config dir>/crontab` or the configured override), `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION=false` on every spawn (see Environment Variables), and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` only when a `claude_config_dir` is configured (passed as configured, not real-pathed). Every spawn in the ladder sends these params (the fresh spawn, the replacement and amnesia spawns, the b.vub self-heal respawn); a resume passes only the instance ID, and agent-director restores the env it stored with the row at spawn.
   - **Never two live instances (b.av2 SR-6.3).** The instance ID is fixed at `cscb_<key>`, so agent-director refuses a second spawn for a persona that already has a row (`ErrInstanceIdCollision`), and the ladder below decides what happens to that row. Within the process, `spawnForPersona` allows one in-flight launch per persona key: a second call for a key whose launch is still running — from the start pass, a restart or a human-triggered restart — logs `[slack] spawnForPersona: launch already in flight for "<name>" (key=<key>) — joining it` and returns that launch's result instead of starting a second ladder. The entry is cleared when the launch settles, whatever its outcome; different keys never wait on each other. Dry run returns before the check.
   - On `ErrInstanceIdCollision`, call `client.get({claude_instance_id})` and keep the whole row. **`cwd` guard (b.av2 SR-6.2)** first, before any state branch and so before any `findMissing` sweep or reconnect: a row whose `cwd` differs from the persona's working directory by real path (`compareRowToPersona`; a symlink to the directory matches, a row with no `cwd` never does) is never resumed, reconnected or waited on, whatever its state and `resume_enabled`. It logs `[slack] spawnForPersona: "<name>" (key=<key>) row cwd=<row cwd> differs from working_directory=<dir> (state=<state>) — replacing the row: kill+delete+fresh`, then `replaceWithFreshSpawn` kills it (best effort), deletes it and spawns fresh (outcome `spawned`). A row that also fails the `config_dir` check below is handled here. At start the sweep (step 4) has already removed rows in another directory, so this guard mostly catches runtime relaunches. **Unresolvable working directory (b.av2 SR-6.4):** when the row's `cwd` does not match, the persona's working directory has no real path at that moment, and the row's `cwd` has no real path either (`cwdCheckDeferred`), the row cannot be shown to be elsewhere, so it is kept: no kill, no delete. The ladder logs `[slack] spawnForPersona: "<name>" (key=<key>) working_directory=<dir> cannot be resolved to a real path — keeping its row (cwd=<row cwd>, state=<state>); the launch fails and is retried by the restart path`, raises `cwd-unreachable` for the persona and returns `failed`. A row whose `cwd` equals the missing path lexically matches and goes on to the state branches. A row whose `cwd` resolves to an existing directory elsewhere isn't deferred: it is replaced (kill, delete, fresh spawn) like any other `cwd` mismatch. The deferral covers only this `cwd` guard; the `resume_enabled=false`, `config_dir`, `ErrNoSessionId` and `ErrJsonl*` paths below replace the row whatever the directory's state. Otherwise branch on state per the [SR-11 substitution table](#sr-11-substitution-table):
     - **`config_dir` guard before any resume (b.av2 SR-6.2).** `resumeOrFreshSpawn` compares the row's `config_dir` label (from the collision `get` above) with `personaConfigDirLabelValue` of the persona's current effective `claude_config_dir`, through `compareRowToPersona`. It runs after the `resume_enabled=false` branch and after the `reconcileMissingFirst` sweep (b.4dk), immediately before `client.resume(...)`, so it covers both the `ended`/`missing` resume and the dead-session resume. When the directory stopped resolving since the pre-launch check (`configDirResolved: false`), there is no verdict: the row is kept, nothing is launched and the persona is held as above (`deferred`). A missing or different label means no resume: agent-director's resume carries only the instance ID and would bring the session back under its old `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. The row is deleted (killed first on the dead-session paths, whose row may still be live) and spawned fresh through `replaceWithFreshSpawn`, outcome `spawned`. The old history stays in the old config dir; no transcript was lost, so neither `diagnoseJsonlMissing` nor an amnesia action runs. Log: `[slack] spawnForPersona: "<name>" (key=<key>) config_dir label missing (label absent, now=<hex> for claude_config_dir=<dir>) — not resuming; spawning fresh`, or `config_dir label changed (was=<old hex>, now=<new hex> for claude_config_dir=<dir>)` with the same tail; `<dir>` is `<default>` when none is configured. The fresh spawn then logs `[slack] spawnForPersona: fresh-spawned (after delete) for "<name>" (key=<key>)` (`after kill+delete` on the dead-session paths and for the `cwd` guard).
     - `ended` / `missing` + `resume_enabled` → `config_dir` guard above, then `client.resume(...)`; `ErrNoSessionId` / `ErrJsonlMissing` / `ErrJsonlNeverWritten` → `client.delete(...)` + fresh `client.spawn(...)`; `ErrSpawnNotFound` (row vanished between the dead-session verdict and resume — operator delete, expire, race) → fresh `client.spawn(...)` directly with no delete (the row is already gone), action `spawned`. On `ErrJsonlMissing` specifically, `diagnoseJsonlMissing` runs **before** the delete (see the b.wrb / b.fwu note below) — the delete+fresh policy itself is unchanged — and the resulting fresh spawn returns action `fresh-after-amnesia` (definite lost/never-created verdict) or `fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia` (b.fwu — diagnosis inconclusive) rather than `spawned`, so the startup summary never counts a memory-loss recovery as an ordinary clean spawn. `ErrNoSessionId` and `ErrJsonlNeverWritten` take the same delete+fresh recovery but skip the diagnosis entirely and return plain `spawned` (b.jgf — see the note below).
     - `ended` / `missing` + `resume_enabled=false` → `client.kill(...)` + `client.delete(...)` + fresh `client.spawn(...)` through `replaceWithFreshSpawn` (never reaches the `config_dir` guard); an `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` on that spawn takes the b.vub self-heal like the other fresh-spawn paths.
     - `waiting` → `reconnectMcp(key)` sends `/mcp reconnect slack-channel-router` via `client.sendKeys(...)`. If that reconnect finds the session dead (`'dead-session'` verdict), fall through to `resumeOrFreshSpawn(..., { reconcileMissingFirst: true })` — see the note below. It finds it dead when the tmux session is gone (`ErrTmuxSendKeys`, again after its one retry) or, b.dup, when agent-director refuses the keystrokes with `ErrSpawnNotInteractive`: the row was ended or marked `missing` after the `get` read it `waiting`, e.g. by the findMissing sweep another persona's launch wait starts with, so its claude process is gone. Either way it raises no spawn-failure notice; any other send-keys error is `'failed'`, with one.
     - `working` → `waitForWaitingAndReconnect(...)` runs an up-front `findMissing({})` sweep via the shared `reconcileMissingSweep` helper (b.m4r — see note below), then polls `client.status(...)` until `waiting`, then sendKeys. While the row reads `working`, the wait also reads the persona's pane and, when it sits idle, the session's transcript, and a row that evidence shows stale is reconnected the same way (b.f2b, see note below). A `'dead-session'` verdict likewise falls through to `resumeOrFreshSpawn(..., { reconcileMissingFirst: true })`. A `'not-reconnected'` verdict (the wait ended with the session alive and nothing typed) returns the action `not-reconnected`; `reconnected` means `/mcp reconnect` was typed. Right before the wait the ladder calls `hooks.onWorkingRowWait` (`LaunchHooks`, given only to the ladder a `spawnForPersona` call starts, never to a call that joins one), where the start pass parks the launch (b.f2b, see the parked-launch note below).
     - **b.4dk dead-session recovery — `reconcileMissingFirst`.** After a reboot/pod-resume a `waiting`/`working` row is frozen live-state but its tmux session is gone. AD's `resume` verb only accepts a terminal (`ended`/`missing`) row, so on such a row it throws `ErrSpawnNotResumable` and the defensive branch (line above) kill+delete+fresh-spawns — losing all history. When `reconcileMissingFirst` is set (only the two dead-session callers set it), `resumeOrFreshSpawn` runs a `findMissing({})` sweep via the shared `reconcileMissingSweep` helper before `resume`. AD's per-row, evidence-based sweep (requires agent-director ≥ 0.8.0, plan b.93m t1.93m.hp: degraded-mode guard removed) transitions the dead row to `missing`, so the subsequent `resume` succeeds and restores the pre-reboot transcript (provided the row passes the `config_dir` guard, which runs after this sweep). Skipped when `resume_enabled=false`. A `findMissing` error → fall through to `resume` anyway (which then kill+delete+fresh-spawns, today's behavior). The `ended`/`missing` collision branch above does NOT set the flag (row already terminal).
     - **b.m4r `working`-row stall — up-front `findMissing` sweep.** A bot killed mid-turn (e.g. `tmux kill-session` on its session) leaves its AD row at `working`. Its `SessionEnd` does fire, but agent-director only soft-refreshes the row (`working` → `working`): it marks a row `ended` only for the `SessionEnd` reasons `logout`, `prompt_input_exit` and `exit`. The row stays `working` until a `findMissing` sweep reconciles it. Without a reconcile, `waitForWaitingAndReconnect`'s poll loop spins on `status` for the full `WAIT_FOR_WAITING_TIMEOUT_MS` window (10 min) before the timeout verdict finally returns `'dead-session'` — the channel stays down that whole time. `waitForWaitingAndReconnect` therefore runs AD's per-row, evidence-based `findMissing({})` sweep before entering the poll loop (requires agent-director ≥ 0.8.0, same b.93m t1.93m.hp capability as the `reconcileMissingFirst` path). A genuinely-dead row reconciles to `missing`, so the **first** status poll hits the ended/missing branch and returns `'dead-session'` in seconds; a genuinely-alive long-turn row is untouched by the evidence-based sweep and keeps the existing polling behavior (long-turn guard preserved). On any `findMissing` error, the sweep is logged and skipped and the poll loop proceeds unchanged (today's behavior). This prefers AD's `findMissing` verb over a CSCB-side tmux probe, mirroring `resumeOrFreshSpawn`'s `reconcileMissingFirst` branch (docs/engineering-guide.md, "Avoiding Duplicated Effort"). The sweep leaves a live session's row alone, so a `working` row that is stale while its session runs idle is handled by the b.f2b positive-idle rule below, not here.
     - **b.f2b stale `working` row — the positive-idle rule.** agent-director can leave a row `working` after the turn ended, with the session idle at its prompt, and nothing moves it back. The pane alone can't prove the session idle: Claude Code 2.1.280 can draw a live turn with no spinner (an API retry or stall shows static rows with no glyph and no timer). So while the row reads `working`, the wait reads its evidence every `WORKING_ROW_READ_INTERVAL_MS` (5 s; the first status poll reads at once, later polls in between read nothing, because every agent-director call goes through one queue shared by every persona) and folds it into the wait's run (`staleWorkingRowIsIdle`, `WorkingPaneWatch`):
       - **The pane.** `readPane` (last 40 lines, through `withOutageDetection`). `classifyWorkingPane` reads it as `prompt` when its bottom 12 lines show a dialog, anchored to Claude Code's layout (`paneShowsDialog`): a numbered option list (an option `1.` and, below it, an option `2.`) with the `❯` cursor on one of its options, plus a question line ending in `?` above it, a dialog option or footer (`DIALOG_NEEDLES`: a permission dialog's deny option, the select-menu footers, the pre-session dialogs' options) on or below it, or its options inside the dialog's box (`│`). Otherwise it is `busy` for Claude Code's spinner line anywhere (`BUSY_SPINNER_LINE_RE`, in the spinner's own shape: at column 0 a spinner glyph, a space, a short message with no ellipsis, which can be several words (a custom `spinnerVerbs` verb or a task's `activeForm`), then an ellipsis ending the line or followed by ` (` and the turn's status; a line that starts with `●`, which Linux also draws before every reply and tool call, counts only as a single word and an ellipsis ending the line, or with a status that starts with an elapsed time, a token arrow or `thinking`), for `to interrupt` or `esc to cancel` in the bottom lines (`BUSY_PANE_NEEDLES`), or for an API retry row there (`BUSY_API_RETRY_ROWS`: `No response from the API after … · retrying` with its `A proxy or gateway that buffers…` line, `Rate limit reached · Retrying in`, `Waiting for API response · will retry in`, `· attempt n/m`); `blank` with no text; else `idle`. A prompt outranks a busy sign. The patterns are verified against Claude Code 2.1.280.
       - **The transcript**, read only when the pane is `idle` (`readPersonaTranscript`): the row is fetched (`get`, through `withOutageDetection`), its transcript located with the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (`locateTranscript`: the row's `jsonl_path` when it names the row's `claude_session_id`, else `<claude_config_dir>/projects/<cwd slug>/<claude_session_id>.jsonl`), and its last 256 KiB read (`readTranscriptTurnState`, `src/session-transcript.ts`). It is `ended` when its last conversation entry is a completed turn (Claude's final reply, or the user's interrupt marker), else `open` (a Stop hook's context or blocking error after the reply keeps the turn open); a failed `get`, a row that names no transcript or a file that can't be read is `unreadable`.
       - **The run** (`foldWorkingPaneRun`, the positive-idle rule). An `idle` pane read with an `ended` transcript continues an idle run only while the screen and the transcript's snapshot (path, device, inode, size, mtime) are both unchanged, and otherwise starts one. A `prompt` pane continues or starts a prompt run. A `busy` or `blank` pane, a failed pane read, or an idle pane whose transcript is `open` or `unreadable` ends the run: no evidence.
       - An idle run spanning `STALE_WORKING_WINDOW_MS` (60 s) means the row is stale: `reconnectMcp`, as the `waiting` branch does, whatever `session_restart_delay` is (the reconnect is part of the launch, not an auto-restart). A live turn, an API retry included, never ends its transcript with a completed turn, so it is never taken for idle and never typed into (the b.rmy invariant).
       - A prompt run spanning the window is logged and reported through the `blocked-on-prompt` not-connected notice (saying whether auto-restart is disabled), once per wait; nothing is typed and the wait goes on.
       - A failed pane read is no evidence: the wait's first failure is logged and the wait goes on. An idle pane whose transcript gives no evidence is logged, again only when the reason changes, and the wait goes on.
       - Each poll that reads the row `working` without reconnecting it is one more deferral in the persona's run on the row (`noteWorkingRowDeferral`, the run kept per persona in `workingRowDeferredSince` and shared with the restart path); any other state ends the run. Once the run has lasted `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS` (10 min) from its first deferral, the `unproven-idle` not-connected notice is raised (*Not connected*: its session reads working but CSCB can't prove it's idle, so it won't type into it), whatever `session_restart_delay` is, once per episode; the wait goes on.
     - **b.f2b a cancelled wait.** The persona's teardown cancels the wait (`cancelWorkingRowWait`, see [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65)). Each wait registers itself per persona (`workingRowWaits`); a cancel sets its flag and wakes it from its poll sleep (`sleepUnlessCancelled`). The wait checks the flag after each agent-director call, types nothing once it is set, logs that it was cancelled and returns `'cancelled'`, which the ladder reports as `not-reconnected`. A cancel that finds a launch in flight that hasn't started its wait yet records the key (`cancelledLaunchWaits`, forgotten when that launch settles), so a wait it starts is cancelled from its first check.
     - **b.f2b a wait that ends without a reconnect.** Every path that ends the wait with the session alive and nothing typed returns `'not-reconnected'` (`'ok'` always means `/mcp reconnect` was typed): a transition to `pending`, `ask_user` or `check_permission`; `ErrSpawnNotFound` with a live tmux session; and the timeout, when the row is live but not `waiting` or the tmux fallback reads alive. A row that reads `waiting` at the timeout is reconnected. Each of these give-up lines ends with what actually happens next for the restart delay in effect (`reportWaitEndedDisconnected`): with auto-restart on, the health check's recovery; with `session_restart_delay` 0, that nothing will reconnect the persona, and the not-connected notice is raised (once per episode; see Health-Check Poller): `blocked-on-prompt` when the row ended at `ask_user` or `check_permission`, `unproven-idle` when the timeout finds it `working`, otherwise `auto-restart-disabled` with a fixed, token-free cause (`waitEndedNotice`). At any delay the timeout on a `working` row is one more deferral, so a run that has lasted `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS` raises the `unproven-idle` notice there too. The lines are listed in docs/engineering-guide.md, Log Messages.
     - **b.ecw terminal verdicts key on the claude PROCESS, not the raw tmux session.** Once b.93m Part E landed (AD ≥ 0.8.0: degraded-mode guard removed, `findMissing` verdicts per-row and evidence-based), the two dead-session branches in `waitForWaitingAndReconnect` decide off AD's process-level verdict rather than a raw `tmux has-session` probe. AD probes the *claude process*; CSCB's `_hasTmuxSession` probes the *tmux session* — a lingering tmux shell with a dead claude process would flip the two, so each branch documents which object it keys on:
       - **ended/missing branch (in-loop):** keys on the process. `ended` means the process exited with a `SessionEnd` reason agent-director marks ended (see the b.m4r note above); `missing`, after the up-front sweep, is an evidence-based verdict that the process is provably gone. Returns `'dead-session'` directly — **no tmux probe**. A dead process in a live tmux shell is still a dead bot; routing it to `resumeOrFreshSpawn` lets b.vub's `selfHealTmuxCollisionAndRespawn` reap the orphan tmux shell on `ErrTmuxSessionCreate`. (The former tmux-alive → `'ok'` behavior deferred a dead channel to the health-check for minutes.)
       - **timeout branch:** keys on the process via a **fresh** `reconcileMissingSweep` (the 10s memo has long expired at the 10-minute deadline) + one `status` call. `ended`/`missing` → `'dead-session'`; `waiting` → reconnect (b.f2b: the turn ended at the deadline); any other live state (working/ask_user/check_permission/pending) → `'not-reconnected'` (b.f2b), the process left alive (a process mid-long-turn is not an error — the b.rmy/b.3ce long-turn guard, now keyed on the process). The raw tmux probe survives here **only as a fallback**: on `ErrSpawnNotFound` (AD row gone, nothing to consult) or any other `status` error, fall back to `_hasTmuxSession` (alive → `'not-reconnected'`, gone → `'dead-session'`) so an AD outage can't manufacture a false `'dead-session'` — the b.rmy invariant.
       - **ErrSpawnNotFound branch (in-loop):** keys on the tmux session by design (b.c3o) — no AD row exists, so there is nothing to reconcile or consult; only a direct probe of the deterministic session name is available. Alive → `'not-reconnected'` (b.f2b), gone → `'dead-session'`.
     - **b.m4r shared memoized sweep — `reconcileMissingSweep`.** Three sweep callers go through one shared `reconcileMissingSweep` helper rather than calling `client.findMissing({})` inline: this `working`-row up-front sweep, `resumeOrFreshSpawn`'s `reconcileMissingFirst`, and the tick/restart escalate-dead path (via the exported `sweepDeadTmuxChannel` wrapper, which prepends its unconditional operator log then delegates here — `reconcileMissingSweep` itself stays module-private). b.jdc adds two more, both through `reconcileAndReadRowState` (the sweep, then one `status` read of the row): the ladder's prompt row whose tmux session is gone, and the restart path's deferrals on a prompt row once they have run for 10 min. Because `findMissing({})` is a whole-store, idempotent sweep, a fleet restart — `startupSessionManager` runs once per persona at `concurrency=3`, and every `working`-row collision plus each dead-path `reconcileMissingFirst` would otherwise fire its own whole-store sweep — is collapsed by single-flight (concurrent callers share one in-flight sweep promise) plus a 10s TTL memo (a caller arriving just after the last successful sweep reuses that result). This sheds the redundant N-sweep amplification without changing recovery behavior; failures are never memoized, so the next caller after an error re-sweeps. The post-reboot fleet-wide case (b.nk5 — `/tmp` wiped, every channel dead-tmux at once) is served correctly: one in-flight-shared sweep reconciles the whole store for every simultaneously-escalating channel. The 10s memo TTL is harmless against the ~120s health-check tick cadence: a memoized-stale answer on the escalate-dead path leaves the restart's same-run re-probe (b.d61, Auto-Restart step 3) reading the row alive, which costs at most **one** extra tick, because the following tick's escalate-dead sweeps again well past the TTL. (The `reconcileMissingFirst`/`working`-row callers are startup-clustered within one boot rather than tick-spaced, so they benefit from the memo directly.)
     - `check_permission` / `ask_user` → never typed into (b.rmy). **b.jdc:** a session that dies under a prompt keeps its row in that state (agent-director only refreshes a row at SessionEnd and leaves reaping to its findMissing sweep), so the ladder first probes the persona's own tmux session (`hasPersonaTmuxSession`, exact target `=slack_bot_<key>`; `launchOnPromptRow`). Alive, or a probe that fails → no-op, as before (the poller relays the prompt; if the persona stays disconnected, the restart path reports it). Gone → the memoized `reconcileMissingSweep`, then the row is read again: `ended` or `missing` is a dead session, recovered through `resumeOrFreshSpawn`; any other state, or a failed read, is a no-op left to the restart path (which escalates a gone tmux session itself).
     - `pending` → no-op.
     - **b.wrb `ErrJsonlMissing` diagnostic — legible amnesia, unchanged policy.** When resume throws `ErrJsonlMissing` the delete+fresh recovery is unchanged, but `diagnoseJsonlMissing` runs first (before `tryDelete`, while the doomed row's `jsonl_path` / `claude_session_id` / `cwd` / `started_at` are still readable) to make the loss legible. It is a sibling of the b.zak safeguard (step 4a-quater), which runs *before* the resume path; this one runs *inside* it, on the row resume actually failed to open. Never throws. Two things happen:
       - **Path + provenance logging.** AD's `ErrJsonlMissing` description enumerates every transcript candidate it stat'd as `<source> <path> (<stat error>)`, joined by `"; "` — a capability AD's bug `b.1ba` delivered, introduced in agent-director v0.10.0. When the description carries that enumeration, `parseJsonlMissingCandidates` parses it and logs the candidates verbatim, labelled *paths+sources reported by agent-director*. The parsed source tokens are `persisted` (AD's recorded `jsonl_path`), `fallback` (its `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`-aware recomputed path), and `history` (an archived `session_history` entry). When the description does **not** match that shape — a pre-0.10.0 AD, or any AD whose message wording differs — the parse yields nothing and CSCB reconstructs the candidates locally instead: the persisted `jsonl_path` column plus a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`-aware fallback via `resolveJsonlPath` and the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (its own, else the top-level one), labelled explicitly *locally computed* so a reader never mistakes a CSCB-side reconstruction for what AD actually stat'd. The parse keys off those literal source tokens, not any version string, so it degrades cleanly in both directions.
       - **lost / never-created / inconclusive classification (b.fwu).** Using b.zak's read-only `makeDefaultArchiveCount` / `rfc3339ToEpochSeconds` helpers over `message_archive_db`, bounded by `started_at`, and the b.av2 SR-7.4 scope from `personaArchiveEvidenceScope` (the same helper step 4a-quater's Layer 2 uses), `diagnoseJsonlMissing` counts archived messages only in the persona's `delivery: all` channels since `started_at` and returns one of `'lost' | 'never-created' | 'inconclusive'`:
         - **lost** — archive shows ≥1 message since spawn in the persona's `delivery: all` channels, real conversation history destroyed. Made operator-visible via `recordStartupError('jsonl-transcript-lost-on-resume', …)` (startup only) **and** a notice through the notifier to the persona's destination ("my conversation memory has been lost and I was started fresh").
         - **never-created** — strictly evidence-based: the archive was actually consulted and returned 0 messages since spawn, and that zero is attributable — the persona has no `delivery: mentions` channel and DMs off, so its `delivery: all` channels carry all of its traffic. The transcript was never written (Claude creates the `.jsonl` lazily on first message; expected and lossless). One quiet log line only.
         - **inconclusive (b.fwu)** — "no evidence either way": the AD row fetch failed, `started_at` was absent or unparseable, the count was 0 but the persona has a `delivery: mentions` channel or DMs on (b.av2 SR-7.4: the archive cannot attribute messages from `mentions` channels or DMs to a persona, so the zero proves nothing — `UNATTRIBUTABLE_ZERO_REASON`; a persona with no `delivery: all` channel counts 0 without the archive being consulted, so once `started_at` parses it always gets this reason, even when `message_archive_db` is not configured), `message_archive_db` is not configured, or the archive read failed. Previously these fell through to a reassuring `never-created`/console-only `unknown`, silently downgrading a possible real loss. Now each emits `recordStartupError('jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive', …)` carrying the reason (row-fetch failed / started_at absent or unparseable / zero count not attributable / archive unconfigured / archive read failed) **and** a notice through the notifier to the persona's destination, worded as *uncertainty* ("on restart I was started fresh; I could not determine whether my prior conversation history was preserved") — deliberately distinct from the `lost` wording, since a false "your history was destroyed" is its own harm. When `message_archive_db` is unconfigured the operator message states diagnosis is impossible because no message archive is configured, with a hint to enable it.

         All three outcomes still take the same delete+fresh recovery (policy unchanged). `lost` and `never-created` surface as the `fresh-after-amnesia` action; **inconclusive** surfaces as the new `'fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia'` action (see the honest-summary note below). This is distinct from b.zak's `jsonl-transcript-lost` class (which fires *pre-resume* to warn of an impending wipe); `jsonl-transcript-lost-on-resume` records a wipe that just happened on the row resume tried and failed.
     - **b.jgf `ErrJsonlNeverWritten` — the ceremony-free branch.** agent-director 0.10.0 split the old "resume cannot open a transcript" condition into two typed errors, and CSCB branches on both. `ErrJsonlMissing` means a transcript path was recorded but is not there now: history *may* have existed and been destroyed, and that ambiguity is exactly what the b.wrb / b.fwu diagnosis above exists to resolve. `ErrJsonlNeverWritten` asserts the opposite — the session produced no transcript at all — which proves there is nothing to lose, so the fresh spawn is lossless by construction. It therefore shares the delete+fresh recovery but none of the ceremony: `diagnoseJsonlMissing` and the `fresh-after-*` actions are gated on `instanceof ErrJsonlMissing`, so this branch emits one informational log line, raises **no** persona notice, records no startup-error class, and returns plain `spawned` — the same shape as the `ErrSpawnNotFound` branch. Returning a successful action is the load-bearing part: `restart.ts` treats `failed` as retryable, so before b.jgf this error fell to the generic failure tail and the channel retried the one operation that could never succeed, on a doubling backoff, posting a spawn-failure message each pass. This branch is also the resume-path counterpart of the b.zak Layer 2 policy in step 4a-quater: a channel that has been idle since spawn has no transcript yet because Claude creates the `.jsonl` lazily on first message — normal, not an error, and it must not alarm.
   - `ErrSpawnNotFound` race after collision → single retry-spawn.
   - `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` from the first spawn, a resume or a replacement spawn (`replaceWithFreshSpawn`) → the b.vub self-heal, `selfHealTmuxCollisionAndRespawn`: it kills the persona's own tmux session by its exact name (`tmux kill-session -t =slack_bot_<key>`), then retries `client.spawn` once. The kill is exact but not ownership-checked: a session made by hand with that exact name is killed too. The b.fmk version removes it.
   - **Raw tmux calls address one session exactly (b.1ix).** Four paths still call tmux directly: the self-heal kill (`kill-session`), the approver's pane read and Enter for an `ended`/`missing` row (`capture-pane`, `send-keys`), the liveness probe (`has-session`, `hasPersonaTmuxSession`) and the reconnect's `start-server`. Each goes through one runner in `src/session-manager.ts` (`_runTmux`, the only place `src/` starts tmux; the `_setTmuxCommandRunner` seam lets unit tests see every argv), and each target is exact. tmux resolves a bare `-t <name>` to the session of that exact name when there is one and otherwise to the one session whose name starts with it; persona keys can prefix one another (`dev`, `dev_2`), so a bare `slack_bot_dev` reaches `slack_bot_dev_2` once `slack_bot_dev` is gone. A session target is `=<name>`, a pane target `=<name>:` (the session's active pane): tmux 3.2a, the version in the `/ci` image, refuses `=<name>` as a pane target (`can't find pane`) even when the session exists. The `/ci` scenario `tests/integration/test-11-exact-tmux-targets.sh` runs each path against real tmux with `slack_bot_dev` and `slack_bot_dev_2`.
   - Other errors → `notifySpawnFailure(key, error)` raises a spawn-failure notice through the per-persona notifier to the persona's destination. A notice raised while the persona has no serving client is held in that persona's queue (step 6); in dry run it is only logged. A failed post of a startup spawn-failure notice records the `spawn-failure-post` startup error (outside startup, a `[slack] spawn-failure-post:` line) at the notice's first failed attempt only; after a destination failure the hold keeps the notice, so the record can be followed by a `cleared:` line and delivery.
   - **Scope of this ladder.** It classifies only errors raised by the spawn/resume verbs (`spawn`, `get`, `resume`, `delete`, `kill`, `sendKeys`, `status`) — it is not a catalog of every AD error class CSCB handles. Decide-path errors are classified at their own call site: the only CSCB caller of AD's `decide` verb is `handlePermissionClick`, so `ErrAlreadyDecided`, `ErrInvalidFlags`, `ErrAmbiguousRequest`, and b.qi1's `ErrRelayFallenBack` are branched on there (see [Click handler (SR-4)](#click-handler-sr-4)) and never reach this ladder or its generic tail.
   - **Pre-session dialog approval (b.uhv / b.k54 / b.vub — defense-in-depth).** `approvePreSessionDialogs(key, …)` runs after every fresh spawn (9 sites across `runPersonaLadder`, `resumeOrFreshSpawn` and `replaceWithFreshSpawn`, including the `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` self-heal respawns) and after every successful `resume`, because a resumed bot faces the same dev-channels dialog. One loop handles both known dialogs; `PRE_SESSION_DIALOG_NEEDLES` holds their option labels, `TRUST_DIALOG_NEEDLE` (`'Yes, I trust this folder'`) and `DEV_CHANNELS_DIALOG_NEEDLE` (`'I am using this for local development'`), both verified against Claude Code 2.1.120. Each dialog pre-selects its accept option, so a single Enter accepts it.
     - Every `DIALOG_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` (500 ms) it asks `client.status` for the `cscb_<key>` row and returns as soon as the row reaches a live state (`waiting`, `working`, `ask_user`, `check_permission`). `ErrSpawnNotFound` also returns.
     - A `pending` row is read through `client.readPane({ n_lines: 40, allow_pending: true })`. When any needle is on screen it sends Enter through `client.sendKeys({ text: '', allow_pending: true })`. Enter is never sent without a visible needle, so no stray Enter reaches a live prompt.
     - A resumed row stays `ended`/`missing` while the dialog blocks `SessionStart`, and AD refuses to read or type into such a row. For those states the loop reads the pane with raw `tmux capture-pane -p -t =slack_bot_<key>:` and sends Enter with raw `tmux send-keys -t =slack_bot_<key>: Enter`: the persona's own session, addressed exactly (see Raw tmux calls below). A session that isn't there reads as an empty pane, so no Enter is sent.
     - An `ended`/`missing` row with no needle for `DIALOG_DEAD_GRACE_POLLS` (20) polls in a row counts as a dead spawn: `recordStartupError('dev-channels-approve-spawn-died', …)` at startup, then return. A needle resets the count.
     - At the `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS` cap (5 min) it records `dev-channels-approve-not-ready` at startup and raises a spawn-failure notice for the persona.
     - A fresh spawn that shows no dialog reaches a live state on its own and the approver returns quietly. The trust patch (step 4a-bis, repeated before every launch) is the primary fix for the trust dialog, so the expected path is that it ran for this launch and no trust dialog appears; this loop covers unprovisioned cwds, clobbered trust state and dialog changes in later Claude Code releases.
   - **b.f2b parked start launches.** One persona's `working` row must not hold up the others. `startupSessionManager` runs each launch through `launchOrPark`: when the ladder calls `onWorkingRowWait` (right before `waitForWaitingAndReconnect`, which can take up to `WAIT_FOR_WAITING_TIMEOUT_MS`), the pass stops waiting for that launch, frees its pool slot and records the persona as `waiting-in-background`. `followParkedLaunch` logs that the pass goes on without it, then, when the launch settles, `background launch for <ref> settled: <action>`; a rejection is logged and recorded as `spawn-failed`, as the pass records one, and never rethrown. The launch stays in `inFlightLaunches` until it settles, so the health check skips the persona (Health-Check Poller) and a restart joins it, as for any launch in flight; a teardown cancels its wait and then waits for it to settle (`whenLaunchSettled`). So neither the pass nor the health check and detection tick that start after it (steps 8 and 9) wait out one persona's wait. Only the start pass parks: an apply bring-up, a launch after a retry and a restart relaunch pass no hooks and wait for the launch to settle.
   - **b.wrb / b.fwu honest startup summary.** `startupSessionManager` tallies each persona's `SpawnPersonaResult['action']` into a per-outcome breakdown and prints `startupSessionManager: complete — N persona(s): N resumed, N fresh-spawned, N fresh-after-amnesia, N fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia, N reconnected, N no-op, N failed, N not brought up, N not reconnected`. The last bucket (b.f2b) counts `not-reconnected` launches (the session runs but the `working`-row wait typed nothing, or its teardown cancelled the wait); they count in `succeeded` too. Since the pass parks every launch it starts once it waits on a `working` row, the bucket counts only such a launch the pass joined rather than started. Parked launches are counted in no bucket: when there are any, the summary is followed by `startupSessionManager: <n> persona(s) still waiting in the background for a working row to settle — not counted above; each logs its outcome when it settles (b.f2b)`. `fresh-after-amnesia` (a fresh spawn replacing a resume where the archive gave a definite lost/never-created verdict) and `fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia` (b.fwu — the same recovery but the diagnosis was inconclusive, so it is *unknown* whether history was preserved) are counted and reported separately; each has its own loud, grep-friendly follow-up line when its count is `> 0`, pointing at the per-persona `ErrJsonlMissing diagnostic` lines. The returned `StartupSessionManagerResult` carries the same breakdown (`resumed` / `freshSpawned` / `freshAfterAmnesia` / `freshAfterInconclusiveAmnesia` / `reconnected` / `notReconnected` / `noop`) alongside the retained `succeeded` (any non-failed action) and `failed` totals, `notBroughtUp` (personas whose outcome was `broken` or `retrying` when the pass reached them, including one launched later by its retry), `waitingInBackground` (b.f2b: launches parked when the pass returned), plus `perPersona` (each persona's key and action, `not-brought-up` with its `outcome` and failures, or `waiting-in-background`). A b.jgf `ErrJsonlNeverWritten` recovery counts as an ordinary `fresh-spawned`, not as either amnesia bucket: no history existed, so counting it as memory loss would overstate the damage. The fresh spawns of the b.av2 SR-6.2 `cwd` and `config_dir` guards also count as `fresh-spawned` (action `spawned`); they add no action or summary line of their own, and their per-persona log lines above say why the row was replaced. A launch that joined an in-flight one returns that launch's action.
6. **Held notices flush per persona (b.av2 SR-7.2)** — the connection manager's status listener (`createPersonaUpFlushListener`) calls the notifier's `flush(key)` each time a persona reports `up`: after its bring-up in step 5 and after every reopen. Only that persona's queue is posted, in raised order, through its own client; other personas' queues are untouched. A notice is held while its persona has no client (`clientFor` answers only for a serving persona), so notices raised before a persona comes up, such as start-pass notices from steps 4a-quater and 5, post once it is up. The hold is the same for a channel and a `dm` destination: every persona notice (spawn failure, restart cap, outage onset and all-clear, the history and transcript-loss warnings, non-persistent JSONL storage, lost message) reaches a `dm` destination as a DM to `dm.contact`, and for a held notice the DM is opened only when it is flushed, never before the persona's client is validated. A persona that is never brought up keeps its held notices, at most 20: past that the oldest is dropped with a log line and never posted (see the `persona-notifier.ts` entry).
