No bring-up failure is ever among them (step 5). A flushed notice whose post the destination refuses is then kept by the destination hold and retried with backoff (Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures)). Dry run holds nothing: its notices are logged when raised.
7. **SR-2.1 poller start** — outside dry run, `main()` calls `resetAllToHealthy` with the applied persona keys (a clean outage slate before any persona is brought up) and then `startPermissionPoller({ getClient, clientFor, destinations: personaDestinations, destinationHold: personaDestinationHold, getPersona: getAppliedPersona, isPersonaUp, intervalMs: personaConfig.agent_director_poll_interval_ms })`. This runs right after the connection manager and the bring-up controller are built, before `Bun.serve` and step 4. The poller waits for no persona: it skips the rows of a persona that is not up (`isPersonaUp`, the shared up predicate) and reads them again on the first tick after it is up; `clientFor` and `destinationHold` are the same per-persona lookup and destination hold the notifier uses. Single-threaded interval loop; skipped-tick WARN at 5+ consecutive skips. Dry run logs `[slack] Running in dry-run mode — Slack disabled` instead and starts no poller.
8. **Health-check poller** — `startHealthCheck()` begins backstop liveness checks via `client.status(...)`, over the personas that are up (see Health-Check Poller). It starts once the step 5 pass returns, so it can be running while a retrying persona is still retrying, and while a start launch the pass parked on a `working` row is still waiting (b.f2b). A persona joins the tick only once it is up, which is when its own retry launches it, and the tick skips a persona while a launch for it is in flight, a parked one included. A restart scheduled meanwhile runs after the restart delay, and its kill and launch defer to a launch still in flight (Auto-Restart steps 4 and 5).
9. **Reload detection tick (b.av2 SR-8.2)** — `reload.startDetection()`, the last statement of `main()`, right after step 8. The controller arms the tick only once the step 5 pass has returned (`runStartBringUp` settled), so, like the health check, it never races the pass's bring-ups: every persona's bring-up has read its credentials by then, which is what the tick compares against. A launch the pass parked may still be in flight (b.f2b); that keeps SR-8.2's intent, and a teardown of that persona cancels its wait and then waits for its launch to settle (`whenLaunchSettled`). Its first pass runs 5 s later. See [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83).

CSCB does NOT maintain its own `sessions.json` registry. agent-director owns Claude session-id state internally, surfaced via `client.resume(...)` and the SR-11 state machine.

### Disconnection

1. Transport closes → `onsessionclosed` fires
2. A pending session is simply removed. A registered session is removed with `unregisterByMcpSessionId()`, which returns its persona key; a stale close of a session already replaced by a newer one returns nothing and leaves the newer session registered
3. `restartDisconnectedPersona(key, via)` resolves the key to its applied persona and calls `scheduleRestart(key, persona.working_directory)`; a key that is no applied persona is logged and not restarted. The SSE-abort path of a `GET /mcp` stream does the same

**Session drop when a persona stops being up (b.av2 SR-6.3, SR-6.4).** A session is registered only while its persona is up. When a running persona's outcome changes from `up` to `broken` or `retrying` (a refused reopen, or a Web API call refused for the persona's bot token, bug b.ujn; both log `persona-credentials-refused`), the bring-up controller calls its `onLeftUp` listener once, which the server sets to `createNotUpSessionDropper`. It stops the session's SSE keep-alive and calls `dropPersonaSession(key)`, which removes the persona's MCP session-ID mappings and registry entry before closing the transport, so the close finds no session and steps 2–3 schedule no restart. When a session was registered it logs `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): MCP session dropped — the persona is not up (<outcome>: <cause>); its instance and agent-director row are kept`. Nothing else happens: no restart, kill or delete, no agent-director call, nothing posted to Slack. A lost Slack connection being reopened (`lost`, or `retrying` in the `reopen` phase) keeps the persona `up` and drops nothing. While the persona is down, any new connection from the instance is refused (Connection, step 3). A credentials-broken persona recovers through a confirmed change to its credentials file (apply step 6's recovery bring-up) or at the next start, and the instance registers again through that bring-up's launch, which reaches the kept row through the collision ladder.

**Session drop at a persona teardown (b.av2 SR-6.5).** A persona teardown drops the persona's session through the same `dropPersonaSessionAndKeepAlive` (keep-alive stopped, registry entry removed before the transport closes), before its instance is killed, so neither the close nor the kill schedules a restart. It logs no drop line of its own.

### Auto-Restart

Restart guards, backoff, the cap latch and every restart dependency are keyed by persona key (b.av2 SR-6.3), and restart log lines name it as `persona=<key>`, e.g. `[slack] Scheduling restart for persona=<key> in <n>s (backoff)`.

Auto-restart only acts on a persona that is up. It is separate from bring-up retries (Server-Managed Startup step 5), which have their own per-persona timers on the SR-3.2 backoff with no cap and never touch this module's counter, backoff or cap. In particular, a directory that is missing at bring-up is a bring-up retry. A persona that is running and whose directory later disappears stays on this path: the health check raises `cwd-unreachable` for it (b.av2 SR-11), and its relaunches follow the backoff and cap below.

After `scheduleRestart(key, cwd)` is called:

1. **Delay check** — if `session_restart_delay` is 0, restart is skipped immediately. The delay comes only from the loaded persona config (`getRestartDelay`). Nothing then reconnects an alive persona that is not connected, so the health check reports it through the not-connected notice instead (Health-Check Poller step 3, b.f2b). A launch's own reconnect of a `waiting` or stale `working` row still runs (Server-Managed Startup step 5).

   **Shutdown check** — next, during graceful shutdown no timer is armed and the relaunch gate is not asked: `[slack] Skipping restart — server is shutting down (persona=<key>)`. Shutdown cancels every bring-up, so no persona is up, and stopping the HTTP server aborts every MCP stream, which lands here; asking the gate would log healthy personas as not relaunched. The timer callback repeats this check first when it fires.

   **Up check (b.av2 SR-6.4)** — next, `RestartDeps.canRestart(key)` (the relaunch gate, see step 5) is asked. A persona that is not up (its bring-up is broken or retrying, or its Slack connection is not serving) gets no timer: `[slack] Not scheduling restart for persona=<key> — the relaunch gate refused it (the persona is not up, or is no longer in the applied configuration)`. The timer callback asks again right after its shutdown check, before the liveness check, and once more right after the liveness check returns, before any reconnect or kill (the probe is an async agent-director call, and the persona may stop being up while it runs). After an `'escalate-dead'` reconnect it is asked again right after the second liveness probe, before the kill (step 3, b.d61). A persona that stopped being up by any of these checks (for example, Slack refused its token on a reopen) logs `[slack] Skipping restart for persona=<key> — the persona is no longer up; its instance is left as it is`. Nothing further is probed, reconnected, killed or launched, and no success or failure is recorded, so a not-up persona's instance and agent-director row are left alone. `launchSession` asks the gate once more (step 5).
2. **Backoff timer** — a `setTimeout` fires after `nextBackoffDelay(key, session_restart_delay)` seconds: `min(base * 2^preFailureCount, 900)`, so the delay doubles on each consecutive failure from the `session_restart_delay` base up to a 900 s (15 min) ceiling (`src/backoff.ts`, SR-25). `scheduleRestart` accepts `opts.humanTrigger`; when set, the computed delay is clamped **down** to `HUMAN_TRIGGER_DELAY_CEILING = 5` s (never raised) so a message lost to a down persona doesn't leave it down for a 900 s backoff. The clamp only shortens the wait — it does not reset the failure counter and does not bypass the re-entrancy guard, so each attempt still counts toward normal backoff/cap accounting. The `scheduleRestart` path itself does not consult the cap — it is cap-exempt (the cap only gates the health-check tick and the inbound message triggers, which check `isAtCap` themselves before firing, see below). Two triggers reach this path from inbound messages, both in `src/persona-routing.ts` and both keyed by the receiving persona: the b.9cj streamless branch, for a session that is *registered* for the persona yet lacks a `_GET_stream` (it calls `scheduleRestart(key, session.cwd, undefined, { humanTrigger: true })`; it does not call `notification()`, since the SDK's `send()` would evaporate silently with the stream gone), and the b.kvq no-session branch, for a persona with **no** registered, connected session (the "No live session for persona … — dropping message" line; it calls `scheduleRestart(key, persona.working_directory, undefined, { humanTrigger: true })`). Both go through `decideLostMessageState` (`src/lost-message.ts`), so the restart fires only in the `starting-now` state: no restart already pending or active, auto-restart enabled and the persona under the failure cap. Either way one lost-message notice naming the sender and the recovery state goes to the persona's destination through the notifier, and nothing else is posted in the source conversation (Data Flow, Inbound step 7). Guards and restart are keyed by the receiving persona's key, so there is no owner resolution and a recovery never spawns a second instance. At the cap the inbound path does **not** bypass the cap; only a server restart clears the in-process counter and revives a capped persona. In all cases the dropped message itself is lost — recovery starts a session but never delivers or replays that message.

   **Serialized work (b.av2 SR-6.6).** When the timer fires, it is removed from the pending map and the persona is marked active (`activeLaunches`) at once; the work below (the shutdown and up checks, the liveness probe, reconnect, kill and launch) is then submitted through the per-persona serializer, so it starts only after any teardown or bring-up retry already running or queued for the persona has settled, and its checks see the state at that moment. See [Per-persona serialization](#per-persona-serialization-bav2-sr-66).
3. **Liveness check** — `isSessionAlive(key)` calls `client.status({claude_instance_id})` and returns true when the state is in `AGENT_DIRECTOR_LIVE_STATES`; `ErrSpawnNotFound` → dead. When alive, `restart.ts` skips the reconnect as "already reconnected" only when the session is connected **AND** its standalone GET SSE stream is present (`RestartDeps.hasSessionStream`, b.9cj); a connected-but-streamless session (`connected === true` but the SDK dropped `_GET_stream`) cannot receive messages, so it is *not* waved through — it proceeds to recovery. If alive and not skipped, `reconnectSession()` first probes AD state. When the row is `working`, it defers the reconnect while a launch for the persona is in flight or the positive-idle rule has not shown the row stale, reconnects once it has, and escalates once the tmux session is gone (see **`working` defer** below). When the row is `ask_user` or `check_permission`, it types nothing: it probes the persona's tmux session first, escalating a gone one, and otherwise defers (see **Prompt rows** below). When the row is `waiting`, it reads the pane once first (see **`waiting` rows** below). When the row is `pending`, its session has not started, so it defers and types nothing (see **`pending` rows** below). When the status call throws, nothing is known about the session, which may be mid-turn, so nothing is typed (b.f2b): the failure is logged, the persona's idle evidence is forgotten and the adapter returns `'transient'`; the next tick retries, and during an agent-director outage the liveness probe reads the persona dead, so the tick relaunches it instead. Otherwise `reconnectMcp(key)` runs — main returns `'ok'` | `'failed'` | `'dead-session'` (from a `/mcp reconnect slack-channel-router` send-keys with retry; `'dead-session'` when the tmux session is gone, or, b.dup, when agent-director refuses the keystrokes with `ErrSpawnNotInteractive` because the row was ended or marked `missing` since the status read, or read so), adapter-mapped to `'success'` | `'transient'` | `'escalate-dead'`. A `'success'` reconnect calls `recordSuccess` (resetting the failure count and cap latch); the non-success branches do **not** `recordFailure`. `restart.ts` never re-enters `scheduleRestart` on a reconnect verdict. On `'transient'` (including the `working`, prompt-row, `pending`, `waiting`-pane and status-error defers) it returns, and the **next health-check tick** re-observes the persona and, if it is still not deliverable (alive-but-disconnected or connected-but-streamless) or has since gone dead, calls `scheduleRestart` again, so a failed/deferred reconnect is retried on a bounded cadence.

   **`escalate-dead` recovery (b.sv7, b.d61).** For the dead-tmux `escalate-dead` case CSCB recovers itself internally, in the same restart run:
   - **Sweep.** The `_buildReconnectSessionAdapter` mapping awaits `sweepDeadTmuxChannel(key, verdict)` (`src/session-manager.ts`) before returning the verdict. The verdict (`EscalateDeadVerdict`) says what proved the session dead: `dead-session` when `reconnectMcp`'s keystrokes failed twice with `ErrTmuxSendKeys`; `row-not-interactive` when agent-director refused them with `ErrSpawnNotInteractive` because the row was already `ended` or `missing` (b.dup; `reconnectMcpWithCause` reports which); `working-tmux-gone` from the `working` defer; `prompt-row-tmux-gone` from a prompt row whose tmux session is gone (b.jdc). That wrapper emits an operator-visible log line worded by the verdict (`[slack] escalate-dead: persona=… verdict=… — <evidence>, triggering internal findMissing reconciliation …`: `tmux session provably dead` for the three tmux verdicts, `row not interactive (…)` for `row-not-interactive`, whose tmux session may still be there) then runs the memoized `reconcileMissingSweep` (b.m4r), which reconciles the frozen row to `missing`. The one escalation that runs no such sweep is a prompt row's deferral that has already swept and read the row dead (see **Prompt rows** below).
   - **Re-probe.** `restart.ts` then calls `isSessionAlive(key)` again (`reprobeDeadAfterEscalate`). A thrown probe counts as dead, as the first probe's does. The shutdown check and the up check (step 1) are asked again after it.
   - **Dead** → the run goes on to the kill and relaunch (steps 4 and 5) at once, with the same accounting as any dead-session relaunch. Waiting for the next tick instead would add a full `health_check_interval` plus another backoff delay.
   - **Still alive** (the sweep failed, or a memoized sweep result predates the kill) → it returns as for `'transient'`, and the next tick retries.

   The external `~/startup/find-missing-loop.sh` is now belt-and-braces only — recovery no longer depends on it, and removing it stays a separate operator decision. Not counting at the reconnect keeps the failure count tied to actual launch attempts (the single counting site, SR-25.1) rather than a non-launch reconnect site — the sweep and the re-probe record neither success nor failure. Each outcome logs one line; they are listed in docs/engineering-guide.md, Log Messages.

   **`working` defer (b.9a7, b.rmy, b.d61, b.f2b).** A `working` row means the persona may be mid-turn, and typing `/mcp reconnect` into its pane would corrupt the turn (b.9a7 hazard 2). The row alone is no proof of a live turn, though: when the persona's tmux session is killed mid-turn, its `SessionEnd` only soft-refreshes the row (see the b.m4r note under Server-Managed Startup), so agent-director keeps it `working` until a `findMissing` sweep reconciles it to `missing`. Deferring on the row alone would repeat every tick, and the persona would never relaunch (b.d61). So the adapter checks, in order:
   - **Launch in flight** (`isLaunchInFlight(key)`) → `'transient'`, with no tmux probe. The launch owns the session's lifecycle, and a tmux session it has not created yet is no proof of death.
   - **Tmux session alive** → the positive-idle rule decides (b.f2b). The adapter probes the persona's own tmux session, `slack_bot_<key>` (`hasPersonaTmuxSession` in `src/session-manager.ts`, through the `_hasTmuxSession` prober seam). A `working` row with a live tmux session is never declared dead (the b.rmy invariant). It can be stale, though: agent-director may leave it `working` after the turn ended, and deferring on it forever would strand the persona. So `checkWorkingRowPane(key, persona)` makes one evidence read per attempt, the pane and, when it shows an idle screen, the transcript, located with the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` (the adapter gets the persona from `getAppliedPersona`; without it only the row's persisted `jsonl_path` is read). It folds the read into a run kept per persona across attempts (`workingRowPaneRuns`), with the same `classifyWorkingPane` / `foldWorkingPaneRun` rules as the launch wait (Server-Managed Startup step 5):
     - the same idle screen and the same transcript, ended with a completed turn, unchanged at every read across reads spanning `STALE_WORKING_WINDOW_MS` → `'reconnect'`: the run is forgotten and the adapter goes on to `reconnectMcp` below, as for any other alive row;
     - a running turn (the b.9a7 deferral line), a blank or unreadable pane, an idle pane whose transcript doesn't end with a completed turn or can't be located or read, or idle evidence not yet held for the window → `'transient'`, nothing typed; every case but the last ends the run, and a pending idle run makes the health check schedule the next attempt on its first undeliverable tick (Health-Check Poller step 3);
     - a prompt or dialog → `'transient'`, nothing typed; once shown across reads spanning the window, the `blocked-on-prompt` not-connected notice is raised (once per episode).

     The run is forgotten when an attempt reads the row in another state or its status call fails (`forgetWorkingRowEvidence`), when any launch for the persona starts (`spawnForPersona`), and with the persona's not-connected episode (`forgetNotConnectedEpisode`: its MCP session registered again, the health check found it deliverable, or it was torn down).

     **Bounded deferral (b.f2b).** Every `'defer'` is also one more deferral in the persona's run on the row (`noteWorkingRowDeferral`, shared with the launch wait), and so is a failed tmux probe below. Once the run has lasted `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS` (10 min) from its first deferral, the `unproven-idle` not-connected notice is raised, once per episode: its session reads working but CSCB can't prove it's idle, so it won't type into it; the notice says CSCB keeps checking. Without it, a row whose idleness can never be proven (an unreadable transcript, a compaction summary, a screen that keeps changing) would be held back from for good with nothing reported, since nothing else escalates it at a non-zero delay. The run ends (`endWorkingRowDeferral`) when an attempt reads another state (a failed status call leaves it as it is), when `'reconnect'` is returned, when a launch for the persona starts and with the episode.
   - **Probe throws** → `'transient'` as well: a failed probe is no proof the session is dead.
   - **Gone** → `sweepDeadTmuxChannel(key, 'working-tmux-gone')`, then `'escalate-dead'`. Nothing is typed. The sweep reconciles the row to `missing`, and the re-probe above relaunches the persona in the same run.

   The deferral lasts only while a launch is in flight, or the tmux session exists and the positive-idle rule has not shown the row stale. Each branch logs one line; they are listed in docs/engineering-guide.md, Log Messages.

   **Prompt rows (b.f2b, b.jdc).** `/mcp reconnect` and Enter typed into a session that waits on a question (`ask_user`) or a permission dialog (`check_permission`) could confirm the dialog's default, so the adapter never types into those states. The row alone is no proof anyone is waiting on the prompt, though: a session that dies under a prompt keeps its row in that state until a `findMissing` sweep reaps it, and deferring on the row alone kept such a persona "blocked on a prompt" for good, never relaunched, with a *Waiting on a prompt* notice about a session that no longer existed (b.jdc, `/ci-live` run 6). So `promptRowReconnectVerdict` checks, in order, probing before it notifies:
   - **Launch in flight** (`isLaunchInFlight(key)`) → `'transient'`, with no tmux probe and no notice: the launch owns the session.
   - **Tmux session gone** (`hasPersonaTmuxSession`, the exact-target prober) → `sweepDeadTmuxChannel(key, 'prompt-row-tmux-gone')`, then `'escalate-dead'`, with no notice. The re-probe above relaunches the persona in the same run.
   - **Alive, or the probe throws** (no proof the session is dead) → one more deferral in the persona's run on the row (`checkPromptRowDeferral`, the run kept per persona in `promptRowDeferredSince`). Once the run has lasted `PROMPT_ROW_SWEEP_AFTER_MS` (10 min) from its first deferral, each deferral first runs the memoized `reconcileMissingSweep` and reads the row again: `ended` or `missing` means the claude process is gone (a tmux session can outlive it), so the run ends and the adapter returns `'escalate-dead'` for the re-probe to relaunch it. Otherwise the adapter defers (`deferPromptRow`, `'transient'`): it logs the deferral, raises the persona's `blocked-on-prompt` not-connected notice (once per episode) and types nothing. The run ends when an attempt reads another state or finds the tmux session gone (a failed status call leaves it as it is), when it escalates, when a launch for the persona starts and with the not-connected episode (`forgetNotConnectedEpisode`).

   Once the prompt is answered, a later tick reconnects the persona when it can tell the session is idle again: its row reads `waiting`, or the positive-idle rule shows a `working` row stale. The restart path runs only with auto-restart on, so the notice says exactly that, and to type `/mcp reconnect` if the persona stays disconnected (a row left `working` whose idleness can't be proven is never reconnected on its own).

   **`waiting` rows (b.f2b).** Before typing into a `waiting` row, the adapter reads its pane once (`checkWaitingRowPane`, the same `classifyWorkingPane`). A running turn defers (`'transient'`); a prompt or dialog defers too and raises the `blocked-on-prompt` notice (once per episode). Anything else goes on to `reconnectMcp`, a failed read included: the `waiting` row is agent-director's own idle signal. A findMissing sweep can still mark the row `missing` before the keystrokes land (b.dup): agent-director refuses them (`ErrSpawnNotInteractive`), `reconnectMcp` answers `'dead-session'`, and the adapter escalates, so the re-probe relaunches the persona in the same run, with no spawn-failure notice.

   **`pending` rows (b.dup).** A `pending` row's session has not started (SessionStart has not fired). agent-director refuses send-keys to it (`ErrSpawnNotInteractive`), which `reconnectMcp` would read as a dead session, and the session connects its MCP servers on its own once it starts. So the adapter defers it (`deferPendingRow`, `'transient'`): it logs the deferral, types nothing and raises no notice (a launch whose session never leaves `pending` raises its own spawn-failure notice from its dialog approver). Before b.dup the adapter typed into it and the refusal raised a spawn-failure notice on every attempt.
4. **Kill zombie** — `client.kill({claude_instance_id})` is best-effort; `ErrSpawnNotFound` is ignored. The kill adapter (`_buildKillSessionAdapter` in `src/server.ts`) first asks `isLaunchInFlight(key)`: when a launch for the persona is already running, it logs `[slack] killSession (restart adapter): launch already in flight for persona=<key> — not killing` and returns without killing. Mid-ladder the row can read dead, so a kill here would take down the process the running launch is bringing up; the relaunch in step 5 joins that launch instead (b.av2 SR-6.6).
5. **Relaunch** — `launchSession(key, personaConfig, { canLaunch: canRelaunch })` asks the relaunch gate (`createPersonaRelaunchGate` in `src/persona-start.ts`) first, then looks up the persona with that key (`false` when there is none; asking the gate first keeps a key a confirmed apply removed from counting as a failure), and otherwise collapses `spawnForPersona`'s richer outcome to a boolean — the single counting site (SR-25.1). The b.f2b action `not-reconnected` (a `working`-row wait that ended with the session alive and nothing typed, or that its teardown cancelled) maps to `true`: the session runs, so it is not a failure. Resume vs fresh is decided by the SR-1.4 collision-then-act dispatch (no separate `sessionId` arg from CSCB — agent-director owns resume state), the same persona-keyed ladder as startup, so the b.av2 SR-6.2 `cwd` and `config_dir` guards apply to runtime relaunches too. A relaunch runs only the launch: it never repeats the start bring-up's credentials, working-directory or Slack steps (Server-Managed Startup step 5): a row in another directory is replaced, and a row whose `config_dir` label is missing or differs is spawned fresh rather than resumed. Like every launch, the relaunch is preceded by the trust patch for the persona's working directory in its effective config dir (the pre-launch patcher at the top of the ladder; see startup step 4a-bis), and a patch failure there is logged only. Its spawn or resume is likewise immediately preceded by the reply-guard steps (startup step 5), so the persona's record is rewritten with its current effective `stop_hook_bootstrap`; a relaunch that only reconnects writes nothing. The relaunch goes through `spawnForPersona`'s one-in-flight-launch-per-persona rule (b.av2 SR-6.3), so a relaunch that overlaps a still-running launch for the same persona (the start pass or another trigger) joins it and receives its outcome. `restart.ts` counts each `launchSession` result it receives, so overlapping restarts that join one failed launch count that failure once per caller. A joined launch's failure record and notice follow the mode (startup or runtime) of the launch that actually ran, not of the caller that joined it. On a `true` outcome, `recordSuccess` resets the counter and cap latch; on `false`, `recordFailure` increments it.

   **Relaunch gate.** The gate (`createPersonaRelaunchGate`, over the connection manager and the bring-up controller) answers true only while the persona's Slack connection is serving, its bring-up outcome is `up` and its key is in the applied set (see the applied-set guard under Server-Managed Startup step 5). A key outside the applied set is refused first, whatever its status, with `[slack] persona=<key>: not relaunched — it is no longer in the applied configuration`, and restart.ts then logs its usual `Not scheduling restart` line. Besides step 1's up check, `launchSession` asks it, which covers a change during the kill. When that check answers false, `launchSession` returns `'skipped'` without spawning or running the trust patch or the reply-guard steps, and `restart.ts` records neither success nor failure, so the counter, backoff and cap latch stay as they were and no cap notice is raised. The gate's own log lines are in the `persona-start.ts` entry of the Module Map. Once the persona is up again it is eligible, and its next restart counts normally.
6. **Cap latch** — after the 5th consecutive failure (`RESTART_FAILURE_CAP = 5`, exported from `src/restart.ts`), `shouldNotifyCap(key, …)` fires once per episode and `onCapReached(key)` calls the session manager's `notifyRestartCapReached(key)`, which raises a synthetic `ErrSpawnCapReached` spawn-failure notice through the per-persona notifier to the persona's destination, with the remediation "restart the server to retry — automatic restarts are suspended for this persona"; **no** further timer is scheduled. A capped persona has no registered session; an inbound Slack message hits the no-session branch in `src/persona-routing.ts`, whose `decideLostMessageState` detects the cap (`isAtCap(key, RESTART_FAILURE_CAP)`) and does **not** fire a restart — firing one would only burn a spawn attempt against a persona that cannot come up. The persona's destination gets a lost-message notice in the `restart-limit-reached` state, saying automatic restarts are suspended and the server must be restarted. Only a **server restart** clears the in-process counter (lost on restart by design) and gives the persona a fresh attempt.

### Health-Check Poller

A periodic backstop that runs alongside the reactive disconnect path. It catches three failure modes that the reactive `onsessionclosed` path misses: (a) sessions that die without triggering a close event (e.g., an externally-killed tmux session); (b) sessions that are alive but MCP-disconnected — an AD live-state row (`waiting` etc.) whose MCP transport is gone, `isSessionAlive === true` yet unreachable; and (c) connected-but-streamless sessions (b.9cj) — `isSessionAlive === true` and `isSessionConnected === true` in the registry, but the SDK has silently dropped the `_GET_stream` map entry, so messages cannot reach the bot. Without (c) an idle streamless session would stay broken forever, invisible to the reactive path. The tick recovers all three.

On each tick:

1. **Persona iteration** — for each persona key / working-directory pair from `HealthCheckDeps.getPersonas()` (production: `buildPersonaWorkList(personaConfig, canRelaunch)`, one entry per applied persona however many channels it lists, rebuilt every tick). The relaunch gate leaves out every persona that is not up: its bring-up is `broken` or `retrying`, or its Slack connection is not serving (never brought up, connecting, retrying its bring-up, broken or stopped). A persona a confirmed apply removed is absent too: the list is built from `personaConfig` (see the applied-set guard under Server-Managed Startup step 5). Such a persona is absent from the whole tick: no `cwd-unreachable` update, no reconnect and no relaunch, including a persona that was launched and whose connection later broke or stopped. A directory-broken persona's directory is therefore watched only by its bring-up retry, never by this tick. It rejoins the tick once it is up. The gate's one line per persona per state is described under Auto-Restart step 5. Every guard call, the disconnected streak and the `cwd-unreachable` outage flag are keyed by persona key (b.av2 SR-6.3); log lines name it as `persona=<key>`, e.g. `[slack] health-check: persona=<key> is at cap — skipping tick (SR-25.3/25.4)`:
   - **Skip if restart pending/active** — `isRestartPendingOrActive(key)` returns true; a relaunch is already in flight.
   - **Skip if a launch is in flight (b.f2b)** — `HealthCheckDeps.isLaunchInFlight(key)` (the session manager's `isLaunchInFlight`) returns true: a start launch parked while it waits for a `working` row, a launch after a bring-up retry, or a restart's launch. The launch owns the session, and its wait reconnects the persona or reports it. Silent; the streak is cleared as for a pending restart.
   - **Skip if at failure cap** — `isAtCap(key, RESTART_FAILURE_CAP)` (injected as `HealthCheckDeps.isAtCap`) returns true; a persona at the consecutive-failure cap is skipped this tick (SR-25.3/25.4), including the alive-but-disconnected reconnect path — a capped persona gets no tick-driven reconnect. The inbound drop branch likewise detects the cap and declines to fire a restart, so a **server restart** is the only path back — it clears the in-process counter and gives the persona a fresh attempt.
   - **Working-directory check** — `statRoute(cwd)` on the persona's working directory clears `cwd-unreachable` for the persona when it exists and raises it otherwise.
   - **Liveness check** — `isSessionAlive(key)` calls `client.status({claude_instance_id})`; the state is checked against `AGENT_DIRECTOR_LIVE_STATES`. `isSessionConnected(key)` (b.9a7, wraps the same registry-`connected` adapter used by restart.ts) reports whether the MCP session is currently connected. `hasSessionStream(key)` (b.9cj, wraps the shared `getSessionByPersona` → `hasGetStreamKey(transport)` probe wired into both `HealthCheckDeps` and `RestartDeps`) reports whether the standalone `_GET_stream` is present.
2. **Dead session** — if the liveness check fails, `scheduleRestart(key, cwd)` is called, delegating to the same restart path used by `onsessionclosed`.
3. **Alive but not deliverable** — if `isSessionAlive` is true but the session is either not connected (`!isSessionConnected`, b.9a7) **or** connected-but-streamless (`!hasSessionStream`, b.9cj), the tick tracks a per-persona consecutive-failing streak and calls `scheduleRestart(key, cwd)` only once the streak reaches **two consecutive ticks**. Both cases land in this branch and share the one streak/debounce, which suppresses the freshly-launched false positive: a booting session that connects (and re-opens its stream) between ticks is never poked. Observing the persona as fully healthy — alive, connected, AND stream present — (or dead) resets/consumes the streak. A tick that skips the persona clears it too: a restart pending or active, a launch in flight, the cap, or the relaunch gate leaving it out of the tick's work list (not up), so a persona that comes back up starts a fresh count. `_resetHealthCheckState` clears the whole map. `restart.ts` then reconnects an alive-but-disconnected row (deferring a `working` row while a launch for it is in flight or the positive-idle rule has not shown it stale, never typing into a prompt or a running turn, and typing nothing on a failed status call, per Auto-Restart step 3), recovers a streamless one, or relaunches a dead one. This gives time-bounded recovery on the `health_check_interval` (default 120 s) even for idle personas that receive no inbound traffic.
   - **Two-tick exception (b.f2b).** While the reconnect adapter holds an idle run for the persona's `working` row that one more attempt can conclude (`HealthCheckDeps.hasPendingWorkingRowEvidence`, production `hasPendingWorkingRowEvidence`), the tick calls `scheduleRestart` on the first undeliverable tick. Each attempt makes one evidence read, so the next read comes one tick sooner. It never applies to a booting session: any launch for the persona forgets the run first. A prompt run doesn't count.
   - **Delay 0: the not-connected notice (b.f2b).** With `session_restart_delay` 0, `scheduleRestart` only logs `Auto-restart disabled (delay=0)` and returns, so nothing would reconnect the persona. When `HealthCheckDeps.isAutoRestartDisabled()` is true, the tick therefore also calls `HealthCheckDeps.notifyNotConnected(key, cause)` (production `notifyDisconnectedWithAutoRestartDisabled`) right after `scheduleRestart` in this branch: the `auto-restart-disabled` not-connected notice through `notifyPersonaNotConnected`, worded by `cause`, which the tick takes from the same `isSessionConnected` answer the branch used: *Not connected* (`disconnected`, cause `its connection has been down on two health checks in a row`) or *Not receiving messages* (`streamless`, `found on two health checks in a row`). One latch covers every notice text, so a notice already raised in the episode by the launch wait, a prompt report or an `unproven-idle` report counts for it. The dead branch (step 2) raises none.
   - **Healthy: the episode ends (b.f2b).** A tick that finds the persona alive, connected and with its stream also calls `HealthCheckDeps.endNotConnectedEpisode(key)` (production `forgetNotConnectedEpisode`), as registration (`handleInitialized`) and the teardown do: the latch and the restart path's idle evidence are forgotten, so a later episode is reported again.

The interval is controlled by `health_check_interval` in `config.json`. If the value is `0`, `startHealthCheck()` returns immediately and no interval is created. `stopHealthCheck()` clears the interval during graceful shutdown, before `cancelAllRestartTimers()` runs.

**Ordering invariant**: `startHealthCheck()` is called only after `startupSessionManager()` returns, which is once every persona has a bring-up outcome and every `up` persona's start launch has settled or been parked waiting for a `working` row (b.f2b). Moving it earlier in the startup sequence would risk the poller racing with in-progress start launches. A parked launch stays in flight until it settles, and the tick skips a persona whose launch is in flight (`isLaunchInFlight`), so the poller never races it either. Bring-up retries that finish later don't delay it; their launches go through `spawnForPersona`'s one-launch-in-flight guard, which a restart the tick triggers also respects.

**Ordering invariant (reload detection)**: `reload.startDetection()` is called right after `startHealthCheck()`, for the same reason, and the controller itself refuses to arm the tick before the start bring-up pass has returned. The held credentials digests the tick compares against exist only once each persona's bring-up has read its file. A parked start launch doesn't hold the tick back: its bring-up has read its credentials, and a teardown of that persona cancels the launch's wait and waits for it to settle (b.f2b).

### Reload Detection Tick (b.av2 SR-8.2, SR-8.3)

The reload controller (`src/reload.ts`) notices edits to `config.json` and to the credentials files it references, and keeps `config.json.pending` in step with them. It also processes the operator's confirmation, `config.json.apply`, and applies a confirmed change (see [Confirmation](#confirmation-sr-85) and [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86)). Only that apply writes the record, swaps the applied set or calls a lifecycle operation (the teardowns of step 2 and the bring-ups of step 6). Detection itself creates no Slack client; a bring-up at apply does, through the connection manager.

Each pass runs in this order: derive the pending state from the files, process `config.json.apply` against it, derive the state again if the apply changed what is applied, then refresh the pending file (write it, or delete it) against that state. So a confirmation is always processed before the pending state is refreshed.

#### The timer

One serialized, self-re-arming 5 s `setTimeout` chain (`createReloadTickDriver`, `src/reload-timer.ts`), in the `cron-scheduler.ts` pattern, never `setInterval`. The next pass is armed only once the previous one has settled, 5 s after its end, so passes never overlap. There is no file watcher; each pass reads the files afresh.

`startDetection()` arms it once, after an applied start's bring-up pass returns (Server-Managed Startup step 9). A start launch the pass parked while it waits for a `working` row (b.f2b) may still be in flight then. SR-8.2's intent holds: every persona's bring-up has read its credentials, so the digests the tick compares against exist, and an apply that tears that persona down cancels the launch's wait and then waits for it to settle (`whenLaunchSettled`). `start` runs nothing itself, so the first pass runs 5 s after it. `stopDetection()` at graceful shutdown stops the driver and makes any pass still due do nothing. `stop()` is idempotent. The driver is single-use; a second `start`, or a `start` after `stop`, is logged and ignored. A pass that throws past the controller's own catch is logged by the driver (see the Reload lines table) and the chain re-arms. Nothing is armed or logged at import or construction.

#### What a pass reads

Each pass reads `config.json` once, stat-first through `readPersonaConfigBytes`, so a FIFO or device never blocks it. Unless in dry run, it then reads every distinct `credentials_file` the file names (`referencedCredentialsPaths`, parsed tolerantly, so an invalid file still names its paths) once each, through `readCredentialsFile`. It then reads `config.json.apply`, and finally `config.json.pending`. Each of these files is read once, stat-first and capped (see [Size cap](#reload-files-configjsonlast-applied-configjsonpending-configjsonapply)). The credentials bytes are dropped at the end of the pass. The preview needs a few more facts, gathered with the least extra I/O (see [The change plan](#the-change-plan-sr-86)).

#### When a change is pending (SR-8.3)

A change is pending when either holds:

- **The configuration changed**: the bytes of `config.json` differ from the applied bytes (the record's at a start from the record).
- **Credentials changed**: the change plan lists at least one credentials-changed persona. It is the only place this is decided. Such a persona is present in both the applied set and a valid candidate, with an unchanged `credentials_file` path (by real path) and no destructive change, and has a file whose digest (`credentialsDigest`) differs from the one its bring-up holds, which is the content it connected with, is retrying with or broke with. Only digests are compared, and they are held in memory only, never written to disk. A persona that holds no digest never counts, as in dry run. A persona broken by a credentials file another applied persona shares (an SR-1.5 record-start collision) holds the digest of its own path's bytes, read stat-first and hashed, never parsed, with no token used. Re-saving that file with different bytes therefore makes a change pending for it. This is how the remaining persona recovers once a confirmed apply removes the collision by changing the other persona: its own declaration is unchanged, so nothing else would re-check it. While the collision stands, such a pending change can't bring either persona up.

The candidate is what a confirmation would apply. Changed bytes are validated in default mode, so a real-path collision is a validation error there; a validation failure is an `INVALID` candidate carrying the loader's message. A missing or unreadable `config.json` while a record exists is also an `INVALID` candidate. Personas added, removed or with a changed `credentials_file` path are configuration changes, not credentials changes.

In dry run no credentials file is read, and credentials never count as changed.

After a start from the record, the first pass is the SR-8.7 comparison: an edit made before the start differs from the record, so it stays pending and the pending file is written after the start.

#### The fingerprint

The pending state is identified by its fingerprint (`reloadFingerprint`, `src/reload-fingerprint.ts`). It is a SHA-256 over the configuration file's bytes plus, for each referenced credentials file in declaration order, its path and its bytes or a missing/unreadable marker. A missing or unreadable `config.json` contributes a marker and no credentials entries. In dry run it covers the configuration file alone.

#### The pending file lifecycle

`config.json.pending` exists only while a change is pending. Its first line is a fixed header (`claude-slack-channel-bots: pending configuration change (written by the server)`), its second `fingerprint: sha256:<64 hex>`, then a blank line and the preview body.

- **Pending.** The file is written through the durable write (`durableWriteFileSync`) whenever it is missing or its bytes differ from what this pass would write, without a log line. The preview is logged only when the logged state changes: from nothing pending, or to a different fingerprint. So a rewrite with the same fingerprint logs nothing, even when the preview text changed because a fact did (an added persona's working directory was created, say). The log form is under [The preview in the log](#the-preview-in-the-log).
- **Nothing pending.** The file is deleted through the durable delete (`durableUnlinkSync`). Once it is actually gone, `reload-nothing-pending` is logged once, when the state changes from pending to nothing (a revert) or when the first pass after a start actually removes a leftover pending file. It is never logged at a clean start, and a first pass that finds no file logs nothing. The line has two variants: when this pass removed the file it ends `…; no change is pending, and the pending-change file "<pending>" is removed`; when there was no file to remove (for example it had never been written, or was deleted by hand) it ends `…; no change is pending`. A pending file placed by hand while nothing is pending is deleted without a line.

#### The change plan (SR-8.6)

Every pass builds one change plan (`buildChangePlan`, `src/reload-plan.ts`) of the candidate against the applied configuration. The preview, the log lines, the pending decision and a confirmed apply all come from it (the apply uses the plan of the pass that matched the confirmation, see [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86)), so what is applied is what was previewed. Personas are matched by key; a `name` edit that changes the key is a removal plus an addition.

| Class | A persona present in both, or a setting, when… | Effect stated |
|---|---|---|
| Added | its key is only in the candidate | brought up and launched, or why it cannot come up |
| Removed | its key is only in the applied set | `DESTRUCTIVE:` its live session is destroyed |
| Destructively modified | `name` (same key), `credentials_file` or `working_directory` changed; paths compared by real path | `DESTRUCTIVE:` its live session is destroyed, then it is brought up fresh. Listed in no other class |
| Modified in place | the set of channel IDs (`channels`), a kept channel's mode (`delivery`), `permission_prompts`, `dm.enabled` or `dm.contact` changed. A channel reorder is not a change | applied in place immediately, instance kept |
| Changed credentials | same `credentials_file` by real path, and its current digest differs from the held one (see above); never in dry run or with nothing held | by the bring-up state and `problem` (see below): up, a new connection opens, then the old one closes; retrying, it retries with the new content; broken by its credentials now (`isCredentialsBroken`: outcome `broken` with a local credentials cause, or with Slack's refusal, `persona-credentials-refused`), it is brought up. With a `problem`, an up persona keeps its current connection, a retrying one keeps retrying with its current content and a credentials-broken one stays broken |
| Next launch | `claude_config_dir` (by real path) or `stop_hook_bootstrap` changed. It is inherited when the top-level default changed and the persona's value equalled the old default before and the new default after; otherwise it is the persona's own | takes effect at its next launch, instance kept; for `claude_config_dir`, that launch starts fresh (the conversation is not resumed; SR-6.2) |
| Server-wide setting | any other top-level key's resolved value changed (path settings by real path) | once applied, recorded and effective at the next server start after that; `stop_timeout` and `exit_timeout`, which only the CLI uses, are taken by the CLI from the record once applied |
| Inherited default | top-level `claude_config_dir` or `stop_hook_bootstrap` changed | lists the personas present in both, not destructively modified, whose value changes through it; takes effect at each one's next launch (fresh for `claude_config_dir`). With no such persona: recorded, no instance affected |

A credentials change's `problem` is why the new content is not locally valid (missing, unreadable, bad shape or prefix). For a persona that is up, the apply's credentials step (step 4) then keeps the current connection and logs `persona-credentials-change-failed`; a retrying persona keeps retrying with its current content; a credentials-broken persona stays broken. The preview's `problem` is only a forecast: step 4 checks the file again when it runs (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)). A persona may be both modified in place, next-launch and credentials-changed. Anything else present in both is unchanged. `no effective change` holds when every persona is unchanged and no server-wide setting changed: a whitespace or key-order edit, or a key added with its default value.

The plan also carries `configDirsChanged`: whether the set of effective config directories (`effectiveClaudeConfigDirs`) differs between the applied and the candidate personas, so the apply knows to refresh the agent-director template (SR-8.6 step 5, see [Template refresh](#template-refresh-bav2-sr-86-step-5)). The apply selects step 5 whenever `configDirsChanged` is true, even for a plan with `no effective change` (`applyStepsFor`) (a `claude_config_dir` moved to another path that is a symlink to the same directory changes no persona but does change the template). It is compared lexically, not by real path, because the template's memory-read rules are written from the lexical paths; it is not rendered in the preview. An invalid plan carries `false`.

How the pass gathers the plan's facts:

- **Real paths**: `resolveRealPath`, memoized per pass, called only when the two path strings differ.
- **Credentials digests**: from the reads the fingerprint already made; none in dry run.
- **Bring-up state**: the injected `bringUpState`, asked only for a credentials-changed persona. It sets the change's `credentialsBroken` (`isCredentialsBroken`) and `retrying` (outcome `retrying`, or no outcome yet because its first Slack attempt is in flight). A persona held for its `claude_config_dir` (bug b.g57) is `retrying` with no connection, so its line says it retries with the new content.
- **Credentials problem**: `credentialsReadProblem` over the bytes the fingerprint already read (none in dry run), asked only for a credentials-changed persona. It becomes the change's `problem`: a token-free cause such as `credentials file does not exist` or `credentials file is invalid: …`.
- **Added personas**: `credentialsReadProblem` over the bytes already read (skipped in dry run, so the preview then gives no credentials cause), `checkPersonaWorkingDirectory` with no other personas (a valid candidate has no real-path collision), then the injected `checkConfigDir` (production: `checkLaunchConfigDir`, wired in `server.ts`), whose failure's `problem` is the cause: `claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (<errno>)`, with `: a symlink on its path points to nothing` after the errno for a dangling symlink. A directory not created yet under an existing ancestor passes. All three checks run, so one failure never hides another, and the causes are listed in that order. These are the local steps 1 and 2 of the SR-6.1 bring-up plus the `claude_config_dir` check the bring-up runs before step 3; no Slack call is made, so a token Slack would refuse is not detected. An unchanged persona is not probed.
- **Changed claude_config_dir** (bug b.g57): the same injected `checkConfigDir`, asked by the plan (`configDirProblem`) only for a persona present in both whose effective `claude_config_dir` changed (its own, through a changed top-level default, or alongside a destructive modify), with the candidate's declaration. Its `problem` becomes the change's `configDirUnresolvable`, and the line warns: a confirmed change is still applied, and the persona is then held at its next launch (at the confirmation, for a destructive modify) until the directory resolves, as the `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` hold does. A directory not created yet under an existing ancestor passes.

A fact that cannot be gathered (the bring-up state query, an added persona's checks or a changed `claude_config_dir`'s check throw) is carried as unknown (`FACT_UNKNOWN`), and the preview says so rather than claiming either answer: the added persona `is added; whether it can come up could not be checked.`, the changed `claude_config_dir` `…; whether it can come up at that launch could not be checked` (for inheriting personas `whether "<name>" (key=<key>), … can come up at that launch could not be checked`, on a `DESTRUCTIVE:` line `whether it can come up could not be checked`), the credentials-changed persona `…; whether it is broken by its credentials now could not be checked` (after `…, but it cannot be used (<cause>)` when the content is bad). The header counts are unchanged. It is logged once, and the latch re-arms after a pass that gathers every fact.

#### The preview (SR-8.4)

The preview body sits after the header and fingerprint lines and the blank line. Every line is one line (control characters escaped with `escapeCause`). Personas are named by their JSON-quoted name with the key beside it, and credentials files by path. No line carries a setting value (except the new path of a destructive path change), a token, credentials content or a digest.

A valid candidate opens with a header: `A configuration change is pending; nothing has been applied.` and the counts (`renderChangePlanCounts`). A persona modified in place or with an own next-launch change counts as modified in place; one inherited from a top-level default counts only under server-wide settings. A persona both modified in place and credentials-changed counts in both. Then come, in order: removals, destructive modifies, additions, the other affected personas in candidate order (one line each, stating every effect), then the changed server-wide settings in the fixed top-level key order.

A removal, an in-place change, a credentials change, an addition that cannot come up and two settings:

```text
A configuration change is pending; nothing has been applied. personas: 1 added, 1 removed, 0 destructively modified, 1 modified in place, 1 with changed credentials; server-wide settings: 2 changed.
DESTRUCTIVE: persona "alpha" (key=alpha) is removed: its live session will be destroyed (its instance is torn down).
persona "delta" (key=delta) is added but cannot come up: credentials file does not exist; working directory does not exist.
persona "bravo" (key=bravo): channels changed: applied in place immediately, instance kept.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed: a new connection opens, then the old one closes, instance kept.
server-wide setting port changed: once applied, it is recorded and takes effect at the next server start after that.
server-wide setting claude_config_dir changed: inherited by "bravo" (key=bravo), "charlie" (key=charlie); takes effect at each one's next launch, which starts fresh (the conversation is not resumed), instance kept until then.
```

Other line forms:

```text
DESTRUCTIVE: persona "bravo" (key=bravo) working_directory changed to "/srv/work/bravo2": its live session will be destroyed, then it is brought up fresh.
persona "delta" (key=delta) is added: it will be brought up and launched.
persona "bravo" (key=bravo): stop_hook_bootstrap changed: takes effect at its next launch, instance kept.
persona "bravo" (key=bravo): claude_config_dir changed: takes effect at its next launch, which starts fresh (the conversation is not resumed), instance kept until then.
persona "bravo" (key=bravo): claude_config_dir changed: takes effect at its next launch, which starts fresh (the conversation is not resumed), instance kept until then; but at that launch it cannot come up: claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (ENOENT: a symlink on its path points to nothing).
server-wide setting claude_config_dir changed: inherited by "bravo" (key=bravo), "charlie" (key=charlie); takes effect at each one's next launch, which starts fresh (the conversation is not resumed), instance kept until then; but at that launch "bravo" (key=bravo), "charlie" (key=charlie) cannot come up: claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (EIO).
DESTRUCTIVE: persona "bravo" (key=bravo) working_directory changed to "/srv/work/bravo2": its live session will be destroyed, then it is brought up fresh; but it cannot come up: claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (ENOENT).
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed: it has no connection yet, so it retries with the new content, instance kept.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed: it is broken by its credentials now, so it will be brought up.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed, but it cannot be used (credentials file does not exist): the current connection is kept, instance kept.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed, but it cannot be used (credentials file does not exist): it keeps retrying with its current content, instance kept.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed, but it cannot be used (credentials file does not exist): it stays broken by its credentials.
persona "delta" (key=delta) is added; whether it can come up could not be checked.
persona "charlie" (key=charlie): credentials file "/srv/personas/charlie/credentials.json" changed; whether it is broken by its credentials now could not be checked.
server-wide setting stop_hook_bootstrap changed: once applied, it is recorded; no persona inherits it, so no instance is affected.
server-wide setting exit_timeout changed: once applied, it is recorded, and the CLI takes it from the record from then on (the running server does not use it).
A configuration change is pending; nothing has been applied. no effective change: applying it would change no persona and no server-wide setting.
INVALID: loadPersonaConfig: malformed JSON in "/srv/cscb/config.json" at line 1, column 17. Nothing will be applied.
INVALID: the configuration file "/srv/cscb/config.json" does not exist. Nothing will be applied.
```

An invalid candidate is that one `INVALID:` line with the loader's full error, and no header or persona lines. A missing file reads `does not exist`, an unreadable one `cannot be read (<code>)`. The loader's error echoes no rejected value and no unknown key name that could be a token (such a key is counted, not shown).

#### The preview in the log

The preview goes to the server log once per change of the logged state (see the Pending bullet above), identified by fingerprint:

- **Valid candidate**: every body line, each as `[slack] reload-preview: <line>`. The header line also ends ` (preview in "<pending>")`. A `no effective change` preview is logged this way too.
- **Invalid candidate**: exactly one line, `[slack] reload-invalid: the pending configuration is invalid and nothing will be applied: <error> (preview in "<pending>")`. No `reload-preview` line is logged for it.

The ` (preview in …)` suffix is dropped when this pass's pending-file write failed, so the log never points at a file that doesn't hold the preview. A write whose directory sync failed counts as written. The apply-time lines are under [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86).

#### Confirmation (SR-8.5)

The operator confirms a pending change by renaming `config.json.pending` to `config.json.apply`. Nothing else triggers an apply: no endpoint, MCP tool or CLI subcommand reads, writes or creates the confirmation (b.av2 SR-8.8).

- **Read, then delete.** Each pass reads `config.json.apply` once through `readPersonaConfigBytes`; when it is absent nothing happens. It is then deleted with the durable delete (`durableUnlinkSync`) before anything is decided or applied, so a confirmation is used once. A delete whose directory sync failed counts as removed and logs one plain line.
- **Fingerprint match.** The confirmation's fingerprint line (`parsePendingFingerprint`) is compared with the fingerprint this pass computed over the current `config.json` and credentials files (`reloadFingerprint`, see [The fingerprint](#the-fingerprint)). On a match the pass hands its own bytes and change plan to the apply (`applyConfirmed`); nothing is read again and nothing is diffed a second time.
- **Stale.** A mismatch (`config.json` or a credentials file it references changed after that preview was written), an unreadable confirmation (a directory, a non-regular file, or one over the size cap) or one with no well-formed fingerprint applies nothing and logs one `reload-stale-confirmation` line (`renderStaleConfirmationLogLine`), naming the path and the reason, never the content. The fingerprint covers only `config.json` and the credentials files, not the record, so a copy of a pending file whose change was already applied still matches while those files are unchanged: it is applied again as a no-op (`reload-noop`), not refused.
- **Undeletable confirmation.** When the delete fails, the confirmation is still acted on once (applied on a match, else the stale line), and one plain cannot-remove line is logged. The pass keeps its identity in memory (`ignoredConfirmation`: a SHA-256 of its bytes, or the fixed `UNREADABLE_CONFIRMATION_IDENTITY` for one that can't be read). A later pass that finds the same identity only retries the delete, silently; different content is processed afresh, and the memory is cleared once the file is gone. The memory is not persisted, so after a restart such a file is processed once more.
- **Pending file after a consumed confirmation.** The rename removed `config.json.pending`. When the change is still pending after the confirmation (stale, invalid, a failed record write, or a credentials change that step 4 could not use), the same pass writes the pending file again, without a new preview line when the fingerprint is unchanged. An invalid candidate normally gets two `reload-invalid` lines: the pending-time line when its `INVALID` preview is written, then the confirmed line when the operator confirms it. A consumed confirmation counts as the logged pending state, so the pass that consumes it never logs the pending-time line as well: when the confirmation is already there on the pass that first sees the candidate (for example the first pass after a start), that pass logs only the confirmed line.
- **Server down.** A confirmation left while the server was stopped is processed by the first pass after the next start (the SR-8.7 comparison pass). The start itself never reads it.

#### The apply pipeline (SR-8.6)

`applyConfirmed` in `src/reload.ts` runs the apply; the step names, their inputs and the step runner are in `src/reload-apply.ts`.

- **Invalid candidate.** A candidate that fails to parse or validate (or a `config.json` that is missing, unreadable or over the cap) changes nothing: the record is not written and one line carries the full error, `[slack] reload-invalid: the confirmed configuration is invalid, so nothing is applied: <error>`.
- **Valid candidate.** Six steps run in order. Each step completes for every persona before the next starts (`runApplySteps` awaits each step's body). A step that throws is logged (`[slack] reload: apply step <n> (<step>) failed: <thrown value>`) and the next step still runs.

| Step | Acts on | State |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Record and swap | The whole candidate: the record is rewritten with the candidate's exact bytes (durable write), then, in the same synchronous step, the controller's applied state is swapped and `onApplied` is told, which reassigns the server's `personaConfig`. The bring-up controller's applied set, the reply-guard step and the detection tick read the new set from then on | Implemented |
| 2. Teardowns (`teardowns`) | Removed personas (`removed`) and the old half of each destructive modify (`destructiveOld`), as the previous configuration declares them: each is torn down (see [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65) and [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86)) | Implemented |
| 3. In-place updates (`in-place-updates`) | Personas modified in place (`inPlace`), with their previous declaration and the changed settings: each is updated in place (see [In-place update](#in-place-update-bav2-sr-86)) | Implemented |
| 4. Credentials reconnects (`credentials-reconnects`) | Personas whose credentials content changed at the same path (`credentials`, plus `credentialsBroken`) and that are not broken by their credentials when step 4 runs: each that is up is reconnected, and each that is retrying retries with the new content (see [Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)). The step settles once every persona's first reconnect attempt has settled; a reconnect left retrying counts as complete, so step 5 and later ticks are not held up | Implemented |
| 5. Template refresh (`template-refresh`) | The agent-director template, once; selected only when `configDirsChanged`: its memory-read rules are rewritten for the confirmed persona set (see [Template refresh](#template-refresh-bav2-sr-86-step-5)) | Implemented |
| 6. Bring-ups (`bring-ups`) | Added personas (`added`), as the candidate declares them: each is brought up (see [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61)). Also, as a recovery bring-up, each persona whose credentials changed and that step 4 did not reconnect because it was broken by its credentials, with or without an instance; the recovery checks the state again when it runs (see [Recovery of a credentials-broken persona](#recovery-of-a-credentials-broken-persona-bav2-sr-64)). The new half of each destructive modify (`destructiveNew`), as the candidate declares it, is brought up fresh the same way as an added persona | Implemented |

Steps 2–6 are slots (`ApplyStepSlots`). By default the controller fills them with `lifecycleApplySlots` (`src/reload-apply.ts`): step 2 fans `lifecycle.teardown` out over `removed` and `destructiveOld`, step 3 fans `lifecycle.updateInPlace` out over `inPlace` (one update per persona key), step 4 fans `lifecycle.reconnectCredentials` out over the credentials-changed personas that are not credentials-broken, step 5 calls `lifecycle.refreshTemplate` once with the confirmed configuration, and step 6 fans `lifecycle.bringUp` out over `added` and `destructiveNew` and, with `{ recovery: true }`, over the credentials-changed personas step 4 did not reconnect; production binds all five to `src/persona-lifecycle.ts`. A persona with only next-launch changes is in no step's input (see [Next-launch and server-wide settings](#next-launch-and-server-wide-settings-bav2-sr-86)).

**Credentials-broken is decided when the step runs.** The plan splits credentials-changed personas into `credentials` and `credentialsBroken` at the preview, but a persona can become credentials-broken between the preview and the apply, for example when its bot token is refused by a Web API call. So step 4 asks the bring-up state again (`credentialsBrokenNow`, over the controller's `bringUpState` and `isCredentialsBroken`), and falls back to the plan's split only when the state can't be read. Step 4 also re-checks inside its operation: a persona found credentials-broken there resolves `{ kind: 'credentials-broken' }` and is left to step 6. Step 6 takes every credentials-changed persona step 4 did not reconnect (a per-apply set in `lifecycleApplySlots`); only when step 4 did not run in the apply does step 6 ask `credentialsBrokenNow` itself. The recovery then decides inside the persona's serializer turn: a persona no longer credentials-broken by then (a pending reconnect of an earlier change brought it up) gets the change as step 4 would have applied it, so no confirmed change is skipped by both steps. Within a step every persona's operation runs at once (each through the per-persona serializer), and the step settles once all of them have settled: one persona's rejection is logged (`[slack] reload: apply step <n> (<step>) failed for persona "<name>" (key=<key>): <thrown value>`) and never stops another. The per-step barrier holds when one apply has both: every teardown settles before step 3 starts, and the bring-ups start only after steps 3–5. The controller's `applySteps` replaces the default bodies entirely; only tests set it.

A `name` change changes the key (b.av2 SR-2.1), so the plan lists it as a removal of the old key and an addition of the new one: the old persona is torn down in step 2 and the new one brought up fresh in step 6, without the old conversation.

Step 1's swap has these effects before step 2 runs, because routing, `isPersonaUp`, the permission poller, MCP admission, `/interject` and the notifier all read `personaConfig` at call time:

- **Removed persona.** It stops receiving events at once: each is dropped with `[slack] persona-routing: no applied persona with key=<key> — event not delivered to it`, and a notice for it is dropped (`persona-notifier: no applied persona with key=<key> — notice dropped`). A new MCP session from its directory matches no applied persona and is not admitted, the permission poller leaves its rows unread, and `/interject` answers 404 (`Persona not found in the applied config`). An MCP session of it that is still registered stays connected, but each tool call it makes is refused with the tool error `Tool "<name>" refused: persona key=<key> is not an applied persona.` (the session's tool handler in `registry.ts` looks the persona up in the applied set at call time). It is never restarted or relaunched (see the applied-set guard under Server-Managed Startup step 5). Its instance and Slack connection still run until its teardown in step 2 removes them.
- **Kept persona.** For a change to `channels`, `delivery`, `permission_prompts` or `dm.*`, see [In-place update](#in-place-update-bav2-sr-86). A change to `stop_hook_bootstrap` or `claude_config_dir` takes effect at the persona's next launch or relaunch, which reads the new declaration (`launchSession` and the bring-up retry's launch look the persona up in `personaConfig`); the running instance is untouched (see [Next-launch and server-wide settings](#next-launch-and-server-wide-settings-bav2-sr-86)).
- **Destructively modified persona.** Its key stays applied with the new declaration, so MCP admission matches a registering session against the new `working_directory` at once, and a session from the old directory that registers again is not admitted as that persona. Its old instance still runs until step 2 tears it down (see [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86)).
- **Added persona.** It is in the applied set, and step 6 brings it up.
- **Credentials change.** Nothing changes yet: the persona keeps the content it holds until step 4 (or step 6's recovery bring-up) acts on it.

- **Record write failure.** A write that fails before its rename leaves the old record intact. A write whose rename succeeded but whose directory sync failed (`DurableWriteUnsyncedError`) has the previous bytes written back, best effort. Either way one `reload-record-write-failed` line is logged, the applied state is not swapped, no step runs and the change stays pending.
- **Server-wide settings.** Step 1 records them; the server keeps its start-time values (`configInEffect`) until the next start, which runs the record (see [Next-launch and server-wide settings](#next-launch-and-server-wide-settings-bav2-sr-86)).
- **No effective change.** `applyStepsFor` selects no step except step 5, and that only when `configDirsChanged`. Step 1 still rewrites the record, so `config.json` and the record match byte for byte again, and `reload-noop` is logged.
- **Unchanged personas.** A persona whose declaration and credentials are unchanged is in no step's input: it is neither touched nor re-probed, and its connection, instance and held digest stay as they are.
- **Success.** After the last step, `reload-applied` is logged with the plan's counts. The pass then derives the state again against the new applied state; if nothing is pending, the pending file is deleted without a `reload-nothing-pending` line.
- **Shutdown.** When detection is stopped while a step runs, the pass ends after the apply.

#### Failures

- A pending-file write or delete that fails is logged once per episode and retried at the next pass. An episode ends at the next success or when the derived state changes, to nothing pending or to a different pending fingerprint: each new state logs its first failure, and a failure that keeps repeating within one state is logged only once. A write whose rename succeeded but whose directory sync failed is written again at the next pass.
- A delete whose unlink failed logs the cannot-remove line; the file stays, the logged state is unchanged and the delete is retried at the next pass, so `reload-nothing-pending` follows the removal that later succeeds.
- A delete whose unlink succeeded but whose directory sync failed (`DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError`) counts as removed: the pass logs one removed-but-unsynced line, then the removed variant of `reload-nothing-pending` when that is due, and moves to nothing pending. Nothing retries the sync (the file is gone), so the removal may not survive a crash; the next start's first pass removes any leftover.
- A pass that fails unexpectedly is logged once per episode (until a pass succeeds), and the timer carries on.

The lines are listed under Logging, [Reload lines](#reload-lines-bav2-sr-87-sr-103). Every line goes to the server log only; nothing about a pending change reaches Slack.

### Bring-up at apply (b.av2 SR-6.1)

Apply step 6 brings up each added persona, and the new half of each destructive modify, through `PersonaLifecycle.bringUp` (`src/persona-lifecycle.ts`), all of them at once, each through the per-persona serializer; the same operation, with `{ recovery: true }`, brings a credentials-broken persona up again (see [Recovery of a credentials-broken persona](#recovery-of-a-credentials-broken-persona-bav2-sr-64)). It is the start procedure for one persona (Server-Managed Startup step 5), with the same outcomes and the same lines, plus the storage check:

1. **Storage check.** `runPersonaStorageCheck` (`src/jsonl-persistence-check.ts`) runs Layer 1 of the JSONL-persistence safeguard (startup step 4a-quater) for this persona's effective `<claude_config_dir>/projects` root only. A root on `tmpfs`/`ramfs` is recorded in `startup-errors.log` as `jsonl-non-persistent`, and a warning goes to this persona's destination through the notifier, held until its client is validated; a root whose fstype can't be determined is recorded as `jsonl-persistence-check-warning`, with no notice. Layer 2 doesn't run: the launch's collision ladder handles any existing row. No other persona is checked or warned.
2. **Bring-up.** The bring-up controller's `bringUp(persona, applied.personas)`: the `persona-start` line, the credentials check (the persona's credentials file only, read as it stands; nothing is read from the environment), the working-directory check against the confirmed persona set, then Slack validation and connection. The credentials check also runs against the confirmed set (b.av2 SR-1.4, apply part): a credentials file whose real path is another applied persona's makes the persona credentials-broken (`persona-credentials-invalid`, `real path is also the credentials_file of …`), and the other persona is not touched.
3. **Launch.** Only when the outcome is `up`: one `up at apply — launching` line, then `spawnForPersona` with the persona's declaration and the start-time server-wide values, as a runtime launch, so a spawn failure takes the runtime spawn-failure path. Like the launch after a retry, it doesn't ask the relaunch gate again.

A `broken` or `retrying` persona is logged exactly as at start (Logging, [Persona bring-up lines](#persona-bring-up-lines-bav2-sr-103)) and posts nothing. A `retrying` one retries on its own timers, and the operation returns without waiting for them. A new persona with missing or invalid credentials is `broken` on its own: no running persona is touched, re-probed or restarted.

**Shutdown skip.** The operation checks the server's `shuttingDown` flag (`isShuttingDown`) twice, logging `not brought up — the server is shutting down` each time it is set:

- **When the operation starts:** no storage check, no bring-up, no launch; it resolves `broken` with no failures.
- **Right before the launch** (the bring-up returned `up`): no launch; it returns the `up` result.

A recovery bring-up checks it a third time, after its preparation (see [Recovery of a credentials-broken persona](#recovery-of-a-credentials-broken-persona-bav2-sr-64)) and before the bring-up: it resolves `broken` with no failures.

Shutdown has already cancelled every bring-up and stopped every connection, so nothing started here would be released before the exit.

**The detection pass waits for it.** The pass awaits the whole apply, so it is held by the slowest per-persona operation of any of the four kinds (teardown, in-place update, credentials change, bring-up), and by step 5's one template call. A credentials change waits at most for its first reconnect attempt, which the 10 s bounds on `auth.test` and `start()` cap, plus any work already queued for the persona. A bring-up waits for its launch, which can include pre-session dialog approval (up to 5 minutes, `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`) or, when a leftover `cscb_<key>` row is `working`, `waitForWaitingAndReconnect` (up to 10 minutes, `WAIT_FOR_WAITING_TIMEOUT_MS`, then possibly a fresh spawn; about a minute when the positive-idle rule shows the row stale, b.f2b). Unlike the start pass, the apply doesn't park such a launch. A teardown queues behind any restart or retry work already submitted for its key and then waits in `whenLaunchSettled` for a launch in flight, after cancelling that launch's wait for a `working` row, so the launch settles promptly (b.f2b). An in-place update does nothing slow itself, but it too queues behind any restart or retry work already submitted for its persona, such as a restart launch waiting up to 5 minutes on dialog approval; until it runs, steps 4–6 don't start. The next pass is armed only after that, so meanwhile no pending-file refresh runs and no other confirmation is processed. Shutdown is not blocked: `stopDetection()` doesn't wait for the pass (`src/reload-timer.ts`).

### Persona teardown (b.av2 SR-6.5)

Apply step 2 tears down each removed persona, and the old half of each destructive modify, through `PersonaLifecycle.teardown` (`src/persona-lifecycle.ts`), all of them at once, each through the per-persona serializer. It removes everything the server holds for the persona's key, with no graceful wind-down: nothing is paused, nothing waits for the instance to go quiet, and no exit timeout applies. A removed persona left the applied set at step 1, so meanwhile every launch, restart and retry path already refuses it (the applied-set guard, Server-Managed Startup step 5). The old half of a destructive modify is still applied; what the teardown does instead is under [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86).

**At submit (b.f2b).** When the teardown is submitted, before its serializer turn, it cancels the persona's launch wait for a `working` row (`cancelLaunchWait`).
