Neither the teardown nor work queued ahead of it for the key (a restart that joined that launch) then waits it out. A throw is logged as `<step> before its turn failed` and the teardown still runs. The old half of a destructive modify cancels more at submit (see [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86)).

The steps run in this order. A step that fails is logged and the rest still run:

1. Its bring-up retries are cancelled and its bring-up state forgotten (the controller's `cancel`), and its pending restart timer is cancelled (`cancelRestartTimer`).
2. Its launch's wait for a `working` row is cancelled (`cancelLaunchWait`, production `cancelWorkingRowWait`, b.f2b), as it already was when the teardown was submitted (see above); this call catches a wait that started since. Then a launch still in flight for it is awaited (`whenLaunchSettled`), so the kill never races a launch that is bringing the row up. This covers a start-pass launch, which runs outside the serializer. A cancelled wait types nothing and its launch settles promptly, so the teardown doesn't wait up to 10 minutes for it.
3. Its Slack connection is stopped (the connection manager's `stop`), then its inbound dedupe store is dropped (the routing's `forget`) and its ack-tracker entries are dropped (`forgetPersonaAcks`, injected as `forgetAcks`; other personas' entries stay), so a key added again starts clean. The connection goes first, because an event for the key would create a new store.
4. Its cached DM conversation is forgotten (`personaDestinations.forget`), its destination hold is cancelled (held notices dropped, retry timer cleared) and its pre-validation held notices are dropped (the notifier's `forget`). Nothing held is posted.
5. Its tracked permission prompts, their not-posted records and its wedge state are dropped (`forgetPersonaPrompts`). Their Slack messages stay as posted.
6. Its MCP session is dropped with `dropPersonaSessionAndKeepAlive`, the session drop of Disconnection, so the close schedules no restart.
7. Outside dry run, `cscb_<key>` is killed and then its agent-director row is deleted (`killPersonaInstance`, `deletePersonaInstance`, each through `withOutageDetection`); a row already gone counts as success. Its outage flags are reset silently (`resetAllToHealthy`) before these calls, so a success posts no all-clear, and again after them, so a flag a failing call raised doesn't outlive the persona. In dry run no agent-director call is made, and one dry-run line says so.
8. Its restart failure count and cap latch (`forgetFailures`), its health-check streak (`forgetDisconnectedStreak`) and its not-connected episode (`forgetNotConnectedEpisode`, b.f2b: the notice latch and the restart path's idle evidence for its `working` row) are forgotten.
9. Its reply-guard record is deleted and its launched-with directory forgotten (`teardownPersonaReplyGuard`). The Stop-hook launch pass (`stopHookLaunchPass`) then re-evaluates the persona's configured and launched-with config directories against the personas still applied, so the managed hook stays only where a remaining persona needs it.

Afterwards the instance and its conversation are gone and can't be resumed; a persona added again with the same key comes up fresh. Nothing is posted to Slack and nothing is recorded in `startup-errors.log`. A notice raised during the teardown, such as an outage onset from a failing kill, is dropped by the notifier, because the persona is no longer applied (for a destructive modify, the teardown drops it itself). A row that a failed delete leaves behind is removed by the next start's sweep (`absent persona`, Server-Managed Startup step 4); for a destructive modify, whose key stays applied, the step-6 launch meets it instead (see [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86)). The lines are under Logging, [Persona lifecycle lines](#persona-lifecycle-lines-bav2-sr-65-sr-61).

**Not the CLI's bot teardown, and not Graceful Shutdown.** The CLI's `teardownBots` (`stop --stop-bots`, `clean_restart`) is graceful: it pauses each persona's bot and polls its status, killing it only on escalation or timeout, and never deletes a row, so the next start can resume it. Graceful Shutdown leaves every instance and row in place. Persona teardown acts on one removed or destructively modified persona while the server keeps running, kills its bot without waiting and deletes its row.

### Destructive modify (b.av2 SR-8.6)

A persona whose `name` (same key), `credentials_file` path or `working_directory` changed is destructively modified: previewed `DESTRUCTIVE:`, then torn down in step 2 and brought up fresh in step 6. There is no separate path. Step 2 runs the [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65) on the persona as the previous configuration declares it (`destructiveOld`), and step 6 runs the [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61) on it as the confirmed configuration declares it (`destructiveNew`). Both run through the per-persona serializer, and steps 3–5 run between them.

**Fresh.** The teardown kills `cscb_<key>` and deletes its agent-director row, so the launch in step 6 finds no row and spawns a new instance: the conversation is not resumed. The bring-up reads the credentials file the new declaration names, as it stands, with a new bring-up entry. The persona then holds that file's content and digest, so nothing is pending for it until the file changes again. A delete that failed leaves the row behind; the launch then meets it through the collision ladder like any leftover row. A row whose `cwd` or `config_dir` label no longer matches the new declaration is replaced with a fresh spawn. For a `credentials_file` path change with the same working directory and config directory, the row matches both guards, so the step-6 launch may resume it (reconnect or resume, by its state) and the conversation is kept. This is an accepted limit. The next start's sweep doesn't remove such a row, since its key is applied.

**The key stays applied.** Unlike a removed persona, the old half's key is still in the applied set after step 1, so the applied-set guard does not stop work for it. The teardown therefore also does these things:

- **At submit.** Besides the launch-wait cancel every teardown makes, it cancels the persona's bring-up retries and its restart timer as soon as it is submitted, before its serializer turn. Work already queued ahead of it for the key (a restart timer's work, a bring-up retry and its launch) then finds the persona not up or cancelled and launches nothing. Otherwise that work would launch the new declaration, only for the teardown to kill it. From then until step 6, the relaunch gate and the up predicate answer not up for it.
- **After the agent-director calls.** Notices raised during the teardown (an outage onset from a failing kill) are held for a persona with no client, not dropped, so it drops them again (the notifier's `forget`). None reaches the new half's destination.

A destructively modified persona is listed in no other class of the change plan. Its other changed settings, in-place or next-launch, come with the fresh bring-up, and a credentials change needs the same path, so it never applies.

### In-place update (b.av2 SR-8.6)

Apply step 3 updates each persona modified in place (a change to `channels`, `delivery`, `permission_prompts`, `dm.enabled` or `dm.contact`) through `PersonaLifecycle.updateInPlace` (`src/persona-lifecycle.ts`), all of them at once, each through the per-persona serializer. It makes no lifecycle call: no kill, delete, launch, reconnect or MCP session drop, and no Slack or agent-director call. The persona's instance, agent-director row, Slack connection and Web API client, MCP session and conversation are kept, as are its restart, backoff, health, outage and retry state, its reply-guard record, its tracked prompts and its held notices.

**The new values already apply at step 1.** Step 1's swap replaced the persona's entry in `personaConfig`, and every consumer reads that entry at each use, so step 3 has no values left to push. The consumers, and when each sees the change:

- **Inbound delivery and DM intake** (Data Flow, Inbound step 4): from the next event, including the cross-persona `receive_all_shared` and unclaimed-channel results.
- **MCP tool posting scope** (`checkPersonaTarget`, Outbound step 3): from the next tool call.
- **Destination** (`persona-destination.ts`): from the next permission prompt, stuck-prompt warning or notice. The permission poller resolves the persona each tick, the notifier and its flush on each notice, and the destination hold on each attempt.
- **Archive evidence** (b.av2 SR-7.4, the `delivery: all` scope of the JSONL checks): at the persona's next launch. A launch already in flight during the apply finishes with the entry it started with.

**What step 3 does.** When the key is no longer applied (a later apply removed it first; its teardown owns it), it does nothing and logs nothing. Otherwise, when `permission_prompts`, `dm.enabled` or `dm.contact` changed, it forgets the persona's cached DM conversation (`personaDestinations.forget`), so the next post to a `dm` destination opens the DM with the current contact through `conversations.open`. The cache also re-checks the contact on each use, so this is a cheap second guard. It then logs one line naming the changed settings (Logging, [Persona lifecycle lines](#persona-lifecycle-lines-bav2-sr-65-sr-61)). It never rejects, and dry run behaves the same.

**A persona that is not up.** A `retrying` or `broken` persona gets the same update: its cache entry is forgotten when one of those three settings changed, and the line is logged. Its bring-up state and retries are not touched; when it comes up, it serves with the new values.

**Posted and held items.** Prompts already posted stay where they were posted and stay answerable, including a DM prompt after DMs are turned off (b.av2 SR-5.1 exception; see Permission Relay, [In-memory state](#in-memory-state-sr-1)). Nothing held is dropped or moved: a prompt the destination hold is holding is derived again on a later tick and goes to the new destination, and each held notice's retry re-reads the destination, so it goes to the new one too (Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures)).

### Credentials change (b.av2 SR-8.6 step 4)

Apply step 4 applies each confirmed change to a persona's credentials content, at the same path, through `PersonaLifecycle.reconnectCredentials` (`src/persona-lifecycle.ts`), all of them at once, each through the per-persona serializer (b.av2 SR-6.6). The operation calls the bring-up controller's `changeCredentials` (`src/persona-bringup-controller.ts`) over the connection manager's `reconnectCredentials` and `replaceRetryTokens` (`src/persona-connections.ts`). It touches no other persona and makes no agent-director call. When the server is shutting down it logs `credentials change not applied — the server is shutting down` and does nothing.

**State is decided when it runs.** The controller reads the persona's bring-up state again. A persona credentials-broken by now resolves `{ kind: 'credentials-broken' }` and is left to step 6's recovery bring-up. A persona that is neither up nor retrying, or is unknown, cancelled or no longer applied, is skipped.

**The new file is checked first.** It is read once through the same local check a bring-up runs (`checkPersonaLocalSteps`), against the applied set as it stands after step 1. When it is missing, unreadable or invalid, or its real path is another applied persona's credentials file (b.av2 SR-1.4, apply part), nothing changes: the persona keeps its connection or its retry content, one `persona-credentials-change-failed` line is logged, and the change stays pending (b.av2 SR-8.3).

**An up persona is reconnected.** The new connection opens first and the old one closes only once the new one is up (see the `persona-connections.ts` entry of the Module Map). The persona stays `up` throughout, so its MCP session is not dropped, its instance is not touched and nothing is launched.

**A persona held for its `claude_config_dir` is retrying.** It has no connection (bug b.g57; see Config-dir-unresolvable under [Server-Managed Startup](#server-managed-startup-sr-11-event-1) step 5), so it takes the new tokens and digest as a retrying persona does, opens no connection, and connects with them once the directory resolves. Its cached DM conversation is forgotten when it takes the new content. A hold that starts while an up persona's reconnect is in flight or retrying closes that reconnect with the connection; the persona keeps the new content and connects with it on its return.

**Held and live content.** The controller keeps two things per persona: the held content (tokens and `credentialsDigest`, what the reload detection tick compares against, so it decides whether the change is pending) and the live content (`live`, what the persona's own connection uses). Both are set at bring-up. Right before a swap, at the first attempt or later, both become the new file's. While the new connection retries, the held content is the new file's and `live` stays the old. A refusal, at once or later, holds `live` again, so the change is pending again. The first attempt decides what the persona holds:

| First attempt | Connection | Held content (`credentialsDigest`) | Still pending | Line |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| New connection up | The new one is in use; the old one is closed | The new file's | No | `reconnected with its changed credentials; its instance and MCP session are kept` |
| Slack refused a new token | The old one stays in use; the new clients are discarded | The live content (what its own connection uses) | Yes | `persona-credentials-change-failed`, cause `bot_token refused by auth.test: Slack error <code>` or `app_token refused by the Socket Mode open: Slack error <code>` |
| Slack unreachable (a `start()` abandoned at 10 s included) | The old one stays in use; the new one retries on its own SR-3.2 schedule | The new file's | No: it is retrying with it | `its changed credentials cannot reach Slack yet; the new connection retries and the current one stays in use` |

Each line is worded from the persona's state after the attempt. When the persona's own connection was refused while the attempt ran (it is credentials-broken now, for example after a Web API call refused its bot token), it is not said to keep that connection:

- A refused change's `persona-credentials-change-failed` line ends `it stays broken by its credentials, and the change stays pending`.
- An unreachable one logs `its changed credentials cannot reach Slack yet; the new connection retries, and it stays broken by its credentials until that connection is in use`.
- A swap logs `reconnected with its changed credentials and up again; its instance is kept`, and the persona is launched as below.

A reconnect left retrying ends later in one of four ways:

- **It swaps.** The new connection comes into use and the reconnected line is logged (`…; its instance and MCP session are kept`, or `… and up again; its instance is kept` for a persona credentials-broken meanwhile), beside the connection manager's cleared line.
- **It is refused while the persona is still up on the old connection.** The controller holds `live` again, so the change is pending again (and so is an earlier change whose retrying reconnect this one replaced), and logs `persona-credentials-change-failed` ending `the current connection stays in use, and the change stays pending`. `live` is what the connection uses when the refusal lands, which a later swap may have changed since the change was confirmed.
- **It is refused after the old connection was itself refused meanwhile** (the persona is credentials-broken). The persona stays credentials-broken, holding the confirmed content, so nothing is pending, and the refusal is logged as `persona-credentials-refused`.
- **It is cancelled** by a later confirmed credentials change or by the persona's connection being stopped (a teardown, a recovery bring-up, shutdown). Its open `persona-slack-unreachable` episode ends with a cleared line saying why (Logging, [Persona bring-up lines](#persona-bring-up-lines-bav2-sr-103)).

A persona that was credentials-broken meanwhile and whose pending reconnect then swaps is `up` again. It is launched through its launch path's collision ladder, logging `up after its confirmed credentials change — launching`.

**A retrying persona retries with the new content.** A persona that is Slack-unreachable, directory-broken or still on its first attempt takes the new tokens and digest at once, as both held and live content, so nothing stays pending. A Slack-unreachable one gets them through `replaceRetryTokens`; a directory-broken one holds them for when its directory retry reaches Slack. It logs `it retries its bring-up with its changed credentials`. With a locally bad new file, it keeps retrying with its current content, and the `persona-credentials-change-failed` line ends `it keeps retrying with its current credentials, and the change stays pending`.

**The DM destination is forgotten before the swap.** The persona's cached DM conversation is forgotten (`personaDestinations.forget`) in the controller's `beforeSwap` hook, which the manager runs right before every swap, at the first attempt or later, while the persona still reports its old connection. So when the persona is reported up on the new connection and the notifier flushes its held notices, none goes to the old app's DM; the new credentials may belong to another app, with another DM. A throw is logged as `forgetting its cached DM conversation failed: <thrown value>`, and the swap goes ahead.

### Recovery of a credentials-broken persona (b.av2 SR-6.4)

While the server runs, a credentials-broken persona recovers only through a confirmed change to its credentials file. Credentials-broken is `isCredentialsBroken`: outcome `broken` with a local credentials cause (missing, unreadable, invalid or a real-path collision), or with Slack's refusal (`persona-credentials-refused`). Apply step 6 brings up each credentials-changed persona that step 4 did not reconnect because it was credentials-broken, whether or not an instance of it runs, through `PersonaLifecycle.bringUp(persona, applied, { recovery: true })`.

The operation checks the state again when it runs. A persona no longer credentials-broken (step 4 left it here because it was, and a pending reconnect of an earlier change has brought it up since) logs `not brought up again — it is not broken by its credentials now, so its changed credentials are applied as a credentials change` and gets the confirmed change as step 4 would have applied it: the credentials change body runs directly in the same serializer turn (not through the serializer again), with its usual lines, and the operation resolves the persona's outcome after it. Only when that change finds the persona credentials-broken again does the recovery go ahead. Otherwise it logs `broken by its credentials and its credentials file changed — bringing it up again` and clears what the broken bring-up left:

1. Its bring-up state and timers are cancelled (the controller's `cancel`).
2. Its connection is stopped (the manager's `stop`). The bring-up then builds a fresh connection entry with a fresh episode tracker, so a new refusal is logged rather than swallowed by the old episode.
3. Its cached DM conversation is forgotten.

A step that fails logs `<step> before bringing it up again failed: <thrown value>`, and the rest still run. The server's `shuttingDown` flag is checked again once these steps are done, so a shutdown that began meanwhile connects and launches nothing (`not brought up — the server is shutting down`). Then comes the same procedure as [Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61): storage check, bring-up (a new `persona-start` line, the file read as it stands and checked against the confirmed set) and, when up, the launch. The instance and agent-director row were kept while it was down (b.av2 SR-6.4), so the launch reaches them through the collision ladder and the history is kept. A file that is still bad leaves the persona broken with the new cause logged. It then holds the new file's digest, so nothing is pending until the file changes again.

### Template refresh (b.av2 SR-8.6 step 5)

Apply step 5 refreshes the agent-director template once, through `PersonaLifecycle.refreshTemplate(applied)` and `refreshSlackChannelBotTemplate` (`src/agent-director-template.ts`). `applyStepsFor` selects it only when the change plan's `configDirsChanged` is true: the set of effective config directories (`effectiveClaudeConfigDirs`, compared lexically) differs between the applied and the confirmed personas. That also holds for a plan with no effective change. It runs after step 4, which settles once every reconnect's first attempt has settled, and before step 6. So a persona that step 6 brings up with a new directory launches from the refreshed template.

**Memory-read rules only.** The refresh overwrites the template with the params the boot install wrote (`installSlackChannelBotTemplate`'s `params`), with only the `allow` list derived afresh from the confirmed personas (`deriveMemoryReadAllowRules`, the boot derivation). The rules therefore keep the `projects/*/memory/**` scope and never cover a config-dir root. Every other field is kept, including `claude_args` with the start-time MCP config and system-prompt flags (the append file is not probed again), and `name`, `label`, `deny` and `relay_mode`. A changed server-wide setting behind those arguments takes effect at the next start. The refresh belongs to no persona, so it does not go through the serializer.

**Not fatal.** A rejection, either a typed agent-director error or any throw, is logged in one line and nothing else happens. No startup error is recorded, nothing is posted, no persona is touched, and the rest of the apply and later ticks run. Nothing retries it. The rules stay as last installed until a later confirmed change alters the config-dir set again, or until the next start's boot install. Meanwhile, a persona using a directory new to the set gets a permission prompt for each memory-note read. The boot install is different: a rejection there is fatal (Server-Managed Startup step 3). In dry run the refresh makes the same call the boot install makes. The lines are under Logging, [Persona lifecycle lines](#persona-lifecycle-lines-bav2-sr-65-sr-61).

### Next-launch and server-wide settings (b.av2 SR-8.6)

**`claude_config_dir` and `stop_hook_bootstrap`, own or inherited.** These get no lifecycle call at apply. Step 1's swap is all they need, and the running instance and its conversation are kept. The persona's next launch (a spawn, resume, restart or retry launch) reads the new value from the applied set. For `claude_config_dir`, that covers `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the pre-launch directory check, the `config_dir` label, the trust patch, the reply-guard steps and the transcript diagnosis. A changed directory has a different `config_dir` label, so the ladder's `config_dir` guard deletes the row and spawns fresh (SR-6.2), and the old history stays in the old directory. A new path to the same real directory keeps the label, and the persona resumes.

For `stop_hook_bootstrap`, the reply-guard record is written at the persona's next launch (`preLaunchReplyGuard`). The apply rewrites no record, and a neighbour's launch writes only its own. The apply does not run the per-directory install rule either: the next launch's launch pass does, over the new and previous directories.

**Inherited defaults.** The top-level `claude_config_dir` and `stop_hook_bootstrap` act only through the personas that inherit them. The loader has already resolved them into each persona, so a changed default takes effect at each inheriting persona's next launch, exactly as an own change does.

**Server-wide settings.** Step 1 writes them to `config.json.last-applied`. The server then runs `configInEffect(startTime, applied)` (`src/reload.ts`): the confirmed personas over every server-wide setting it started with. Running consumers keep their start-time values until the next start, which runs the record. These include the listener's `port` and `bind`, the intervals, timeouts and restart delay, `resume_enabled`, the cron paths, the template's server-wide arguments, and `ack_reaction`, `reply_chunk_limit` and `reply_chunk_mode` (read through `getReplySettings()`). The exceptions are `stop_timeout` and `exit_timeout`. Only the CLI uses them, and it reads them from the record (`readAppliedPersonaConfig`), so once applied they apply to its next `stop` or `clean_restart`. The preview says so.

**Several changes at once.** A destructive change overrides everything else (see [Destructive modify](#destructive-modify-bav2-sr-86)). Otherwise the kinds combine. A persona can be modified in place (step 3), have its credentials content changed (step 4, after step 3) and have next-launch changes (its next launch).

### Per-persona serialization (b.av2 SR-6.6)

Lifecycle operations on one persona run strictly in sequence. Operations on different personas are independent, except that an apply finishes each step for every persona before the next step starts. The mechanism is a per-key promise chain, `createPersonaSerializer` in `src/persona-serializer.ts`. `server.ts` builds one instance at module scope (`personaLifecycle`) and hands its `run` to every user. `run(key, operation)` starts an operation only once every operation already submitted for that key has settled; a rejected operation doesn't stop the next one.

| Runs through the serializer | Doesn't |
|---|---|
| A fired restart timer's work: its shutdown and up checks, the liveness probe, reconnect, kill and launch (`RestartDeps.serialize`) | Scheduling a restart, the backoff, the cap, the `activeLaunches` guard and the human-trigger clamp |
| Each bring-up retry attempt with the launch it triggers: a directory re-check with its Slack step and launch, and the launch after a Slack retry reached `up` | The start bring-up pass and its launches: the detection tick, the only source of applies, is armed only after that pass returns. A launch the pass parked on a `working` row (b.f2b) may outlive it; a teardown cancels its wait and waits for it to settle (`whenLaunchSettled`) |
| The apply's teardown (step 2), in-place update (step 3), credentials change (step 4) and bring-up with its launch (step 6, recoveries included; a recovery that finds the persona no longer credentials-broken runs the credentials change body directly in the same turn, never through the serializer again); the launch after a pending credentials reconnect swapped in for a refused connection; the network close of the socket detached when a Web API call refused the persona's bot token (submitted, never awaited, bug b.ujn) | The connection manager's own Slack retries and reopens, including the later attempts of a credentials reconnect left retrying; marking a persona credentials-broken on a Web API bot-token refusal, which happens at once so an operation holding the persona's turn cannot delay it |

`spawnForPersona`'s one-launch-in-flight rule stays inside the operations that launch.

**Checks run when an operation starts, not when it is submitted.** For a persona being torn down, this means:

- **Restart timer.** A timer still pending is cancelled by the teardown and never fires. Restart work already running or queued ahead of the teardown finishes first: its checks find the persona out of the applied set, so it launches nothing, and then the teardown runs.
- **Bring-up retry.** Retry work queued behind the teardown finds its entry cancelled and does nothing. Work running ahead of the teardown finishes first, and its launch is refused because the persona is no longer applied.
- **Start-pass launch.** It runs outside the serializer, so the teardown itself waits for it (`whenLaunchSettled`) before anything is killed. That includes a launch the pass parked in the background (b.f2b), which can still be waiting for a `working` row when the apply runs; the teardown cancels that wait first, so the launch settles promptly.

**Re-entrancy rule.** An operation running for a key must never await `run` or `whenIdle` for the same key: both wait for the running operation, which would then wait for itself forever. Code that already runs inside a key's operation calls the next body directly, as the directory re-check calls its launch and the apply bring-up calls its launch. It may submit work for the same key without awaiting it, and may await operations for other keys.

### start command

`start` (CLI subcommand) has one prerequisite: the configuration file (`resolveServerConfigPath()`) or its last-applied record `config.json.last-applied` beside it must exist, else it prints `missing prerequisite: config.json not found at <config path>, and no config.json.last-applied at <record path>` and exits 1. With a record, `config.json` may be absent, since the server starts from the record (Server-Managed Startup step 2a). No environment variable stands in for either file. It reads no Slack token (b.av2 SR-10.2): each persona's tokens are in its credentials file, which only the server reads. Neither file's contents are checked here; the server validates them at start and `start` reports that failure (below). It then **self-daemonizes**: the parent process spawns a detached background child, waits for its startup outcome and exits, leaving the child running as the server. Detection of parent vs. child is via the `_CLI_DAEMON_CHILD` env marker.

The parent spawn (`CliDeps.spawnDaemon`) uses `detached: true` (child becomes its own session leader via `setsid`), `stdio: ['ignore', logFd, logFd]` (stdin from `/dev/null`, stdout/stderr to `STATE_DIR/server.log` — no inherited pipes), and `child.unref()`. This isolates the server from the launcher's session and process group, so killing the launching shell's group does not kill the server. The child re-execs `start` with the marker set, redirects logging to `server.log`, and calls `startServer()`.

**Early-failure report (b.av2 SR-1.7, SR-8.7).** Before the spawn the parent records `server.log`'s size. It then polls every `DAEMON_STARTUP_POLL_MS` (100 ms) for up to `DAEMON_STARTUP_WAIT_MS` (30 s), long enough for the startup gate, the start resolution and the template refresh that precede the PID file:

- The daemon exits, or cannot be launched: `[slack] Server failed to start (exit code N | killed by <signal> | could not be launched: …). From <log>:` followed by the last `DAEMON_FAILURE_LOG_LINES` (20) non-empty lines this run appended to `server.log` (read from the start if the log shrank, i.e. rotated), or `…; it wrote nothing to <log>`; exit 1. These lines carry the server's own fatal reason, such as the `[slack] Fatal: configuration error — …` line with the conversion error for a pre-persona file, the `[slack] Fatal: last-applied record error — …` line with its deletion hint, the `reload-record-write-failed` line, or the PID-conflict error when a server is already running. Server log lines never carry a token.
- `STATE_DIR/server.pid` names the child's PID (the server writes it once it is listening, after the start resolution): `[slack] Server starting in background (PID n)`, exit 0.
- The wait expires: `[slack] Server is still starting in the background (PID n) after 30s — its log is <path>`, exit 0. The daemon is never signalled.

The clock, sleep and log reads (`now`, `sleep`, `fileSize`, `readFileFrom`) are `CliDeps` seams, as are the log-file operations: `openLogAppend` / `closeFd` open and close the parent's `server.log` descriptor, and `initLogging` performs the child's redirect (and `clean_restart`'s).

**Marker-leak guard (b.acn)**: the `_CLI_DAEMON_CHILD` marker alone is untrusted, because it can leak from an operator wrapper script (e.g. `start-all.sh` / `cscb-up`) into the launching environment. If leaked, the parent daemonize branch would be skipped and the server would run in-place inside the launcher's session — dying when that process group is killed. A genuine daemon child is always a session leader, so `start` requires the marker **and** session-leadership to trust it. `isSessionLeader()` reads the session id (field 4 after `comm`) from `/proc/self/stat` on Linux and compares it to the pid; it fails open (treats the process as a leader) where `/proc` is unavailable, preserving prior behavior on non-Linux platforms. A set-but-not-leader marker is treated as a leak: it is deleted and `start` falls through to the parent path to re-detach properly.

### stop command

`stop` (CLI subcommand) sends SIGTERM to the running server via the PID file at `STATE_DIR/server.pid`. If the process does not exit within `stop_timeout` seconds (default 30 s), a SIGKILL is sent. `stop_timeout` comes from the configuration the server runs (`readAppliedPersonaConfig`): the last-applied record when it exists, else `config.json`; when that configuration cannot be loaded (a pre-persona file or an unreadable or invalid record included), `stop` logs one line saying so, with the cause, and uses 30 s. A brief 2 s confirmation poll (`STOP_KILL_WAIT_MS`) follows the SIGKILL. Both waits check every `STOP_POLL_MS` (100 ms) through the `CliDeps` clock and sleep seams (`now`, `sleep`), so tests drive them with a fake clock. Stale PID files (process no longer running) are silently removed. A non-zero exit from this phase causes `stop` to exit 1.

Plain `stop` leaves the managed bots running (they are meant to survive server restarts — see [Graceful Shutdown](#graceful-shutdown-sr-11-event-11)). The **`--stop-bots`** flag (b.4dk) additionally exits the bots gracefully, mirroring `clean_restart`'s order: the server daemon is stopped **first**, then the shared `teardownBots` closure runs over the persona set of the configuration the server runs, with its `exit_timeout`: the last-applied record's when it exists, else `config.json`'s (the same per-persona pause/poll/kill sequence on `cscb_<key>` as `clean_restart`, minus the restart phase). An edit to `config.json` that was never applied therefore does not change which personas are torn down. A config with no personas tears down nothing.

Stopping the server first prevents its `onsessionclosed`/`scheduleRestart` handler (`initPendingSession` in `src/server.ts`) from respawning a just-exited bot mid-teardown — the respawn would delete the bot's `ended` row and history before SIGTERM lands. `directorPause` is an agent-director client subprocess that needs no live CSCB daemon (SessionEnd hooks are wired by agent-director into the spawned claude process and call the AD binary), so teardown works fine after the server is down.

Before teardown runs, `stop --stop-bots` initializes the agent-director Client singleton via the non-exiting startup gate (`initClient` in `CliDeps`, b.qwo) — the same init the `clean_restart` algorithm covers in step 3 below (see there for the b.qps / `incident-2026-09-18-clean_restart.md` root cause). An AD-unreachable initialization or teardown failure is a **loud** `exit(1)`, not a swallowed skip; a config-load failure, a pre-persona file or an unreadable or invalid record included, is best-effort (logged, teardown skipped, but the server stop still succeeds, since the server is already down). A pause-timeout kill escalation does NOT guarantee an `ended` row — that residual case is recovered later by the dead-session `findMissing`→`resume` path.

### clean_restart (SR-11 Event 12)

`clean_restart` (CLI subcommand) stops the server daemon first, then concurrently pauses every managed Claude Code spawn via agent-director, then starts a fresh server. The stop-first ordering prevents the health-check poller and auto-restart logic from interfering with the teardown. It logs to `STATE_DIR/clean_restart.log` via `initLogging()`; its four fatal lines (`failed to load config:` in step 1, `agent-director initialization failed:` in step 3, `bot teardown failed — aborting restart:` in step 6, `start failed with exit code N` in step 8) are also written to the terminal's stderr, through the `console.error` captured before the redirect, so an operator sees why it exited without opening the log. If the redirect fails, every line stays on the terminal and a fatal line appears once. `CliDeps` exposes injectable `directorStatus`, `directorPause`, and `directorKill` adapters (production builds them with the exported `createDirectorOps(getClient)`, which calls `getClient().status/pause/kill` on each operation and maps only `ErrSpawnNotFound` to "no row") plus an optional `initClient` (b.qwo) that installs the AD Client singleton before any per-persona work — tests that pre-install a stub via `setClientForTests` omit it. Each adapter takes the persona's instance ID, `cscb_<key>` (`personaInstanceId`); no label lookup is involved. The per-persona teardown (steps 4–5 below, `teardownPersona`) runs for every persona at once inside a shared `teardownBots(personas, exit_timeout)` closure reused by `stop --stop-bots` (b.4dk). Teardown log lines name the persona as its JSON-quoted name with its key, e.g. `[slack] teardownBots: no spawn row for persona "Ops Bot" (key=ops_bot_5e2526f3) — skipping`.

Algorithm:

1. **Init logging + load config** — `initLogging()` redirects output to `clean_restart.log`. `loadConfig(resolveConfigPath())` (production: `readAppliedPersonaConfig` on the server's config path) provides the persona set and `exit_timeout` from the configuration the server runs: the last-applied record when it exists, else `config.json`. Config load failure is fatal, a pre-persona file included (the loader's conversion error), and so is a record that exists but cannot be read or validated (its deletion hint in the message); the CLI never falls back to `config.json` then, and never writes any reload file.
2. **Stop server daemon** — shells out to `claude-slack-channel-bots stop`, which sends SIGTERM and escalates to SIGKILL after `stop_timeout`.
3. **Init agent-director client** (b.qwo) — this short-lived CLI process does not run the server startup gate, so `getClient()` would otherwise throw. `initClient` (`CliDeps`) installs the Client singleton via the non-exiting `runStartupGate` variant. A gate failure is a distinct loud `exit(1)` — the restart never proceeds to teardown or `start` on top of bots it could not reach. Skipping this init was the b.qps / `incident-2026-09-18-clean_restart.md` root cause: the throw was swallowed and every bot reported a bogus "no spawn row — skipping".
4. **Per-persona precheck** — `directorStatus(cscb_<key>)` returns `null` only when the row is genuinely absent (`ErrSpawnNotFound` from `client.status`); "no spawn row — skipping" is printed only in that case. Terminal states (`ended`, `missing`) → skip; otherwise proceed. Any error reaching agent-director (connection refused, uninitialized client, binary unreachable) **propagates** — it is no longer collapsed into a `null`/"no row". Such rejections surface after the parallel loop as a loud aggregate error (see step 6).
5. **Pause + poll** — `directorPause(cscb_<key>)` is called; if it rejects, escalate immediately to `directorKill(cscb_<key>)`. After a successful pause, `directorStatus(cscb_<key>)` is polled with exponential backoff (100 ms start, capped at 2 s, total budget `exit_timeout`). The first poll observing `null` / `ended` / `missing` is treated as clean exit. If the budget is exhausted, `directorKill(cscb_<key>)` force-terminates the spawn. On both the escalation and timeout kill paths the kill's outcome decides the persona's fate (b.dnt): a kill failure other than the benign already-gone race (`ErrSpawnNotFound`) is itself a teardown failure surfaced as a rejection in step 6. Teardown is **kill-but-never-delete**: rows are paused/killed but never deleted, preserving each row's `claude_session_id` so the next start can resume with context.
6. **Loud AD-unreachable failure** (b.qwo, b.dnt) — after the parallel loop, any settled rejection (an agent-director connectivity error raised by a precheck in step 4, a poll-loop `directorStatus` in step 5, or (b.dnt) an escalation- or timeout-path `directorKill` that failed with anything other than the benign already-gone race, never a normal terminal-row skip and never an `ErrSpawnNotFound` already-gone kill, both of which resolve quietly) is logged and re-thrown as an aggregate error (`teardown incomplete for N persona(s); other personas may already have been paused or killed; rows are never deleted, safe to retry`). `clean_restart` catches it and `exit(1)`, aborting the restart **before** starting a new server. A silent no-op teardown is thus impossible.
7. **Start new server daemon** — shells out to `claude-slack-channel-bots start`. Reached only if teardown completed without an AD-unreachable failure.
8. **Exit** — a non-zero exit code from `start` is propagated and the process exits with that code, logged as `[slack] clean_restart: start failed with exit code N`. Because `start` now waits for the daemon's startup outcome, a server that fails early (for example on a config error) makes this exit 1; the reason is in `server.log`, not `clean_restart.log`.

Per-persona polling cadence (100 ms → 2 s cap, `exit_timeout` total) is unrelated to the SR-2.1 permission poller's `agent_director_poll_interval_ms`.

### credentials command (b.av2 SR-12)

`credentials <persona>` (CLI subcommand) writes one persona's credentials file from the operator's terminal; it is the setup wizard's credentials command. It loads the configuration file as it stands (`loadConfigFile`, production `loadPersonaConfig`), never the last-applied record: a persona the wizard has just declared is found before any confirmation, and on a running server the reload tick previews it as added but unable to come up (`credentials file does not exist`) until the file is written, then previews it afresh (the fingerprint covers every referenced credentials file). It resolves the argument by name or key (`resolvePersonaTarget`), prints `Credentials file of persona "<name>" (key=<key>): <path>`, and runs `runCredentialsScript(credentials_file)` (production: `bash <CREDENTIALS_SCRIPT_PATH> <path>` with stdio inherited, SIGINT and SIGQUIT ignored in the CLI process while it runs), exiting with its status. Exit 2 with `CREDENTIALS_USAGE` unless given exactly one non-empty argument; exit 1 with the loader's error when the configuration file can't be loaded, or with the declared personas listed when none has that name or key (the argument is never printed, in case a token was typed there). No agent-director, server or record access. The tokens are read by the script at its own no-echo prompts and never pass through the CLI process or any argv.

### Graceful Shutdown (SR-11 Event 11)

On `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT`, the shutdown handler:
1. Flips the `shuttingDown` flag (idempotent guard).
2. Calls `stopPermissionPoller()` to stop the SR-2.1 tick.
3. Calls `stopHealthCheck()`, stops the reload detection tick (`reloadController.stopDetection()`, so no further pending-file check runs), stops the cron scheduler, then `cancelAllRestartTimers()`, the bring-up controller's `cancelAll()` (every pending directory re-check; a re-check already running does nothing more, and no launch after a retry starts), the destination hold's `cancelAll()` (every persona's retry timer is cleared and its held notices are dropped, not posted, with one `shutting down` line per persona that had notices held, see Logging, [Persona destination lines](#persona-destination-lines-bav2-sr-103); an attempt already in flight still completes but no longer changes the hold's state; held prompts need nothing, since they live in agent-director and are derived again after the next start) and `stopAllKeepAliveTimers()`. The manager's Slack retries stop with `stopAll()` in step 5.
4. Stops the HTTP server, drains pending + active MCP transports. Each aborted stream reaches `scheduleRestart`, which arms no timer during shutdown (Auto-Restart step 1, shutdown check).
5. Closes every persona's Slack connection through the connection manager (`stopAll()`), logging `[slack] Disconnecting persona Slack connections`.
6. Calls `closeClient()` inside a try/catch to release the agent-director Client handle. The library's `close()` is idempotent and never throws; the catch is belt-and-suspenders.
7. Removes the PID file.

The bots themselves are NOT killed or paused on shutdown — same as the prior behavior and consistent with SR-11 Event 11.

A confirmed apply already under way is not awaited: stopping detection doesn't wait for the pass. Its queued or running bring-ups at apply bring nothing up and launch nothing ([Bring-up at apply](#bring-up-at-apply-bav2-sr-61), shutdown skip). Its teardowns still run to completion; one whose agent-director kill and delete run after `closeClient()` logs them as `… failed` teardown lines, accepted noise before the exit.

## SR-11 Substitution Table

State transitions and event triggers follow `t1.qfc.bg` SR-11 in full. Where the predecessor SRD references a CLI verb, this implementation calls the equivalent typed library method:

| `t1.fp3.63` CLI verb | This implementation's library equivalent |
|---|---|
| `claude-director spawn` | `client.spawn(SpawnParams)` |
| `claude-director resume` | `client.resume({ claude_instance_id })` |
| `claude-director kill` | `client.kill({ claude_instance_id })` |
| `claude-director pause` | `client.pause({ claude_instance_id })` |
| `claude-director delete` | `client.delete({ claude_instance_id: [...] })` |
| `claude-director list --label/--state` | `client.list({ label, state })` |
| `claude-director get` | `client.get({ claude_instance_id })` |
| `claude-director status` | `client.status({ claude_instance_id })` |
| `claude-director send-keys` | `client.sendKeys({ claude_instance_id, text })` |
| `claude-director decide` | `client.decide({ claude_instance_id, decision })` |
| `claude-director version` | `client.version({})` |
| `claude-director find-missing` | `client.findMissing({})` — driven through the shared memoized `reconcileMissingSweep` (b.m4r) from the dead-session pre-resume path (b.4dk, `reconcileMissingFirst`) and, via `sweepDeadTmuxChannel`, from the tick/restart escalate-dead path (dead-tmux self-recovery, including a `working` row whose tmux session is gone, b.d61, and an `ask_user` or `check_permission` row whose tmux session is gone, b.jdc). A prompt row also drives it directly: the collision ladder when its tmux session is gone, and the restart path's deferrals on it once they have run for 10 min (b.jdc). Also runnable by operators as a periodic sweep (SR-5.4). |

## Configuration

### config.json (`SLACK_STATE_DIR`/config.json, default ~/.claude/channels/slack/config.json)

The server's configuration is the persona config described in the next section: a `personas` array plus the server-wide settings below. The operator edits `config.json` in the state directory (`resolveServerConfigPath()`), but what a start runs is the last-applied record beside it (see Reload files below) whenever one exists; `config.json` is read at start only when there is no record, and is then validated, recorded and applied (b.av2 SR-1.7, SR-8.7; Server-Managed Startup step 2a). No environment variable supplies the bind address, port or anything else. With no record, a `config.json` that cannot be used stops the start with one line, `[slack] Fatal: configuration error — <message>`, and exit code 1, before any port is bound, the PID file is written, a Slack connection opens or anything is spawned:

- a missing file: `The configuration file "<absolute path>" does not exist. The server requires the configuration file to start.` (`cannot be read`, with the errno code in parentheses when there is one, in place of `does not exist` for any other read failure: `EISDIR` for a directory, `not a regular file` for a FIFO, socket or device, which is never opened for a blocking read);
- a pre-persona file (top-level `routes`, `default_route` or `default_dm_session`): the loader's conversion message, unchanged (see Pre-persona rejection below);
- malformed JSON: `loadPersonaConfig: malformed JSON in "<path>" at line <L>, column <C>.`, giving the 1-based position of the first invalid character and none of the file's text;
- unknown keys (b.av2 SR-10.3): an unknown key's name is shown only when it is a plain setting name, matching `ECHOABLE_KEY_NAME_RE` (`/^[A-Za-z_]{1,48}$/`: ASCII letters and underscores, up to 48 characters); any other name, including one with a digit such as `channels2`, is counted, not shown, since it could be a pasted token (`describeUnknownKeys`). The running server's detection tick writes loader errors into `config.json.pending` and `server.log` every 5 s, so an echoed key name would leak there. The forms are `…unknown top-level field(s) in config.json: "chanels".`, `…: 1 field whose name is not shown (it is not a plain setting name, so it could be a pasted secret).` and `…: "chanels", plus 2 fields whose names are not shown (they are not plain setting names, so they could be pasted secrets).`; persona entries, `dm` and `channels[j]` use the same wording (`unknown field(s) in the persona entry: …`, `… in dm: …`, `… in channels[<j>]: …`);
- any other validation failure: the loader's error, unchanged.

The CLI (`stop`, `stop --stop-bots`, `clean_restart`) takes its settings and persona set from the same configuration the server runs, through `readAppliedPersonaConfig`: the record when it exists, else `config.json`. `start` only checks that one of the two files exists. See the command sections under Session Lifecycle.

Server-wide settings (the persona entry keys are in the next section):
- `bind` — HTTP server bind address (default: 127.0.0.1)
- `port` — HTTP server port (default: 3100)
- `session_restart_delay` — seconds before auto-restarting dead sessions (default: 60, 0 = disabled)
- `health_check_interval` — seconds between health-check polls (default: 120, 0 = disabled)
- `exit_timeout` — seconds `clean_restart` and `stop --stop-bots` wait for a Claude session to exit cleanly before force-killing it (default: 120)
- `stop_timeout` — seconds the `stop` command waits after SIGTERM before escalating to SIGKILL (default: 30)
- `mcp_config_path` — path to MCP config file for Claude launch (default: ~/.claude/slack-mcp.json)
- `append_system_prompt_file` — optional path to a file appended to every managed session's system prompt via `--append-system-prompt-file`; missing file silently skipped
- `system_prompt_mode` — controls whether `append_system_prompt_file` is applied (default: `"append"`; valid: `append`, `none`). `"append"` passes `--append-system-prompt-file` to Claude when launching sessions; `"none"` skips the flag entirely so only `CLAUDE.md` is used
- `cozempic_prescription` — cozempic cleaning intensity used before `--resume` launches (default: `"standard"`; valid: `gentle`, `standard`, `aggressive`); has no effect if cozempic is not installed
- `message_archive_db` — optional path to the SQLite message archive (`src/message-archive.ts`); absent means no archive
- `agent_director_poll_interval_ms` — permission-poller interval in milliseconds (default: 1000; 200 to 3 600 000). The old `claude_director_poll_interval_ms` name is rejected with a rename message
- `claude_config_dir` — optional path to a Claude on-disk config directory used for managed sessions; when set, launches carry `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` so the persona authenticates against a specific account. `~` is expanded and the path is resolved to absolute. A persona's own `claude_config_dir` overrides this top-level value. When neither is set, Claude's own default applies.
- `resume_enabled` — boolean (default: `true`). When `false`, the session manager skips `--resume` entirely and always performs a fresh launch, even when a stored session ID exists. Use this to work around versions of Claude Code that crash on `--resume` (e.g. the v2.1.120 "sandbox required but unavailable" regression). Affects both startup (Server-Managed Startup step 5) and auto-restart (Auto-Restart step 5).
- `stop_hook_bootstrap` — boolean (default: `true`). Whether a persona gets the Slack Reply Guard reminder. The top-level value defaults to `true` when absent; a non-boolean is rejected at either scope. A persona's own `stop_hook_bootstrap` overrides the top-level value. The effective value applies per persona, at its next launch: it is written to the persona's record `reply-guard/<key>` before each spawn or resume, and the guard reminds only when that record reads `true`. It also feeds the install rule of step 4a-ter, which keeps the CSCB-managed Stop hook in `<effective_claude_config_dir>/settings.json` while any persona counting toward that directory is enabled.
- `cron_table_path` — optional path to the cscb_cron crontable file (default: `<config dir>/crontab`, where `<config dir>` is the directory of the loaded config file). Anything that is not a non-empty string is rejected; `~` is expanded and the path is resolved to absolute, yielding a required string on the resolved config. Boot-fixed: only the crontable's contents hot-reload at runtime; its location never does.
- `cron_log_path` — optional path to the cscb_cron log file (default: `<config dir>/cron.log`). Same non-empty-string validation, tilde expansion, required-resolved-string, and boot-fixed notes as `cron_table_path`.
- `cron_log_max_bytes` — optional positive integer (>= 1); no default. Any value that is not a positive integer is rejected when set. When absent, log pruning is disabled — this bullet states the contract only; the prune mechanics ship in a later Epic.

The path keys are pointers only: schedules never live in `config.json` — the crontable file is the single source of truth.

### Reload files (config.json.last-applied, config.json.pending, config.json.apply)

Three files sit beside the configuration file, in the same directory, named by appending a suffix to its name (`reloadFilePaths` in `src/reload.ts`, b.av2 SR-8.1). The CLI never writes any of them.

| File | Contents | Written by | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config.json.last-applied` (the record) | A byte copy of the last applied configuration | The server's reload controller | At a start with no record, after `config.json` validates and before anything is applied; and rewritten with the confirmed bytes at step 1 of a confirmed apply (including a no-op). The server never deletes it |
| `config.json.pending` | A fixed header line, the pending state's fingerprint (`fingerprint: sha256:<hex>`), a blank line and the SR-8.4 preview: a header with the counts, then one line per affected persona and changed server-wide setting stating its effect, removals and destructive modifies starting `DESTRUCTIVE:`; or `no effective change`; or one `INVALID:` line with the validation error (see [The preview](#the-preview-sr-84)) | The server's reload detection tick | Present only while a change is pending: written when missing or stale, deleted when nothing is pending (see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83)) |
| `config.json.apply` | The operator's confirmation: `config.json.pending` renamed, so it carries that preview's fingerprint line | The operator (the server never writes it) | Consumed by the next detection pass: read, deleted, then applied when its fingerprint matches the current files, else `reload-stale-confirmation`. One made while the server was down is processed by the first pass after the start (see [Confirmation](#confirmation-sr-85)) |

- **The record is what runs.** Every start runs the record when it exists and reads `config.json` only when it doesn't (Server-Managed Startup step 2a). The record is validated in record mode, where two personas sharing a real path are a bring-up failure rather than a validation error. The CLI reads the record the same way for its settings and persona set.
- **Pending is not applied.** The pending file only reports; nothing in it is applied, and editing or deleting it changes nothing (the next pass rewrites it while the change is still pending). Only its fingerprint line matters once it is renamed to `config.json.apply`; the apply uses the files as the matching pass read them, never the preview text. It holds no token, credentials content or digest.
- **Durable writes.** Every server write of a reload file is atomic and durable (`durableWriteFileSync` in `src/atomic-write.ts`): a uniquely named temporary file beside the target, fsync, rename over the target, then fsync of the directory. A reader sees the old bytes or the new, never a partial file, and the new bytes survive a crash once the write returns. A record write that fails stops the start (`reload-record-write-failed`), including one whose rename succeeded but whose directory sync failed (`DurableWriteUnsyncedError`): that record is in place and the next start runs it, but it is not known to be durable, so the start is refused. At a confirmed apply the same failures apply nothing, and after a failed directory sync the previous record is written back (see [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86)). The pending file and the confirmation are deleted with the matching durable delete (`durableUnlinkSync`: unlink, then fsync of the directory); a delete whose directory sync failed (`DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError`) counts as removed. A pending file that reappears after a crash is removed by the next start's first pass; a confirmation that reappears is processed by it.
- **Size cap.** This is the one full statement of the cap; other sections refer here. None of these files, nor `config.json` or a credentials file, is read past 64 KiB (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES`), with one exception below. The cap lives only in the shared readers `readPersonaConfigBytes` (`config.ts`) and `readCredentialsFile` (`persona-credentials.ts`), so the start, the bring-up, the CLI and the detection tick judge one file the same way (the same digest or unreadable marker). A larger file is refused on its `fstat` size before any read, and the read itself is bounded (`readFdAtMost`, at most one byte over the limit), so a file that grows after the stat is refused too and never read in full. A larger file counts as unreadable: a record refuses the start, a `config.json` is an `INVALID` candidate (or refuses a start without a record), a confirmation is stale, and a credentials file is `persona-credentials-unreadable` with the unreadable digest marker. The read fails with code `EFBIG` (`FILE_TOO_LARGE_CODE`), worded `is larger than the 64 KiB limit` (`configReadFailurePredicate`). The server's own `config.json.pending` can exceed the cap for a very large single change (hundreds of personas renamed or credentials files changed at once) even while `config.json` is under it: each pass then finds it unreadable and writes it again, and its rename to `config.json.apply` is refused as stale (`is larger than the 64 KiB limit`); the fix is to split the change into smaller edits and confirm each.
  - **The one exception: the file guard's read.** `credentialsFilesToProtect` (the SR-5.2 file-send guard, reached through `protectedCredentialsFiles()`) reads `config.json` with `readPersonaConfigBytes(path, fs, { uncapped: true })`, which skips both size checks and reads the whole file. It must, so a credentials path named only in an oversized, unapplied `config.json` stays protected. The read is still opened and stat'ed first, so a directory, FIFO, socket or device is refused, never read. Its bytes feed only `referencedCredentialsPaths` and are never recorded, applied or logged. No other caller passes `uncapped`.
- **Recovery.** A record that cannot be read or validated stops the start, and the logged line says that deleting the record makes the next start apply `config.json` as it stands.

### Persona config

The persona loader in `src/config.ts` (`resolvePersonaConfig`, `parsePersonaConfigBytes` and `loadPersonaConfig`) accepts the persona shape of b.av2 SR-1. It is the only configuration shape; the server and the CLI both load it, from the last-applied record or `config.json` (see Reload files). The operator reference, with a complete example that `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` loads through this loader, is README.md's [Personas (config.json)](../README.md#personas-configjson); this section describes the loader's rules, not the operator's steps.

**Entry points and purity (b.av2 SR-13.1).** `resolvePersonaConfig(raw, configDir, home)` parses, defaults and validates an already-parsed value. It never opens, reads, creates or writes a file and never requires a path to exist; the only file-system access is resolving the real paths of persona working directories and credentials files for the cross-persona rules below. `parsePersonaConfigBytes(bytes, source, configDir, options)` is the one JSON-parse path: it parses the exact bytes that were read and delegates to `resolvePersonaConfig`, naming `source` in errors; the reload controller validates the record and `config.json` through it. `loadPersonaConfig(path, home?)` reads the file once and delegates to it; it never writes, renames or converts the file. Nothing runs at import.

**Record mode (b.av2 SR-1.5, record-start part).** With `record: true` (the last-applied record at a start, and the CLI's read of it) every rule below runs except the shared-real-path rejection. At a start from the record such a collision is a bring-up failure of each persona involved instead (Server-Managed Startup step 2a).

**Top level.** A required `personas` array, which may be empty, plus the server-wide settings. The server-wide settings are the ones listed in the `config.json` section above, with the meaning, default and validation given there, including path resolution for the top-level path keys. Unknown top-level keys are rejected, named only when they are plain setting names (see the unknown-keys message above), and the renamed poll-interval key `claude_director_poll_interval_ms` gets a rename message naming `agent_director_poll_interval_ms`. Three more server-wide keys, the reply settings, are listed here; they drive the inbound ack step and `reply` chunking (see Data Flow) through `getReplySettings()` in `server.ts`, at their start-time values.

| Key | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ack_reaction` | non-empty string | absent | Emoji name of the acknowledgement reaction. Absent means no acknowledgement. |
| `reply_chunk_limit` | positive integer | `4000` | Largest reply chunk, in characters. |
| `reply_chunk_mode` | `length` or `newline` | `newline` | How a long reply is split. |

**Persona entry.** Each element of `personas` is an object with these keys:

| Key | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name` | non-empty string, required | — | Persona identity; the persona key is derived from it by `src/persona-identity.ts`. No format rule (b.av2 SR-1.2): any non-empty string loads (see Names below). |
| `credentials_file` | path, required | — | The persona's credentials file. |
| `working_directory` | path, required | — | The instance's working directory. |
| `channels` | array of channel entries | none | Channels the persona is in. The same channel ID may not appear twice in one persona. |
| `dm.enabled` | boolean | `false` | The DMs switch. Inbound: on, a DM to the persona's app is delivered to its session with `via` `dm`; off, it is dropped with a `persona-dm-dropped` line (see Data Flow, Inbound). Outbound: on, the persona's tools may also target a `D…` DM conversation, and `reply` may target a user ID, which it opens with `conversations.open` on the persona's client before posting; off, every DM and user-ID target is refused with no Slack call, so the persona never opens or posts in a DM (see Data Flow, Outbound). It is read at each tool call. Exception: a permission prompt already posted in the persona's DM is still updated after DMs are turned off (see Permission Relay, [In-memory state](#in-memory-state-sr-1)). A group DM (`channel_type` `mpim`) is never delivered either way; an @mention in a group DM is decided by the channel rules instead. |
| `dm.contact` | Slack user ID | absent | The user who receives prompts and notices when `permission_prompts` is `dm`, in the persona's DM with that user (opened with `conversations.open` on the persona's client when none exists). |
| `permission_prompts` | `dm` or a channel ID, required | — | Where permission prompts and notices go: that channel, or, for `dm`, the persona's DM with `dm.contact` (see Permission Relay, Per-tick flow). |
| `claude_config_dir` | path | top-level `claude_config_dir` | Claude config dir for the persona. Absent at both levels means Claude's own default. |
| `stop_hook_bootstrap` | boolean | top-level `stop_hook_bootstrap` | Whether this persona gets the reply reminder; written to its `reply-guard/<key>` record at each launch and applied from its next launch. |

**Channel entry.** Both keys are required: `id`, a Slack channel ID matching `^[CG][A-Z0-9]+$`, and `delivery`, either `all` (every message) or `mentions` (only messages that mention the persona or hold `<!here>` / `<!channel>`, with or without a label; `<!everyone>` and user-group mentions do not count). `dm.contact` must match `^[UW][A-Z0-9]+$`, and a channel ID in `permission_prompts` uses the channel ID format.

**Path rule.** Persona paths (`credentials_file`, `working_directory`, per-persona `claude_config_dir`) must be absolute, exactly `~`, or start with `~/`. The tilde is expanded under the home directory passed in (the OS home when not given) and the result is made absolute. Relative paths are rejected.

**Names (b.av2 SR-1.2, SR-2.2).** A persona's name has no format rule beyond being a non-empty string. It and the key derived from it are shown exactly as written wherever a persona is named: log lines, previews, loader errors (`personaEntryStyle` renders the persona reference for any non-empty name; cross-persona errors echo each name involved), Slack notices, MCP tool results, agent-director labels, the instance ID and the tmux session name. Nothing masks or rejects a name that happens to look like a token.

**Unknown keys.** An unknown key in a persona entry, its `dm` object or a channel entry is rejected, naming the persona and the key when the key is a plain setting name (other names are counted, not shown; see the unknown-keys message under config.json). A token-like key such as `bot_token` is rejected the same way: credentials live only in the credentials file.

**Per-entry rejections.** On top of the type and format rules above, a persona entry is rejected when:

- `permission_prompts` is missing
- it has zero channels and DMs off (`dm.enabled` not `true`), so it can receive no messages
- `permission_prompts` names a channel the persona isn't in
- `permission_prompts` is `dm` but `dm.contact` is missing or DMs are off (both are named when both are wrong)

**Cross-persona rejections (b.av2 SR-1.5).** Once every entry has parsed, the loader rejects:

- a duplicate name or key. Each persona's name and key are compared, by exact string, with every other persona's name and key, so a name equal to another persona's derived key is a duplicate too. The error names the duplicated name or key.
- a key that starts with another persona's key (b.1ix follow-up; `checkPrefixRelatedKeys`), such as `dev` and `dev_2`, or `dev` and `"Dev Bot"` (key `dev_bot_<hash>`). agent-director 0.10.0's verbs (read-pane, send-keys, kill, the has-session inside resume) pass `slack_bot_<key>` to tmux bare, and tmux takes a session whose name starts with a bare target when none has that exact name, so once `slack_bot_dev` is gone they could act on `slack_bot_dev_2`. Every name derived from a key is fixed text around the whole key (`cscb_<key>`, `slack_bot_<key>`), so two derived names start one another only when their keys do, and this one rule covers them all. Checked after the duplicate rule, so equal keys keep its message, and in record mode too: the relation depends on the names alone. The error says which key starts with which, names both tmux sessions and, when `suggestNonPrefixingName` finds one, suggests a new name for the persona with the shorter key: the key plus `_main`, else `_main_2` to `_main_99`, the first whose key starts no other key, is the start of none and equals no other name or key. It finds none when another key is the start of every candidate (`d` beside `dev`, or `dev_main` itself), and the error then gives only the rule.
- a shared `working_directory`, then a shared `credentials_file`. Each is compared by `resolveRealPath` (real path, with a lexical `path.resolve` fallback when realpath fails for any reason), so a symlink to another persona's path, or `~/x` against its absolute form, is a duplicate. Only the same setting is compared: one persona's working directory against another's credentials file is not a collision. The error names the setting and the expanded path; when the two expanded paths differ (only a symlink can cause this), it shows both and the real path they share.

Each error names both personas, each as its array position plus the `src/persona-identity.ts` rendering. Personas are scanned in array order and the first colliding persona is reported against the earliest persona it collides with.

One persona listed in several channels loads: the rule is one working directory per persona, not one per channel.

**Pre-persona rejection (b.av2 SR-1.7).** If the top level has `routes` (in any shape), `default_route` or `default_dm_session`, the loader stops with the conversion message before any other check. The message names the key and says the configuration must be rewritten by hand as personas. The file is never written and nothing is auto-converted. The CLI uses the same loader. When it is the file a start or the CLI reads (there is no record), such a file stops the server's start, makes `clean_restart` exit 1 before stopping anything, and makes `stop` fall back to its default `stop_timeout` (with `--stop-bots`, bot teardown is skipped). A record with a pre-persona key is an invalid record: it stops the start with the deletion hint.

**Check order and errors.** The loader checks, in order: the value is an object, the pre-persona keys, unknown top-level keys, `personas` is present and an array, the server-wide settings, each persona entry in array order, the name/key rule, the prefix-related key rule, then the shared-path rule (`working_directory` before `credentials_file`). It reports the first violation only, so a per-entry error anywhere wins over any cross-persona one. Entry errors name the entry's position and, when `name` is a non-empty string, the persona as its JSON-quoted name with its key (the `src/persona-identity.ts` renderer), plus the setting. Errors don't echo rejected values: they may repeat only the persona name and IDs that already passed their format rule (e.g. a `permission_prompts` channel ID that isn't in the persona's channels, or a duplicated channel ID). The shared-path errors are the deliberate exception: they show the expanded, absolute paths, which have already passed the path rule. A malformed-JSON error gives only the line and column of the first invalid character (`src/json-position.ts`) and omits the parser's detail, which could quote file content. An unknown key's name is shown only when it matches `ECHOABLE_KEY_NAME_RE`; other unknown keys are counted, so a token pasted as a key name never reaches the start log, the pending file or `server.log`.

**Not checked at load time.** The loader doesn't read the credentials file and doesn't check that any directory or file exists. Those are bring-up checks: the credentials-file check is in `src/persona-credentials.ts` and the working-directory check is in `src/persona-bringup.ts` (see the Module Map). The server runs them at start, for each persona, as steps 1 and 2 of its bring-up (`checkPersonaLocalSteps` in `src/persona-start.ts`, run by the bring-up controller; see Server-Managed Startup step 5). A credentials failure leaves that persona `broken`; a working-directory failure leaves it `retrying`, and its directory is re-checked until usable. The `claude_config_dir` check (`checkPersonaConfigDir`, `src/persona-bringup.ts`) is a bring-up check too, run before the persona's Slack connection opens: an unresolvable directory holds the persona with no connection (`persona-config-dir-unresolvable`) until it resolves.

### Credentials files (`credentials_file`)

Each persona's Slack tokens live only in its own credentials file: a JSON object with exactly `bot_token` and `app_token` (shape and checks in the `persona-credentials.ts` entry of the Module Map; operator reference in README.md's [Credentials files](../README.md#credentials-files)). `config.json` holds only the path. The server reads the file at the persona's bring-up and, outside dry run, in each reload detection pass (a digest only; see [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83)). It never writes, copies or chmods it and never checks its mode. The operator writes it, by hand or with the setup wizard's credentials command, `claude-slack-channel-bots credentials <persona>` (`## Credentials command` in `skills/setup-slack-channel-bots/SKILL.md`), which runs the packaged `scripts/write-credentials.sh` and creates it with mode 0600; see Security Model. A changed file on a running server is a pending credentials change, applied only once confirmed ([Credentials change](#credentials-change-bav2-sr-86-step-4)).

### agent-director state.db (~/.agent-director/state.db)

Persistent CSCB-spawn registry — owned by agent-director, not CSCB. CSCB reads this transitively via `client.list/status/get/resume(...)`. Same-user invariant verified at SR-5.1 startup gate.

### startup-errors.log (~/.claude/channels/slack/startup-errors.log)

Append-only log of startup errors, fatal and non-fatal, written by `recordStartupError` (`src/startup-errors.ts`). One timestamped line per entry; a typed agent-director error is surfaced as its `errName` plus, when it has a description, `message="…"` (`describeAgentDirectorFailure`), except `ad-template-install`, which keeps the name and description verbatim. Rotation is operator-owned via `docs/logrotate-startup-errors.conf`.

### cron.log (`cron_log_path`)

Append-only plain-text log of every cscb_cron fire outcome, written by `src/cron-log.ts`. Location is the config-resolved `cron_log_path` (default `<config dir>/cron.log`); boot-fixed — the path never hot-reloads. Deliberately plain text, not JSONL, so `grep no-session cron.log` yields human-triageable lines.

Each outcome entry is one physical line of five space-delimited fields: `<ISO-8601 UTC ms timestamp> <identity|-> <target|-> <outcome> <detail>`. The `detail` field leads with key=value tokens (`prompt=`, `status=`, `errno=`, `line=`) then free text. Every value is newline-escaped, so one record is always exactly one physical line. UTC matches server.log so an outage window can be cross-correlated.

The `outcome` field is one of nine classes:

| Outcome | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `delivered` | prompt delivered to the target persona |
| `no-session` | `/interject` answered 503: the target persona is not up (broken or retrying), or has no live connected session — dropped |
| `unknown-persona` | no persona in the applied config has that name or key |
| `prompt-missing` | prompt file did not exist at fire time |
| `prompt-unreadable` | prompt file existed but could not be read |
| `prompt-oversize` | prompt file exceeded the size ceiling |
| `parse-error` | the crontable line could not be parsed |
| `http-error` | localhost POST hit an unexpected HTTP status or a network failure (503→`no-session`, 404→`unknown-persona` are classed separately) |
| `fanout-deferred` | all-bots fire skipped — emitted by the cron dispatcher while fan-out remains deferred to E4; kept so old lines stay interpretable |

A per-fire summary line shares the five-field layout with `summary` in the outcome position (a line kind, not an outcome class): `<ts> <identity> - summary delivered=N failed=M`. An `info` line kind (same layout) carries informational markers, and a `warn` line kind carries operator-actionable conditions — the crontable vanishing or becoming unreadable — with `identity`/`target` defaulting to `-` because they are not fire-scoped. Both tokens are distinct from every outcome class, so `grep warn cron.log` matches exactly the warn lines and neither kind pollutes the outcome triage views.

Append-only: no rotation, no pruning, no read-back (pruning ships in a later Epic). The writer `mkdir`s the log's missing parent directory on first append — deliberately unlike `cron-bootstrap.ts`'s no-mkdir invariant, which is crontable-only (D-Q1), since the log is server-owned output. Write failures never break dispatch — a failed append logs one `[slack] cron-log` line, drops the fd, and self-heals on the next append. This module is a writer other code calls.

### permission-trail.jsonl (~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl)

Append-only **JSON Lines** event store for the CSCB-side visibility trail of the AD↔CSCB tool-permission relay (SRD `t1.cdb.4g`). One JSON object per newline-terminated line. The file path honors `SLACK_STATE_DIR` — default `~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl`. Owned by `src/permission-trail.ts`; every emit goes through `emitTrail()` (auto-stamps `ts`) or `emitTrailEvent()` (caller-supplied `ts`).

**Line schema** (enforced at the emitter — SR-V-1 / SR-V-4.5):

| Field | Type | When present | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ts` | string | always | RFC 3339 with ≥ ms precision (SR-V-4.3, SR-V-4.5). Substituted with a `[slack]` warning if missing/invalid. |
| `event` | string | always | Stable hierarchical identifier in the `cscb.*` namespace (e.g. `cscb.chat_post.attempted`, `cscb.click_handler.invoked`, `cscb.chat_update.attempted`, `cscb.decide.attempted`, `cscb.block_actions.received`). |
| `request_token` | string | when AD has minted one | AD-side UUID (SR-V-1.1). Absent for events emitted before token assignment (SR-V-1.2). |
| `claude_instance_id` | string | whenever known | Primary join key when `request_token` is absent (SR-V-1.2). |
| `channel` | string | events touching Slack | Slack channel id (SR-V-1.3). |
| `message_ts` | string | events touching Slack | Slack message `ts` of the prompt or closure (SR-V-1.3). |
| _per-event-class fields_ | any | event-class specific | Passed through verbatim — no truncation (SR-V-3). |

**Example line** (a `chat.postMessage` attempt emitted by the SR-2.1 poller):

```json
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:39:58.123Z","event":"cscb.chat_post.attempted","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","channel":"C0B1ZJJLJ9M","text":"*Permission* requested for `Bash`","blocks":[{"type":"section","text":{"type":"mrkdwn","text":"…"}},{"type":"actions","elements":[{"type":"button","action_id":"perm_allow_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","text":{"type":"plain_text","text":"Allow"}},{"type":"button","action_id":"perm_deny_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","text":{"type":"plain_text","text":"Deny"}}]}],"ok":true,"slack_ts":"1717536002.000100"}
```

**No-truncation guarantee.** The canonical trail line persists every field at full fidelity (SR-V-3.1) — the full `blocks` array including every `action_id`, the full `text`, the full Slack API response.
