The 300-character `[slack] RAW <event> event persona=<key>: …` summaries the event router (`src/persona-event-router.ts`) logs for each `message` / `app_mention` event, before handing it to `persona-routing.ts`, are a **separate human-readable log** kept truncated intentionally per SR-V-3.2; they do not replace the canonical store and the two coexist.

**`jq` smoke-check** — confirm the file is valid JSONL and pivot by correlation key:

```sh
# Every line parses as JSON
jq -c . ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl | head -3

# Every event for one request_token, in time order
jq -c 'select(.request_token=="6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd")' \
  ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl

# Every event on a Slack channel within a time window (SR-V-5.2)
jq -c 'select(.channel=="C0B1ZJJLJ9M" and .ts>="2026-06-04T20:00:00Z" and .ts<="2026-06-04T21:00:00Z")' \
  ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl
```

**Federation.** AD emits a complementary JSONL log on its side (`t1.n4v.14`). The two logs join on `request_token` + `claude_instance_id` per SR-V-4. No shared store; the operator stitches the trail via `jq` or equivalent.

**Retention.** None. The file is append-only and the system does not self-protect against disk pressure (SR-V-5.5). Operators rotate / archive / delete out-of-band.

#### `cscb.poller.row_decision` (SR-V-2.3)

Emitted once per `permission_requests` row per tick (and once per `non_conforming_skipped` spawn per tick) from `src/permission-poller.ts`. Some rows emit no row decision: rows of a spawn skipped for its `persona` label (missing, or naming no applied persona), rows of a spawn whose persona is not up (b.av2 SR-6.4; no `get` is made, so there are no rows to decide), and new rows whose persona has a refused `dm` destination (DMs off or no contact) or no Slack client (logged instead; see [Per-tick flow](#per-tick-flow-sr-2)). The `action` field is one of six stable identifiers; the set is open to extension per SR-V-2.3 — new poller branches may add identifiers without an SRD edit.

| `action` | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `post_attempted` | New row seen this tick; `chat.postMessage` was issued for this `request_token`. |
| `already_tracked` | Row's composite key already in `livePermissions`; no-op this tick. |
| `reconciled_closed` | Row no longer in the tick's projection; `getPermission` returned a verdict; the closure `chat.update` is sent when the posting persona's client is available, otherwise skipped and logged. The entry is dropped either way. |
| `not_found_generic_deny` | `getPermission` threw `ErrPermissionRequestNotFound`; the generic-deny closure `chat.update` is sent when the posting persona's client is available, otherwise skipped and logged. The entry is dropped either way. |
| `non_conforming_skipped` | The spawn's `permission_requests` was `null`/`undefined`; the row was excluded from the closure sweep this tick. `request_token` is **absent** on this event per SR-V-1.1. |
| `transient_retry` | Transient error on `getPermission`; the entry was left alive for the next tick to retry. |

Fields: `ts`, `event="cscb.poller.row_decision"`, `claude_instance_id`, `action`, and `request_token` (omitted on `non_conforming_skipped`). The envelope `ts` is the only timestamp on the event — there is no per-tick `tick_at` field.

#### `cscb.chat_post.attempted` (SR-V-2.4)

Emitted from `postPermissionPrompt` in `src/permission-poller.ts` for **every** permission-prompt `chat.postMessage` call — success **and** failure — and once for a failed `conversations.open` of a `dm` destination (no post follows it). A refused `dm` destination makes no Slack call and emits nothing. Successes are trail-only; failures land in BOTH the trail (as `ok=false` with a Slack error class string) AND `server.log` via `logViaDeps` (`b.emk` defense-in-depth — the trail is for after-the-fact debugging, `server.log` is for real-time operator visibility). The pre-`b.emk` asymmetry where only failures had a console log is gone — successes are still silent in `server.log`, but failures now appear on both surfaces. For a destination failure (every failed open, and every failed post except a payload error) the `server.log` side is the destination hold's one `persona-destination-failed` line per episode, not a line per attempt (see [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures)); a failed DM open that returned no conversation ID carries `error` `no_conversation_id`. A payload error (`invalid_blocks`, `msg_too_long`, …) logs `[slack] permission-poller: chat.postMessage failed for <id>[ (reason=<reason>)]: <description>` on each attempt, built with `describeSlackCallFailure` so no raw error object (and no Authorization header) is logged. While the persona is held no attempt is made, so no event is emitted.

Fields:
- `ts`, `event="cscb.chat_post.attempted"`, `claude_instance_id`, `request_token`, `channel` (the conversation posted to: the channel, or the `D…` DM conversation for a `dm` destination; absent on a failed `conversations.open`).
- `text` — the full posted text string, untruncated (SR-V-3.1).
- `blocks` — the full posted Block Kit array, untruncated; both Allow and Deny `action_id`s are present and decodable via `parsePermissionActionId` (SR-V-1.4).
- `ok` — `true` on success, `false` on failure.
- On success: `slack_ts` — the `ts` Slack returned for the posted message (the Slack-side message id, distinct from the envelope `ts`).
- On failure: `error` — the **Slack platform error class string** (e.g. `"channel_not_found"`) from `WebAPIPlatformError.data.error`; falls back to `"network_error"` / `"unknown_error"` for non-platform exceptions. Never the JS `Error.name`.

**Combined example** — a row's first-tick emissions:

```json
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:39:58.110Z","event":"cscb.poller.row_decision","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","action":"post_attempted","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd"}
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:39:58.123Z","event":"cscb.chat_post.attempted","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","channel":"C0B1ZJJLJ9M","text":"🤖🛠️ permission request: Bash","blocks":[{"type":"section","text":{"type":"mrkdwn","text":"🤖🛠️ *Bash*\n`ls`"}},{"type":"actions","elements":[{"type":"button","text":{"type":"plain_text","text":"Allow"},"style":"primary","action_id":"perm_allow_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd"},{"type":"button","text":{"type":"plain_text","text":"Deny"},"style":"danger","action_id":"perm_deny_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd"}]}],"ok":true,"slack_ts":"1780600244.439969"}
```

#### `cscb.chat_update.attempted` (SR-V-2.5)

Emitted for every `chat.update` that closes out a permission prompt. Two surfaces emit: the poller's `renderClosureUpdate` (`src/permission-poller.ts`) and the click handler's verdict-render (`src/permission-click-handler.ts`). Both success and failure emit. Successes are trail-only; failures land in BOTH the trail (as `ok=false` with the Slack platform error class string) AND `server.log` via `logViaDeps`/`logDeps` (`b.emk` defense-in-depth). Successes stay silent in `server.log`, preserving the original SR-V-2.5 asymmetric-behavior fix. The `server.log` lines are `[slack] permission-poller: closure chat.update failed for <id> token=<token> (verdict=<tag>)[ (reason=<reason>)]: <description>` and `[slack] permission-click: decision chat.update failed for <id>[ (reason=<reason>)]: <description>`, both built with `describeSlackCallFailure`.

Fields:
- `ts`, `event="cscb.chat_update.attempted"`, `claude_instance_id`, `request_token`, `channel`, `message_ts` (the prompt `ts` being updated).
- `text` — the new closure text, untruncated.
- `blocks` — the new closure blocks array, untruncated.
- `verdict_tag` — one of the 9 values below.
- `triggered_by` — `"poller"` or `"click_handler"`.
- `ok` — `true` on success, `false` on failure.
- On failure: `error` — Slack platform error class string (e.g. `"message_not_found"`); never JS `Error.name`.

**Verdict tags** — closure rendering identity. The set is open to extension.

| `verdict_tag` | Emitted from | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `operator_allow` | poller | `decision=allow`, `decision_reason=null` — operator allowed via click or TUI. |
| `operator_deny` | poller | `decision=deny`, `decision_reason="operator"`. |
| `timeout` | poller | `decision=deny`, `decision_reason="timeout"`. |
| `find_missing` | poller | `decision=deny`, `decision_reason="find_missing"` — spawn ended before the human responded. |
| `unknown` | poller | Any other `decision`/`decision_reason` pair — fail-closed generic deny per SR-5.2. |
| `not_found` | poller | `getPermission` returned `ErrPermissionRequestNotFound`. |
| `click_handler_allow` | click handler | Allow button click whose `chat.update` rendered the verdict. |
| `click_handler_deny` | click handler | Deny button click whose `chat.update` rendered the verdict. |
| `click_handler_relay_fallen_back` | click handler | b.qi1 — not a verdict. AD refused the click with `ErrRelayFallenBack`; the `chat.update` strips the buttons and redirects the operator to the session's tmux pane. The AD request row stays open, so a later poller closure may still render over the same message. |

**`triggered_by` values** — `"poller"` (SR-2.4 reconciliation) or `"click_handler"` (SR-4.5 verdict render). Open to extension.

**Example** — poller-reconciled `operator_allow` closure:

```json
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:40:02.456Z","event":"cscb.chat_update.attempted","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","channel":"C0B1ZJJLJ9M","message_ts":"1780600244.439969","text":"*Permission* — Allowed","blocks":[{"type":"section","text":{"type":"mrkdwn","text":"*Permission* — Allowed"}}],"verdict_tag":"operator_allow","triggered_by":"poller","ok":true}
```

#### `cscb.poller.wedge_detected` (b.fae F4)

Emitted from `postWedgeWarning` in `src/permission-poller.ts` when the wedge detector trips — a spawn seen in `check_permission` with an empty `permission_requests` array for `wedgeTripTicks` consecutive ticks (~90 s). Exactly one **successful** (`ok=true`) event per wedge episode: the `warningFired` latch (set only after a successful post) suppresses further posts until the detector re-arms. A failed post emits an `ok=false` event without latching and the poller retries on a later tick (throttled ~30 s), so an episode with Slack trouble can log several `ok=false` events before the one `ok=true`. Only an attempt that reaches Slack emits the event (a post, or a failed `conversations.open` for a `dm` destination): a refused `dm` destination and an unavailable persona client emit none. The envelope shape mirrors `cscb.chat_post.attempted`: success is trail-only; failure lands in BOTH the trail (`ok=false`) AND `server.log` (`b.emk` convention), where a destination failure appears as the destination hold's one episode line and a payload error as the per-attempt `wedge warning postMessage failed` line. While the persona is held no attempt is made and no event is emitted.

Fields:
- `ts`, `event="cscb.poller.wedge_detected"`, `claude_instance_id`, `channel` (the conversation posted to: the persona's destination channel, or its `D…` DM conversation; absent on a failed `conversations.open`).
- `text` — the full one-shot warning message posted to the destination, prefixed with the persona reference (read-pane + kill/respawn remediation; send-keys explicitly not offered).
- `ok` — `true` on success, `false` on failure.
- On success: `slack_ts` — the Slack `ts` of the posted warning.
- On failure: `error` — the Slack platform error class string.

#### `cscb.block_action.received` (SR-V-2.9)

Emitted once per inbound `block_actions` action by the event router (`src/persona-event-router.ts`) when a persona's connection delivers an `interactive` event, regardless of whether the `action_id` decodes. This is the diagnostically critical surface for **"I clicked Allow and nothing happened"** — decode-failure cases are explicitly in scope. The classification helper lives in `src/permission-click-handler.ts` (`emitBlockActionReceived`) so the server stays a thin wiring layer.

Fields:
- **Always present**: `ts`, `event="cscb.block_action.received"`, `channel`, `message_ts`, `user`, `raw_action_id`.
- **On decode success**: `claude_instance_id`, `request_token`, `decision` (`"allow"` | `"deny"`). `parse_failure_reason` is omitted.
- **On decode failure**: `parse_failure_reason` (table below). Decoded fields are omitted.

**Parse-failure reason set** — open to extension per SR-V-2.9:

| `parse_failure_reason` | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `foreign_action_id` | `action_id` does not match the `perm_(allow|deny)_*` prefix — not a CSCB permission button at all. |
| `malformed_token` | `action_id` matches the `perm_(allow|deny)_*` prefix but `parsePermissionActionId` returns `null` (body broken). |

**Not a parse failure: `stale_prompt`.** A click on a closed prompt decodes fine — the `action_id` is still well-formed. Stale clicks surface as `cscb.click_handler.invoked{live_pending: false}` instead.

#### `cscb.click_handler.invoked` (SR-V-2.6)

Emitted from `handlePermissionClick` in `src/permission-click-handler.ts` for every parsed-as-permission click. Emitted exactly once per call, after `parsePermissionActionId` succeeds and before the AD `decide` call. Decode-failure clicks (foreign or malformed action_id) do NOT emit this event — they show up only as `cscb.block_action.received{parse_failure_reason}`.

Fields: `ts`, `event="cscb.click_handler.invoked"`, `claude_instance_id`, `request_token`, `decision`, `channel`, `message_ts`, `user`, `raw_action_id`, `live_pending` (boolean).

`live_pending` is the result of `getLivePermission(claude_instance_id, request_token) !== undefined` at handler entry. `true` means a `LivePermission` entry existed (happy path); `false` means the click decoded fine but the entry had already aged out or been dropped — the operator's "I clicked but the prompt was already closed" diagnostic.

**Example sequence** — happy-path Allow click:

```json
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:40:01.111Z","event":"cscb.block_action.received","channel":"C0B1ZJJLJ9M","message_ts":"1780600244.439969","user":"U_OPERATOR","raw_action_id":"perm_allow_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","decision":"allow"}
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:40:01.112Z","event":"cscb.click_handler.invoked","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","channel":"C0B1ZJJLJ9M","message_ts":"1780600244.439969","user":"U_OPERATOR","raw_action_id":"perm_allow_cscb_ops_bot_6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","decision":"allow","live_pending":true}
```

**Note on the `message` / `app_mention` RAW-line truncation (`src/persona-event-router.ts`).** The `[slack] RAW <event> event persona=<key>: …` summaries, cut to 300 characters of the event JSON, are INTENTIONALLY preserved per SR-V-3.2 — they are a separate human-readable log. The canonical store is full-fidelity by construction (no `.slice` in the trail emit path).

#### `cscb.ad_decide.attempted` (SR-V-2.7 call-side)

Emitted from `handlePermissionClick` (`src/permission-click-handler.ts`) once per `decideWithToken` invocation, on success and on every error path. This is the CSCB-side half of SR-V-2.7; AD's complementary SR-A-2.4 emission lives in `t1.n4v.14`. The two halves join on `request_token` for after-the-fact debugging.

Fields:
- `ts`, `event="cscb.ad_decide.attempted"`, `claude_instance_id`, `request_token`, `decision` (submitted — `"allow"` | `"deny"`).
- `result_class` — one of the 6 values below.
- On `result_class="other"`, and on the agent-director/tmux carve-out (`ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, `ErrTmuxNotAvailable`): additionally `raw_error_message`, the original error's `.message` after `redactSlackLogText` (URL-like and token-like text replaced), otherwise untruncated. For the other named classes, `raw_error_message` is OMITTED.
- `submitted_decision_reason` — currently never sent on the wire from CSCB. Documented for future use per SR-V-2.7's "when present" rule.

**Response-class set** — match the AD error identifiers in `src/agent-director-errors.ts` so the trail's classification stays consistent with the rest of the codebase. Open to extension.

| `result_class` | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ok` | `decideWithToken` returned without throwing. |
| `ErrAlreadyDecided` | The request was already closed (race with poller reconciliation or peer click). |
| `ErrInvalidFlags` | AD rejected the call shape — typically a missing required field. Should not happen under contract. |
| `ErrAmbiguousRequest` | AD couldn't uniquely identify the request — defense-in-depth backstop per SR-4.4. |
| `ErrRelayFallenBack` | b.qi1 — the request's relay window elapsed, so Claude re-asked at its own tmux pane and AD will not record the verdict. The click did not land; the session is unaffected. |
| `other` | Any other thrown value (non-AD error, transport, etc.). `raw_error_message` carries the original `.message`, redacted. |

**Example** — happy-path `ad_decide.attempted` after a click:

```json
{"ts":"2026-06-04T20:40:01.130Z","event":"cscb.ad_decide.attempted","claude_instance_id":"cscb_ops_bot","request_token":"6f3a1d2c-aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddddddddddd","decision":"allow","result_class":"ok"}
```

**Operational logging.** The existing `[slack] permission-click: ErrInvalidFlags from decide for ...` / `ErrAmbiguousRequest` / `decide failed for ...` `console.error` lines stay alongside the trail event. They serve a different purpose — live stderr monitoring — and are intentionally not redundant with the canonical after-the-fact debugging surface.

#### Trail file location and query recipes — SR-V-5 / SR-V-6

The file is the surface; `tail`, `grep`, and `jq` are the readers. No running CSCB process is required and nothing is perturbed (SR-V-6.2).

**Path:** `~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl`
**Directory override:** `SLACK_STATE_DIR` (sets the directory; file name is always `permission-trail.jsonl`)

**Single-side recipes:**

```sh
# Live stream
tail -f ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl

# SR-V-5.1: every event for one interaction
jq -c 'select(.request_token=="X")' ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl

# SR-V-5.2: events on a channel within a time window
jq -c 'select(.channel=="C..." and .ts >= "T1" and .ts <= "T2")' \
  ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl
```

**Cross-side join** (AD trail + CSCB trail, ordered by `ts`, filtered to one interaction):

```sh
cat ~/.agent-director/ad-trail.jsonl ~/.claude/channels/slack/permission-trail.jsonl \
  | jq -sc 'sort_by(.ts) | map(select(.request_token=="X"))[]'
```

**Join keys** for stitching the CSCB trail against the AD trail (SR-V-4):

| Key | Emitted by (CSCB surface) | When |
|---|---|---|
| `request_token` | every CSCB event after AD mints one | primary join key (SR-V-1.1) |
| `claude_instance_id` | every CSCB event when known | join when no token yet (SR-V-1.2) |
| `channel` + `message_ts` | every Slack-touching event (`chat_post.attempted`, `chat_update.attempted`, `block_action.received`, `click_handler.invoked`) | Slack pivot (SR-V-1.3) |

The AD trail (file owned by agent-director — see `t1.n4v.14`) emits the complementary side; the operator joins by `request_token` and orders by `ts`.

**SRD §10 question → answering event class** — CSCB side (questions 2–5; question 1 is AD's):

| SRD §10 question | Answered by |
|---|---|
| Q2: Did CSCB issue a `chat.postMessage` carrying the expected Block Kit blocks? | `cscb.chat_post.attempted` — `text` and `blocks` fields. |
| Q3: What `ts` did Slack return? | `cscb.chat_post.attempted` — `slack_ts` field. |
| Q4: Was a `block_actions` click received against that `ts`? | `cscb.block_action.received` — `message_ts` field equals the post's `slack_ts`. |
| Q5: Did the closure render on the original `ts`? | `cscb.chat_update.attempted` — `message_ts` field equals the post's `slack_ts`. |

### Removed pre-Epic-2 files

The previous tmux-direct architecture wrote `~/.claude/channels/slack/sessions.json` to persist tmux session names and discovered Claude session UUIDs. Both responsibilities have moved to agent-director — `sessions.json` and `sessions.json.last` no longer exist. Operators upgrading from a pre-Epic-2 install can safely delete the stale files; CSCB will not read them.

### server.pid (STATE_DIR/server.pid)

Written at startup with the server's process ID. Used by the CLI `stop` command to send SIGTERM (with SIGKILL escalation after `stop_timeout`) to a running server, and by startup to detect a conflicting already-running instance. Removed on graceful shutdown.

### reply-guard/<key> (STATE_DIR/reply-guard/<key>)

One file per persona, named by its key, in the record directory `reply-guard/` under the state directory (honours `SLACK_STATE_DIR`; `src/reply-guard-record.ts`). It holds the exact text `true` or `false`: the persona's effective `stop_hook_bootstrap`.

- **Written** atomically (`.tmp` + rename) by `preLaunchReplyGuard` immediately before each spawn or resume of that persona (Server-Managed Startup step 5), never on a reconnect and never for another persona's launch. The directory is created on first write.
- **Read** by `stop-hooks/slack-reply-guard.sh`, which takes the record directory as its first argument (from the managed hook's command) and reads `<dir>/<CSCB_PERSONA>`. It reminds only when the record reads `true` (one trailing newline tolerated). The start and launch passes of step 4a-ter also read it: a record reading `true` keeps the managed hook in the persona's directories.
- **Fail-open.** A missing argument, an unset, empty or non-key `CSCB_PERSONA`, or a missing, unreadable or other-valued record means no reminder.
- **Deleted** by `teardownPersonaReplyGuard(stateDir, key)` (a missing record is success), which also forgets the persona's launched-with dir. The persona teardown calls it for a removed persona (see Session Lifecycle, [Persona teardown](#persona-teardown-bav2-sr-65)). A failed write at launch also deletes any stale record.

### Environment Variables

The server (`server.ts`) requires no environment variable and reads no Slack token from the environment: each persona's `bot_token` and `app_token` come only from its credentials file (`credentials_file`, see Persona config), and nothing supplies the bind address or port except the applied configuration (the last-applied record, else `config.json`). The CLI reads no token either: `start` checks only that `config.json` or its last-applied record exists (see [start command](#start-command)). `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` and `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` are read by nothing; setting them has no effect.

Optional:

- `SLACK_STATE_DIR` — the state directory (made absolute; default `~/.claude/channels/slack`). `config.json` and its reload files, the inbox, `server.pid`, the reply-guard records (`reply-guard/`), the logs and the permission trail live under it. `resolveServerStateDir` reads it at call time; `server.ts` computes its paths once at import.
- `SLACK_DRY_RUN` — set to `1`, `true`, or `yes` to enable dry-run mode (`isDryRun()` in `tokens.ts`). No credentials file is read and no Slack call is made: each persona's bring-up runs only the working-directory check, then the connection manager marks it up with a distinct placeholder identity derived from its key (`U000DRY_<key>` / `B000DRY_<key>`), and builds no Slack client. Spawns, the start sweep and the permission poller are skipped. The reload detection tick still runs over `config.json` alone: it reads no credentials file, credentials never count as changed, and the fingerprint covers the configuration file only. All MCP tool calls (`reply`, `react`, `edit_message`, `fetch_messages`, `download_attachment`) are stubbed — each returns a `[dry-run]` placeholder and logs the call to the server log — and persona notices are logged, not posted. The HTTP/MCP server still starts normally so Claude Code sessions can connect and exercise tool calls without a Slack workspace.
- `CSCB_LOG_MAX_BYTES`, `CSCB_LOG_KEEP`, `CSCB_AD_VERBOSE`, `CSCB_HTTP_VERBOSE` — log rotation and verbosity; see [Logging](#logging).
- `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` — the Slack API base URL override, for the docker integration suite only (Test 10 points a live start at the loopback Slack stub, `tests/integration/fixtures/slack-stub-server.ts`). Never set it on an operator's host; no shipped doc describes it. `main()` in `server.ts` reads it once per start, through `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride(process.env, log)` in `persona-slack-clients.ts`, and passes the result to the connection manager as `slackApiUrl`. It is honoured only for an `http:` URL whose host is one of the literal loopback addresses, `http://127.0.0.1…` or `http://[::1]…` (any port and path, and forms the URL parser normalises to them, such as `127.1` or the long form of `::1`), with no user info, query or fragment. `localhost` is not accepted: it would be the one host resolved by name. When honoured, one `[slack] Slack API base URL override in use: <origin> (CSCB_SLACK_API_URL)` line is logged, naming only the URL's origin (never its path), and all three client call sites in `persona-connections.ts` build their clients with the full normalised URL as `slackApiUrl`: the validation client for `auth.test`, the long-lived Web API client, and the Socket Mode client's `clientOptions`, so `apps.connections.open` goes there and the WebSocket URL it returns is used as given. Any other value is ignored with one `[slack] Warning: CSCB_SLACK_API_URL ignored: …` line that names the accepted forms (`http://127.0.0.1` or `http://[::1]`) and never echoes the value, and the start goes on against the default Slack API. Unset or empty, nothing is logged and every client's options are exactly the SR-3.3 sets. When it is honoured, the persona tokens go only to whatever listens on that loopback port, which is trusted with them.

Optional CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR (set at spawn time via SpawnParams.extra_env when configured per persona or top-level) is propagated by agent-director to the spawn's tmux session, enabling per-persona Claude account selection.

CSCB_PERSONA and CLAUDE_MANAGED_CHANNEL are set at spawn time via SpawnParams.extra_env for every managed session, both to the persona key, and are propagated by agent-director to the spawn's tmux session. An instance uses its `CSCB_PERSONA` key to target itself in crontable lines and in `/interject` requests, and the Slack Reply Guard reads it to find the instance's own `reply-guard/<key>` record.

CSCB_CRONTABLE_PATH is set at spawn time via SpawnParams.extra_env for every managed session — unconditionally, for every persona — and is propagated by agent-director to the spawn's tmux session. It carries the resolved tilde-expanded `cron_table_path` (the default `<config dir>/crontab` or the configured override) so bots can self-schedule by locating and appending to the crontable, the single source of truth for scheduled prompts (bots are first-class writers alongside the server).

CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION is set to `false` at spawn time via SpawnParams.extra_env for every managed session (`PROMPT_SUGGESTION_OFF_ENV` in `persona-identity.ts`, spread last so no other entry replaces it; a resume restores it from the row's stored env). Claude Code reads it before its server-side flag and the `promptSuggestionEnabled` setting, so no suggested prompt shows in the session and the end-of-turn suggestion fork never starts. That fork runs the session's PreToolUse hooks and is denied, so agent-director flipped idle personas from `waiting` to `working` (b.svb); the stale-`working` recovery (b.f2b) still covers such rows.

## Logging

### Why console.error/console.log are overridden directly

Bun bypasses `process.stderr.write` overrides — the runtime writes directly to the file descriptor, so patching `process.stderr.write` has no effect. `src/logging.ts` works around this by replacing `console.error` and `console.log` themselves before any logging occurs.

### initLogging()

`initLogging(logFilePath)` in `src/logging.ts` opens the target file in append mode and replaces both `console.error` and `console.log` with wrapper functions that:

1. Format all arguments to a single string (JSON-serializing objects)
2. Prepend an ISO-8601 timestamp: `[2024-01-01T00:00:00.000Z] message`
3. Roll the file if it has grown past the threshold (see **Rotation** below)
4. Write the line synchronously via `writeSync` to the open file descriptor
5. Fall back to the original `console.error`/`console.log` if the write fails

The originals are captured at module load time so the fallback always refers to Bun's native output.

### Rotation (b.brv)

Rotation is built into `src/logging.ts` so it applies on every machine that runs CSCB — no per-host logrotate config. `maybeRotate()` runs before each write: it `fstat`s the active fd and, when the size crosses the threshold, closes the fd, shifts `<path>.N-1 → <path>.N` down to `<path>.1`, renames the active file to `<path>.1`, discards the oldest generation beyond `CSCB_LOG_KEEP`, and reopens a fresh active file. It is best-effort — any filesystem error is swallowed rather than thrown into the caller's hot path, and a failed reopen after rotation is recovered on a subsequent write rather than disabling file logging.

| Env var | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `CSCB_LOG_MAX_BYTES` | 10 MiB (`10485760`) | Rotate when the active file reaches N bytes. Values `<= 0` or non-numeric ignored. |
| `CSCB_LOG_KEEP` | 5 | Rotated generations retained. `0` = keep none (the active file is `rm`ed on rotation, i.e. truncate). Values `< 0` or non-numeric ignored. |

Rotated generations are **not** compressed. Applies to both `server.log` and `clean_restart.log` (any file passed to `initLogging`). This is distinct from `startup-errors.log`, whose rotation remains operator-owned via `docs/logrotate-startup-errors.conf`.

### agent-director logger filter (b.brv)

The agent-director `Client` is constructed with a `logger` in `src/agent-director-startup.ts`, and since `initLogging()` patches `console`, everything the Client logs lands in `server.log`. At info level the Client emits a per-poll `SubprocessClient: <verb> ok { … }` success dump for every `list`/`status`/`get`/`decide` call — with CSCB polling continuously, these dumps historically dominated the log (2 GB / 137 MB observed in b.brv). `makeFilteredAdLogger(console)` in `src/agent-director-logger.ts` wraps the console: it drops those routine `SubprocessClient: <verb> ok` records at `log`/`info` level while passing `warn`/`error` through unfiltered. Setting `CSCB_AD_VERBOSE` to a truthy value (`1`/`true`/`yes`/`on`) returns the base logger unwrapped, restoring the full chatter for debugging.

### Slack library output (b.av2 SR-10.3)

Because `console` is patched, anything a Slack library logs lands in `server.log`. The persona clients' library loggers are built in `src/persona-slack-clients.ts` (see Security Model, Slack library logging):

- **Socket Mode** (`socketModeSlackLogger`): only the library's fixed-text connection-health lines get through, each written through the connection manager's log as `[slack] persona Socket Mode: personas[<i>] "<name>" (key=<key>): <text>`. The text is `A ping wasn't received from the server before the timeout of <n>ms!` or `A pong wasn't received from the server before the timeout of <n>ms!` (both warnings), or `Failed to send ping to Slack` (the library's error detail is dropped). After any of the three the library closes the connection and CSCB logs `persona-connection-lost`. Every other line is dropped, including the handshake error that carries the WebSocket URL and its ticket; the server logs its own connection lines instead.
- **Web API** (validation and long-lived, `redactingSlackLogger`): the library's lines are written as its console logger would (`[WARN]  web-api:WebClient …`, default INFO) with URL-like text replaced by `<redacted-url>` and token-like text by `<redacted-token>`. Default-level lines are Slack's response warnings, rate-limit waits and a failed request's message, which on a 429 names the request URL (for `filesUploadV2`, the short-lived upload URL).

### HTTP request access line (b.3k6)

The `/mcp` endpoint's `fetch` handler in `src/server.ts` is hit on every MCP round-trip — client polls, notifications, tool-call responses, and each SSE stream open — so its one-line-per-request access log (`[slack] HTTP <method> <path> session=…`) is the highest-frequency routine success line left in `server.log` after b.brv silenced the `SubprocessClient` dumps. It shares their signal/noise profile: routine and continuous at steady state, useful only when debugging a specific transport/session-routing problem. It is gated behind `isHttpVerbose()`, which reads `CSCB_HTTP_VERBOSE` (truthy: `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on`, case-insensitive) — off by default. Unlike `CSCB_AD_VERBOSE`, the env var is checked per request rather than once at startup, so toggling it takes effect without a restart. Real events (session connect/disconnect, no matching persona, errors) are logged unconditionally elsewhere and are unaffected by this flag.

### Persona bring-up lines (b.av2 SR-10.3)

A persona's bring-up and its retries log to `server.log` only, with the `[slack]` prefix; none goes to `startup-errors.log` or to Slack. Diagnostic lines use `formatPersonaDiagnostic` (`persona-diagnostics.ts`): `[slack] <class>: personas[<i>] "<name>" (key=<key>)`, then ` path="<path>"` when the class has one, then `: <cause>`. The full class list is in the `persona-diagnostics.ts` entry of the Module Map. No line carries a token. The operator-facing catalogue of these classes, with each one's cause and fix, is the shipped debugging skill `skills/debug-slack-channel-bots/SKILL.md`.

| Line | When | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| `persona-start: … : bring-up starting` | Once per applied persona as its bring-up starts, before any of its outcome lines | entry, name, key |
| `persona-credentials-missing` / `-unreadable` / `-invalid` | Once, when step 1 fails | entry, name, key, credentials-file path, cause |
| `persona-directory-missing` / `-unusable` | Once, when step 2 fails and its retry starts; later failed re-checks log nothing, even if the class changes | entry, name, key, working directory, cause |
| Same directory class, cause `cleared: working directory is usable again; continuing the bring-up` | Once, when a re-check finds the directory usable; with a credentials cause still open the cause ends `the persona stays broken until its credentials file is fixed and the change confirmed` instead | entry, name, key, working directory |
| `persona-slack-unreachable` / `persona-credentials-refused` | Logged by the connection manager once when the cause starts and once when it clears (cause `cleared: …`), not on every attempt | entry, name, key, credentials-file path, cause; a `persona-slack-unreachable` start line then the thrown value's message, when it had one, as `message="…"` (`describeLogMessage`: redacted, one line, capped at 300 characters; none for the 10 s timeout markers) |
| `persona-credentials-refused` with cause `bot_token refused by a Web API call (<method>): Slack error <code>` (bug b.ujn) | Once, when a Web API call of a running persona is rejected with `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `account_inactive` or `not_authed` and the manager marks it credentials-broken; later calls on that client are refused locally and log nothing. A pending credentials reconnect that swaps in logs the cleared line `cleared: bot_token no longer refused by a Web API call (was Slack error <code>)`; the usual recovery (apply step 6) stops the connection first and so starts a fresh tracker with no cleared line, logging `broken by its credentials and its credentials file changed — bringing it up again` and the new bring-up's lines instead | entry, name, key, credentials-file path, cause |
| `persona-config-dir-unresolvable: … path="<dir>": claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path (<code>[: a symlink on its path points to nothing]); its Slack connection is closed and its launch waits until it resolves` (bug b.g57) | Once, when the controller's check right before step 3, or a launch, finds the persona's effective `claude_config_dir` unresolvable and the hold starts; later failed re-checks log nothing. `<dir>` is the configured directory, tilde-expanded and absolute (`<home>/.claude` when none is configured); `<code>` is the errno code (`unknown` when there is none) | entry, name, key, config directory, errno code |
| Same class, cause `cleared: claude_config_dir resolves to a real path again; continuing the bring-up` (or `…; the persona stays broken until its credentials file is fixed and the change confirmed` for a persona Slack had refused when the hold started) | Once, when a re-check finds the directory resolvable; Slack validation and connection with the held credentials follow | entry, name, key, config directory |
| `persona-connection-lost` / `persona-slack-unreachable` / `persona-credentials-refused`, cause `cleared: the persona's Slack connection was closed while its claude_config_dir cannot be resolved` | Once, when a later launch's hold closes the connection of a persona whose Slack episode of that class is still open (a lost connection still reopening, a Slack retry, a refusal); logged by the manager's `stop` with an end reason, right after the hold's `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` line. The recovery connects on a fresh episode, with no restored line. A teardown or shutdown close logs nothing (b.av2 SR-6.5) | entry, name, key, credentials-file path |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): up after its claude_config_dir resolved — launching` | After the cleared line, once the persona's Slack step reports `up` | name, key |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): no longer applied — its claude_config_dir retry stops` | A re-check came due for a persona no longer applied | name, key |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): claude_config_dir retry failed: <error>` | A re-check attempt threw | name, key, error |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): closing its Slack connection for its claude_config_dir failed: <error>` | The manager's `stop` threw or rejected when a later hold closed the persona's connection | name, key, error |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): up after its bring-up retry (directory\|Slack) — launching` | Once, when a retry brings the persona up and the controller launches it | name, key, which retry |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): launch after its bring-up retry failed: <error>` | That launch threw | name, key, error |
| `persona-credentials-change-failed: … path="<path>": the confirmed credentials change cannot be used: <cause>; the current connection stays in use, and the change stays pending` | Once per failed confirmed credentials change (apply step 4), or when a reconnect left retrying is refused later while the persona is still up. `<cause>` is the credentials check's cause (missing, unreadable, invalid, or the real-path collision naming the other persona) or Slack's refusal (`<key> refused by <check>: Slack error <code>`). For a retrying persona the line ends `it keeps retrying with its current credentials, and the change stays pending`; for an up persona whose own connection was refused while the attempt ran (credentials-broken by then) it ends `it stays broken by its credentials, and the change stays pending`. Logged by the bring-up controller through `formatCredentialsChangeFailed` | entry, name, key, credentials-file path, cause |
| `persona-slack-unreachable` with cause `cleared: the new connection of its confirmed credentials change is no longer retried: a later confirmed credentials change replaced it` (or `…: the persona's connection was stopped`) | Once, when a credentials reconnect left retrying is cancelled, by a later confirmed change or by the persona's connection being stopped, while its unreachable episode is open; so the reconnect's unreachable start line always gets an end | entry, name, key, credentials-file path, cause |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): handling its confirmed credentials change failed: <error>` | A hook the bring-up controller runs at a credentials reconnect's swap threw; the swap goes ahead | name, key, error |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): up after its confirmed credentials change — launching` | Once, when a pending credentials reconnect swaps in for a connection Slack had refused meanwhile, and the controller launches the persona | name, key |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): launch after its confirmed credentials change failed: <error>` | That launch threw | name, key, error |

The restart and relaunch-gate lines for a persona that isn't up are under Session Lifecycle, Auto-Restart. The b.av2 SR-6.4 lines for a persona that isn't up but still has a running instance are with the path that logs them: `Session refused: …` (Session Lifecycle, Connection step 3), `MCP session dropped — …` (Session Lifecycle, Disconnection), `permission-poller: … is not up — …` and `… is up again — …` (Permission Relay, Per-tick flow step 2), `/interject: refused for persona …` (Endpoint Inventory), and the start sweep's and ladder's `cannot be resolved to a real path — keeping its row(s)` (Server-Managed Startup steps 4 and 5). For an unresolvable `claude_config_dir` (bug b.g57) the session manager logs `[slack] spawnForPersona: "<name>" (key=<key>) claude_config_dir could not be resolved during the launch — keeping its row; not launching` when the directory stopped resolving mid-ladder, the restart module's kill adapter logs `[slack] killSession (restart adapter): persona=<key> claude_config_dir cannot be resolved to a real path — not killing; its row is kept`, and the JSONL safeguard's Layer 2 logs `[slack] jsonl-persistence-check: "<name>" (key=<key>) claude_config_dir="<dir>" cannot be resolved to a real path (<code>) — transcript not checked this pass`. With no hook installed, or for a persona the controller doesn't hold, the session manager logs the `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` line itself on every attempt. None of them carries a token or a diagnostic class label.

### Persona destination lines (b.av2 SR-10.3)

The destination hold (`persona-destination-hold.ts`) logs to `server.log` only, with the `[slack]` prefix; see Permission Relay, [Destination hold](#destination-hold-bav2-sr-71-failures). The `persona-destination-failed` lines use `formatPersonaDiagnostic`, so they carry the entry position, the JSON-quoted name and the key. `<destination>` is the persona's `permission_prompts` value: the channel ID, or `dm`. `<code>` is the Slack error code only when it is a short identifier (`safeFailureCode`), else `unknown_error`; nothing else from the error reaches a line, so no line carries a token. No trail event or trail field is added for these lines.

| Line | When | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| `persona-destination-failed: … : <step> failed for destination=<destination> with error <code>; holding its permission prompts and notices and retrying with backoff` | Once, when a destination failure episode starts (`<step>` is `conversations.open` or `chat.postMessage`). For `missing_scope` on the open, ` — the Slack app lacks the im:write scope: re-install the app with im:write to grant it` follows the code. Failed retries log nothing, except that a failure at a destination the open episode has not named yet (its `permission_prompts` changed in place) logs one more such line naming it | entry, name, key, destination, step, Slack error code, `im:write` for `missing_scope` |
| `persona-destination-failed: … : cleared: destination=<destination> accepts posts again (was <step> error <code>); delivering what was held` | Once, when the first attempt after the episode opened succeeds | entry, name, key, the destination that post went to, the step and code of the episode's latest opening line |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: more than 20 notices held for "<name>" (key=<key>) while its destination fails — oldest held notice dropped, not posted: <first line>` | Each time a held notice is dropped past the cap | name, key, the dropped notice's first line |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: persona=<key> is no longer applied — <n> held notice(s) dropped, not posted` | When a held-notice retry comes due for a persona no longer applied | key, count |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: persona or client lookup threw for persona=<key>: <error> — held notices wait and retry with backoff` | Once per run of retries whose persona or client lookup throws (`<error>` is `describeThrownValue`); the notices wait the normal backoff | key, thrown value description |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: shutting down — <n> held notice(s) for "<name>" (key=<key>) dropped, not posted` | At shutdown (`cancelAll()`), once per persona with held notices | name (else `persona=<key>`), key, count |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: hold cancelled — <n> held notice(s) for "<name>" (key=<key>) dropped, not posted` | When `cancel(key)` drops a persona's held notices | name (else `persona=<key>`), key, count |

The operator-facing entry for `persona-destination-failed`, with its fixes, is in `skills/debug-slack-channel-bots/SKILL.md`.

### Persona lifecycle lines (b.av2 SR-6.5, SR-6.1)

A confirmed apply's persona teardowns, in-place updates, credentials changes, template refresh and bring-ups log plain `[slack]` lines to `server.log` only, with no diagnostic class label; none goes to `startup-errors.log` or to Slack. A thrown value is rendered only through `describeThrownValue`. A bring-up at apply also logs every Persona bring-up line above, as at start.

| Line | When |
|---|---|
| `[slack] persona teardown of "<name>" (key=<key>): starting` | The teardown of a removed persona or of a destructive modify's old half starts (apply step 2) |
| `[slack] persona teardown of "<name>" (key=<key>): <step> before its turn failed: <thrown value>` | A cancel at submit threw: for every teardown, `cancelling its launch's wait for a working row`; for a destructive modify's old half, also `cancelling its bring-up retries` or `cancelling its restart timer`. The teardown still runs |
| `[slack] waitForWaitingAndReconnect: persona=<key> — cancelling its launch's wait for its working row (b.f2b)` (or `… — its launch in flight will not wait for a working row: any such wait is cancelled at once (b.f2b)`); a wait that runs then ends with `[slack] waitForWaitingAndReconnect: the wait for "<name>" (key=<key>) was cancelled (its persona is being torn down) — nothing typed (b.f2b)` | The teardown cancelled the persona's launch wait for a `working` row (a running one, or one its launch in flight had yet to start). Logged at submit, or at step 2 for a wait that started since |
| `[slack] persona teardown of "<name>" (key=<key>): <step> failed: <thrown value>` | One teardown step failed; the rest still run. `<step>` is a fixed phrase, such as `stopping its Slack connection`, `agent-director kill of cscb_<key>` or `agent-director delete of cscb_<key>` |
| `[slack] persona teardown of "<name>" (key=<key>): complete` (or `…: complete, with <n> failed step(s)`) | The teardown's last step ran |
| `[slack] dry-run: persona teardown of "<name>" (key=<key>): skipping the agent-director kill and delete of cscb_<key>` | A teardown in dry run |
| `[slack] Cancelled restart timer for persona=<key>` | The teardown cancelled a pending restart timer |
| `[slack] persona-notifier: persona=<key>: dropped <n> held notice(s), not posted — the persona was torn down` | The teardown dropped notices waiting for the persona's client |
| `[slack] persona-destination-hold: hold cancelled — <n> held notice(s) for "<name>" (key=<key>) dropped, not posted` | The teardown dropped notices held for a failing destination |
| `[slack] permission-poller: persona=<key>: dropped <n> tracked prompt(s); their Slack messages stay as posted` | The teardown dropped tracked permission prompts |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): updated in place (<settings>); its instance, Slack connection and MCP session are kept` | A persona modified in place was updated (apply step 3). `<settings>` lists the changed settings, comma-separated, in the order `channels`, `delivery`, `permission_prompts`, `dm.enabled`, `dm.contact`. When the DM cache was forgotten the line ends `; its cached DM conversation is forgotten`, or `; forgetting its cached DM conversation failed: <thrown value>` when that threw. The same line is logged for a `retrying` or `broken` persona, which has no instance, connection or session yet |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): in-place update failed: <thrown value>` | The update could not read the applied set; nothing else is done |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): up at apply — launching` | An added persona, or a destructive modify's new half, came up at apply and is launched (apply step 6) |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): launch at apply failed: <thrown value>` | That launch threw |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): storage check at apply failed: <thrown value>` | The storage check threw unexpectedly; the bring-up goes on |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): not brought up — the server is shutting down` | A bring-up at apply found the server shutting down, when it started (nothing is brought up) or right before the launch (nothing is launched) |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): reconnected with its changed credentials; its instance and MCP session are kept` | An up persona's confirmed credentials change: the new connection is in use and the old one closed (apply step 4). Also logged when a reconnect left retrying swaps later |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): reconnected with its changed credentials and up again; its instance is kept` | The same swap, at once or later, for a persona whose own connection Slack had refused meanwhile: it is up again, and the controller launches it (`up after its confirmed credentials change — launching`) |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): its changed credentials cannot reach Slack yet; the new connection retries and the current one stays in use` | The same, when Slack could not be reached with the new credentials |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): its changed credentials cannot reach Slack yet; the new connection retries, and it stays broken by its credentials until that connection is in use` | The same, for a persona whose own connection Slack refused while the attempt ran |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): it retries its bring-up with its changed credentials` | A retrying persona's confirmed credentials change: its later attempts use the new content |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): broken by its credentials now, so it is brought up again rather than reconnected` | Step 4 found the persona credentials-broken when it ran; step 6 brings it up |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): credentials change not applied — the server is shutting down` | A credentials change found the server shutting down |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): forgetting its cached DM conversation failed: <thrown value>` | Forgetting the DM cache right before a credentials reconnect's swap threw; the swap goes ahead |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): broken by its credentials and its credentials file changed — bringing it up again` | A recovery bring-up starts (apply step 6) |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): not brought up again — it is not broken by its credentials now, so its changed credentials are applied as a credentials change` | A recovery bring-up found the persona no longer credentials-broken (a pending reconnect brought it up since); the credentials change runs instead, with its own lines |
| `[slack] persona "<name>" (key=<key>): <step> before bringing it up again failed: <thrown value>` | One recovery preparation step failed (`cancelling its bring-up state`, `stopping its Slack connection` or `forgetting its DM destination`); the rest still run |
| `[slack] template refresh: rewrote the agent-director template '<name>' with the memory-read rules of the applied config directories (<n> rule(s)); its other arguments are the start's` | Apply step 5 rewrote the template |
| `[slack] template refresh: refreshing the agent-director template '<name>' failed (<detail>); its memory-read rules stay as last installed until the config directories change again or the server restarts` | Apply step 5's `makeTemplate` rejected; `<detail>` is a typed error's `errName` (when it passes `isSafeIdentifier`) and its description as `message="…"` through `redactSlackLogText` (the `errName` alone when the description is empty), or, for any other throw or an unsafe `errName`, `describeThrownValue`'s description (its type, a safe `code`, the redacted message and its stack frames). Not fatal and not retried. A plain line with no class label, logged by `refreshSlackChannelBotTemplate` |

A persona whose operation rejects as a whole is logged by the reload controller (`reload: apply step <n> (<step>) failed for persona …`, Reload lines below).

### Reload lines (b.av2 SR-8.7, SR-10.3)

The reload controller (`src/reload.ts`) and its tick driver (`src/reload-timer.ts`) log their start, detection and apply lines to `server.log` only, with the `[slack]` prefix, through the injected `log`; none goes to `startup-errors.log` or to Slack, and none carries a token, file content, credentials digest or fingerprint. Its class list is `RELOAD_DIAGNOSTIC_CLASSES`, separate from the persona classes; it holds `reload-record-write-failed`, `reload-nothing-pending`, `reload-preview`, `reload-invalid`, `reload-applied`, `reload-noop` and `reload-stale-confirmation`. A confirmed apply emits the last three, `reload-invalid` and `reload-record-write-failed`; see [Confirmation](#confirmation-sr-85) and [The apply pipeline](#the-apply-pipeline-sr-86). See Server-Managed Startup step 2a for the rules behind the start lines and [Reload Detection Tick](#reload-detection-tick-bav2-sr-82-sr-83) for the detection lines.

| Line | When | Class |
|---|---|---|
| `[slack] Starting from the last-applied record "<record>"` | A start with a valid record | — |
| `[slack] No last-applied record: recorded the configuration file "<config>" as "<record>"` | A start with no record, after the record is written | — |
| `[slack] reload-record-write-failed: cannot write the last-applied record "<record>" (<errno code>); the server does not start and nothing is applied` | A start with no record whose record write fails before the rename; the server exits 1. The errno code appears only when the error has one | `reload-record-write-failed` |
| `[slack] reload-record-write-failed: the last-applied record "<record>" was written but its directory could not be synced (<errno code>), so it may not survive a crash, and the next start will run it; the server does not start` | A start with no record whose record was renamed into place but whose directory fsync (or open) failed; the server exits 1. The errno code appears only when the error has one | `reload-record-write-failed` |
| `[slack] Fatal: configuration error — <message>` | A start with no record whose `config.json` is missing, unreadable or invalid; exits 1 | — |
| `[slack] Fatal: last-applied record error — <cause> Deleting the last-applied record "<record>" makes the next start apply the configuration file "<config>" as it stands.` | A start whose record exists but cannot be read or validated; exits 1. Unclassed: SR-10.3 gives it no class | — |
| `[slack] reload-preview: A configuration change is pending; nothing has been applied. <counts, or no effective change: …> (preview in "<pending>")`, then `[slack] reload-preview: <line>` for each further preview line | A detection pass whose pending state changes to pending with a new fingerprint, for a valid candidate: every line of the preview, the same text as the pending file's body. Only the first line carries the suffix, and it is dropped when this pass's pending-file write failed | `reload-preview` |
| `[slack] reload-invalid: the pending configuration is invalid and nothing will be applied: <error> (preview in "<pending>")` | The same, for an invalid candidate: a single line (not a multi-line preview), with the loader's full error, or `the configuration file "<config>" does not exist.` / `… cannot be read (<code>).` / `… is larger than the 64 KiB limit.`; no `reload-preview` line. The suffix is dropped when the write failed | `reload-invalid` |
| `[slack] reload-invalid: the confirmed configuration is invalid, so nothing is applied: <error>` | A matching confirmation of an invalid candidate; the same errors as above. Nothing is applied and the record is untouched. It follows the pending-time line logged when the `INVALID` preview was written; when the confirmation was already present on the pass that first saw the candidate (for example at start), that pass logs only this line | `reload-invalid` |
| `[slack] reload-applied: applied the confirmed configuration change without a restart (personas: <n> added, <n> removed, <n> destructively modified, <n> modified in place, <n> with changed credentials; server-wide settings: <n> changed); the last-applied record "<record>" now holds it` | A confirmed valid change with an effect, after step 1 and every bound later step. The counts are the preview header's | `reload-applied` |
| `[slack] reload-noop: the confirmed configuration has no effective change, so no persona and no server-wide setting changed; the last-applied record "<record>" was rewritten with it` | A confirmed `no effective change` candidate, after the record was rewritten | `reload-noop` |
| `[slack] reload-stale-confirmation: the confirmation "<apply>" <reason>; nothing is applied` | A confirmation that doesn't match the current files (`does not match the configuration as it stands (…)`), can't be read (`cannot be read (<code>)`, `is larger than the 64 KiB limit`) or has no fingerprint (`holds no well-formed fingerprint (…)`). Once per confirmation | `reload-stale-confirmation` |
| `[slack] reload-record-write-failed: cannot write the last-applied record "<record>" (<errno code>); the confirmed change is not applied and stays pending` | A confirmed apply's record write failed before the rename; nothing is applied | `reload-record-write-failed` |
| `[slack] reload-record-write-failed: wrote the last-applied record "<record>" but could not sync its directory (<errno code>); <restore outcome>; the confirmed change is not applied and stays pending` | A confirmed apply's record write whose directory sync failed. `<restore outcome>` is `the previous record was written back`, `the previous record was written back, though its directory could not be synced either`, or `writing the previous record back failed too (<errno code>), so the next start may run the unapplied change` | `reload-record-write-failed` |
| `[slack] reload: cannot remove the confirmation "<apply>" (<errno code>); it was acted on once and is ignored until its content changes` | The confirmation's delete failed. Once per confirmation content; later deletes are retried silently | — |
| `[slack] reload: removed the confirmation "<apply>" but could not sync its directory (<errno code>); it may reappear after a crash` | The confirmation's unlink succeeded but its directory sync failed; counts as removed | — |
| `[slack] reload: apply step <n> (<step>) failed: <thrown value>` | A step body (2–6) threw as a whole; the next step still runs. Described by `describeThrownValue` | — |
| `[slack] reload: apply step <n> (<step>) failed for persona "<name>" (key=<key>): <thrown value>` | One persona's lifecycle operation in a default step body (2, 3, 4 or 6) rejected; the step's other personas and the next step still run. Described by `describeThrownValue` | — |
| `[slack] reload: updating the server's applied configuration failed: <thrown value>` | `onApplied` threw at step 1; the apply goes on | — |
| `[slack] reload: cannot check <what>: <thrown value>; the preview says it could not be checked` | A fact the preview needs could not be gathered: `<what>` is `whether the added persona "<name>" (key=<key>) can come up`, `the claude_config_dir of persona "<name>" (key=<key>)` (a changed one) or `the bring-up state of persona "<name>" (key=<key>)`; the preview carries it as unknown. Once, until a pass gathers every fact again. Described by `describeThrownValue` | — |
| `[slack] reload-nothing-pending: the configuration file and the credentials files it references match what is applied; no change is pending, and the pending-change file "<pending>" is removed` | Once, when the pass that removed the pending file changes the state from pending to nothing pending, or when the first pass after a start actually removes a leftover pending file (including a removal whose directory sync failed). Never at a clean start | `reload-nothing-pending` |
| `[slack] reload-nothing-pending: the configuration file and the credentials files it references match what is applied; no change is pending` | Once, when the state changes from pending to nothing pending and there was no pending file to remove | `reload-nothing-pending` |
| `[slack] reload: cannot write the pending-change file "<pending>" (<errno code>); retrying at the next check` | A pending-file write failed before its rename; once per episode. An episode ends at the next success or when the derived state changes | — |
| `[slack] reload: wrote the pending-change file "<pending>" but could not sync its directory (<errno code>); writing it again at the next check` | A pending-file write whose directory sync failed; once per episode. An episode ends at the next success or when the derived state changes | — |
| `[slack] reload: cannot remove the pending-change file "<pending>" (<errno code>); retrying at the next check` | A pending-file unlink failed; the file stays. Once per episode (an episode ends at the next success or when the derived state changes) | — |
| `[slack] reload: removed the pending-change file "<pending>" but could not sync its directory (<errno code>); it may reappear after a crash, and the next start removes it` | A pending-file delete whose unlink succeeded but whose directory open or fsync failed (`DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError`). Treated as removed: the removed variant of `reload-nothing-pending` follows when due. Once per such removal; not retried | — |
| `[slack] reload: detection check failed: <thrown value>; checking again at the next tick` | Logged by the controller (`reload.ts`): a detection pass failed unexpectedly; once per episode (until a pass succeeds). The thrown value is described by `describeThrownValue` (no message) | — |
| `[slack] reload: detection check failed: <thrown value>; checking again in 5 s` | Logged by the tick driver (`reload-timer.ts`), only for a pass that throws past the controller's own catch; every such pass, then the chain re-arms. Described by `describeThrownValue` | — |
| `[slack] reload: detection timer start() called more than once — ignoring (the timer is single-use)` (or `… called after stop() …`) | A programming error: the single-use tick driver was started twice or after stop | — |

The operator-facing entries, with fixes, are in `skills/debug-slack-channel-bots/SKILL.md`.

### Log file locations

Both paths are rooted in `SLACK_STATE_DIR` (default: `~/.claude/channels/slack/`).

| Process | Log file |
|---------|----------|
| Server daemon (`server.ts`) | `STATE_DIR/server.log` |
| `clean_restart` subcommand | `STATE_DIR/clean_restart.log` |
| cscb_cron fire log (`cron-log.ts`) | `cron_log_path` (default `<config dir>/cron.log`) |

All three are opened in append mode — multiple restarts accumulate in the same file rather than overwriting it; only the first two are subject to the size-based rotation above.

## Endpoint Inventory

The HTTP server serves `/interject` and `/mcp` only; any other path is a 404. Neither triggers or describes a reload (see Security Model, Reload is file-triggered only), and a new endpoint must not either.

### POST /interject

Injects a message into one persona's live Claude session without going through Slack. Localhost-only. Handler: `handleInterject` in `src/interject.ts`, wired by `server.ts`.

**Request body** (JSON, max 32 KB)

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|-------|------|----------|-------------|
| `persona` | string | yes | Target persona's name or key (exact, case-sensitive match) |
| `message` | string | yes | Message content to inject |
| `sender` | string | no | Display name; defaults to `"interject"` |

**Success response**

`200 OK` — `{ ok: true, persona: <name> }` (the persona's name, even when the request used its key)

**Status codes**

| Status | Condition | Body `error` |
|--------|-----------|--------------|
| 200 | Notification handed to the persona's session | — |
| 400 | Invalid JSON | `Invalid JSON` |
| 400 | Missing, empty or non-string `persona` (including a body with only the old `channel` field) | `Missing or invalid field: persona (string) required` |
| 400 | Missing, empty or non-string `message` | `Missing or invalid field: message (string) required` |
| 403 | Non-localhost origin | `Forbidden` |
| 404 | No applied persona has that name or key, or there is no persona config | `Persona not found in the applied config` |
| 405 | Non-POST method | `Method Not Allowed` |
| 413 | Body exceeds 32 KB | `Request body too large (max 32KB)` |
| 503 | The persona is in the applied config but not up (broken or retrying), even when its instance is running or its session is still registered; the body also carries `persona` (its name) | `Persona is not up` |
| 503 | The persona is up but has no registered, connected session | `No active session for this persona` |

**Behavior**

- Resolves `persona` against the applied persona config (`resolvePersonaTarget`); 404 if absent
- Asks the injected `isPersonaUp` (the server's shared up predicate, `createPersonaUpPredicate`; b.av2 SR-6.4) before any session lookup. A persona that is not up gets 503 and one line, `[slack] /interject: refused for persona "<name>" (key=<key>) — the persona is not up; nothing delivered`; nothing reaches its instance
- Calls `getSessionByPersona()` with the persona's key; 503 if none or not connected
- Logs `[slack] /interject: delivering to persona "<name>" (key=<key>) sender="<label>" message="<first 80 chars>"`
- Sends `notifications/claude/channel` to that session only, with `content: message` and `meta: { user, ts }` — no `chat_id`, `message_id` or `via`. `user` is the `sender` label; `ts` is `String(Date.now() / 1000)`, which has at most three fractional digits. The absence of `via` is what the Slack Reply Guard uses to tell an injected message from a delivered Slack one (see [Slack Reply Guard](#slack-reply-guard-stop-hook)).
- Does not await the notification: a later rejection is logged as `[slack] /interject: notification to persona … failed: …`
- Does not call the Slack API

## Security Model

- **Inbound delivery** (b.av2 SR-4.2): every inbound event goes through the receiving persona's pipeline (`src/persona-routing.ts`), whose delivery rules are in `src/delivery-decision.ts`. A persona receives its configured channels, each by its `delivery` setting, and, with `dm.enabled` on, direct messages to its own app (b.av2 SR-4.3). A group DM (`channel_type` `mpim`) is never delivered; an @mention in a group DM arrives without `channel_type` and is decided by the channel rules, so it is delivered only to a persona that lists that conversation ID. Its own messages are dropped, and that is the only sender filter: any other author, a workspace user who has never posted before included, reaches the persona wherever it receives: there is no sender allowlist and no per-sender approval step
- **Outbound scoping** (b.av2 SR-5.1): every MCP tool resolves the calling session's persona and its client at call time and may target only that persona's configured channels and, while its `dm.enabled` is on, a `D…` DM conversation or (for `reply` only) a user ID, which is opened with `conversations.open` on the persona's client before the post (`checkPersonaTarget`). With `dm.enabled` off, no tool call opens a DM (`conversations.open`) or acts on a `D…` or user-ID target for that persona, including a `chat.postMessage` to a user ID, which Slack would turn into an implicit DM open. A refused target returns a tool error naming the persona and the target, and no Slack call is made. The call posts as the persona, with no username or icon override. The server's own posts to a persona's destination (prompts, the stuck-prompt warning, notices) are held to the same DMs switch, though not through `checkPersonaTarget`: a `dm` destination with DMs off is refused with no Slack call (`persona-destination.ts`); the one exception is an update to a prompt already posted in a DM (see [In-memory state](#in-memory-state-sr-1))
- **File exfiltration guard**: `assertSendable()` (`src/lib.ts`) blocks uploading files from the state directory (outside `inbox/`) and every persona credentials file named by the applied configuration or by the configuration file as it currently stands (b.av2 SR-5.2), compared by real path so a symlink to one is refused too. **State-directory rule:** a file is refused when it is inside the state directory and not inside `inbox/`, by either of two checks: one on the paths as given, and the same check on the real paths of the file and of both directories. So a symlink outside the state directory that points into it (for example at a credentials-holding `.env` there) is refused, and one that points into `inbox/` is allowed. Both checks respect path-component boundaries: `<state dir>/inbox-old/x` is not in the inbox, and `<state dir>2/x` is not in the state directory. A path that does not exist gets only the as-given check. The list is rebuilt per call from the reload controller's `protectedCredentialsFiles()` (`credentialsFilesToProtect` over the applied set, which is the last-applied record's set at a start from the record, plus every `credentials_file` string in `config.json` read tolerantly now), so a credentials file named only by an unapplied edit is protected too. That read of `config.json` is the one read the 64 KiB size cap does not cover (`readPersonaConfigBytes` with `uncapped`): the whole file is read, so a path named only in an oversized unapplied `config.json` is still refused. It is still opened and stat'ed first, so a non-regular file is never read (see Reload files, Size cap). Before `main()` builds the controller there is no applied set, and only `config.json`'s paths are refused. It never opens the file. `reply` checks every file up front and re-checks each one right before its upload
- **Attachment download token scope**: `download_attachment` sends the persona's bot token only to `https://files.slack.com` URLs. External (non-Slack-hosted) files are refused, and a redirect off that origin is never followed with the Authorization header, so the token cannot reach a third-party host
- **Localhost restriction**: `/interject` is the only localhost-restricted endpoint; it accepts requests only from 127.0.0.1/::1/::ffff:127.*
- **Session scope guard**: agent-director enforces session scope via `relay_mode='on'` at spawn time. Permission requests are routed through agent-director's internal relay machinery (SR-2.1 poller), which is scoped to the spawned session. Sessions not spawned by CSCB have no relay route and no `PermissionRequest` or `PreToolUse` hook entries in `settings.json` — the `.sh` hook files are not shipped.
- **Credentials-file secrecy**: each persona's credentials file is the server's only token source (b.av2 SR-1.4, SR-10.2). The persona credentials module (`src/persona-credentials.ts`) and the bring-up checks (`checkPersonaLocalSteps` in `src/persona-start.ts`, run by the bring-up controller) read it once per persona at each bring-up (at start, at apply, and at a recovery bring-up) and once at each confirmed credentials change (apply step 4), and hand the tokens to the connection manager; the controller holds them in the persona's entry for a later directory retry and never logs, returns or re-reads them; beside them it holds only a SHA-256 digest of the bytes read (or a missing/unreadable marker). A persona whose credentials file another applied persona shares (a record-start collision) has its own path read only to be hashed: the bytes are never parsed and no token is taken from them. The reload detection tick reads each referenced credentials file every 5 s outside dry run, through the same stat-first reader, holds the bytes only for that pass (read once per path, hashed into the fingerprint), compares only digests with the held one, and drops the bytes when the pass ends; the server holds no token at module scope, and none of that code reads an environment variable. The CLI reads no token at all: it opens no credentials file. A credentials path that is not a regular file (a directory, FIFO, socket or device) is `persona-credentials-unreadable` and is never read: the file is opened non-blocking, so a FIFO cannot stall the open, and its type is checked through the same descriptor before any read. A file over the size cap is unreadable the same way and never read in full (see Reload files, Size cap). It never writes, copies, caches or persists a credentials file or any value from one; a digest is held in memory only, never written to disk or logged. It does not check or change the file mode. A bad token is described only by its key and the rule it breaks, never by its value, so no diagnostic line or failure result carries a token, and the success value redacts itself if logged.
  - **Who writes the file** (b.av2 SR-1.4, SR-12): the operator, by hand or with the setup wizard's credentials command (`## Credentials command` in `skills/setup-slack-channel-bots/SKILL.md`): one line, `claude-slack-channel-bots credentials <persona>`, which the operator runs in their own terminal once the persona is declared in `config.json` (the line stays short, so a copy from an 80-column terminal keeps it whole). The CLI finds the persona in the configuration file as it stands and runs the packaged `scripts/write-credentials.sh` with `bash` for its `credentials_file` (see the `cli.ts` and `scripts/` entries of the Module Map). That script is the only shipped writer and the only place mode 0600 is guaranteed. It turns tracing and automatic export off (`set +xa`), checks the path and that `curl` is installed before any prompt, reads both tokens without echo, and validates the bot token with `auth.test` and the app token with `apps.connections.open` (each token reaches `curl` only on its standard input, as a `--config -` header, so no token is on any command line or in the process list; a check passes only when the reply's leading field is `"ok":true`). Only then does it write exactly `bot_token` and `app_token` to a temporary file created exclusively with `mktemp` beside the target under `umask 077` and `chmod 600` (removed by a trap on any failure or interrupt), check the file is not empty, and rename it onto the target; if the target appeared, vanished, became or stopped being a symlink, or became a directory while it ran (checked again after the rename, removing the moved temporary file from a directory that appeared at the path), nothing is written. The rename replaces a symlink at the path with a regular file, leaving the old tokens in the file it pointed to. An empty answer at the replace prompt declines, and `curl -q` ignores the operator's `~/.curlrc`. The server never writes the file and never polices its mode, and the CLI process itself reads no token. No shipped skill asks for, accepts or prints a token, or displays a credentials file's content: the wizard never takes a token in chat and checks only that the file exists and its mode, and the debugging skill's shape check (`jq`) reports key names and prefixes only. Rotation is the same command for the persona, then a confirmed credentials change (see Reload).
- **Reload is file-triggered only** (b.av2 SR-8.8): a change is applied only when the operator puts a confirmation at `config.json.apply` beside `config.json`. That rename is the only trigger, made by the operator or by an agent the operator explicitly directs. No HTTP endpoint, MCP tool or CLI subcommand (listed or hidden) triggers, confirms or performs a reload, and no MCP instruction text, tool definition or CLI usage text describes it; a confirmation's content is never logged (the stale line names only its path and the reason). This is deliberate opacity, not a security boundary: the server and the persona instances run as the same OS user, so a bot could make the rename itself.
  - **The surfaces as built.** The CLI accepts only `start`, `stop` (with `--stop-bots`) and `clean_restart`; any other first argument (`reload`, `apply` and the like included) prints the same usage text and exits 1, writing nothing. The HTTP server serves only `/interject` and `/mcp` (see Endpoint Inventory). The MCP server lists five tools (`reply`, `react`, `edit_message`, `fetch_messages`, `download_attachment`), and any other tool name is an `Unknown tool` error. The start, stop and clean_restart sections above describe no reload behaviour: those commands only read the last-applied record through `readAppliedPersonaConfig` and never write a reload file.
  - **Pinned by AC 74 tests.** `tests/cli.test.ts` (`unknown subcommand`) checks the usage text lists exactly the four subcommands and the one flag, carries no reload wording, and that hidden spellings take the unknown-subcommand path with the temp tree unchanged. `tests/registry.test.ts` (`tool list and instructions`) checks every tool definition and the MCP instructions carry no reload wording, and that `reload` and `apply_config` tool calls are refused. The shared term list is in the testing guide's Unadvertised Reload Checks section.
- **Reload output stays local** (b.av2 SR-7.2, SR-8.3): the reload detection tick's output (the pending file, the preview lines, every detection line and every apply line) goes only to `config.json.pending` and the server log, never to Slack; nothing about a pending or applied change is posted or held as a notice. Neither the pending file nor any reload line carries a token, credentials content or a digest: credentials changes are named by persona and path only, the change plan they are rendered from holds no digest, an added persona's can't-come-up cause names keys and rules only (`credentialsReadProblem`), and the pending file's fingerprint is a SHA-256 over the files, from which no content can be read back. An `INVALID` line repeats the loader's error, which echoes no rejected value, no file text and no unknown key name that isn't a plain setting name (`ECHOABLE_KEY_NAME_RE`), so a token pasted into `config.json`, even as a key, never reaches either.
- **Slack validation outcome secrecy** (b.av2 SR-10.3): outcomes from `src/persona-slack-validation.ts` carry only the class, the token key, the check, and the Slack error code or reason kind (plus an HTTP status or `retryAfter` number). The log lines `src/persona-slack-episodes.ts` builds from them carry those fields plus the persona reference (entry position, name and key) and the credentials-file path.
