Neither ever carries the raw error object, its message, the request, a header or a token value. The connection manager (`src/persona-connections.ts`) uses them. The Web API auth-failure watch (`src/persona-web-api-watch.ts`, bug b.ujn) copies only the Slack error code and the method name (only when it matches `SLACK_METHOD_NAME_RE`) from a rejected call; it never logs, stores or modifies the error, the caller still sees the original rejection, and its local refusal (`WebApiCallRefusedLocallyError`) carries only that code and method name.
- **Persona Slack client error secrecy** (b.av2 SR-3.3): no persona Slack client (`src/persona-slack-clients.ts`) attaches the original request, and so the Authorization header, to its errors. `attachOriginalToWebAPIRequestError: false` is set by every option builder, so on every client the connection manager builds: the short-lived validation client (`validationWebClientOptions`), the Socket Mode client's own Web API client for `apps.connections.open` (its `clientOptions`, from the private `validationRequestLimits()`: the validation client's limits without its logger), and the long-lived Web API client (`longLivedWebClientOptions`). A credentials reconnect builds its validation, Web API and Socket Mode clients through the same factory and option sets, so it adds no token surface; the new tokens are held like the old ones and replace them only at the swap (or at once for a retrying persona). The `persona-credentials-change-failed` line carries only the persona reference, the path and a token-free cause (a credentials-check cause or the Slack classifier's refused cause). The connection manager logs a thrown value only through `describeThrownValue` (constructor name, a short `code`, the message through `redactSlackLogText` as `message="…"`, stack frames), and the exported `unhandledRejection` handler (`src/persona-connection-errors.ts`) logs the same way, so neither writes a token value. `server.ts` installs the handler once in `main()`; the event router, `connectPersonaSlack` and the bring-up controller also log thrown values only through `describeThrownValue`.
- **Slack API endpoint override is loopback only** (E14 Task 4): every persona Slack client talks to the library's default Slack API, except when `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` holds an `http://127.0.0.1…` or `http://[::1]…` URL (literal addresses; `localhost` is refused) with no user info, query or fragment (the integration suite's Slack stub). Any other value is ignored with a warning that does not echo it. The honoured line logs only the URL's origin. The persona tokens then go only to whatever listens on that loopback port, which is trusted with them. See Environment Variables.
- **Slack library logging** (b.av2 SR-10.3): every persona Socket Mode client is built with `socketModeSlackLogger(persona, log)` (`src/persona-slack-clients.ts`) in place of the library's console logger, which `initLogging()` would route into `server.log`. Under Bun, a failed WebSocket handshake's error text holds the full WebSocket URL, and its `ticket=` query is a short-lived bearer for the app's socket, which the library prints at its default level; "Failed to retrieve a new WSS URL" prints whatever the Web API error says. So the logger is an allowlist, not a filter: only whole lines matching the library's fixed-text ping/pong timeouts are forwarded as they are, and a failed ping is forwarded as `Failed to send ping to Slack` without its runtime error text. The socket client passes the logger on to its own `apps.connections.open` client (whose `clientOptions` carry none) and its WebSocket, so their lines go through the same allowlist. The connection manager logs every connection failure itself, through `describeThrownValue`. The validation and long-lived Web API clients get `redactingSlackLogger()`: the token travels only in the Authorization header and they don't attach the original request, but a failed request's message can hold the request URL (a `filesUploadV2` upload URL is a short-lived bearer), and a runtime's header check could echo a header value, so every argument has URL-like text replaced by `<redacted-url>` and token-like text (a deliberately wide pattern) by `<redacted-token>` before it reaches the console.
- **Persona names and paths are echoed as written** (b.av2 SR-1.2, SR-2.2): a persona's name and its key are shown everywhere a persona is named (log lines, loader errors, previews, Slack notices, tool results, agent-director labels, the instance ID and the tmux session name). A name has no format rule and is neither rejected nor masked: the operator chooses it, and it is not a credential. Persona paths (`credentials_file`, `working_directory`, `claude_config_dir`) and `ack_reaction` are echoed as written in errors and log lines for the same reason. Unknown key names are echoed only when they are plain setting names (`ECHOABLE_KEY_NAME_RE`), since a key could be a pasted token. Message content is archived and debug-logged unfiltered. Redaction covers exactly two things: the Slack libraries' own log output (the allowlist and `redactingSlackLogger` above, and `redactSlackLogText` inside every describer of a thrown value), and CSCB's own credentials, which no loader or credentials error echoes (a bad token is named only by its key and rule).
- **No token in any output** (b.av2 SR-10.3, AC 20): no log line, error, server-written file (`config.json.pending`, `config.json.last-applied`, `reply-guard/<key>`, the permission trail), preview, Slack notice or MCP tool result carries a Slack token value, credentials content or a credentials digest. The last-applied record is a byte copy of a config file that passed the loader, which rejects unknown keys, and credentials live only in the credentials files. What persona, reload and apply output may carry:
  - the persona reference (entry position, JSON-quoted name and key), paths, class labels and fixed wording;
  - Slack error codes that are safe identifiers, and Web API method names only when they match `SLACK_METHOD_NAME_RE` (the `persona-credentials-refused` "Web API call" cause from `src/persona-web-api-watch.ts`, and `WebApiCallRefusedLocallyError`);
  - errno codes, in `persona-config-dir-unresolvable` (its cleared line carries none) and the JSONL check's "transcript not checked" line; the launch's "could not be resolved" line and the restart kill adapter's "not killing" line name only the persona and the setting;
  - thrown values only through `describeThrownValue` or `describeSlackCallFailure` (the teardown's "before its turn failed" line, the connection manager's reconnect failures); `persona-credentials-change-failed` carries a credentials-check or refused cause, never a thrown value; the `template refresh:` failure line carries an agent-director error's `errName` (only when it passes `isSafeIdentifier`) and its description as `message="…"` (`describeLogMessage`), or the `describeThrownValue` description of any other value (a typed error whose `errName` fails the check included).
  - every log line, startup-error record and notice on a Slack or agent-director path (the persona connections, the permission poller and click handler, restart, the health check and its stat route, the outage state, the session manager's launches and orphan cleanup, the JSONL check, the CLI's teardown and `stop --stop-bots`, `isSessionAlive`) renders a thrown value through `describeThrownValue`, never the raw error or its raw message (b.av2 SR-10.3): the message appears only as `message="…"`, through `redactSlackLogText`, on one line and capped at 300 characters (`describeLogMessage`). An agent-director `errName` is printed, by that describer or directly, only when it passes `isSafeIdentifier`; `describeAgentDirectorFailure` (one copy in the session manager, one in the permission poller) renders `<errName> message="<description>"` with the description through `describeLogMessage`, and any other value through `describeThrownValue`. The session manager's startup-error records (`spawn-failed`, `orphan-cleanup*`, `jsonl-transcript-lost-on-resume`, `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive`) carry that description in their message, never the error itself; the JSONL diagnosis's fallback description is redacted the same way. The spawn-failure notice posted to Slack shows the checked label and the redacted description, capped at 300 characters after redaction. The CLI's `stop --stop-bots` initialization-failed and teardown-failed lines print the message of a failure CSCB built (`StartupGateFailedError`, `TeardownIncompleteError`) and describe anything else. The permission trail keeps `raw_error_message` for forensics (SR-V-2.7), redacted by `redactSlackLogText` before it is written;
  - deliberate exceptions, which print an error's own text (the `RAW_ERROR_ALLOWED` list of the source audit in `tests/secrecy-audit.test.ts`, each with its reason): the `[slack] Fatal:` catch-alls (`src/cli.ts`, `src/server.ts`), the only text of an unexpected start failure; the CLI's config-load lines (`stop`, `stop --stop-bots`, `clean_restart`, `credentials`), which carry the loader's token-free message and, for a record, its deletion hint; the `clean_restart` `fatal(...)` lines, which in production receive the CSCB-built errors above; the boot template install's failure, recorded verbatim; the startup gate's message, which may embed agent-director's description or a construct error and is the same text the server writes to `startup-errors.log`; npm postinstall's agent-director probe, run with no config or credentials loaded; and paths that touch neither Slack nor agent-director (MCP roots and connect, the message archive's open, cron, permission trail and cron log writes, cozempic, the PID read, postinstall's skill symlinks, the Stop-hook and trust patches' catch-alls);
  - MCP tool errors name the persona, the target and at most a Slack platform error code. The lost-message notice, `not up` state included, carries fixed wording and the sender's label only.
